# fleetmux design baseline

> This document fixes design constraints; it is not the temporary configuration of a demo.
> Historical implementation base: `0c43b9c`; the Pi/profile increment comes from `feat/pi-agents`, and the Redis v2 base modules landed separately on the local `main@5f973d5`.
> **Passing development verification does not mean reviewed, merged or deployed to production.** Verification status of the old implementation: §10.
> **Redis blocking communication + control detail display.** Target design: §11. §11 explicitly overrides the old file-delivery / mechanical result relay requirements below; **the v2 base modules and the all-Pi daemon/bridge/control have an integration candidate, and partial closed loops with real Redis/SDK/TUI are verified; full acceptance is still not done (see §14)**, so merging the modules must not be described as the running code being upgraded.

## 1. Extend the original design; build no new framework

Kept: **one supervisor + N workers + one daemon + one private Redis**.

- The supervisor judges, dispatches and reviews results; it does not do the workers' tasks for them.
- Workers execute the dispatched work; different duties live in their own role prompts, and an agent does not change its identity on its own.
- The daemon handles delivery, state, verification, timeouts, rotation and history records; it does not rely on a model claiming "success" and does not read the screen to decide that a turn has ended.
- pi is another agent CLI adapter; it does not replace the daemon and adds no second dispatch or memory/handoff system.
- **Every mechanism lives inside the fleet framework; no system facilities are attached.** Guarding, watchdog, heartbeat, restart, periodic checks and the like are done by the fleet's own roles: the daemon manages the agents (watchdog, rotation, restarting the supervisor), and the supervisor manages the daemon with the commands written in AGENTS.md (it restarts the daemon itself when the heartbeat times out). No crontab, launchd, systemd, external resident script or other system-level facility may be added to run the fleet. External callers (such as a scheduled-job driver script) starting and stopping a fleet are not covered by this rule.
- The original task/service modes, command-line entry points, request/result protocol and worker alias routing are kept.

Design sources: the original README (under the earlier project name) and live code; the business side's original requirements (not published with the repo); later explicit requirements on the shared-page layout, generation labels, the official Claude CLI, control/history in one column and manual dispatch.

**This round is not a redesign. Switching CLIs is no excuse to change the division of labor above, and temporary acceptance roles or parameters must not become the default design.**

## 2. One main page with a fixed layout

```text
Fleet agents (one workspace)
┌────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┐
│ supervisor-1.N                 │ [worker-1.N] [worker-2.N] ...  │
│ live supervisor session        │ worker tabs share one pane     │
├────────────────────────────────┤                                │
│ [history] [control]            │ live window of the selected    │
│ history shown by default       │ worker                         │
└────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────┘
```

| Part | Constraint |
|---|---|
| supervisor | Owns the top left alone; never merged with control into one column |
| workers | Tabs of one pane on the right; not one workspace per worker |
| history | Built-in browser at the bottom left; shown by default; browse historical screens and handoff notes by agent/generation, full-text search |
| control | A **background tab in the same pane** as history; opened only for troubleshooting, takes no screen space by default |
| daemon | The scheduler in the control terminal; not another AI and not a second supervisor |

At startup a temporary control workspace may be created; once the main layout is ready its terminal moves into the history column. Only the old workspace that is now empty is closed; other windows are never force-closed. If the move fails, the control console must be kept; the daemon is never killed to hide a UI.

Moving control only changes the layout: it must not restart Redis/daemon/agents, change request states or interrupt tasks.

### 2.1 Linux unified left-sidebar layout (ported to the 42-item acceptance baseline)

This section overrides the historical "Linux content mirror" below and its description of the right-hand tools/split: the Linux default becomes **left sidebar | center with Supervisor on top and History/Control below | Workers on the right**, with history selected by default. Layout ratios, selection, zoom, PTY, the history overlay and scrollbars keep sharing the original geometry.

- Native GTK HeaderBar: left-aligned tools `◫ + ↺ ← →`, a 1px vertical rule, fleet title/status; the system window buttons keep GTK's default left/right position, size, spacing and behavior. The vertical rule aligns with the center of the sidebar border stroke `(sidebar + 0.5) * cell`.
- `sidebar_min` is still computed from the real tool width, the header row's left offset and the page's cell width; native buttons on the left count toward the offset, native buttons on the right take no sidebar space. The server's existing split between effective width and requested width, collapse, hiding in narrow windows and overflow guards are unchanged.
- `native_header`, sidebar direction and explicit content mirror are independent, and none is persisted. Linux keeps one header-gap row; with the GUI handshake `native_header:1` the grid tools are not drawn a second time. A plain terminal/old viewer shows clickable tools and the title in the same gap row without moving the shared panes/PTYs; before the sidebar metadata arrives, no tools are drawn and the hit geometry does not change.
- `FLEETMUX_LAYOUT_MIRROR` is only an explicit test entry for the old layout, default false; `FLEETMUX_NATIVE_HEADER=0|false|no` is the regression entry for the grid top bar, `1|true|yes` forces native mode, any other value follows the platform.
- **macOS follows the d82c48d2 acceptance baseline**: the two-row header band, row-2 tabs/cards, the native traffic-light position and the 81px inset computation are all unchanged; main's old Mac single-row/78px geometry is not ported. The IME caret/candidate, page scrollbar, input diagnostics and restore/PTY reclaim fixes are not rolled back.
- GUI/server/web ship as matching versions; restarting the server at deployment needs separate authorization. Code porting and targeted tests do not equal the full gate, real-machine re-verification or live deployment.

#### Historical note: Linux content mirror (superseded by the section above)

Layout change: **macOS keeps the existing content layout drawn above** (sidebar on the left, supervisor/history stack in the center, workers on the right); **on UTM Linux it is mirrored left-right**: workers on the left, a center column with supervisor on top and history/daemon below, the fleet sidebar on the far right. This is a runtime property of the same `Layout` (`Layout::set_mirror`, derived from the platform at startup, not written to `layout.json`, derived again on resume), and it must live in the geometry, not in a CSS patch: one mirror in `rects()`, and drawing, mouse hits, both ratio drags, the sidebar width drag, zoom rectangles, the history overlay, scrollbars, PTY sizes and restore all follow it; the bottom status line is not mirrored. The macOS row-0 top bar is not mirrored either (tools on the left, fleet title/status on the right); the Linux grid no longer keeps a row-0 top bar, which is taken over by a GTK headerbar the window draws itself (see below).

Top bar requirements: close/minimize/maximize must be **native system buttons** with the system's default position, size, spacing and behavior; they must **not** be faked with self-drawn HTML, and the macOS traffic lights must **not** move to follow the terminal font size; the tools (sidebar toggle/new bash/restore fleet/navigation) and the fleet title/status on the right sit in the same top-bar container and share one vertical center.

- macOS: the transparent title bar puts the native traffic lights over grid row 0; the page shifts the whole row down by the native buttons' center and shrinks the grid height accordingly (the last row is not cut off). Row 0 is that unified top-bar container: tools on the left, fleet title/status on the right, same height, same center.
- Linux (A26 rev5/rev7, rev 6 `utm_topbar` + rev 10 `top_container_alignment`): **one full row of native headerbar**, holding the two top containers of the two confirmed sketches. The window title bar is a GTK `GtkHeaderBar` (`gtk_window_set_titlebar`, client-side decoration; GTK asks the window manager not to draw its own title bar), so the same row holds: ① GTK's own close/minimize/maximize: native controls whose position, size, spacing and behavior all follow the system `gtk-decoration-layout` default (top right); the code hard-codes no layout and they do not move with the terminal font size; ② the **sidebar top container** (right, directly above the mirrored layout's right sidebar): a `│` separator + the toolbar `◫ + ↺ ← →` (exactly this order, laid out explicitly with `pack_start`, not with a `pack_end` loop that would reverse it), right-aligned next to the native buttons; ③ the **workspace top container** (left): the current fleet's title (bold) and `· line1` (gray), left-aligned with the same margin. Both containers fill the whole row, so their top edge, bottom edge, height and vertical center match by construction; the split between them is not a constant: `header_split` uses the headerbar's own allocation, the `sidebar`/`cols` geometry of `0x85` `topbar`, and the cell width the page reports (`cell` of the `inset` ipc, see `PROTOCOL.md` §15.4) to put the split on the grid sidebar's divider column at every allocation. **A42-LINUX-SIDEBAR-ALIGN-03 (field report)**: the page takes its width in whole cells, so the canvas it really draws is `cols · cell` (on the UTM machine 160 columns × 8 px = 1280 px, 6 px less than the 1286 px window); the denominator must be this canvas, which puts the split at `(cols − sidebar) · cell`; using the headerbar's own width is off by 5 px. The toolbar's five cells must also be the 28 px the layout requires: the theme's 16 px horizontal padding + 1 px border on headerbar buttons stretches each cell to 50 px (274 px for the whole toolbar), and that is the main reason the split got clamped by `row_w − tools_min` to 122 px inside the workspace. Now the five cells are set to 28 px by an `fm-tool` rule attached only to their own style context (GTK's native window buttons and the global theme are untouched); five cells + 8 px margin = 148 px, and in the 34-column sidebar on that machine there are exactly 152 px right of the divider column, so the split is not clamped and lands on the grid divider column. If the user drags the sidebar narrower than this floor (or the window/font size makes the toolbar wider), the window computes the minimum column count it needs from the same geometry and reports it to the server through the optional `sidebar_min` of the existing `0x06` `inset`; the server keeps the **effective** sidebar width at `max(requested width, 24, inset+15, sidebar_min)` (when the content's minimum columns do not fit, it hides per rev12 instead of clamping only the header), so the grid split, drawing, hits and the header split move together, and "only the header clamped to the floor while the body stays put" no longer happens. The width the user requested is still stored in kv and not overwritten; when collapsed the sidebar width is still 0. `sidebar_min` is the larger of two measured constraints in the same coordinate space, not a fixed column count: ① the toolbar must fit: the grid divider column `(cols − sidebar) · cell` must not pass the row's right end minus the toolbar's intrinsic width (the row's right end is where GTK's native window buttons start); ② the sidebar's own pixel width must hold both the toolbar and the native window buttons overlaid on its right end (the button width is measured from their real allocation, not a theme constant); otherwise the controls would hang over a page area that does not belong to the sidebar. So both at the legal drag floor of 24 columns (`SIDEBAR_MIN_W`) and with a narrow cell (e.g. 7 px) the body widens too instead of only the header being clamped; the semantics of requested width (kv) and effective width (`max(requested, 24, inset+15, sidebar_min)`, hidden when the content's minimum columns do not fit) are unchanged. The wire upper bound of `sidebar_min` is `u16::MAX` (`PROTOCOL.md` §6.3): such a large value is likewise decided only by the layout's minimum-content-column rule (hide), and the comparison uses a wider type, so debug builds do not panic and release builds do not wrap around into a sidebar wider than the window. It follows sidebar width drags, sidebar collapse, window resizes and font-size changes. The buttons send the same prefix keys that the row-0 cells invoke (`^b s` / `^b c` / `^b r` / `^b (` / `^b )`), so both platforms share one action implementation. The page draws **no** window controls or resize handles (no HTML fakes), reports no inset and adds no padding; the mirrored layout's grid **no longer keeps a row-0 top bar** (`sidebar::topbar_cols` is 0 when mirrored, row 0 is content), so there are never two bars ("title bar + a second toolbar"); the title/status text and the sidebar band geometry go to the window process via the `topbar` key of `0x85` (`overlay::topbar_for`). The macOS content layout and the grid row-0 top bar are unchanged (tools/native buttons on the left, fleet title/status on the right, native positions untouched). This row **follows the fleetmux theme** (A26 rev7, rev 12 `linux_header_theme`): the window process builds the headerbar CSS from the already resolved palette (`GuiOpts::theme`, i.e. the page's `hello.theme` background/foreground): background, foreground, the gray status text and divider border mixed from the two, and the buttons' hover/pressed states, attached only to this headerbar's own style context, so it writes no GTK/GNOME global theme, affects no other widget, and is not hard-coded to always black or always white (a light theme gives a light headerbar with dark text); without a theme GTK's own look is kept. The native buttons are still GTK's own controls; only their colors follow the theme.

- Separate header height and tab alignment (A26 rev7, rev 10 `macos_header_tab_separation` + rev 11 `workspace_tabs_align_first_fleet`): `Layout::header_rows()` gives the **header band** above the content: on macOS the row-0 top bar plus one blank separator row (`TOPBAR_ROWS = 2`), on Linux one blank row under the native headerbar (`HEADER_GAP = 1`, set by `Layout::for_platform`, not written to `layout.json`). `rects()` starts the content on the row after this band, `hit_test` returns no tab, divider column or pane inside the band (only macOS row 0 is still the top-bar tool area), and `ratio_from_y_in` converts with the same band; the first card row of `render::sidebar` uses the same `top` (shared by drawing, hits, blank-area double-click and the close placeholder), so "the supervisor/worker tab row at the top edge of the workspace" and "the first row of the first fleet card in the sidebar" start on the same row with the same control center line, and one clear row separates the global header from the tab row (not local padding or a text offset). The same metric holds after dragging the sidebar width, collapsing/expanding, changing the font size or resizing the window.

## 3. Identity and generations: every display uses the same label

| Logical slot | First generation, generation=0 | First handoff, generation=1 | Later |
|---|---|---|---|
| `supervisor` | `supervisor-1` | `supervisor-1.1` | `supervisor-1.2`, … |
| `worker-1` | `worker-1` | `worker-1.1` | `worker-1.2`, … |
| `worker-2` | `worker-2` | `worker-2.1` | `worker-2.2`, … |

- Logical slots are stable; a handoff only increments the generation. Old generation names keep routing to that slot **within the same run**.
- The `label` in the Redis agent state is the source of the display identity; the cmux tab, pi `--name`, terminal title, results and history page must agree.
- Do not override the identity with a working-directory name or a model name; do not invent a separate numbering by hand; do not patch it with a rename afterwards.
- The original cmux tab may keep an `fx-` decoration prefix; it is not a new logical slot or generation.
- `worker-1..N` is the default. The original `--worker-names` feature is kept for **callers that explicitly assign role slots**; demos or generic sessions must not switch to names such as `checker` on their own.
- **Worker slot names end in `-<number>`** (every worker carries a `-1`-style suffix so its name reads as an identity): role-based names carry a number too, e.g. `proto-1`, `term-iface-1`, `reviewer-1`, and their generations read `proto-1` → `proto-1.1` → `proto-1.2`, the same shape as `worker-1` → `worker-1.1` in the table above. `roster.SLOT_SUFFIX_RE` (`.*-[0-9]+`) is checked only where **new slots** are created; one bad entry rejects the whole roster, and the message names the entry and gives the fix (`workers[1] (proto): … rename it proto-1`): roster rows of `start --roster` / `--kit-fleet`, `start --worker-names` (all before provisioning), rows added by the §16 hot reload, rows added by `start --resume --roster`, and the workers of a named sub-fleet (§29) roster that is **newly declared or whose `roster` file changed** (the parent's `start` checks all entries). **Names that already exist in this run are never rechecked**: resume (§17), the first roster re-read after resume, roster changes by `fleet advisor on|off`, and another up of a sub-fleet this run declared earlier all keep old names working. Not covered by this rule: the supervisor `supervisor`, the advisor `advisor` (their display names already are `supervisor-1` / `advisor` → `advisor-1.1`), and sub-fleet names `subfleets[].name` (a fleet name: session name `fleetmux:<name>`, `fx:subfleet:<name>`, `sub/<name>/`; not a slot, no generation; its `supervisor_slot` is a parent worker, already checked with the parent roster). The night roster uses the same names as the day roster (`same_slots`), profile / live switches do not rename, and §19's `M<n>` sub-fleets use `worker-1..N`; none of these adds names. Known gap: a sub-fleet roster edited while that sub-fleet is down starts straight from the new file at the next up, without a name check (edits made while it is up are checked by the sub-daemon's own §16 hot reload).
- Role prompt, CLI type, model and agent identity are different concepts. Changing the model does not mean renaming.
- Recreating the whole fleet is a new run and does not pretend to inherit the old run's generations; the old run's files are kept and do not claim to belong to the new history page.

## 4. Handoff and history keep the original implementation

1. The daemon reads usage at idle delivery boundaries (since §32 also once when a turn ends, the slot is idle and no message is waiting, without waiting for the next message); mid-turn (outside delivery boundaries / idle) the §34 probe decides separately, without waiting for idle.
2. Once `handoff_ratio` is reached, the old generation is asked to fill in a three-section handoff draft.
3. The model's content is merged into a machine skeleton; if the model wrote nothing or failed, the skeleton is used, so a missing handoff text never blocks rotation.
4. Save the old screen and the handoff note; start the new generation in the original pane/tab position.
5. The new generation only reads the handoff and replies `READY`; only then does it receive waiting tasks.
6. The retired session is closed, the history page refreshes, and late callbacks are dropped by generation. A fleet paused because a handoff timeout was held is unpaused automatically once the old generation is confirmed closed and the new one is READY, under the exception in §23 "hold exit".

- The normal default threshold is **0.6 (60%)**. 1%/5% are only for explicit rotation tests and are restored to the normal value afterwards.
- When a new session is already over the threshold right after READY, it is not reopened again and again; the original `rotate.impossible` anti-loop mechanism is kept.
- pi's auto-compaction is only insurance for long turns and does not replace the fleet's cross-session handoff and history; forced mid-turn rotation is in §34.
- The original close-failure/delivery-failure handling is kept; "the machine skeleton is available" must not be inflated into a guarantee that rotation succeeds under any window/process failure.

## 5. Manual dispatch by the supervisor must close the loop

The user can say in the **pi supervisor's input box**:

```text
Ask worker-1 to reply OK, and relay its reply to me.
```

### service mode

```text
The user types plain text in the supervisor
  → pi input adapter
  → original daemon queue (checks the supervisor's current session/generation)
  → Python assigns a real ui-<uuid> request ID
  → original request / inbox / supervisor routing
  → the worker executes and returns {"reply":"OK"}
  → original JSON acceptance and publish
  → the result is queued back to the supervisor, which relays it to the user
```

- The model must not just say "notified", and must not invent a `request_id`.
- Text a human types in a terminal must not silently disappear for lack of a daemon delivery ID.
- The callback correlation check stays; this does not let any idle callback through: human input is first registered as a formal request.
- The supervisor relaying a result reports on a completed request; it must not dispatch the same request again.
- The original machine interface `request` is unchanged: `id/payload/reply.required`, optionally with `to/instruction/reply.allowed/timeout_s`.
- `accepted` only means queued; processing is complete only with a final result.
- The wire format is still JSON; "reply OK" maps to `{"reply":"OK"}`, not to an unvalidated string protocol.

### task mode

When no task is running, plain text creates a task through the original `submit()`; when a task exists, it adds instructions through the original queue. The original dispatch, artifact checks, COMPLETED/BLOCKED and budget lifecycle are reused; no separate task engine is built.

**Boundary of a lifecycle run (branch fix/lifecycle-end-input)**: in a run with `--lifecycle`, the task reaching a terminal state (completed / blocked / budget_exhausted) ends the run (§lifecycle_close: hold or close); text typed into the supervisor pane after that is **always rejected** (`Fleet.typed_task_allowed`: the reply says that task <id> has ended (<status>), this run is over, and a new run must submit its task spec with `task -`) instead of becoming a goal-only new task as the previous paragraph says; dev_task / review_loop tasks can only be task specs (deliver / review_file), and typing when no task exists is rejected right away with a pointer to the way in, instead of waiting for `validate_deliver` to report "needs deliver". Other lifecycles such as smoke still create a task as in the previous paragraph when none exists. Incident: in a deepseek fleet (97c82305), after dev_task was judged blocked, the orchestrating session typed its ruling into the pi supervisor pane; it was submitted as a new task and rejected for lacking deliver, so the ruling never reached the supervisor.

### Input boundary

- The current natural-language enqueue adapter targets the **pi supervisor** and the v2 **Claude supervisor** (UserPromptSubmit hook, see the Claude supervisor subsection of §11); it does not claim that the Codex supervisor has the same entry.
- The initial startup prompt, the daemon's `.inbox` pointer and slash commands are not enqueued again.
- Manual input to a worker remains that worker's own interaction; it does not pose as a supervisor dispatch or as the result of an existing request.
- Image attachments are not forwarded directly; the user is told plainly to use a file path instead; an image is never silently dropped with the rest treated as a normal request.
- Input that is not READY, from an old generation/thread, empty text and the like is rejected explicitly; a failed queue confirmation shows an error/unconfirmed state, never a fake success.

## 6. CLI and authentication boundaries

| Role | CLI options | Completion notice |
|---|---|---|
| supervisor | pi (v2 default, `openai-codex/gpt-6-astra` high, see the "Codex/FlashNext through pi" subsection of §11) / original Codex (optional); official Claude (v2 only, see the Claude supervisor subsection of §11) | Codex notify / pi agent_settled / Claude Stop |
| worker | original Codex / official Claude Code CLI / pi | notify / Claude Stop / agent_settled |

Explicit requirement: **Claude must be started by the fleet directly as the official `claude` CLI.**

- pi does not proxy the Claude subscription API; Claude Code subscription/OAuth credentials are never handed to pi; the official client is never impersonated.
- Local Flash Next may be used through pi's OpenAI-compatible provider; an unavailable model is never silently replaced with a cloud model.
- Every pi agent must resolve a model: its own value (supervisor `--supervisor-pi-model` / combination table `default.supervisor_pi_model`, worker roster row `pi_model`) or the fallback `--pi-model`; without one it is rejected. CLI/model/role are configurable, but the execution chain the user chose is never replaced without reason.
- Codex models (ChatGPT subscription, pi provider `openai-codex`) and FlashNext run through pi (matching subsection of §11); the official Codex CLI stays optional.
- The official CLI does not mean zero risk of bans or rate limits either; the terms of service still apply. Insufficient quota does not authorize switching accounts, moving credentials or bypassing limits.

Isolation keeps its original intent: Codex uses the original isolated home; pi gets a new home per run that only links `auth.json/models.json` from the selected pi config source, disables discovery of personal extensions, MCP, skills and so on, and loads only the fleet bridge and the role instructions explicitly.

**pi has no OS sandbox**; `--allow-unsandboxed-pi` is only a confirmation and creates no isolation. The original Claude workers likewise use the official CLI's existing permission flags; this must not be advertised as Codex's workspace-write sandbox. A role ban is not a permission boundary.

## 7. Callbacks, delivery and failure handling

### The pi adapter must

- Final completion is decided only at `agent_settled`; after `agent_end/turn_end` there may still be tools, retries, compaction or continuation.
- The generation is fixed in that process's callback arguments; every daemon delivery maps to a unique inbox msg_id.
- **Old v1 compatibility path only**: extract only the `.inbox/msg-*.md` ID in the fixed trailing envelope that belongs to the current agent's cwd, never another agent's path quoted in the body. **v2 follows §11: the file-envelope dependency is removed and the identity comes straight from the Redis delivery metadata**; do not treat old regressions as new design requirements.
- Partial JSON from an error/abort must not pass acceptance; a startup failure or non-READY must not be reported as ready.
- Do not loosen the expiry, duplicate or cross-agent callback checks to "fix no reply"; fix the input registration or the correlation source instead.
- Context usage comes from the pi API; the session file is used for activity/stall checks; pi is never parsed with the Codex rollout format.

### Original failure semantics are kept

- service: pause stops delivery/rotation, but requests keep queuing, and those with a deadline (`timeout_s` / `--request-timeout`) keep counting (§43: no deadline by default; an expiry holds only the slot running it and does not pause); BLOCKED/failures go to a human.
- task: ends with the original completed/blocked/budget_exhausted/cancelled lifecycle.
- Timeout, invalid, skipped, stalled and other results are kept; errors are never disguised as model success.
- A new run's private Redis uses the project's `.runtime/redis/<token>/redis.sock` (see §13); an older config pinned to an unmanaged `/tmp/fleetmux-<token>.sock` is for compatibility only. Never fall back to localhost:6379 or write to a production Redis.
- `max-busy` originally counts only workers, not the supervisor, and does not govern the startup instant; it must not be advertised as a complete model concurrency gate.

## 8. Configuration and runtime boundaries

- Regular usage: see the [README](../README.md); per-machine paths and live versions are maintained in the deployer's runbook; this design hard-codes no machine paths.
- The current implementation keeps one fleet registry per machine; local verification must not compete with an existing fleet for windows/registrations.
- Local verification may only handle tests or authorized ordinary development work; "it runs locally" never authorizes running trading duties across machines.
- Do not restart/replace live services on other machines, and do not auto-deploy. A doc change does not authorize production operations.
- Model window sizes come from the real backend/model configuration. Changing a number in the client does not enlarge the server.

## 9. Where the implementation lives and regression requirements

| File/area | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `fleetmux/fleet.py` | Original orchestration, CLI selection, formal request registration, callback checks, result relay |
| `fleetmux/pi_adapter.py` | pi home, argv (reusing the original label), callback structure validation |
| `fleetmux/pi_extension.ts` | pi lifecycle bridge, title, supervisor input bridge, this agent's delivery ID |
| `fleetmux/cmux_manager.py` | Original terminal/tabs/layout; docks control into the history column |
| `fleetmux/handoff.py` / `history.py` | Original handoff skeleton, history page; not rewritten in this round |
| `fleetmux/agents/*.md` | Original role boundaries, command tables, reply formats |

Keep at least these regressions:

- [ ] The UI/session name/routing/history identity of the first and successor generations agree.
- [ ] A worker handoff stays in the original right pane; the supervisor stays at the top left.
- [ ] history/control share a column, history selected by default; moving control kills no process.
- [ ] A failed move keeps the console; the original workspace is not closed while it holds unrelated surfaces.
- [ ] Dispatch from the supervisor's real input box → worker reply → supervisor relay, not just a test of the request CLI.
- [ ] A body quoting another agent's inbox still correlates with its own delivery ID.
- [ ] Old-generation, duplicate, error and aborted callbacks do not pollute new requests.
- [ ] Codex/Claude old-path regressions pass; Claude still execs the official CLI directly.
- [ ] Demo thresholds are not left behind as normal configuration.

## 10. Verification and delivery status (as of this baseline)

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| Full local test run | 91 passed, including the real TS extension contract tests |
| Isolated-directory test on the deployment host | 90 passed, 1 skipped for missing Node (that item passed locally); the live install was not replaced |
| All-pi + Flash Next on real cmux | READY, request results, two-role handoff and the history browser tested for real |
| Original identity and pi session name | Unit-tested and checked in real sessions; a cross-inbox ID bug found in one real test was fixed with a regression |
| Supervisor manually asks a worker to reply OK | Real TUI input loop passed: worker `reply=OK`, supervisor relayed it |
| control and history in one column | Moved in the real current instance with the heartbeat continuing; startup code and tests updated |
| Normal rotation threshold | The current local instance is back at 0.6 |
| Real mixed Claude task | Not tested for real yet; only unit tests of routing/the official CLI call chain, so no end-to-end pass may be claimed |
| Independent review | Earlier, Fable lacked quota and Opus timed out; no complete review verdict |
| Merge/production deployment | Not done; development demo status does not imply deployment |

The real-machine evidence files for the table above (window/handoff tests, naming and input loop, control/history column confirmation) are kept in the deployer's project and not published with the repo.

Future changes are checked against this document first; a new requirement must record explicitly which constraint it replaces, and only then do the implementation and regressions change. Never change only the demo or only the screenshots, and never take an old report's temporary state as the new design.

## Configuration file (everything merged into one file)

- There is a single config file, `fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml`, one block per table: `cli_pairs` (supervisor × worker pairs and real smoke-run records), `lifecycle_modes` (lifecycle modes), `walls` (wall texts / same-shell tier drops / aliases), `watchdog_prompts` (dialogs the watchdog may answer / markers that must be held), `capability_matrix` (the advisor's capability experiment table), `models` (model cards, by card id), `profiles` (profile cards, by id), `accounts` (account pool, empty in the repo), `tools` (tool pool, empty in the repo). All reads go through `fleetmux/fleet_config.py`.
- The deployer's private file has the same format: `$FLEETMUX_CONFIG`, otherwise `~/.fleetmux/fleet.yaml`, not in the repo. A block written in the private file **replaces the repo's block of the same name as a whole** (no deep merge); only `accounts / tools / walls / lifecycle_modes` may be written, and any other block is an error: `cli_pairs` is the real smoke-run gate, `watchdog_prompts` is the dialog allowlist, and model cards and the capability table may only change in the repo after review.
- Explicit paths (`--prompt-table`, a task's `agent_registry` directory, temporary tables in tests) still work: a file whose top level consists only of block names is read by block, otherwise the whole file is that one table; profile-card directories are still read file by file. Hot-reload semantics are unchanged: whether to re-read is decided by the modification time and size of both files.
- Role prompts (`fleetmux/agents/*.md`) are prompts, not configuration, and stay separate files.

## 11. Redis communication and layered display v2 (development wiring done, acceptance pending)

This section states target constraints; it is not a deployment announcement.

### What it replaces

- Replaces the file-path linkage of §7: the body, logical message ID, delivery ID, generation/session/execution authorization all live in Redis; the agent adapter receives with a blocking read and injects straight into the business context, never making the model read msg Markdown or poll Redis.
- Replaces the old behavior in §5/§9 of "every operator result is handed to the supervisor model to relay": simple results, timeouts and failures become UI-only cards generated by code, with zero mechanical relay turns; a business follow-up is created only when further judgment/planning/summarizing is needed.
- Replaces pi's old "must finally print the protocol JSON" display: a structured finish tool is planned to collect candidates while keeping the original JSON schema/external protocol; the daemon accepts them after agent_settled. The UI renderer shows a summary; control shows the full parameters/result.
- Keeps generation/delivery verification, error rejection, the external request/result, the original task/service and rotation rules; strengthens PEL/lease/fence/idempotent recovery, and claims no exactly-once side effects.

### Display boundary

- supervisor: user instructions, dispatch summaries, business judgment, conclusions and explicit failure notices.
- worker: the current task, the actual work, business results and explicit failure notices.
- control: the full communication envelope/JSON, IDs, timestamps, ACKs, routing, retries, heartbeats, verification and error chains; details can be looked up by task/slot.
- Transport information does not enter the model context; necessary business text may enter the context without being repeated in full in the window. **Exception**: when official Codex is delivered to through `codex queue`, the end of the body keeps one opaque delivery-number line `(fleet delivery fx-<16hex>)`, used only to match "settle only deliveries this generation received" (`codex_queue.bound`), with no business meaning. UI-only uses a non-model session entry; a hidden custom message must not be misused to bring notices into the context.
- The user-facing `[Operator result ...] ... Read that whole file ...` no longer appears; instead it shows `worker-1 处理超时（约 10 分钟），未取得有效结果` (worker-1 timed out (about 10 minutes), no valid result). A timeout does not mean execution stopped; an unconfirmed stop must be stated.
- No global filtering of JSON/paths/assistant text; no hiding of business content, real failures or items awaiting authorization.
- control/history still share one pane and history is still selected by default; concentrating details in control does not license layout changes or focus stealing.

### Implementation and delivery boundary

- Redis Streams blocking consumption, acknowledgement and recovery, still with the single daemon; the private Unix socket transport is unchanged, its path moves to the in-project run directory per §13, and it must never silently fall back to the file mailbox/production Redis.
- The pi native extension path comes first; input and display capabilities of the official Claude/Codex CLIs are verified separately. A mixed chain is not claimed as supported until verified, and CLI/authentication paths are not switched on a whim.
- v1/v2 are distinguished explicitly per run; in-flight instances are never hot-changed. The 91 tests/demos of the old §10 do not prove v2 passes.
- v2 must test: no message files/model reads; zero model calls for ACK/heartbeat/timeout cards; traceable control details; old generation/duplicate/crash recovery; slow UI never blocks scheduling; regressions of manual input, naming, layout, handoff and the old interfaces.

### Original delivery record of the development wiring (later integration verification: §14)

- Requirement: "all instance communication moves to Redis". A new `start` defaults to and only allows `transport_version=2`, still requiring `--allow-development-v2`; creating a new v1 run is refused, and message files can no longer be generated by default. Old/unversioned configs are still read as v1, only so existing runs can wind down normally and use status/stop/callback; in-flight instances are not hot-changed. At the time v2 supported only **Pi supervisor + Pi workers**, refusing Claude/Codex v2 before provisioning and profile retirement; no fallback to the file mailbox, no proxied Claude, no switch to a headless CLI. ("All-Pi only" was superseded by the Codex supervisor + official Claude worker subsection below; the three bans in the second half still hold.)
- **(Ruling)** A resident ACP session + pane front end (`dsh --profile acp` driven by the fleet's own pane front end, see §41) is not the "headless CLI" banned above: the session is resident, has a pane, accepts manual input and interruption, and delivery still goes through bridge + Redis.
- **(Second ruling, replacing the form of the previous item)** A dsh worker now runs the third-party dsh-TUI in its pane (`dsh --profile dsh-tui`, DSH in the same process) + the bridge plugin `dsh_bridge.mjs` that the fleet loads into that process via `--patch`, the same shape as pi (TUI + `pi_extension_v2.ts`), see §41; it is still not the banned headless CLI (a real terminal UI, manual input and interruption possible, delivery still through bridge + Redis). The v0 ACP pane front end `dsh_pane.py` has been deleted.
- `Fleet` keeps the single `submit/request/assign/on_supervisor/on_service_supervisor` judgment and artifact acceptance, the worker FIFO, pause/max-busy, label/generation and the handoff skeleton. `runtime_v2.py` only wires transport and event effects; `agent_bridge.py` is a resident JSONL child process that does its own blocking reads of inbox/UI/reply and registers/renews leases over the production connection. In v2 the body comes from Redis; delivery uses no `.inbox` files and no cmux paste.
- `pi_extension_v2.ts` starts the bridge on session_start and stops it idempotently on session_shutdown, using the installed Pi 1.1.0 API. A turn started by a custom sendMessage is correlated from agent_start (the real SDK does not fire before_agent_start); manual turns are isolated. fleet_decision/fleet_result only collect candidates; the daemon verifies after the final settled notice. terminate only skips redundant follow-ups under Pi's whole-batch tool semantics. UI Stream → appendEntry/registerEntryRenderer; transport IDs are stored only as non-model metadata.
- Simple service results and invalid/skipped/timeout/stalled use presentation cards; no mechanical supervisor relay is added automatically. A task worker's report still forms another bound supervisor business delivery, and COMPLETED still needs literal artifact acceptance.
- Event XREADGROUP and legacy `fx:events` → processing List → deterministic registration → acknowledgement; the old `fx:reply:* / fx:result:* / fx:results` are kept. Effects are first prepared on a side-effect-free overlay, then the intent is persisted, and a preflight Lua applies the public result/UI, delivery completion and the event XACK together. Normal Lua execution isolation is not exception rollback; a prepared intent can be replayed, an applied one does not redo effects, and any unclear write failure that leaves applying pauses for investigation; never blindly re-RPUSH or rerun tools.
- timeout/cancel/disconnect/aborted/unfinished keep the active lease/PEL with status held; Escape is not taken as proof of a stop. The handoff successor's launch gate waits for confirmation that the old tab/process tree is retired; a failed close forbids the successor from running. A daemon restart within the same run does not start_all; when safety is unproven it is held/paused; an abnormal exit or a daemon signal exit keeps Redis. Redis is closed only on an explicit fleet stop after every runtime has confirmed retirement.
- The v2 private Redis uses AOF `everysec`: a host/Redis crash still has a window of about **1 second** not fsynced. Non-terminal messages and dedup evidence get no TTL/trimming; there is no full acceptance yet of capacity archiving cleanup. The v2 replayable side-effect transaction of the browser broker is not implemented yet; the command refuses explicitly.
- The new TS extension contract tests and effect-planning unit tests pass, and type checking passes; these are not model or real-TUI tests. Real Redis could not start in the sandbox because of `Failed opening Unix socket: bind: Operation not permitted`, so the real bridge/daemon integration suite did not pass. **The development gate cannot be removed** until independent review, the full real-Redis suite, real SDK/supervisor TUI input, all crash boundaries/slow UI, a 1000-message reconciliation, and AOF window/log capacity and performance percentile measurements are done. Full commands and progress are kept in an internal report; old v1 demos are not taken as v2 acceptance.

### Codex supervisor + official Claude Code workers (replaces "v2 allows all-Pi only")

Decision: first make v2 support **Codex supervisor + official Claude Code workers**, then use it to run 6 Claude workers developing in parallel, with Codex `gpt-6-astra` as supervisor. (The default pair later became pi supervisor + official Claude workers; this pair stays optional, see the "Codex/FlashNext through pi" subsection below.) This subsection replaces the "v2 allows all-Pi only" constraint above and in §14; the three bans (no fallback to the file mailbox, no proxied Claude, no switch to a headless CLI), the single daemon, the task/service shape and the slot/generation identity are all unchanged.

| Item | Constraint |
|---|---|
| Support matrix | (Superseded by `fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml#cli_pairs` and the matrix in the "Codex/FlashNext through pi" subsection below; this cell is the original record of the day) v2 only allows `pi+pi` (task/service) and `codex+claude` (**task only**); later `pi+claude` was also allowed (task/service, i.e. the default pair pi Astra supervisor + official Claude workers, see the matrix in the "Codex/FlashNext through pi" subsection below). A Claude supervisor, Codex workers, mixing Pi with official CLIs, and `codex+claude` service mode are refused with a reason before provisioning/profile retirement (`runtime_v2.v2_cli_problem`, inventory uses the same rule); `--allow-development-v2` is still required. |
| Dispatch | The original `task` / `request` commands enter the v2 events Stream; the original `submit/on_supervisor` judgment and artifact acceptance are unchanged; the supervisor's final reply is still the AGENTS.md decision JSON. The Pi-only `fleet_decision/fleet_result` rewrite and the `_v2` role files are used only for Pi slots; official CLI slots keep the original text. |
| Delivery | The single daemon, after `offer` at the original idle delivery / handoff boundary, `receive`s as `tui-<slot>-g<N>` and hands over `body.text` (the business body) along the path of that slot's current-generation CLI, never writing `.inbox`/message Markdown (`RuntimeV2.type_into`; startup reminders take the same route): ① **Claude worker** (hook channel, `claude_hook.enabled`: Claude and not the supervisor, on by default, run config `claude_hook_delivery: false` turns it off): the body is written to the private Redis hook mailbox `<prefix>:hook:<slot>:g<N>:mailbox` and delivered by the Stop hook the session is blocked waiting in, as `{"decision":"block","reason":body}`, never typed into the input box; when no hook has been waiting for more than 10 seconds (`GRACE`), an asyncRewake fallback hook wakes the session with exit 2 and delivers; the fallback does not take an item while another turn is running (`TurnWatch`), and input the operator queued goes first. The first startup prompt still goes as a CLI argument; when a sub-fleet returns a slot, a hook worker starts the next generation instead of typing into the old session. ② **Official Codex supervisor / agent**: `codex queue --thread <this generation's session> --message=<body>` queues into that TUI's own queue, no typing; the body ends with a delivery-number line `(fleet delivery fx-<16hex>)` (unique per delivery, written to a Redis `cq_*` record before the CLI is called), the only transport information that enters the model context (see the exception above). A CLI failure or an uncertain result means hold, never a fallback to typing; queued but unsettled items are not withdrawn (Codex has no official withdraw), and queuing again is refused while the same slot has an unsettled item. ③ **pi slots** are unchanged (extension `pi.sendMessage`). Typing (cmux paste plus Enter; Claude bracketed, since non-bracketed multi-line was measured to stall in the input box or split into two prompts) remains only for: non-hook Claude slots (Claude supervisor, Claude workers with the hook channel off) and the role-switch prompt typed into an adopted pane when a subfleet takes over (R1-09). Apart from the delivery number above, no message/delivery/fence or other transport IDs are carried. A delivery failure means hold (fail closed). As the receiver, the daemon renews the lease while the tab exists; stall/timeout/tab gone still hold under the original rules. |
| Collection | The v2 branch of Claude Stop / Codex notify (`fleet.tui_turn`): a READY turn sends `bridge_session` → `register_slot` → `startup` (READY gate); afterwards, only when this generation's lease is `received` and this turn was really started by that delivery, the final text is mapped to a candidate (supervisor decision JSON; worker Done/Evidence/Not done/Blocked text or JSON with the same keys, where a heading may carry a parenthesized qualifier before the colon, e.g. `Evidence (with X):`) and `settled` is sent. "Started by that delivery" is judged per channel: Claude hook worker = the bind this session's hook wrote (message_id / delivery_id / session) matches the lease, and the latest starting input of this turn is that delivery's `Stop hook feedback:` isMeta line or the rewake line (the operator retyping the body verbatim does not count); non-hook Claude slot = the user prompt in the transcript; official Codex = rollout proof: same session, same generation, `turn_trigger == "queue"`, exactly one UserMessage whose full text equals the text queued this time (including the delivery number), atomically marked settled (`codex_queue.bound` / `mark_bound`), never looking at notify's `input-messages`. Manual turns, duplicate callbacks and old-generation callbacks do not settle; the daemon still checks identity/lease/fence/schema per the original settle. When no candidate can be parsed, success is never faked: the supervisor gets the original three corrections; for an official-CLI task worker that delivery is recorded failed, and through the original delivery path (new delivery/fence, same generation) a correction is queued asking it to "resend the report only in the Done/Evidence/Not done/Blocked format"; the correction goes to the **head** of that slot's pending queue (changed: it used to go to the tail, so follow-ups the supervisor had already queued were delivered first, their replies used up the 2 attempts, and the task was judged blocked without the correction ever arriving), at most 2 times (`TUI_REPORT_RETRIES`); only if it still fails is the task judged blocked; the counter resets after a valid report. Pi / opencode workers get the same 2 corrections (changed for gap G1: in an incident a reviewer, after rotation, replied with plain text "Done" without calling fleet_result, and the old rule judged the task blocked and held two analysts who were working), with the correction text changed to "call fleet_result exactly once to resubmit this report". |
| Hook timeout | Claude worker (hook channel): Stop hook timeout = `claude_hook_timeout` (default 86400 seconds); `MARGIN` (120 seconds) before it expires a keepalive turn is delivered (not settled, not reported); settings `env` sets `CLAUDE_CODE_STOP_HOOK_BLOCK_CAP=0` (consecutive deliveries are consecutive Stop blocks); an asyncRewake fallback hook and a StopFailure hook are installed too (when an API error ends the turn only StopFailure fires: it audits and writes the turn-end marker; task mode also sends a `stop_failure` event, the in-flight delivery is settled as not adopted per §24 "an API error holds only that slot" and redelivered to the same slot after backoff, and the StopFailure group carries the same asyncRewake for the redelivery; service mode likewise sends a `stop_failure` event and, per §24 "service mode: API error / model refusal", fails the request immediately, holding only that slot, with the StopFailure group again carrying an asyncRewake). Non-hook Claude slot (supervisor): v2 Stop hook timeout = `profile_ready_timeout` + 30 seconds; a READY turn waits for the daemon's reply at most `profile_ready_timeout` (READY is not lost while the daemon is still opening tabs one by one); a normal turn waits at most 30 seconds to settle, and a persisted event is not lost because of the wait timing out. v1 is still 30 seconds. Ignored/stale turns write `turn.ignored` / `turn.stale` audits. `profile_ready_timeout` has no startup flag yet. |

**Nature of this change**: typing the body into the official interactive TUI is none of the three bans (not a file mailbox, not through pi, not headless). The §11 delivery record's "delivery uses no cmux paste" is in the Pi implementation part, and the v2 plan's acceptance list says "pi no longer receives through cmux paste", so this subsection reads it as Pi-scoped; the v2 plan's §8 candidate for official Claude, "a prompt-submit hook injects the body, the terminal only keeps a short title", was not adopted and not measured this time. If "no cmux paste" is to be extended to all of v2, this chain must move to that candidate or go back to being a blocker. (Update: Claude workers now use the Stop hook mailbox and official Codex uses `codex queue`, see the "Delivery" row above; the Claude supervisor still types.)

**Limits**: since A2, UI-only cards are rendered by the daemon with `tui_cards` into `<run>/cards/<slot>.log` and the history page's Cards view, outside the model context; `codex+claude/service` is therefore unblocked (`adapter_missing: []`), but stays `unverified` until a real smoke passes (it can only start with `--lifecycle smoke`); manual input to a Codex supervisor is not queued (the §5 input boundary is unchanged). `max-busy` defaults to 0 = unlimited, so 6 workers can be delivered to in the same round; it does not govern the startup instant.

**Verification**: locally `python3 -m pytest -q tests` 592 passed (baseline 554), with the new `tests/test_v2_codex_claude.py` (real private Redis + real hook entry + fake cmux/CLI: matrix, dispatch onto the bus, body delivery without message files, Stop reply into v2, old-generation/duplicate/manual callbacks refused, 6 workers delivered in the same round, max-busy, typing failure holds, READY gate, lease renewal, Claude bracketed, settling still works when transcript tool output exceeds 1MB, ignored turns leave an audit). Real cmux smoke: isolated `FLEETMUX_HOME/FLEETMUX_STATE`, private Redis `/tmp/cfv2x/redis`, 1 Codex (gpt-6-astra) supervisor + 1 Claude (sonnet) worker; the task "write worker-1/ok.txt=OK" went through READY → dispatch → worker report → supervisor COMPLETED → artifact acceptance, with no `.inbox`/msg files and no error/warn in the audit. The first two smokes exposed and fixed two issues (under the `/tmp` symlink the Codex rollout cwd comparison failed, so READY was dropped; Claude's non-bracketed multi-line paste is unreliable, and the 2nd run needed one manual Enter to submit); after the fixes the 3rd run passed with no manual intervention. The smoke writes a trust entry for the temporary run directory into `~/.claude.json` (existing v1 behavior).

**Not accepted**: 6 workers truly concurrent (only 1+1 tested; whether a cold start of 7 tabs fits within the 180-second READY gate is unmeasured); `shutil.which('claude')` inside cmux resolves to the cmux claude shim (the smoke used `--claude` to point at the real binary); rotation with handoff (generation+1) on a real TUI; profile live switch to an official CLI; lease renewal/stalls in long tasks; paste behavior after a Claude version upgrade; manual input in a worker tab colliding with a delivery (Claude queues the paste, untested); independent review, merge and deployment are all not done.

### Claude supervisor adapter (route 2.0b; combination table `claude+pi` task/service, `claude+claude` task)

This subsection adds the "Claude supervisor" missing from the §6 table; the three bans, the single daemon, the task/service shape and the slot/generation identity are unchanged. These three rows of the combination table have `adapter_missing` cleared and remain `unverified`: they can only start with `--lifecycle smoke`; after a real smoke passes, a human changes them to `verified` and fills in `verified_by`.

| Item | Constraint |
|---|---|
| Startup | `start --supervisor-cli claude [--supervisor-model M]` (default `opus`); the fleet starts the official `claude` directly, and the supervisor rules from the original `supervisor.md` / `service_supervisor.md` are written as AGENTS.md + CLAUDE.md, without the `fleet_decision` rewrite. |
| Delivery / decision JSON / READY / correction | All use the TUI path of the Codex+Claude subsection above: the daemon receives as `tui-supervisor-g<N>` and bracketed-pastes into Claude; Stop hook → `tui_turn` → `fleet_decision` candidate → the original `on_supervisor` / `on_service_supervisor`. The READY gate and the three corrections (task judged blocked, service paused) are the original logic. |
| Manual input queued (§5) | Only a non-adopt v2 Claude supervisor gets the `UserPromptSubmit` hook (`claude-prompt`). Allowed through: the current-generation delivery body the daemon typed in, the current-generation startup prompt (only before READY), `/` commands, empty text; refused with an explanation: not READY, old generation, image placeholder (use a file path instead). All other manual text is sent as an `operator_input` event (the original submit / `[Operator instruction]` / `ui-<uuid>` request semantics; Python still generates the ID), and then the prompt is **blocked** from reaching the model, with the reason showing `指令已排队` (queued (ID)) / `输入被拒绝` (refused: reason) / `输入未确认` (unconfirmed, check control first). An accepted input is then typed back into the supervisor as a formal delivery. When the hook lets a prompt through, stdout is empty (its output would enter the model context). |
| Known degradation | A 30-second hook timeout is treated as a non-blocking error and the prompt is let through (inferred from Claude Code hook semantics, unmeasured): the manual text then reaches the model and Stop only records `turn.ignored`, never settling; the daemon's wait cap of 20 seconds stays inside the hook timeout. Blocked manual text does not enter the Claude transcript; it is kept in the hook prompt, the Redis audit and the delivery that follows. Official TUI supervisors (Codex / Claude) have no UI-only session entries: the daemon renders cards with `tui_cards` into `<run>/cards/supervisor.log` and the history page's Cards view (A2), outside the model context. |

**Verification**: `tests/test_claude_supervisor.py` (real private Redis + real Stop / UserPromptSubmit hooks and the `claude-prompt` entry + fake cmux; workers are the official Claude TUI and the Pi bridge respectively); `scripts/smoke_claude_supervisor.sh` runs the combination table and regressions in its offline mode; the `--live pi|claude` mode needs cmux, real models and `start --lifecycle` (block D) before it can run. **Not accepted**: UserPromptSubmit interception and paste queuing on a real Claude TUI, a real smoke, a rehearsal of a Fable supervisor reading the script.

### The Claude supervisor also receives through a Redis hook (isolated candidate, not deployed)

Requirement: a Claude Code supervisor must also receive Redis messages through the hook. This overrides "the Claude supervisor still types" in the Claude supervisor adapter above and in §27: a v2 Claude supervisor started directly by Fleet now reuses, in both task and service, the existing Stop blocking mailbox, asyncRewake, keepalive, READY correction and delivery/session/generation verification; runtime business messages, corrections and handoffs are no longer pasted into the input box. `--no-claude-hook-delivery` and `clear_after_result` only control workers and cannot send the supervisor back to typing. The first startup prompt is still an official CLI argument. A new `start` pins `claude_supervisor_hook_delivery: true` into the run config; old v1 and old v2 runs without this marker (including `--resume`) keep their original channel, so a code update on disk never forces an old supervisor with short timeouts/no rewake onto the hook. Upgrading the channel requires a new run; old generations are not hot-changed.

- `UserPromptSubmit` still registers operator text with the single daemon; once accepted it is delivered formally through the hook. Stop feedback is not a manual submission; manually retyping an in-flight body verbatim still must not be let through or settled. On a deployment host, Claude Code 2.1.307 was measured to fire UserPromptSubmit for asyncRewake's `<task-notification>`, so only a notification that fully matches the official notification shape, whose session/generation `via=rewake` bind, current received lease and body all match, is let through, without registering a duplicate request; keepalive/READY nudges likewise only accept a real rewake bind. Letting through on a notification prefix or body equality alone is not allowed; Stop settlement still verifies the transcript origin separately, and manually copying the notification wrapper cannot settle either. The original boundaries of the startup prompt, slash commands and registered mid-turn rotation prompts are kept.
- A blocked manual prompt produces no model turn/Stop: the session's transcript byte boundary is registered and the operator-yield wait released; rewake ignores only the blocked turn before that boundary and never clears the busy state of a later turn. Stop additionally records the exact bind it ended; while settlement is unconfirmed a leftover bind must not be taken as still running, and a new delivery must not borrow an old Stop's proof. When leaving the wait, only its own stopping flag is cleared. When the supervisor's rewake fires and this generation is confirmed still idle, a keepalive is delivered so the next Stop waits again; the keepalive bind is written only after Redis atomically checks this generation/session, the latest rewake, other live hooks and the bind's Stop-end proof, so a new delivery whose transcript has not yet hit disk is not overwritten. No wakeup while the turn is busy or another hook is alive.
- **P1 fix (local candidate)**: Stop expiry and rewake expiry share one Redis Lua rearm guard; Stop must pass the actual `slot=name` explicitly, the lease key and `fx:agent:<name>` both use that slot, and it atomically checks run, slot, generation, session, live hooks and the exact Stop-end proof of the current bind. Both paths compete for the same rearm record; a keepalive already sent or consumed, and a rearm newer than the call's start time, cannot be overwritten by a late writer. The record is kept until the next real Stop boundary; deleting the bind is never used to reopen the door for an old writer. The full rewake notification of an expired keepalive is only blocked, not registered as operator_input. The worker's v2 Stop uses the same guard; v1 keeps its original channel; there is no process scanning or sweeping.
- **Untested premises and failure boundary of slash / queued input**: it is not proven by measurement that `/status`, `/model` and custom commands fire UserPromptSubmit or a final Stop in every Claude CLI version; nor can it be inferred from a dequeue, the absence of assistant lines or a period of waiting that the CLI is idle. When the supervisor's Stop yields to queued input, or a UserPromptSubmit for a slash command arrives, a wait boundary is recorded for this run / slot / generation / session (reusing `HUMAN_HOLD=600` seconds). Only a real Stop of the same session, or a confirmed byte boundary of a blocked ordinary prompt, can release it; an assistant line does not release it early. rewake and keepalive do not deliver while the boundary is unreleased, and expiry of the human TTL does not mean recovery. On the first tick after 600 seconds the daemon holds the supervisor through the existing hold path, marks the existing received delivery uncertain, pauses dispatch and writes the notice/control reason; even without a UserPromptSubmit or an in-flight delivery it never silently waits a day. The mailbox is kept: no typing, no redelivery, no false completion. If a CLI-local slash command really sends no Stop, this conservatively holds the slot; check control / the session and use the existing recovery flow; this fix does not claim automatic recovery from that CLI behavior.
- A supervisor-specific channel declaration makes clear it is still the supervisor, dispatching only by the existing decision JSON; reusing the worker hook does not change its identity. Timeouts and StopFailure never fake success; the supervisor does not enter the §24 worker API auto-retry branch and still follows the original watchdog/hold semantics.
- This candidate first covers the directly started supervisor on the deployment host. The §19/§29 case of adopting an old worker pane as a sub-fleet supervisor also uses the hook mailbox since branch `fix/adopted-supervisor-hook`: the child daemon delivers into the supervisor mailbox of the **child Redis**, the parent-process hook pinned in the pane waits for items on the child Redis via `delegated_route`, and the route moves along on handover / return (details in §19 "hook redirection"). It applies only to new runs: `start` pins `adopted_supervisor_hook_delivery: true` into the run config, and on delegation the parent daemon writes `adopt.hook: true` into the child config only when that switch is present and the delegated slot itself uses the hook channel (`claude_hook_delivery` not false, `clear_after_result` not on); old runs without the switch, old sub-fleets already running, and sub-fleets whose supervisor is a typing worker keep typing, not hot-changed.
- The single daemon, private Redis, task/service protocol, manual entry, acceptance, generations and fixed layout are unchanged; live runs are not upgraded. The earlier 11-dispatch acceptance on the deployment host belongs to the typed path and does not prove this hook path passes. New regressions, real TUI acceptance, independent review and candidate deployment each record their actual results.

### Codex / FlashNext always run through pi; Claude only through the official CLI

Requirement: apart from Claude Code, everything else, including Qwen FlashNext and Codex, runs through pi; Codex through pi was the original decision and had been overwritten later. This subsection replaces the earlier subsection's practice of setting the combination table's `default` to an official Codex supervisor (implementing this section's "the pi native extension path comes first"); the three bans, the single daemon, the task/service shape, the slot/generation identity, the one-page layout and the handoff flow are all unchanged.

| Item | Constraint |
|---|---|
| CLI boundary | Claude agents are started only by the fleet directly as the official `claude` CLI; pi is **forbidden** from using a Claude subscription or Claude credentials (§6). Codex models go through pi (ChatGPT subscription login, provider `openai-codex`), FlashNext through pi's OpenAI-compatible provider. The official Codex CLI path is kept as an option, not deleted. |
| Default pair | `fleet.yaml#cli_pairs` `default = {supervisor_cli: pi, worker_cli: claude, mode: task, supervisor_pi_model: openai-codex/gpt-6-astra, supervisor_pi_thinking: high}`. When `start` gets no `--supervisor-cli` it takes the default CLI; when the supervisor is pi, each of these two keys is used only for the item given neither by `--supervisor-pi-model` / `--supervisor-pi-thinking` nor by an explicit `--pi-model` / `--pi-thinking` (a hand-typed flag is never overridden by a combination-table default). **An explicit `--supervisor-cli pi` does not read these two keys** (`fleet.resolve_supervisor`); the model must come from `--supervisor-pi-model` or `--pi-model`. A Claude worker needs no `--pi-model`. `--allow-unsandboxed-pi` must still be given explicitly (a confirmation, never granted automatically). `default` accepts only `supervisor_cli / worker_cli / mode / supervisor_profile (Codex supervisor only) / supervisor_pi_model / supervisor_pi_thinking`; any other key is an error at load time. |
| Per-agent models | Resolution order (high → low): supervisor = `--supervisor-pi-model` (or the legacy profile mapping, only for the default supervisor) > explicit `--pi-model` > (default supervisor only) `default.supervisor_pi_model`; worker = live profile card `model` > roster `pi_model` > `--pi-model`; thinking is resolved separately by the same rule: supervisor `--supervisor-pi-thinking` (or the mapping) > explicit `--pi-thinking` > `default.supervisor_pi_thinking` (default supervisor only) > `off`, worker profile card > roster `pi_thinking` > `--pi-thinking` > `off` (the `--pi-thinking` flag defaults to unset so an explicit value can be told apart; config still records `off` when unset). A night pair must therefore give `--supervisor-pi-model` explicitly. Any pi agent without a model → `start` refuses and lists the slot names. `--supervisor-pi-model` / `--supervisor-pi-thinking` set the supervisor; roster worker rows may carry `pi_model` / `pi_thinking` (only when the run's worker_cli is pi; not on the same row as `profile`, otherwise the whole roster is refused, §16 semantics). Unset ones fall back to the run-level `--pi-model` / `--pi-thinking` (fallback, original meaning unchanged). config records `pi_models` / `pi_thinkings` (by slot name). A roster hot reload that changes a row's `pi_model` / `pi_thinking` → once that slot is idle, the next generation starts under the same slot name (the same path as a roster card change). §12 model-card counting (`model_usage`: `Usage.from_agents` / `default_problem` / `check_slots`) counts **the pi model each slot resolves for itself** (`pi_adapter.for_agent`), and roster `pi_model` rows are also bound by that model card's `max_concurrent` (start refuses, hot reload refuses the whole roster). A profile card's `model` still beats the roster / flags. |
| Night pair | pi Astra supervisor + pi FlashNext workers: built from the already verified `pi+pi` row plus per-agent models (`--supervisor-pi-model openai-codex/gpt-6-astra --supervisor-pi-thinking high --pi-model flashnext/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-Uncensored-oQ4e-100K-MTP`, or roster row `pi_model`); the combination table gets no extra schema. The §25 night switch is unchanged. |
| Legacy flags | An explicit `--supervisor-cli codex [--supervisor-profile X]` still starts the official Codex TUI. Without `--supervisor-cli`, with pi as the default supervisor: `--supervisor-profile fleet-astra` (or `--profile fleet-astra` when the worker is not codex) maps to a pi supervisor `openai-codex/gpt-6-astra` + high and prints an explanation to stderr; any other profile name is an error that shows the two new usages; the mapped name given together with a different `--supervisor-pi-model` → error. Explicit `--supervisor-cli pi` + `--supervisor-profile X` → error (no mapping). A non-pi supervisor with `--supervisor-pi-model/-thinking` → error (not ignored). **Never silently start the official Codex TUI.** |

**Support matrix (v2; `fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml#cli_pairs` is authoritative; this table is a snapshot)**:

| supervisor ↓ / worker → | pi | claude (official CLI) | codex (official CLI) |
|---|---|---|---|
| pi | task / service: verified (including night Astra + FlashNext) | task / service: **verified** (default pair; real `scripts/smoke_pair.sh pi claude task|service` 4/4, on an uncommitted branch tree) | not in the table → refused |
| codex (official, optional) | task / service: verified | task / service: verified | task: unverified; service not in the table → refused |
| claude (official) | task / service: verified | task: verified (plus the §19 adopt sub-fleet); service not in the table → refused | not in the table → refused |

unverified can only start with `--lifecycle smoke`. The two `pi+claude` rows were changed to `verified` with `verified_by` filled in on the strength of worker-2's real smokes (S1d / S2, script exit 0, 4/4 PASS), so the default pair can start with any `--lifecycle`; the smokes ran on an uncommitted branch tree, and the independent review after commit still has to confirm.

**Measured (uncommitted branch tree)**: a default start (no CLI / model flags, isolated `FLEETMUX_HOME` / `FLEETMUX_STATE`, `--lifecycle smoke`) brought up "pi Astra supervisor + 2 Claude workers"; one task did 6 sequential dispatches, all 6 worker receipts committed, and one supervisor rotation `supervisor-1` → `supervisor-1.1`; in addition, `--lifecycle service` (the existing lifecycle, default CLI / model unchanged) did 5 independent submissions (`fleet.py request`, each with its own id, its own committed receipt and ok result), with one real supervisor rotation between the 1st and 2nd, supervisor-1.1 routing the 2nd–5th (footer after rotation `(openai-codex) gpt-6-astra • high`; after `fleet stop` the workspace closed with no leftover processes); the supervisor model is proven by the pi footer `… 1.0%/400k (auto)  gpt-6-astra •` and the pi session file entries `model_change openai-codex/gpt-6-astra` and `thinking high`. pi rewrites its own process title, so `ps` only shows `pi` and the process arguments are unavailable. **The window title is not changed**; it keeps the §3 `supervisor-1` label baseline (ruling).

Night Astra + FlashNext: after `scripts/smoke_pair.sh` was changed to read the final UI cards / audit from the run's persisted Redis data once the smoke lifecycle closes the run (the old snapshot missed result cards written at the same instant as COMPLETED), an independent rerun had script exit 0, 4/4 PASS, 0 audit errors, no `execution.held`, with footers proving the supervisor `gpt-6-astra • high` and the workers `(flashnext) Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-…` (uncommitted branch tree).

**Not accepted**: the codex / claude supervisor pairs have not been rerun with the new smoke script; the independent review after commit is not done. Merging, testing and queuing are handled by the fleet's trusted program as deliver; workers do not merge on their own; this task does not include deployment.

## 12. Dynamic agent profile routing (Phase 5, explicit live opt-in)

This section replaces the Phase 4 constraint "any live=true is unsupported", as authorized for this round. Phase 1 registry/router/planner, Phase 2 task integration, Phase 3 stable slot assignment and Phase 4 authorization and metadata application stay; Phase 5 adds a controlled respawn at the original delivery boundary of the single daemon. The default is still **advisory + metadata**, not a second dispatcher. This round authorizes dev validation in this repo only; it does not touch any trading production system and does not restart existing fleets. Phase 5 used the v1 queue/delivery protocol at the time; the v2 development wiring added in §11 is a separate opt-in and does not mean real mixed-CLI Phase 5 has passed acceptance.

### Identity, planning and authorization

- `flashnext-coder`, `claude-reviewer` and `researcher` are profile ids; they never become a worker id, Redis key, CLI session name, cmux label or history identifier. After a switch `worker-1` becomes `worker-1.1` of the same slot; the old label still routes to that slot within the same run.
- A card requires `id/display_name/cli/capabilities`; `model/profile/role_file` are optional. JSON uses the standard library; YAML works only when PyYAML is already installed. A lower rank is better.
- The router selects by capability overlap: review/security prefer low risk, code prefers low latency/cost, and ties are broken by a stable sort on id. A code-change plan contains an implementation and an independent review that depends on it.
- A task's `auto_agents` is a strict boolean, default false; `agent_registry` is an existing directory, only expanduser is applied, no shell/env/glob expansion. The single daemon stores `dispatch_plan` and `profile_assignments` at submit; operator artifacts are unchanged.
- Assignment uses existing slots in plan order; when capacity is short, slots are reused, and a profile that appears again keeps its original slot. `depends_on` is still honored by the supervisor; no scheduler is added.
- The supervisor uses the assignment's `worker` as `dispatch.to`; the instruction may contain `Recommended profile: <id>` / `Subtask: <id>`. A recommendation must be authorized by the current task/slot/subtask assignment; without a marker the profile must be uniquely determined. Mentioning an id in the body authorizes nothing; capabilities come only from a valid registry card.
- The whole decision is validated before it is queued; a wrong/unknown/conflicting recommendation goes through the original correction and three-strikes mechanism. The head of the queue is validated again before the actual delivery and after the new generation's READY (task id, assignment, registry, live switch); a failure blocks, with no fallback to another CLI/model.

### Two switches and the runtime mapping

- A switch happens only when **the task spec has `apply_profiles_live: true` and the fleet config has `allow_profile_live_switch: true`**. Both are strict booleans, default false. The config value is written by `start --allow-profile-live-switch`; the config permission does not opt any task in automatically, and task intent is not inherited from an old config `apply_profiles_live`.
- If a task requests live but the config does not allow it, submit refuses explicitly; a live task also needs `auto_agents: true` and a valid assignment. It is validated again before delivery; without a profile it cannot silently fall back to metadata mode. The service protocol adds no live profile intent.
- `pi`: the card model overrides `pi_model`; when unset the existing value stays; the default coder is the local FlashNext model (`fleet.yaml#models.flashnext-coder`). It still needs `allow_unsandboxed_pi`, Pi >= 1.1.0 and a separate run home; only Pi auth/models are linked, Claude credentials are not used.
- `claude`: exec the official Claude Code CLI directly; the card model overrides `worker_model`, default `sonnet`; it does not go through Pi. Claude Stop settings are saved per generation so an old process never reads the new generation's hook.
- `codex`: researcher stays on Codex; the card `profile` overrides the worker/shared profile and the card `model` may override the model. When unset the original defaults apply. The required profile file must exist; the isolated CODEX_HOME is kept.
- `profile` means a Codex config profile name; setting it on Pi/Claude is reported as unsupported, with no guessing of CLI arguments. `role_file` is resolved relative to the registry and must exist; path/symlink escapes are refused; the role is appended as extra instructions and the base fleet protocol is kept. By default nothing is appended.
- Independent review worker (requirement: a separate acceptance worker that does the acceptance on its own): the `claude-reviewer` card uses `role_file: reviewer.md` and `model: opus`. `reviewer.md` is the fleet copy of an internal multi-round review guide (top principle, read-only, review only the given scope, P0/P1/P2, report template, PASS = zero P0 and zero P1); when the guide changes, both copies are updated. Per the item above it is appended after the worker's base protocol; the reply is still Done/Evidence/Not done/Blocked, and the supervisor's artifact acceptance is unchanged. To enable: `start --allow-profile-live-switch` + task `auto_agents: true` and `apply_profiles_live: true`; the review subtask of a code-change plan is then routed to `claude-reviewer`. Without live it is only a metadata recommendation and the worker keeps its original CLI/role. **Profile hot switching with the v2 Codex supervisor + official Claude worker combination is not yet tested for real** (listed as "not accepted" in the matching subsection of §11); there are only fake regressions.
