With fleet `worker_cli=claude`, submit plans with `preferred_cli=claude` (among cards with the same capability, claude cards win); the code-change plan = `claude-coder` (claude/opus/worker.md) implements + `claude-reviewer` reviews depending on it, and both cards pass the v2 matrix's `preflight_profile`; pi/codex fleets pass no preference and the plan is unchanged. Research still has only the Codex `researcher` card, so a live switch to it in this combination is still refused.

### Model cards

- **Two card layers**: seven model cards (the `models` block of `fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml`: fable / opus / sonnet / haiku / sol / astra / flashnext) (initial assessment from the overall design v2 §3) are data only and **take no part in routing** (profile cards live in the `profiles` block); the four profile card ids are unchanged (`claude-coder` / `claude-reviewer` / `flashnext-coder` / `researcher`); they link to a model card with `model_card` and inherit every assessment field they do not set themselves (a field set on the profile card wins). `claude-coder`, `claude-reviewer` → `opus`, `flashnext-coder` → `flashnext`; `researcher` is not linked (whether the Codex default is Sol or Astra is undecided). The `cli` of a profile card and its model card must match.
- **Assessment fields** (all optional; missing / null = no limit or unknown): `quota_pool` (quota pool name), `hosts` (machines it can run on, a list of strings), `domains` (allowed business domains), `context_window` (budget-side window, positive integer; not the capability ceiling), `handoff_ratio` ((0, 1]), `max_concurrent` (tickets in this fleet, ≥1), `roles_allowed` / `roles_denied` (one role may not be listed on both sides), `judgement` (`high` / `mid` / `low-mid` / `low`), `night_ok` (strict bool), `measured` (object or null, filled back by the bookkeeper), `evidence` (justification, string). A validation failure refuses to load the whole registry, as with the original fields.
- **Role vocabulary** (English slug ↔ role in the overall design): `supervisor` supervisor, `orchestrator` orchestrator, `implementer` implementer / mechanical implementation, `fixer` fixer, `reviewer` review / code acceptance, `plan-reviewer` plan review, `falsifier` falsifier, `consultant` consultant, `researcher` research worker / web searcher, `reconciler` reconciler / assertion extraction / evidence collector, `summarizer` plain-language writer / formatting, `material` material processing / evidence trimming / incident fixtures / generation side, `proposal-expert` proposal_only expert, `final-writer` conclusions written for the user / final external copy, `incident-analyst` incident post-mortem hypothesizer, `long-runner` long runs without a hard timeout, `line-checker` line-by-line verification, `protocol-json` protocol JSON output.
- **router** (`route_task(..., host=, role=, occupancy=)`, filter first, then sort; each parameter None = no filter): `host` is compared with the card's `hosts` (case-insensitive, full name or first label, `studio.local` matches `studio`); a `role` listed in `roles_denied` or absent from `roles_allowed` excludes the card; `occupancy` ({model id: slots it will occupy}) excludes the card once it reaches `max_concurrent`. When everything is filtered out, the error states which filter removed each card. `build_dispatch_plan` passes `host` / `occupancy` through and passes the role per step: implementation `implementer`, review `reviewer`, research `researcher`; planning passes none (the vocabulary has no planning role). The host filters only when the fleet config carries `host` (`start --host studio`); without it there is no filter (the real host name is unverified).
- **Concurrency (`fleetmux/model_usage.py`)**: the unit counted is the **model** (profile cards by `model_card`, unlinked ones by their own id; `claude-coder` + `claude-reviewer` both count as `opus`); **a slot counts once**, regardless of generation (a rotation / a card change in the same slot is not counted twice); a slot runs the card frozen in this generation's `runtime_launch` (written before the successor spawns, so later checks in the same tick already see a switch decided earlier), otherwise the fleet default, matched to a model card only by exact (cli, model string); no match means not counted, no guessing; **the old generation holds its ticket until its tab is really closed**: during a live switch the slot occupies both the old model (`retired_pane` + `retired_launch`) and the new one, and the old ticket is released only once the old tab is confirmed gone, never early; **a new generation that fails to start rolls back its reservation**: when the switch fails and the successor tab is closed, `runtime_launch` is written back to the old value and the failed new generation holds no ticket; if the successor tab cannot be closed, it keeps holding the new model; a dead slot holds nothing unless its tab is still alive (`Fleet.model_usage` asks cmux about the tab; if the query fails it counts as alive, so no early release); a retiring slot holds until it has really retired; the supervisor is not counted. Real dispatch paths: ① submit planning uses `Usage.for_planning(active)` as occupancy (if a model already runs on a reusable live slot, that slot no longer counts as occupied), and `assign_profiles(..., running=)` lets a new profile land first on an idle slot already running the same model; ② a live switch at delivery first passes `Fleet.card_gate`: host mismatch → the task blocks (no silent swap); model full → the message stays in the queue, `profile_state=pending`, audit `profile.capacity_wait` (logged once while the reason is unchanged), retried every tick, switched as soon as a ticket frees; ③ **the default runtime is bound by its card too** (`model_usage.default_problem`): a `start` without profiles (before provisioning, SystemExit), new rows from a roster hot reload (whole roster refused, audit `roster.rejected`) and sub-fleet delegation (refused by `subfleet.check`) all count tickets + host against the default model's card; switching back to the default uses `Fleet.card_gate(slot, None, registry)`; a default that matches no model card (including a Codex profile name) = no limit, no guessing. On resume, or with an old config already over its tickets, only a `capacity.over` warning is logged (what is already running is not killed); ④ roster / night switches use `model_usage.check_slots` (the whole roster counted by model + host; default slots also count against the default model's own card), for B to consume (see worker-3/C-interface.md in the fleet workspace). `max_concurrent` is the ticket count of this fleet, not across fleets.
- **Unverified fields**: a card's `unverified` lists fields that lack external facts (`context_window` of Sol / Astra left empty; Fable's `model`; Haiku's `judgement`; FlashNext's `hosts`, `roles_allowed`); profile cards inherit it.
- **`quota_pool`**: names only the provider's quota family (`claude` / `codex` / `free-studio`). The actual accounts go in the `accounts` block of the deployer's private config file (`$FLEETMUX_CONFIG` or `~/.fleetmux/fleet.yaml`, format in `fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml#accounts`); each account carries `provider` (= the quota family of this field), `meter`, `hosts`, `caps`; they are not written into cards and not committed to the repo.
- **handoff_ratio per profile**: the `handoff_ratio` of the profile card (with inheritance) matching the slot's `runtime_profile` is one layer of the §22 rotation-threshold resolution (after the roster / config `handoff:` table, before `--handoff-ratio`; the integration branch integration/batch-20261013 unified this into one resolution, with no second check). It gives only the ratio; the window is not taken from the card (the §22 built-in table / measurements are unchanged). Currently FlashNext 0.5, Opus 0.6 (same as the default); the rest are unset.
- `judgement` / `night_ok` / `domains` / `quota_pool` / `measured` are only data in this item: read by the night switch (B), the brake (E) and the bookkeeper.

### The single meaning of the metadata

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `desired_profile` | Target intent of the head-of-queue assignment; not proof that it is running |
| `advisory_profile` | Phase 4's first advisory profile; metadata mode uses it and no longer occupies current |
| `runtime_profile` | Profile spawned by this generation; empty for the initial default process; not yet READY while starting |
| `current_profile` | Applied profile that passed READY in this generation; empty when spawn/startup of this generation fails; no longer means advisory |
| `runtime_ready` | Whether this generation passed READY; whether a profile is in effect must be judged from this together with state |
| `runtime_launch` | Frozen snapshot of the card id, actual CLI/model/profile override and role content |
| `profile_state` | `none/pending/switching/ready/applied-live/applied-metadata/blocked` |
| `profile_capabilities/reason/task` | Capability JSON of the target card, status note, related task id |

Metadata-only mode does not change runtime/current/generation: within the safety boundary the first advisory is accepted as `applied-metadata`, a different advisory is `pending`, and the existing runtime keeps running. busy/handoff only record pending; no intent is none. `applied-metadata` is never proof that the actual model has switched.

### Live switch order and failure handling

1. Entered only when the worker is idle and has no unconfirmed idle state left by an abort; starting/busy/handoff/dead cannot switch. While Codex has unfinished native children, the switch stays queued.
2. Validate both switches, assignment, card, role, CLI binary, Pi permission/version/home and the Codex profile file. A missing dependency blocks first, keeps the old runtime, and never silently swaps CLI/model.
3. Save the old screen and the switch history; first increment the generation, keep the stable slot/alias and fence old callbacks. Call the existing `spawn()` to create the successor tab; the actor command pins the generation, and the new CLI waits at the `launch_ready=0` gate.
4. Use the original `tab_into` to keep the right-hand worker pane; the original `kill_pane` retires the old process tree/tab and confirms the old tab is gone before opening the gate to run the new CLI. An actor that has not been granted the gate within 60 seconds fails on its own and does not run the target CLI.
5. The queue is not popped; it waits for the new generation to return a strict `READY`; every CLI is checked for failure/abort/text. Then runtime ready/current and `profile_state=ready` are marked. Profile startup has a default 180-second limit (`profile_ready_timeout`), enforced even when stall_timeout=0; failure or timeout blocks and refuses a late READY.
6. The next idle delivery validates the head of the queue again; the frozen launch must match the current card/role content, and drift blocks. Only after passing is it delivered as `applied-live`. An identical profile that is already READY is not respawned again; a normal context handoff keeps the selected runtime.

Retirement, layout or spawn failures revoke the gate, disable the new generation and try to clean up the successor tab; the old generation is not restored and the model is not rolled back. An old tab that failed to close is recorded in `retired_pane` and needs manual handling. A tab whose startup failed may be kept for debugging; `dead/blocked` means further delivery is forbidden, not proof that every OS process is gone. Terminating the original task clears the queue; it must be fixed and resubmitted. A model READY proves the configured startup and protocol readiness, not an independent check of backend model weights or credential validity.

### Phase 5 verification and deployment status

- Local `python3 -m pytest -q tests`: **243 passed**, covering the double opt-in, busy/handoff boundaries, the READY gate, old generation/duplicate delivery, failure cleanup, arguments/callbacks and role mapping of the three CLIs, and the Pi supervisor operator-input bridge regression. It uses fake Redis/cmux/CLI and is not claimed as a real TUI or mixed-model acceptance.
- An isolated two-worker dev start was attempted: the sandbox at the time could not connect to the cmux socket, `Operation not permitted (errno 1)`, and start exited before provisioning.
- A further run of `tests/pi_smoke.py`: the temporary private Redis exited with `Failed opening Unix socket: bind: Operation not permitted`; it never reached Pi/model or the live switch. The test subprocesses/temporary directories were cleaned up; no existing fleet was replaced.
- Full commands, results and the boundaries still awaiting acceptance are in [dynamic-agent-routing.md](dynamic-agent-routing.md#dev-validation-2026-10-11). **The above is the historical state of Phase 5 at the time: code/simulated regression was complete; the real dev deployment and the supervisor TUI smoke did not pass because of environment limits; at that time it was not independently reviewed, not merged and not deployed to production.**

## 13. Redis run directory inside the project

Redis runtime files move from `/tmp` into this project. This section replaces the old path constraints of §7/§11; it does not change the private instance, the single daemon, task/service, the v1/v2 protocols or the production authorization boundary.

- A new `start` by default keeps `redis.sock`, `redis.pid`, `redis.log` and `fleet.rdb` in the source project's `.runtime/redis/<8-char token>/`; the v2 AOF subdirectory lives there too. The Redis binary still comes from `--redis-server`/PATH; this round does not bundle a binary.
- The Redis-only directory is separate from `--runs-dir`: the original workspace/history/agent home paths and the latest entry stay, so long timestamped run paths do not exceed the Unix socket limit. The default root is derived from the code location and does not follow the current working directory.
- `--redis-runtime-dir` may explicitly pick another short, writable root, for read-only installs or separate dev validation. The config pins `redis_runtime_root/redis_runtime_dir/redis_instance_id/redis_socket`; new processes connect per the config, independent of the calling shell's environment or current directory.
- `redis_runtime.py` unifies endpoint validation for startup, CLI/hooks/bridge and control. In a new config the socket must be exactly root/token/redis.sock; cross-instance paths, relative paths, parent-directory traversal, symlink escapes and incomplete metadata are refused; an invalid new config can never be downgraded to the old `/tmp` path.
- The root and each new instance directory are owned by the current user, owner-only (700); an existing root with shared permissions is refused explicitly, and fleet never chmods an arbitrary directory for the user. Existing instance directories are neither reused nor deleted. These permissions are not a security boundary between processes of the same account and do not lift the OS sandbox.
- The socket is checked by encoded byte length: at most 103 bytes on macOS (NUL reserved), 107 bytes on Linux. Too long fails before creation/startup with a hint to shorten the path; no `/tmp` symlink, no automatic fallback to `/tmp` or TCP. Still `--port 0` / `--unixsocketperm 700`.
- Older `/tmp/fleetmux-<token>.sock` configs without managed metadata stay strictly compatible so in-flight instances can status/stop/callback normally; old fleets are not migrated or restarted. It is not a fallback for a new start.
- stop keeps the single daemon's Redis shutdown; Redis cleans up socket/PID, while logs/RDB/AOF stay for debugging and recovery. No shared system Redis is introduced and no other Redis is killed.
- **Orphan Redis reclamation**: when a v2 daemon exits non-explicitly (crash, killed, workspace closed) it deliberately leaves Redis running for `--resume`; but when a run is simply abandoned (replaced by another fleet, never stopped), nobody ever shuts its Redis down (in one incident two old runs on one host each left one behind). Fix: `start_redis` writes `owner.json` (`{"run": ...}`) in the instance directory; before `start` really starts a new run, and before `start --resume` reattaches, it scans this user's `redis-server unixsocket:` processes once, and for an instance with `owner.json` it SHUTDOWNs only when all three hold: ① the owner run has no daemon holding the lock; ② no live process has that run path on its command line (not shut while an actor / hook still writes to it); ③ `owner.json` is older than 120 seconds (covers the gap between start_redis and the daemon taking the lock). A sub-fleet (path contains `/sub/`) is left alone as long as any ancestor run is alive; it belongs to the parent's `collect()`; the target run of `--resume` is left alone; instances without `owner.json` (started before this fix, or not by fleet) are never touched. SHUTDOWN saves; AOF / RDB stay in the original directory, and a later `--resume` restarts Redis from the same directory as usual, lossless. A scan failure only prints and never blocks start; tests disable the machine-wide scan through conftest, and `tests/test_orphan_redis.py` limits itself to its own root. This is the only exception to "no other Redis is killed", and only for instances fleet itself started whose owner run was abandoned.
- `.runtime/` is not under version control. This workspace excludes it through Git's local `info/exclude` without changing `.gitignore`; other checkouts need the same exclusion.

## 14. Local integration verification of the Redis mailbox

The Redis mailbox feature was merged. This round froze the framework candidate and re-ran the full regression, real Redis race probes, the contracts of the installed Pi SDK/TUI, and the supervisor TUI input loop with real Redis + Fleet + Pi/FlashNext.

This updates the environment-limited status in the original delivery record of §11; it does not change the runtime protocol or the withdrawal threshold. A new start defaults to, and only allows, Redis v2, and still needs the explicit `--allow-development-v2` permission and all-Pi (later the task mode of Codex supervisor + official Claude workers was also allowed, see the matching subsection of §11); old v1 configs are kept only for reading, draining and stop compatibility, with no hot change. This implements the requirement to move communication into Redis; a new start does not default to file mailboxes. Real-model verification uses a separate PTY, while the cmux layout is still simulated; a separate real cmux layout test does not mean the full v2 chain has passed production acceptance. Full crash/slow UI/1000-message chains, performance/AOF/capacity and mixed CLIs are still awaiting acceptance.

Merging does not hot-switch the current v1 run, does not stop its in-flight tasks, and does not mean other machines or production instances have migrated.

## 15. Inventory and migration of communication across all instances

- Inventory configs, processes, registry and transport, distinguishing running instances, old history and leftover windows that are only registered; the number of historical `.inbox` files is not proof that anything is executing.
- The user's current local Pi dev instance may be replaced in a controlled way by a new v2 run once its in-flight tasks and queue have drained. Old runs/results/handoff records are kept; old generations are not rewritten, and history files are not deleted to fake file-free communication.
- In a new run, the supervisor and every worker must actually verify Redis receipt; acceptance includes no new message Markdown, no model reading message files, and no mechanical supervisor relaying, not just checking config=2.
- The Codex supervisor + official Claude worker (task) combination may create new v2 runs (matching subsection of §11); other native Codex/Claude combinations and protected live/production instances stay explicitly listed as blockers; the communication change does not authorize swapping CLIs, moving subscription credentials, stopping trading services or writing production Redis. Unverified combinations are not claimed as migrated.
- The read-only inventory tool `python3 -m fleetmux.transport_inventory` reports the machines/directories scanned, config versions, related processes and message file counts; it does not connect to Redis, read message bodies or modify any instance.

## 16. Worker roster hot reload

Requirement: workers can be loaded and unloaded while running, controlled by YAML. This section replaces the old behavior "the worker roster is frozen at `start`"; slot/generation identifiers, the one-page layout, the single daemon and the task/service protocols are unchanged.

- Entry: `start --roster <yaml>`, mutually exclusive with `--workers/--worker-names`; the schema is `workers: [{name, role?, window?, handoff_ratio?}]` (the last two and the top-level `handoff:` are in §22); `role` is relative to the yaml's directory and takes precedence over `--roles DIR/<name>.md`. An invalid file at startup refuses to start. A run without `--roster` behaves as before.
- Detection: at the start of every tick the daemon compares the file's mtime/size, then the content hash; the roster changes only in this one place, never inside the per-slot loop.
- Whole-file validation: YAML errors, unknown fields, invalid/duplicate names, a new name not ending in `-<number>` (§3; names already in this run are not checked), zero workers, a missing role file, or a name already used in this run (Redis agent state or workspace directory exists) → the whole file is refused, the current roster stays, and `roster.rejected` is written to the audit (visible in control) listing every reason.
- Add: write `config.json` first (and `latest.json` when it points at this run), then start - hooks/bridge validate membership against the config file; Redis fields are the same as `start_all`, the READY prompt is the same, the tab goes to the end of the right-hand worker column (to the right of the supervisor when there are no workers), and the history page refreshes.
- Remove: mark as retiring - no longer a dispatch target (excluded from decision/request/route validation and profile assignment), but still counted in `max_busy` and in "a worker is busy"; once idle with an empty queue and, in v2, no in-flight delivery, the screen is saved and the old tab closed the same way as a rotation (v2 `retire`), and the slot is removed from the roster and written back to the config. The working directory stays and the history page can still show it. Writing it back into the file before retirement completes cancels the removal.
- Changing only `role`: takes effect in the next generation, no restart.
- Concurrency limit (the limit itself must be hot-reloadable too): top-level `max_busy: <integer ≥ 0>` (0 = unlimited), hot-reloaded by this section's detection; the next `pump()` delivers under the new value, no restart; deleting the key returns to the value of `start --max-busy`. It applies in memory only and is not written back to `config.json` (the first tick after a resume re-reads the roster and applies it again). Non-integer / negative / boolean values refuse the whole file. The budget card's `max_busy` (§ budget card) can still only tighten it. Before this, `--max-busy` was read only once at daemon start, so hot-added workers were stuck in the queue behind the old limit.
- Advisor switch (advisor design v0.7 §2.1): top-level `advisor: on|off`, default off, hot-reloaded by this section's detection, no restart. The roster is the only entry for changes: the user edits the file, `fleet advisor on|off` (changes only the roster; for a run without `--roster` it generates `<run>/roster.yaml` from the current workers and the daemon falls back to reading that file), or the supervisor's decision key `advisor` (the daemon writes the roster and it takes effect immediately; the dispatches of the same decision have already passed the advisor); `start --advisor` only writes `advisor: on` into the roster. config.json no longer stores this switch. A bad value is refused with the whole file; when turning on with a broken kit, enabling is refused, the roster is not written, audit `advisor.rejected`. Turning off clears pending consultation registrations, and slots held waiting for their first config sheet are released with the default (they cannot be held forever); generations already running and generations started from a config sheet do not roll back. Audit `advisor.on` / `advisor.off` (`who`=user|supervisor); the control `STATE` line shows `adv=on|off`.
- Telling the supervisor: no separate message (in task mode a separate message makes the supervisor reply with a RUNNING that has no dispatch, which under the original rule is wrong three times → blocked); instead the current dispatchable worker list is appended to the end of the next business message sent to the supervisor.
- The supervisor adds / retires workers itself (decision: the supervisor can start, stop and unload workers dynamically, without shutting the whole fleet down and starting another): the five supervisor prompts (`supervisor.md` / `supervisor_v2.md` / `service_supervisor.md` / `service_supervisor_v2.md` / `service_supervisor_tui_v2.md`) end part 2 with an "Adding and retiring workers (the roster)" subsection: when to add (more ready independent work than idle workers, and the task description allows it), when to retire (no work left in the plan / parallel width narrows), the task description's limits win (how many at most, which must not be retired, the worktree / role file a new worker needs first); add = a new line `- {name: <role>-<n>, role: <path>}` under `workers:`, retire = delete the line; names are not reused, take the next number, change only `workers:`; dispatch to it only once it appears in the `[Roster change]` list. The placeholder `{{ROSTER}}` is rendered by `roster_notice(cfg)`: when the run has `cfg['roster']` it writes the roster path (with a night roster it also writes the night path and requires both `workers:` to change together, otherwise the day/night switch is refused); without one (started with `--workers` / `--worker-names`) it writes "this run cannot add / retire". The prohibition line now reads "only editing the roster file is allowed"; still no calling the fleet CLI, no typing into tabs, no touching other agents' directories. When the run has a roster, the Codex supervisor's sandbox (`workspace-write`) additionally lists the roster's directory (and the night roster's directory) in `sandbox_workspace_write.writable_roots`, otherwise it cannot change the file (verified with codex-cli 0.164 `debug prompt-input`); the supervisor's `writable_roots` also always include the progress board directory, so `note` is allowed (see contracts §7). **A refusal is also told to the supervisor**: on a whole-file refusal the error text is stored in `rejected` of `fx:roster`, and the end of the next business message gets `[Roster rejected] <errors, one per line>; the roster in effect is unchanged: <current dispatchable list>`, cleared once sent; the same refused content is not reported twice (the content hash is reused); if the roster is changed to a valid one before the notice is delivered, the notice is dropped and only `[Roster change]` is reported. Known boundary: after the advisor switch writes `<run>/roster.yaml` for a run without `--roster`, the daemon hot-reloads it too, but the prompt still says "cannot add / retire"; the few messages sent directly via `self.v2.queue('supervisor', …)` (service `[blocked]` / `[dialog]` etc.) carry neither notice, like `[Roster change]`, and wait for the next message that goes through `Fleet.queue`.
- Known boundaries: a newly added worker that does not reach READY at startup blocks in-flight tasks under the original startup timeout rule; a retiring worker that is held does not retire and needs manual handling; nothing is delivered while paused, so a retiring worker's queue does not drain.

## 17. Resume from a checkpoint

Designed after an internal production-line resume design. This section **changes the §11 rule "a daemon restart in the same run does not redo automatically"**; the rest (single daemon, slot/generation identifiers, one-page layout, task/service protocols, §16 roster semantics) is unchanged.

- **Entry**: `start --resume <run directory|auto>`. `auto` takes the run in `latest.json`; if it already has `result.json`, cannot be read or is not v2 → a new run starts as usual. An explicit directory in those cases is refused with the reason. If the run's `daemon.lock` is held (the daemon is still alive) → nothing is done. Only v2 runs are accepted.
- **Redis**: first connect to the original socket and use it if it answers (only the daemon died); otherwise restart with the config's original `redis_runtime_dir` and original socket, reloading the AOF from the original directory (`fleet.start_redis`, shared with a new start). `redis_runtime.prepare` is not called (it refuses to reuse a directory). The roughly 1-second window of AOF `everysec` is unchanged.
- **Completion is judged by disk/Redis**: task artifacts keep `check_artifacts`; a worker reply already exists in the `result` field of the message hash (no TTL, no trimming), so no separate checkpoint is written; service is judged by whether `fx:result:<id>` exists.
- **New principle (replaces that §11 rule)**: only an explicit `--resume` redoes uncertain in-flight messages, and each redo is counted; a same-run restart without `--resume` still goes through `recover()`: mark uncertain, pause everything, redo nothing. `--resume` itself is the operator's authorization to redo: in-flight deliveries are first marked uncertain under the original rule, then released as "operator authorized the redo" (`resolve_uncertain`); a reattached live window first gets Escape to abort the old turn. **This is the only place where Escape is taken as "stop accepted" rather than as evidence of a stop.**
- **Windows**: only official CLI (Codex/Claude) workers are reattached, when the window still exists, the CLI process is still alive and it was READY last time; a task supervisor always starts the next generation (starting over from the brief); Pi always starts the next generation (the Pi bridge faults permanently on a Redis disconnect and does not reconnect). The next generation takes the original position (`tab_into` the old tab, then close the old tab, as in a handoff); a worker whose old tab is gone goes to the end of the worker column; if the supervisor window is gone too → close leftovers, start the next generation of everything, and rearrange the whole page with `arrange`. The new control tab is merged back into the history column.
- **Reconciliation**: first replay prepared intents (an applying intent of unknown state → fall back to `recover()`); release each slot's in-flight state and cancel the queue; lift the pause (the original pause reason is written to the audit `daemon.resumed`). task: this task's queued/in-flight worker dispatches are requeued verbatim **by the daemon itself** (message field `replay` = which attempt), then the new supervisor gets a brief: goal, artifacts verified/missing, dispatches completed (with reply summaries), dispatches requeued (stated explicitly as not to be dispatched again), and only the missing ones should be added. The design said "it only dispatches what is missing"; the daemon, not the supervisor, redispatches the lost one so that the attempt limit lands on a definite message. service: requests in `fx:reqq` without a result get their timeout window reset and are rerouted; those with `"replay": false` publish `timeout` (reason "not replayed on resume").
- **Time budget**: downtime still counts toward the task's `hours`; on resume, a task already past its deadline is judged `budget_exhausted` in the first tick and `result.json` is written.
- **A run that never submitted a task (A42-LINUX-RESTORE-FAILURE-04)**: a run that failed before its first READY and was closed by the fleetmux sidebar (`resumable: true`) never had a task. **The criterion is strict**: `result.json` resumable + reason contains `startup failed before READY` + `task_id` / `goal` / `artifacts` all empty, and `fx:task` has no id / goal / deadline / lifecycle_spec, a status only of `blocked` / `budget_exhausted`, and empty artifacts - only this one shape counts as "no task". **An empty id or a missing deadline alone does not count**: any other record (id without deadline, deadline without id, unreadable fields) is a corrupt/partial record and always fails closed with the reason; no id/goal/deadline is fabricated, and the expiry budget gate of a real task is not bypassed (a really expired task still needs an explicit `--extend-hours`). **"Empty" must really be empty**: artifacts must parse to a real empty list (missing / `[]`; `{}`, a string, a number or unreadable all count as non-empty → not a placeholder); the deadline must be a usable number (positive, finite; `soon` / `0` / `-5` / `nan` / `inf` are unusable → corrupt); a non-empty string is not enough. **An empty `fx:task` does not mean no task was ever submitted (P-04-CLASSIFY)**: as long as a readable `result.json` still carries a business identity (`task_id` / `goal` / non-empty `artifacts` / `deadline`), an empty `fx:task` is a contradiction and always fails closed; it cannot be taken as "healthy, no task"; a missing `result.json` must be kept apart from one that exists but cannot be read/parsed, and an unreadable result is never treated as an empty result. A truly healthy run that never submitted a task and never wrote a closed result stays valid. There is one more **valid** "never submitted a task" case: a task-mode run closed by the fleetmux sidebar while idle (199 native 11/13 success path, evidence `97-healthy-lifecycle-close-complete.json`): `status=stopped`, `closed_by=user`, `closed_via=fleetmux-sidebar`, `resumable=true`, `base_mode=task`, `task_id`/`goal` empty, `artifacts=[]`, `at`/`closed_at` numeric, reason non-empty - only a strict, complete, contradiction-free match of this shape counts as none (no startup-failure reason required); any mismatch (has goal/artifacts, another close path, not stopped, not task base mode, unusable time, a closed record that is neither the placeholder nor this shape) is refused as corrupt. On restore it takes the same common resume path as the strict placeholder and clears the old closed record (the original text is kept as evidence).
  - The common layer of `start --resume` classifies centrally with the same `lifecycle_hold.classify` (`none` / `task` / corrupt) **before deleting `result.json` and before spawning the daemon asynchronously**: with no task, the daemon resumes only on the original run / original Redis / original slot names - no new fleet, no fabricated task, no fake `status=running`; a corrupt record exits non-zero with an explicit error, `result.json` is not deleted and `spawn_window` is not called. `lifecycle_hold.reopen` writes `status=running` only for a real task (has an id and a usable deadline); before, it treated a missing deadline as 0, so the placeholder was misreported as an "expired task" with a suggestion of `--extend-hours`, and after bypassing that, `RuntimeV2.brief` raised a KeyError on `task['id']`, leaving a non-empty running task without identity.
  - On the daemon side, `RuntimeV2.resume_run` classifies again **before retiring / respawning / replaying any side effect** (second layer): **no task reuses no early return**; it takes the same common resume path as a healthy empty task - first the `replay_intents` safety gate (an unknown intent still holds and leaves the slots alone), then retire / reattach / generations / respawn (with no task, the startup line is "no task yet, reply READY") and `Fleet.resume` lifts paused; **only the task brief is skipped**; finally the normal writer clears the old `blocked` `fx:task` and the closed `result.json` (kept verbatim as `<run>/result.closed.json`, evidence of the failure event), and the run goes back to waiting for a task / READY, after which a new task (real id, real deadline) can be submitted normally. A corrupt record still exits with an explicit error and touches no slot; `brief` without an id raises an error instead of a KeyError.
  - The fleetmux sidebar's ↺ / `^b r`: identity is **the pane id of this spawn** (not the title); only an **explicit non-zero exit** clears `正在恢复…` (restoring...) and leaves one actionable reason on the status line (full output goes to `server.log`, the original output is traceable); exit 0 (the daemon starts asynchronously) and unknown status are not failures; no automatic retry (the operator pressing ↺ again is the retry), and the daemon's kv `restorable` is not touched; the operator killing that pane is recorded as `恢复已取消` (restore cancelled). So the last piece of output can still be read, a reaped pane stays in the store for one more tick (`list` / `info` stop reporting it immediately; `feed_output` can still write to it). **Pane reaping must complete in the same tick (P-04-REAP)**: the child being reaped and the PTY reaching EOF are two separate events; in the tick of the reap it must report the exit, remove the pane atomically from store and layout, write `pane … exited` and give the restore attempt an explicit completion - **without requiring the PTY to be fully closed** (a background child may hold the slave forever). SIGCHLD only wakes the single PTY event loop; the loop first feeds output the reader thread has already sent into the parser, then reaps in **the same tick**: no sleep to guess at draining, no second dispatcher, no unbounded wait, and no dependence on the next PTY / SIGCHLD / other-pane event. The PTY's own reader thread sets `eof` after the last byte: with `eof` the tail is the complete output; without `eof` (the PTY is still open) the tail reaches only what had been parsed at that moment, `pty_open` notes "later output is not included" in the `pane … exited` log, and the status line never falsely claims `没有输出` (no output) - the boundary is "output collected", not "all output". `list` and layout are always consistent; there is never "the list is empty but the layout still has a dead pane". (macOS hangs up the terminal when the pane's session leader exits, so "PTY still open" is reproducible only on Linux; macOS takes the equivalent EOF path.)
- **Attempt limit**: `--max-attempts` (default 2, written to config, may be overridden on resume). Over the limit, a task is judged `blocked` (stating which worker and which instruction), and service records `timeout` with the reason.
- **End marker**: when a task enters any terminal state the daemon writes `<run>/result.json` (status, reason, task id, per-artifact re-verification, atomic write); it is deleted when the same run accepts a new task; but while the supervisor slot is still held (an in-flight delivery withheld at the previous task's terminal state; a pi supervisor has no hold exit, see §24 known gap ⑦-4) a new task is refused with the reason and a hint to start a new run (before this fix it was accepted and the task could never be delivered). It is also written after an explicit `stop` successfully retires all runtimes (service records `stopped`), so `stop` ends the run. On resume, a task already terminal in Redis without `result.json` → it is written then and the run is treated as ended.
- **Roster**: the roster already persisted in config.json is kept; a given `--roster` is written into the config, and the daemon reconciles it with the §16 hot reload in its first tick.
- **Known boundaries (P2)**: a lost "report format correction" message is requeued verbatim to the new generation (which has no context); when only the daemon died and Redis did not, a turn the worker finished during the downtime is refused and done again; Pi is not reattached; the whole-page rebuild when only the history column remains is untested on real cmux; `has_cli_process` judges by process (§23: the executable name without `.exe` is codex / claude, or `node` with a script path containing the CLI name; a bare shell or an unrelated node does not count).



- **Oversized resume brief (branch fix/resume-brief-size)**: after a dev_task run had accepted several hundred dispatches, the "completed dispatches" list of `RuntimeV2.brief` pushed the brief past `message_bus.MAX_BODY` (200000 bytes); `start --resume` raised `ValueError` inside the resume transaction and the daemon died on every start (seen on a channels dev fleet instance). Now `fit_brief`: when the brief exceeds `BRIEF_LIMIT` (120000 bytes) the full text is written to `<run>/handoffs/resume-brief-<task>-<ms>.md`; the brief queued to the supervisor keeps the opening (goal, workers, downtime facts), the most recent progress and the requeued / already-run lists, and states the full-text path with an instruction to read it first; audit `resume.brief_spilled`. Test `tests/test_resume_brief_size.py`. Known gap: the §25 night-switch brief uses `progress()` as well and has no such cap yet.

## 18. Interface contract: task numbers within one fleet + the start gate

Spec: an internal interface-contract spec (approved, including four rulings) and §1-2 of an internal nested-decomposition design. This section covers only **a single fleet** (top-level supervisor = this fleet's supervisor); peer notices (SendMessage / `{{FLEET_PEER}}`), sub-fleets and escalate / veto are the next piece. The single daemon, slot/generation identifiers, one-page layout, task/service protocols, the §16 roster and the §17 resume are all unchanged.

- **Switch**: enabled only with task spec `"contracts": true` (strict boolean); without it, Redis, prompts, report relaying and artifact checks are all as before. `skeleton_worker` is optional, default the first worker.
- **State**: one JSON in Redis `fx:contracts` (phase / decomposition table / registry / pending proposals / task table / reply ownership per worker), carried into resume by AOF; every change is written by the program to `<run>/contracts.json` and copied to `<workspace>/contracts.json`, and the tick re-syncs from Redis; `<run>/contracts.log.jsonl` is an append log. Models only read it.
- **Prompts**: `agents/contracts_supervisor.md` (appendix A) / `contracts_worker.md` (appendix B §4), injected only in contract tasks: at submit they are written into `CONTRACTS.md` in every agent directory and the task message points to it; generations started during the task (handoff / roster hot add) get the same section appended to AGENTS.md. The base `supervisor*.md` / `worker*.md` are unchanged.
- **P0**: the first reply must be `phase: "interfaces"` + `plan` + `interfaces` + an empty dispatch; five checks (no producer / no consumer and not final / cycle / sample self-check / exactly one writer per field) refuse item by item; **a P0 refusal does not count toward the three corrections and has no limit** (P0 is relaxed until everything is resolved), bound only by the task's `hours` budget. The interfaces / skeleton phases are exempt from "RUNNING must dispatch when no worker is busy" (`others_active`; v1 and v2 validation share one entry). In P0 `path` is checked only for being relative; whether it is under the `to` worker's directory is checked at dispatch (P0 has no `to`).
- **P1**: after P0 the daemon itself dispatches `[skeleton]` to the skeleton worker; when that reply arrives it reads `skeleton.md`: one PASS line per interface and no FAIL → everything `declared`, frozen at v1, and `[contract] interfaces frozen at v1` is forwarded to the supervisor with the reply; otherwise back to P0, **with no attempt limit** (as above, bound only by the `hours` budget); literal `PASS/FAIL` copied into `skeleton.md` (header / legend) is not a failure.
- **Task table and dispatch**: a dispatch item must carry `id` (M number) and may carry `provides` / `consumes` (`id@version`) / `edits` / `depends_on` + `serial_reason`; the refusal texts of spec §3.1 / §3.3 / §3.0.1④ are used verbatim. Items whose `depends_on` is unfinished are held by the daemon and delivered automatically once all predecessors are `done`. Reply ownership pops FIFO by "whose reply each message expects, recorded at queue time": a dispatch records its M number, `[contract]` notices and proposal refusals record nothing, so a notice turn never closes another task. A task is `done` = every interface it provides is `ready`; replied but not ready records `failed`, and the supervisor redispatches the same number (attempts+1).
- **Readiness and changes**: a worker reports `Interfaces: IF-x@n provided: ready` → only after the program's literal check (exists / length / contains / json_keys) passes is it `ready`, and `[contract] ready` is delivered to running consumers; otherwise `[contract] not ready` goes to producer and consumers. `interface_change` in the last JSON object at the end of a worker report (`raw_decode` from the back, not the first `{`) is validated per §4.3; if it passes it becomes pending and is forwarded to the supervisor, who approves / rejects with `interfaces[].decision`; compatible / breaking declared by the supervisor itself counts as approved. The program grades by field diff (changing path, removing contains / json_keys = breaking). After a breaking change is approved the old version is `deprecated` and its consumers `stale`, and their next dispatch must consume the new version. Notices go only to workers running the task; the rest rely on reading `contracts.json`.
- **COMPLETED**: additionally requires every non-deprecated interface to be `ready`, otherwise it bounces as before with `Independent verification failed ... IF-x@n not ready (...)`. The artifacts of a contract task may add `not_contains`; `cmd_exit` (argv without a shell, run in the shared output directory, `timeout_s` default 120, max 600, output written to `path` and then checked literally) **accepts only what the caller declared in the task spec** (saved separately as `caller_artifacts` at submit, compared by path + argv + timeout): a supervisor decision that adds or replaces a cmd_exit is corrected as an invalid decision, and an undeclared command mixed into the stored table is only reported as a failure and never run (batch E fix); commands run only at COMPLETED, and result.json / the resume brief only recheck files. Non-contract tasks still ignore these two keys. The decisions of `pending_user` are summarized into `contracts` of result.json.
- **v2**: free-text reports of official CLI workers gain the matching keys only when they contain `Interfaces:` / `Peer notices:` / `Question for supervisor:` lines or a trailing JSON with keys such as `interface_change`; the four sections stay verbatim; the resume brief appends one line of the task table.
- **Known boundaries (P2)**: `cmd_exit` runs only caller-declared commands (contract / frozen tasks only, no shell, time-limited), and the daemon blocks while it runs; a Pi worker can hand in contract fields only as extra keys of fleet_result (whether TypeBox lets extra keys through is untested); `consumers_runtime.session` is filled by §21; contract notices cost running workers an extra turn; header lines such as `Interfaces:` are also extracted separately when they appear in a non-contract v2 report. The P0 / dispatch refusal texts are wrapped in the original "not a valid decision JSON" correction; P0 refusals are not counted, the rest (bad JSON, dispatch validation) still block after the original three consecutive refusals; an official CLI worker that does not reply to a `[contract]` notice in Done/Evidence form goes through the original 2 corrections and is then blocked as well; the profile recommendation of a held item is computed at decision time, not recomputed at release; `writers` can only be enforced for `json_keys`.

## 19. Several fleets side by side + sub-fleet start/stop and window handover (in development)

Design draft: an internal plan (§4–§8). This section **replaces §8 "each machine keeps only one fleet registry"** and **adds one cell to the §11 v2 support matrix**; the single daemon (one per fleet), slot/generation identity, the one-page layout and the task/service protocol are unchanged. The task table / number dependencies / approval path of split requests are a separate block (feat/fleet-contracts); this section only has the low-level start/stop and handover mechanism.

- **Side by side**: the cmux registry name = `fleetmux` + task number (main `fleetmux`, child `fleetmux:M0`, grandchild `fleetmux:M0.1`), one window registry per fleet (`fleet.manager_for(cfg)`). `latest.json` records only the main fleet; a sub-fleet's run directory is `<parent run>/sub/<number>` (a retry is `<number>-attempt-<n>`, the old directory is kept on file), and its state is in its `subfleet.json`. `status` shows the whole tree; a main-fleet `stop` first stops the descendants (each child daemon first stops its own sub-fleets) and reclaims the panes; a child daemon's explicit stop closes its own Redis, and the parent closes the Redis of a crashed sub-fleet, so no orphans are left. `start --resume` of the main fleet: a sub-fleet whose child daemon is gone has its Redis restarted on the original socket/directory and is brought up with `daemon --resume`.
- **Names and titles**: `start --name` sets a human-readable fleet name (default: the first 24 characters of the task goal, then the run token); only when `--name` is given does the main workspace title become `Fleet <name>`, otherwise it stays `Fleet agents`. A child workspace title is `<main fleet name> › worker-1 › 子 fleet M0` (sub-fleet M0), a grandchild `<main> › worker-1 › M0 › worker-2 › 子 fleet M0.1`; past 60 characters the middle levels collapse to `…`, keeping the first segment and the last two. A child workspace is placed after the parent workspace (and older siblings) with `reorder-workspace --after`; the title of the workspace holding the daemon tab, `Fleet daemon · M0`, is unique per fleet. The parent workspace keeps a placeholder tab at the original worker position, `worker-1 → 已委派：子 fleet M0（看侧栏「…」）` (delegated: sub-fleet M0, see the sidebar "..."); this is now only the fallback for a slot still running in a plain pane: an escalated / split-approved slot first moves into the fleet's own tmux, and the parent worker position holds the live session of the sub-fleet supervisor (§44 "worker-area mapping", §29 dual attach). The worker tabs of a sub-fleet carry the prefix `fx-M0/worker-1` (decoration, like `fx-`); the Redis label is still `worker-1`. **No nesting**: a sub-fleet never adds splits/tabs inside the parent workspace (except the placeholder tab).
- **Limits (hard program checks, with a reason)**: `max_depth` 2 (the main fleet is level 1, M0 level 2, M0.1 level 3), `max_children` 3 (live sub-fleets per fleet at the same time), `max_agents_total` 12 (whole tree; a sub-fleet's supervisor is the delegated worker and is not counted twice), `child_workers_max` 3. Source: default ← main config `limits` ← `limits:` in the main roster YAML (read fresh on every check, i.e. hot reload; the rest of the roster is still validated as a whole). The number must belong to this fleet (main level `M<n>`, under M0 `M0.<n>`).
- **Handover** (`subfleet.spawn_child`, run only in the daemon tick, outside the v2 transaction; callers inside the transaction use `subfleet.request`, which only writes a request file, and `subfleet.check` for a synchronous verdict): the worker must be idle, READY, with no delivery in flight, and its tab present. When the slot runs in the fleet's own tmux (§29 dual attach, §44 worker-area mapping) the parent tab stays in place, no placeholder is opened and nothing is released; the child daemon opens a second client on its first tick; the placeholder / move-surface steps below apply only to plain panes. Parent: open a placeholder tab in front of the worker → back up the worker's AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md → create the child run/config/private Redis → post the child task to the child bus (goal = original instruction + the approved split plan + the delivery paths inside the sub-fleet's own workspace (§23; the parent path is no longer given); deadline = the parent task's remaining time) → mark the slot `delegated` (`pane` points at the placeholder tab, the original pane is recorded as `delegated_pane`, `delegate_cfg` points at the child config) → release the original pane from the parent registry and write it into the child registry → launch the child daemon. Any failed step rolls back and refuses with a reason. Child daemon, first tick: start the child workers → `adopt` (cross-workspace `move-surface`) the original pane to the top left of the child workspace → write the supervisor rules into the original worker directory → tell it its role changed and ask for `READY` (when `adopt.hook` is true this goes into the supervisor mailbox in the child Redis and the pane's own Stop hook picks it up; otherwise it is typed).
- **Hook redirection**: the hooks of the CLI process in the pane are pinned at launch (parent config, `worker-1`, generation) and the process cannot change them. After `claude_stop` / `notify` enter the v2 branch they first call `subfleet.delegated_route`: if the slot is `delegated` in the parent Redis and the generation matches → redirect to the `supervisor` slot (generation 0) of the child config. On its first tick the sub-fleet binds the same Claude session through the usual `bridge_session → register_slot → startup(READY)`. Late callbacks with a mismatched generation are not redirected.
  - **Hook mailbox redirection (sub-fleets with `adopt.hook: true`)**: the adopted Claude pane no longer receives by typing. The child daemon puts the role change (`nudge`), READY corrections, dispatches, handoffs and so on into the generation-0 `supervisor` mailbox of the child Redis; the pane's Stop hook (pinned by the parent process as `claude-stop <parent config> worker-1 <generation>`) waits in the switch run with that pinned identity and recomputes its destination with `delegated_route` on every round: when the parent slot turns `delegated` it wakes from the parent mailbox and waits on the child mailbox instead, so the first message "role changed + reply READY" is a Stop hook feedback; on takeback (child Redis closed, parent slot no longer `delegated`) it returns to the parent slot's own mailbox, and the §29 takeback handoff delivery is picked up directly by this Stop hook without waiting for asyncRewake. Stop's stopping / end-proof markers are written to the Redis of the routed destination; before taking an item the hook checks once more that the route has not changed, and if it has, it puts the popped entry back into its original mailbox and never delivers across Redis instances. An unreadable route (parent Redis restarting) counts as unchanged; while the sub-fleet is starting or leaving (child supervisor not yet / no longer live, child Redis closed) and the parent still delegates, the hook waits in place for the route to change: it takes nothing, settles nothing and does not fall back early to the parent channel; only operator-queued input makes it yield. The asyncRewake fallback (`claude_rewake`) follows the route the same way; StopFailure's `turnend` is recorded at the destination. PostToolUse (§34) is not redirected: an adopted supervisor does not rotate. Boundary: the pane has no UserPromptSubmit hook (it was written at launch for a worker), so text typed by hand into the child supervisor pane is not registered as a formal request as in §5; it is handled as an unsolicited turn, as on the old typing path. On a sub-fleet `--resume`, a hook supervisor with a delivery in flight is taken back as usual (the pane belongs to the parent; the sub-fleet does not start a next generation); after Escape aborts the old turn, the asyncRewake of its last Stop hands over the replay (only takeback without rotation was tested; the rewake replay handover was not measured on real Claude Code). Named sub-fleets and the M<n> sub-fleets started by escalate use the same mechanism, identical on the cmux / fleetmux backends (hooks and Redis only, no terminal).
- **Takeback**: the sub-fleet writes `result.json` → the parent tick records the outcome and sends stop → on its explicit stop the child daemon **only releases, never kills** the adopted supervisor (`handback.json`), retires the child workers as usual and closes Redis → when the parent sees the child daemon has exited: `adopt` the original pane back in front of the placeholder tab and close the placeholder (tmux slot / §44 worker-area mapping: the child side only detaches its second client; the parent tab never left and is renamed in place, §29 `parent_detach_view`), restore the worker rule files, close all tabs of the child workspace (the history page files stay in the child run directory), delete the child registry → slot `starting`, type a notice that it is a worker again and ask for `READY` (same generation, same session; `register_slot` is idempotent). If the original session is gone (cmux restart etc.) → the parent starts the next generation at the placeholder with §17's `respawn`. With outcome `completed`, the parent daemon first copies the output the sub-fleet delivered in its own workspace to the parent path (§23), then: the output file the parent wants exists and is non-empty → `returned`, otherwise `failed` (a retry starts a separate `-attempt-<n>`; sub-fleet runs of the same task count toward `--max-attempts`, §23); while the parent task is still running, the parent supervisor gets a `[Sub-fleet M0 completed|failed]` business message. When the parent task reaches a terminal state, running sub-fleets are stopped and taken back with it. (Added later, feat/worker-area-followups) The next generation started when an M<n> sub-fleet is taken back (started right at takeback on the hook channel, or when the original session is gone) uses a dedicated takeback launch prompt, `subfleet.task_return_prompt`: the previous session of this slot was the supervisor of sub-fleet M0, M0 has ended (outcome), the role is over, the parent supervisor collects the result; read AGENTS.md only and reply READY. It no longer falls through to `RuntimeV2.respawn`'s default "resumed after an interruption". No handoff note is written (the `SUBFLEET_RETURN_PROMPT` handoff turn of §29 named sub-fleets is unchanged).
- **Resume and failure**: on a main-fleet `--resume`, a `delegated` slot is neither taken back nor given a next generation (the pane belongs to the sub-fleet, which resumes afterwards); the case of a whole lost page (`page=False` rebuild) with a `delegated` slot is not handled. Any failed step of `spawn_child` (including a `SystemExit` from `start_redis`) rolls back: the child Redis is closed, the pane goes back to the parent, the slot returns to idle, `failed` is recorded, and the parent daemon is unaffected. Before takeback the pane gets one Escape to abort a turn the adopted session may still be running, so that its late Stop is not taken as the READY of "back to worker"; this is the second place besides §17 "Escape is the only thing accepted as a stop", limited to sub-fleet takeback. The `stop` CLI returns accepted at once; the daemon then waits up to 180 seconds per sub-fleet.
- **A held adopted supervisor (real-machine incident, branch feat/worker-area-followups)**: in a real run, the laptop slept, the adopted supervisor of sub-fleet M0 ended that turn with an API error ("Your computer went to sleep mid-response", StopFailure); the child daemon woke, saw the lease had expired, held the supervisor under "lease/delivery deadline expired" and paused; `fleet.sh resume --config sub/M0/config.json` only lifted the pause and the slot stayed held: an adopted supervisor had no hold exit. StopFailure sends no event for a supervisor, the `turn_ended` that `release_held` waits for never comes, and the pane belongs to the parent fleet, so the sub-fleet does not start a next generation. The process was in fact alive (the pane sat at its prompt), so instead of a §43 `claude --resume` restart the channel is reattached: on every tick the child daemon runs `RuntimeV2.adopted_held_tick`; when the supervisor is adopted, on the hook channel, `held`, and this run is not paused (i.e. the operator has resumed): ① the pane exists and Claude is still running (`has_cli_process`; for a tmux slot, the session's process tree) → send one Escape (in case the old turn is still running), settle the held delivery as `cancelled` (`execution_stopped`, justified by the operator's resume, as in `resume_run`), return the slot to idle and put the same message text back at the head of the supervisor queue; the pane's rewake fallback (started on StopFailure, follows the route and waits on the child supervisor mailbox, hook timeout 24 hours) or the next Stop hook hands it over; ② the session is gone → the child task `failed` ("the adopted supervisor was held and its session is gone after the resume"), the parent fleet takes it back per §19, the slot starts a next generation and the parent supervisor gets `[Sub-fleet M0 failed]`; a service sub-fleet has no task and stays held for the parent's liveness check. Audit: `adopted.reattached` / `adopted.lost` / `adopted.release_failed`. The same batch fixed the StopFailure hook writing to the wrong Redis: `claude_stop_failure` used to connect to the parent Redis first and then switch to the child config per route, so the `hook.stop_failure` audit and the `turnend` marker both landed in the parent Redis (and the child-side rewake fallback therefore could not see the turn had ended); it now routes first and connects second, like the Stop hook. Tests: `tests/test_subfleet.py::test_held_adopted_supervisor_is_taken_back_in_its_own_session_after_resume` (real Redis, real `claude_stop_failure` and real `claude_rewake`: held + paused → resume → Escape, rewake hands over the original message, the supervisor's decision is committed), `…_whose_session_is_gone_hands_the_sub_fleet_back` (child failed → parent takes back, slot generation 1), `test_stop_failure_of_the_adopted_supervisor_is_recorded_in_the_child`. Boundary: if the rewake fallback is also gone (Claude alive in the pane but no hook waiting, e.g. after the 24-hour hook timeout), nobody takes the requeued message and the supervisor sits idle with mail in its mailbox; no timeout handover to the parent fleet was built; the whole sleep → resume path was not measured on real Claude Code.
- **Cross-links between control pages** (`subfleet.page_links` → `history.write_page(..., links)`): the history page of every fleet (the browser page next to the control tab) has a top line: with sub-fleets it lists `子 fleet n：` (sub-fleet n:) followed by "M0 · state (outcome) · worker-1"; a sub-fleet's page offers `← 回到父 fleet` (back to the parent fleet). **A click = switch to the other fleet's cmux workspace** (sub-fleets never nest; they are separate side-by-side workspaces in the sidebar; cmux registers only http/https, so the web page cannot switch workspaces itself): on a link click the page stays on its own file and only adds an explicit marker `?fleet-jump=<ms base36>-<random>&rel=parent|child&to=<other page>` (measured on real cmux: jumping from inside a web page to a file:// page outside the page's directory becomes about:blank, while `cmux browser goto` works); this fleet's daemon reads its own history pane URL on every tick (at most once a second, `cmux browser url`) and acts only on a marker (`subfleet.follow_jump`): it accepts only the parent page of this config's `parent_config` (rel=parent) and the child pages in this run's `sub/*/subfleet.json` (rel=child); if the other side's workspace exists in its cmux registry and is not this workspace → `select-workspace`, then `browser goto` this pane back to this fleet's own page (with the hash state last seen); the other side's pane is not touched. Each marker is first recorded in Redis (`history_jump` in the `fx:service` hash) before acting, so repeated ticks, reloading a page with a marker, or a daemon restart only redo "put back this page" and never switch a second time; a marker older than 120 seconds only puts back this page (to avoid stealing focus after a daemon outage). URL changes without a marker (plain refresh, in-page state) are never treated as clicks. A sub-fleet already taken back / failed (workspace closed), a missing registry on the other side, or a failed `select-workspace`: no switch; the other side's history page (marker removed) is left readable in this pane; a relation that does not match or an unrelated page: no switch, put back this page. A fleet with neither parent nor children does not read its pane. The parent page is rewritten and reloaded when a sub-fleet starts, moves to stopping, or is taken back; data is read only from disk (parent config, `subfleet.json`).
- **v2 matrix addition**: a `claude` supervisor + `claude` workers is allowed only when the config carries `adopt` (i.e. a sub-fleet, task mode); a plain `claude` supervisor is still refused. The adopted supervisor does not rotate (rotation would replace the pane that must go back to the parent fleet); context growth relies on Claude's own compaction. On a sub-fleet `--resume` the adopted pane is taken back like a worker, without a next generation.
- **Official Claude worker panes only**: a Pi bridge is bound to the parent Redis socket and never reconnects after a disconnect (§17), so it is refused with the reason stated; Codex workers are not in the v2 matrix.
- **Claude worker session name**: `--name f<main run token>[-<sub-fleet number>]-<slot>` (e.g. `fa1b2c3d4-M0-worker-2`), fixed per slot and kept across rotations (contract spec appendix C). The cmux tab is still set to slot.generation by the fleet's `rename-tab`; **whether `rename-tab` overrides the terminal title Claude writes from `--name` has not been verified on real cmux**; if it is overridden, switch to `…-<slot>.<generation>` per the spec and state "prefix match, take the highest generation" in interface contract 5-A.
- **Fixed along the way**: the v2 daemon main loop used to call `RuntimeV2.tick` directly, so `Fleet.tick` (§16 roster hot reload, §17 writing `result.json` on task terminal state, this section's sub-fleet takeback) never ran in a real v2 daemon; it now calls `self.f.tick()` (which still calls `RuntimeV2.tick` internally), with a regression test.

**Verification**: `python3 -m pytest -q tests` passes in full (new `tests/test_subfleet.py`: two real private Redis instances + two in-process daemons + a fake cmux that records ownership per registry + the real `claude_stop`/`notify` entry points: handover → sub-fleet READY through the parent hook → the child supervisor's decision dispatched to a child worker → delivery → release → parent takes back the same session, READY → parent supervisor gets the result; no delivery and no death verdict while delegated; missing output judged failed, retry numbering; rollback when starting the child Redis fails; Escape before takeback; next generation in place when the session is lost; main resume keeps the delegated slot and brings up the sub-fleet; the four limits and the number range; Pi/busy slots refused; request files executed via tick; status tree; late generations not redirected; tree stop leaves no orphan Redis; main resume brings up the sub-fleet; child resume takes back the adopted pane; parent task terminal state stops the sub-fleet; regression for the v2 main loop running Fleet.tick; cmux_manager's release/adopt/place_after/close_all with a fake `_run`).

**Not accepted / known gaps**: the cross-workspace `move-surface` handover, `reorder-workspace`, and the ordering of tab titles vs `--name` were not measured on real cmux; whether a running Claude session whose CLAUDE.md is swapped mid-way really replies only with decision JSON in the supervisor format (prompt level) was not measured; this is the largest remaining risk of this block. A delegated slot can still be given work by the parent supervisor; the message queues until takeback. The wiring split request → approval → `spawn_child` is left for the next block after contracts merges. A sub-fleet task's output is only checked for "file exists and is non-empty"; formal acceptance belongs to the contracts block. Independent review, merge and deployment are not done.

## 20. Split-request wiring + sub-fleet interface notices / vetoes (in development)

Design draft: an internal plan (§3–§4) and the interface contract spec (§3.0 / §4.2; interfaces inside a sub-fleet "take effect at once + notify the top level, which may veto"). This section wires up the "split request → approval → `spawn_child`" path that §19 left open; the single daemon, slot/generation identity, the one-page layout, the task/service protocol and §16–§19 are unchanged.

- **Worker request**: the report keeps its usual four parts (Done describes the part already done) and ends with exactly one JSON object `{"escalate": reason, "proposal": [{id, instruction, output}], "workers": n}` (the same object as for contract tasks and `interface_change`; `workers` defaults to the number of proposal blocks). The program checks: proposal ids are distinct and all of the form `<task number>.<n>`, and the task is one this worker is doing (contract: task table `to` = it, state running/escalated; plain task: an item of this task dispatched to it with an `id`), then runs `subfleet.check(..., ready=False)` (number ownership, depth, number of sub-fleets, agents in the whole tree, `child_workers_max`, slot type; idle / delivery in flight are not checked, since the worker is settling this very report; they are checked when the sub-fleet actually starts). Fails → the reason goes both to the worker ("carry on yourself") and to the supervisor; passes → stored in Redis `fx:escalations`, and one message goes to the supervisor (the proposal listed block by block + how to answer).
- **Numbers of plain tasks**: a dispatch item may carry an `id` (`M<n>`, inside a sub-fleet `M0.<n>`); only then is it written to `fx:items` with `[M0]` prefixed to the first line of the dispatch, and only then can a split be requested. A request without a number is refused with the reason "the dispatch must carry an id, or use a contract task". `fx:escalations` / `fx:items` are cleared when a new task is submitted.
- **Contract task table**: the report carrying the request is not a delivery; the task row is marked `escalated` (not judged failed); when refused it stays `running` and the refusal message is recorded under that task (the next report settles it as usual).
- **Supervisor decision**: new decision key `escalations: [{task, decision: approve|revise|reject, workers?, note?}]`. Malformed, a task with no pending request, the same reply also dispatching that task, or approve while also dispatching to the same worker → the usual correction path (counts toward the three consecutive refusals). A RUNNING carrying `escalations` is exempt from "must dispatch when nobody is busy".
  - `approve`: the limits are checked again; **failing the limits is not a format error**: the request stays pending and the supervisor gets the reason (it may lower the worker count, revise or reject). Passes → `subfleet.request` (goal = the task's original instruction + the interface ids the parent fleet must check (shapes in the parent `contracts.json`, read-only) + note; split plan = the approved proposal; a contract task carries `contracts: true`, and the sub-fleet runs its own P0 / P1 first; output = the parent paths of all provided interfaces, which the sub-fleet writes into the matching places of its own workspace and which are copied back on takeback, §23) → task row `delegated`, attempts+1. The next tick executes the request: answer `accepted` → recorded as `delegated`; `rejected` (e.g. the worker happens not to be idle) → the request returns to pending, the row returns to `escalated`, and the supervisor is told why.
  - `revise` / `reject`: the worker gets a message tagged `[M0]` (with the note); a contract task row returns to `running` with this message recorded under M0; the worker revises and reports again, or does it itself. The supervisor re-dispatching that number directly also counts as an answer, and the pending request is voided.
- **Two other kinds of escalation (defect F′)**: besides split requests (key = task number; no `kind` means split), `fx:escalations` also holds `dialog:<slot>:g<generation>` (a worker stopped at a non-delete confirmation dialog, `approve | reject`) and `blocked:<slot>` (a worker reported Blocked, `approve | revise | reject`), answered in the same `escalations[]`; details in §24. Answering a `dialog:` / `blocked:` record that no longer exists (dialog gone / already passed to the user) is only logged, not a format error.
- **Sub-fleet return**: `subfleet.collect` appends the task-table outcome after the `[Sub-fleet M0 completed|failed]` sent to the supervisor. Contract tasks: on completed, each of M0's provided files is checked literally against its interface → all pass: interfaces `ready`, consumers notified, M0 `done`, pending items that depend on it released; otherwise / failed → M0 `failed` (the reason states which attempt). Plain tasks only clear the request record.
- **Interfaces inside a sub-fleet**: a passed sub-fleet skeleton (all v1 in effect) and additions / changes approved by the child supervisor **take effect at once** and are written to `<parent run>/subfleet-notices/`; middle levels pass them up unchanged, and the top-level tick records the source and sends the supervisor one `[sub-fleet interfaces] …`. The top level uses `decision: "veto"` in `interfaces[]` (valid only for interfaces reported by a sub-fleet, otherwise corrected) → written to `<source run>/subfleet-vetoes/`; on its next tick the sub-fleet marks the interface `deprecated` (`deprecated_by: veto by the top supervisor`) and notifies the producer / consumers and the child supervisor. **Deviation from spec §4.1 row 4**: compatible / breaking changes of interfaces inside a sub-fleet are also handled as "take effect at once + vetoable", without waiting for top-level approval (no cross-fleet approval channel was built).
- **Guard rails (review r1 P2-01/02/03 + the first real v2 run of `Fleet.tick`)**: `limits()` catches `yaml.YAMLError`; any exception in a single request in `process_requests` writes a `rejected` answer and deletes the request file; a sub-fleet record without `config.json` (killed halfway through starting) is marked `failed` and skipped in tick / stop / resume; child daemon gone, no `result.json`, parent task still running, and the start grace (120 seconds, by `launched`/`created`; a resume relaunch updates `launched`) exceeded → taken back as `cancelled`, outcome `failed`. Each stage of `Fleet.tick` (roster hot reload / writing result.json / syncing contracts.json / sub-fleets / request answers / interface notices / vetoes) catches its own exceptions, writes a `tick.error` log and continues; `BusError` is still raised (an unknown v2 transaction state must stop). Under v2 `contracts.json` is rewritten on every tick against Redis, so an uncommitted version written early inside a transaction is corrected on the next tick. `CONTRACTS.md` is backed up with AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md and restored on takeback.

**Verification**: `python3 -m pytest -q tests` passes in full (new `tests/test_escalate.py`: contract task table escalated / delegated / refused answer back to pending; revise / reject go back to the worker and settle the next report; limit reasons reach both worker and supervisor; approve over the limit is not counted, an unknown task and dispatching in the same reply are counted; sub-fleet return → done/ready/dependencies released, and failed with the attempt count; plain tasks require a dispatch id; re-dispatch voids the request; tick stage exceptions do not kill the daemon, BusError is raised; a bad roster keeps the limits; two real v2 fleets: dispatch M0 → worker report carries escalate → supervisor approves → tick starts the sub-fleet → the sub-fleet goal contains the original instruction and the proposal → completion and takeback → request cleared, the parent supervisor gets `[Sub-fleet M0 completed]`; a bad request is only answered, no repeated crash; a record without config is marked failed; a vanished child daemon is taken back as failed; child interface notices are passed up and a veto lands in the sub-fleet; middle levels pass up).

**Known gaps (P2)**: if a contract notice is queued for the worker between approve and the tick, starting the sub-fleet is refused as "not idle" and returns to pending (no automatic retry); the Pi worker path of `escalate` is not wired (a Pi pane cannot be handed over anyway; `check` refuses); a veto does not cascade to tasks depending on the interface (they are not marked stale; the child supervisor replans after the notice); notice / veto files are written inside the v2 transaction, so a replayed transaction may notify twice; compatible / breaking changes inside a sub-fleet do not wait for top-level approval (see above); the parent supervisor may re-dispatch the same number to another worker while it is `delegated` (not blocked); there is no in-process end-to-end test of contract parent task → contract sub-fleet (only that the request carries `contracts: true` was verified; the sub-fleet submitting a contract task, writing the supervisor version of CONTRACTS.md into the handed-over worker's directory, and restoring the worker version on takeback have never been run); a middle-level fleet passes the grandchild's interface notices up unchanged and **does not copy its own supervisor** (deviates from spec §4.2 "the middle supervisor only receives copies"); `tick.error` logs one line per tick for a persistent fault, without rate limiting; worker.md hard-codes "workers 1–3" while the real limit is the hot-reloadable `child_workers_max`; the whole chain was not measured on real cmux / Claude sessions; independent review, merge and deployment are not done.

## 21. Peer notices: workers send interface changes to each other directly (in development)

Spec: the interface contract spec (§4.3–§4.4, appendix B §5-A / §5-B, the two "peer notices" sections of appendix C). Rule: Claude Code workers talk to each other through Claude Code's own inter-session messages; Pi / Codex go through the fleet's Redis mailboxes; if either end is not Claude, the broker is used; peers pass only facts, and anything uncertain is asked of the supervisor in the report (`Question for supervisor:`). This section only adds the channel and does not change the approval authority of §18 (approval still goes only through the supervisor's decision JSON); the single daemon, slot/generation identity, the one-page layout and the task/service protocol are unchanged. The code is in `fleetmux/peer.py`; fleet.py / runtime_v2.py only get small hooks.

- **Switch**: there is no separate `peer_notices` setting: peer notices are on with contract tasks (task spec `contracts: true`); prompts, Redis and turn handling of non-contract tasks are unchanged; the broker refuses in non-contract tasks (`peer: interface contracts are off for this task`). Spec appendix C's "with `peer_notices: true`, a CLI that is neither claude nor has a broker → refuse at start" is therefore unnecessary: the broker is available to every CLI.
