- **Prompts**: `CONTRACTS.md` (and the AGENTS.md of a generation launched during the task) gets a section 5 after the §18 section, chosen by the worker's current CLI (`agent_cli(runtime_cfg)`, including profile overrides): Claude gets `agents/contracts_peer_claude.md` (5-A: `ToolSearch select:SendMessage,ListAgents` → `ListAgents` filtered by `{{FLEET_PEER_PREFIX}}` → `SendMessage`; for non-Claude consumers use the broker instead), Pi / Codex get `contracts_peer_broker.md` (5-B); **the Pi / Codex prompts never mention `SendMessage` / `ListAgents` / the prefix**. The supervisor gets `contracts_peer_supervisor.md` appended (explaining that `[peer CC]` / unsolicited / `[peer violation]` lines need no approval from it). `{{FLEET_PEER}}` = `python fleet.py peer --config <cfg> --from <slot>`, with the sender pinned in the command (same trust level as `{{FLEET_CHROME}}`: an agent in the sandbox can forge `--from`; the daemon only checks that it is a live worker of this fleet). The prefix `{{FLEET_PEER_PREFIX}}` = `f<root run token>-`.
- **`consumers_runtime`**: each consumer in `contracts.json` gets `cli` (the worker's current CLI) and `session` (for Claude, `claude_session_name`, i.e. the §19 `--name`, fixed per slot and kept across rotations; otherwise `null`). The tick rewrites it on every tick by text diff, so a CLI change at rotation or a roster hot reload is reflected on the next tick.
- **broker `peer`**: CLI `fleet.py peer --from --to <worker|consumers:IF-x> --iface IF-x --text-file <a file in your own directory>`, on the same event path as `request()` (v1 `Fleet.handle`, v2 `runtime_v2.handle` new kind `peer`; unlike chrome, its only side effects are Redis writes, so it can run inside the v2 transaction). Checks per appendix C, with these exact error texts: `--to` is not a live worker of this fleet (slot name / label / Claude session name all accepted; not itself or the supervisor) → `peer: --to must be a worker in this fleet tree or consumers:<IF-id>`; interface not registered and the first line is not `0 -> 1 | new | UNAPPROVED` → `peer: IF-x is not registered`; over 600 characters, a first line not in the fixed format, a first-line interface different from `--iface`, or a body containing a `[Supervisor instruction …]` header → `peer: notice must be <= 600 chars and start with the fixed first line`; `READY` from a non-producer → `peer: only the producer may announce READY`; the 21st message from one sender within an hour (rolling 3600 seconds) → `peer: rate limit 20/hour reached`. Three added messages: `peer: --from must be a live worker of this fleet`, `peer: --text-file must be a file in your own directory`, `peer: no consumer of IF-x has a worker yet` (`consumers:` expands to no dispatched consumer). A valid notice goes as `[peer] <original text>` + one sentence "act on it per CONTRACTS.md, reply in the AGENTS.md format" into the target's v2 mailbox (`contract_queue`, with an empty report ownership, so a notice turn settles no task); the target picks it up on its next idle turn.
- **How copies and supervisor forwards travel**: `[peer CC] <sender> -> <target>:` + original text, the `Peer notices:` / `[worker question]` lines of an unsolicited turn, and `[peer violation]` all go first into the Redis list `fx:peer:notes` and are **appended to the end of the supervisor's next business message** (like §16 `[Roster change]`), never sent alone: a message of its own would force the supervisor to answer a RUNNING with no dispatch, which counts as an error under the existing rule when no worker is busy. Cost: copies wait for the next business message (in a contract task, usually the target worker's reply to the `[peer]`).
- **Logs**: `contracts.log.jsonl` appends `peer` (sender, target, interface, level, state, original text), `peer.rejected` (with error and original text), `peer_notices` (the lines in reports, the only trace of the SendMessage path), `unsolicited`, `peer.violation`.
- **Unsolicited turns**: a turn in which a Claude worker is woken by a peer's `SendMessage` (or by the operator typing) corresponds to no delivery in flight. The existing handling was already "do not settle, only audit `turn.ignored`"; this section has both ignored branches of v2 `tui_turn` (no delivery in flight / the turn was not started by a typed delivery) send the daemon an `unsolicited` event (no reply awaited); the daemon checks run / generation / session and records `turn.unsolicited`, and in a contract task writes the log and forwards the `Peer notices:` / `Question for supervisor:` lines to the supervisor; it touches no lease, pops no report ownership, counts no correction, renews nothing and queues no message, so "idle must dispatch" is not triggered. v1 Claude does the same in the duplicate branch of `on_turn`. The same Stop firing twice: identical to the last entry of `fx:replies:<slot>` (v1 original text / v2 structured value) → recorded as `turn.duplicate` and not forwarded again.
- **Violations**: in the `Peer notices:` line of a report or unsolicited turn, if the x in `from <x>` neither starts with this tree's prefix nor is a worker name / label of this fleet → write `peer.violation` and send the supervisor `[peer violation]`. The program cannot block SendMessage itself; this relies on the prompts on both ends.

**Verification**: `python3 -m pytest -q tests` passes in full (new `tests/test_peer.py`: both section-5 variants injected by CLI and the Pi / Codex text has no `SendMessage`; `consumers_runtime.session` refreshed with CLI / rotation; every broker refusal text + log; delivery into the mailbox + empty report ownership + copy carried by the next business message + drained only once; READY / new interface / session-name targets; rate limit per sender, rolling hour; violation lines; v2 with real private Redis + real `peer` CLI + real Claude Stop hook: `[peer]` typed into a Claude worker, the turn woken by a peer recorded as unsolicited without settling the delivery in flight, then the delivery in flight settles normally, a duplicate Stop is not forwarded again; the same for v1 Claude).

**Known gaps (P2)**: cross-fleet delivery (target in another fleet of the same tree) is refused outright with a request to explain it in the report, and copies go only to this fleet's supervisor, not up the parent chain to the top: the spec needs the root daemon to hold the routes of the whole tree, and §18 contracts themselves cover a single fleet only, so this comes with sub-fleet contracts; if an official-CLI worker does not reply in Done/Evidence format after a `[peer]`, it goes through the usual 2 corrections and then blocked (like the §18 `[contract]` notice); while a Claude worker woken by a peer is still idle in Redis, the daemon may type the next delivery into its input box; Claude queues typed text after the current turn, which is recorded as unsolicited because it "was not started by a delivery", but if Claude merges both into one turn it would be taken as the reply to that delivery (not measured); the exact transcript form of a received `SendMessage`, and the visibility of `--name` and `ListAgents` on real 2.1.x, were not measured in this block; `--from` can be forged by an agent in the sandbox (as with chrome). Violation detection takes the word after `from <x>` in the `Peer notices:` line, so a worker that writes the daemon's `[contract]` notice as "from daemon / from the supervisor" causes a false `[peer violation]`; `fx:peer:notes` / `fx:peer:sent:*` are carried into resume by AOF, but resuming with uncopied notes or a half-used rate window is untested. Independent review, merge and deployment are not done.

## 22. Rotation thresholds configured per model / CLI

Requirement: the handoff of a worker window must be configured per model; Claude Code with a 1M context should not hand off often. Before this, Claude's window was fixed at `--claude-window` (200,000) and the rotation ratio was one global `--handoff-ratio`, so a 1M opus worker rotated at 120k. This section only changes where the threshold comes from; the rotation flow (§4), handoff notes and generation identity are unchanged.

- **Rule**: rotate before a delivery when `used >= ratio × window` (the original rule; since §32 also checked when idle with no message). `window` and `ratio` are resolved independently; the first hit wins:
  1. the worker's roster entry `window` / `handoff_ratio`;
  2. the roster's top-level `handoff:` table, keyed first by `<cli>:<model>` (exact model string) then `<cli>`, value `{window?, ratio?}`;
  3. the `handoff:` table of the same name in `config.json` (static, validated at start, not hot-reloaded);
  3b. ratio only: the `handoff_ratio` of the slot's running profile card (§12 model cards, `runtime_profile` including inheritance), source shown as `card <profile id>` (merged into this level during integration C; the separate `handoff_ratio()` check of the original C branch is no longer used directly for rotation);
  4. built-in: a Claude model name containing `[1m]` / `opus` / `fable` / `sonnet` (lower-case substring) → 1,000,000, other Claude models → `--claude-window`; Codex takes the self-reported `model_context_window` from its rollout and Pi its self-reported ctx_window ("measured"); only an explicit `window` overrides them;
  5. `--handoff-ratio`. Both flags remain as the lowest default layer, for backward compatibility.
- **The model is the current running value**: `worker_model` (Claude, same for the supervisor) / `codex_model` / `pi_model` from `runtime_cfg(name)` (including `runtime_launch.overrides` written by a profile switch); the supervisor uses the same resolution, just without a worker entry.
- **Hot reload**: follows §16 roster detection and takes effect at the next delivery check, without restart; `roster.changed` carries a `handoff` field with old and new values (table `table`, each worker). Validation: ratio is a number in (0,1), window a positive integer (booleans do not count), table-key CLIs only claude/codex/pi, entries only window/ratio; anything invalid rejects the whole file per §16 and the old thresholds stay. A retiring worker keeps its old threshold until retired.
- **Display**: each resolution writes `handoff_window` (or `measured`) / `handoff_ratio` / `handoff_source` (e.g. `window: built-in opus; ratio: --handoff-ratio`) into `fx:agent:<slot>`; after a roster change all are refreshed at once. `status`'s `context` carries these three, and the control line shows `handoff=<ratio>@<window>`.
- **Known boundaries**: the built-in table matches by substring; treating every `opus` as 1M is an assumption (the real window of each model was not checked at the API level; if some opus variant is really 200k, override with `claude:<model>`); model strings match exactly, so an alias (`opus`) and a full name (`claude-opus-…`) are different keys; an adopted sub-fleet supervisor still does not rotate (§19).

**Verification**: `python3 -m pytest -q tests` passes in full (new `tests/test_handoff_limits.py`: built-in table and flags, the five-level order, opus at 300k does not rotate / haiku at 120k rotates, profile running model, worker override + hot reload changing the threshold without restart, bad values reject the whole file and keep the old thresholds, config table validation, Codex measured window untouched unless explicitly overridden, status fields; `tests/test_roster.py` adds bad-value parameters and v2 hot reload into `status`). Independent review, merge and deployment are not done.

**Claude window and self-compaction (added after review r2)**: per the official docs `code.claude.com/docs/en/model-config.md`, bare `opus` (Opus 5.5) and bare `sonnet` are both native 1M, so the built-in table also counts `sonnet` as 1M (haiku still uses `--claude-window`). Claude Code self-compacts at about 967K (CHANGELOG 2.1.247); `autoCompactWindow` can only lower the compaction point, not raise it, so the fleet does **not** set it: rotation at 0.6 × 1M = 600K always comes before self-compaction. A roster that raises window close to 1M must leave its own margin (ratio × window below about 967K).

## 23. Brake and acceptance contract (development batch E; renumbered from §20 to §23 on the integration branch)

Basis: the internal overall design (v2 §1.3 #2 / #3, v1 §1.3, R2, R14, batches 2.1 / 2.3).

### Acceptance contract, layer one: `artifacts_frozen`

- The task spec adds `"contract": {"artifacts_frozen": true}`: the `artifacts` written by the caller are the acceptance contract. When frozen, artifacts must be non-empty; artifacts appended in a supervisor decision are **always ignored**, with audit event `contract.append_ignored` (carrying the dropped entries); a supervisor that wants a change can only go BLOCKED with a reason, and the caller resubmits the task (R14: layer 1 leaves no channel).
- Frozen tasks get the extended checks like `contracts: true` tasks: `not_contains`, `json_keys` (new), `cmd_exit`. `json_keys`: the whole file is a JSON object, or the file ends with one JSON object (the verdict on the last line of a review report), and every listed key is present. `cmd_exit` runs only the argv frozen by the caller (no shell, with a timeout).
- Unfrozen, non-contract tasks: no change; the supervisor can still append, and extended keys are still ignored. `result.json` and the handover recheck of `start --resume` use the same rules (`extended_checks`).

### Brake: budget card + wall table (`fleetmux/brake.py`, `fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml#walls`)

- Default cash cap (overall design v2 ★3): for an account with `meter: usd_equiv`, a card without `max_usd` uses that account's `caps.day_usd / caps.night_usd` (default 40 USD day / 10 USD night), evaluated per scene (night reads batch B's `fx:night.brake_card`); if the caller sets one, the caller's value is used. A missing quota.json is still unknown and does not trip.
- Budget card: task spec `budget`, or `start --budget JSON` for service (written to config `budget`). Fields `pool / max_usd / max_week_pct / max_calls / max_rounds / max_hours / max_busy`. `pool` names an account id or alias from the account pool file (anything not in the file is refused at once); by the account's `meter`: `usd_equiv` watches walls + an equivalent amount `max_usd` (the pool reading's increase over the baseline when the card was opened); `week_pct` watches walls + a weekly amount `max_week_pct` (the pool's absolute percentage). Fields that do not match the meter are refused at once. No real account name is written in code.
- Counting: `calls` = every delivery (counted in one place for v1 / v2); `rounds` = accepted supervisor decisions; `hours` = from card opening (soft line; the task's `hours` is still the hard `budget_exhausted`); `max_busy` can only tighten `--max-busy`. Counters live in Redis `fx:brake`; a new task gets a new card.
- Pool readings come from the quota scheduler's `~/.fleetmux/quota.json` (configurable via config `quota_file`): `{"pools": {"<account id>": {"cash_used_usd" | "week_used_pct", "at"}}}`. Missing / unreadable / older than 1 hour = unknown: shown in `brake.json`, **does not trip**.
- On reaching a line: pause (no delivery, no rotation, everything resumable) + audit `brake.tripped` + `<run>/brake.json` + an optional `brake_notify` argv (`start --brake-notify`, no shell). Under v2 the decision is made inside one transaction and the notification outside it.
- Resume: `fleetmux resume` is refused while the budget line is still exceeded (no matter who last wrote the pause record: a later wall, or F's hold rewriting the pause reason, does not unlock it); it needs `resume --raise LIMIT=VALUE` (the pool cannot be changed); a pause caused by a wall can be resumed directly. A new task opening a new card clears the brake pause of the previous card (a manual operator pause is not cleared).
- One wall table: account-level walls (only the usage / weekly / session limit wording of the Claude / Codex CLIs themselves; generic `rate limited` / `429 Too Many Requests` / `quota exceeded` are not walls: third-party tools such as Tavily / reddit / WebFetch print these words, and worker reports pasted into the supervisor screen carry them too; review r1 P1-01); per-pool walls ("You've reached your Fable limit", "Run /usage-credits to continue", "Set up usage credits"; a bare `/usage-credits` is no longer recognized, and the percentage usage notices in `walls.notices`, "You've used N% of your … limit", are skipped as whole lines and count as a wall at neither level, see §48.4). Sources: the screen tail when stalled (past `stall_timeout`) (v1 in `on_stall`, v2 in the brake transaction of `Fleet.tick`, before the v2 hold); v1 also checks short turn texts (≤400 characters; long reports are not judged as walls). Three more paths do not wait for `stall_timeout`: ① startup answers with a wall instead of READY → slot `walled` (no death verdict, not blocked) + resumable pause; ② short replies of v2 official CLIs (≤400 characters, `tui_turn` carries `wall_text`) and Pi model-error events (`candidate {tool: model, error}`) → the delivery is committed as `walled` (result not used, not forwarded to the supervisor), and the original instruction is redelivered exactly once with "[Resumed after a usage wall]" (a replayed settled hits `settle.late`); ③ the watchdog wall gate (below).
- **On a wall, move down a model chain (branch `feat/wall-fallback-chain`)**: replaces the old "per-pool wall drops one tier within the same CLI; account-level walls and the supervisor only stop". The supervisor chain is, in priority order, Astra → Fable → Opus → Sol → DeepSeek Flash; the worker chain is Opus → Sol → Sonnet → Terra → DeepSeek Flash.
  - **One chain, one source**: chain = `fleet.yaml#discovery.supervisor` / `.worker` (a private file still overrides the whole block, §52). It is resolved into the (cli, model) pairs usable on this machine: read `clis` / `gateways` from the `<fleet home>/discovered.yaml` cache (no live probes), re-ranked for the **current** mode with `discover.rank` (a cache ranked in an old mode is fine); without a cache, use the model cards (`fleet.yaml#models`) plus the models the run's slots are currently running; the cards carry no pi `openai-codex/…` ids, so without a cache the chain usually holds only claude entries. Only models named on the chain are used; unlisted models are never a fallback (reaching the end = stop). The supervisor chain accepts only `cli_pairs.CLIS` (pi / codex / claude). `walls.same_cli_downgrade` no longer takes part in wall decisions; the table stays as a copy of the deployer's `cli_tier_map.yaml` (`brake.downgrade` was deleted).
  - **The chain lives in Redis**: one project config file split into blocks; the most frequently read/written blocks are loaded into Redis, with timed write-back. Every time the daemon starts up (both a new start and `start --resume` go through `Fleet.run`), `Fleet.wall_chains_load` writes the resolved supervisor chain, worker chain, the mode of both chains, `discovery.supervisor_pi_thinking`, and the row states of the combination table for this run's `mode` into one key of this run's Redis, `fx:wall:chains` (JSON); wall decisions (`on_wall` / `wall_tick` / supervisor rotation) read only this key at runtime and no longer read `discovered.yaml` / `fleet.yaml`. If the key is missing (resuming a run started before this version), the first wall resolves once on the spot and writes it. A failed resolution stores an empty chain and the error, and a wall then stops. Existing parts that still read files are outside this item: the wall wording table (`brake.wall_table`, cached by file stamp) and `preflight_profile` on a CLI change (the same combination-table check as at actor startup). There is currently no runtime entry point for changing the chain, so this cut writes nothing back; when one is added, changes are written back on a timer into the `discovery` block of `fleet.yaml`, with the write-back interval as a parameter in the same `fleet.yaml`, and no new yaml file.
  - **Starting point**: the position of the current (cli, model) on the chain: the same entry, or the entry of the same CLI under the mode where that model first matches (`claude-opus-5-5` in the run and `opus` on the chain count as the same entry); if not on the chain (e.g. fable / haiku as a worker, a Codex worker), start from the head of the chain.
  - **Find the first usable entry below (`brake.wall_action`), skipping**: account-level wall → every model of that account; per-pool wall → only that one model; entries already walled in this run (Redis `fx:walls`, `'<cli>:<model>' → {cli, model, account, scope, at, agent}`, shared by all slots; no reset time, skipped for the rest of the run; a later per-pool wall never narrows an account-wall record); entries the combination table does not allow: the `cli_pairs` row of a worker with the **supervisor's current** CLI, and of the supervisor with **each live worker's current** CLI, must be verified with no `adapter_missing` (the smoke lifecycle's allowance for unverified rows does not count). **Account definition**: official CLIs (claude / codex) are one account per CLI; bridge CLIs (pi / opencode / dsh) are one account per provider, the part of the model id before the first `/` (`pi:openai-codex`, `pi:workbuddy-ds`): pi carries both a Codex account and a WorkBuddy account, and under "same CLI = same account" Sol hitting the Codex account wall would also skip deepseek flash on pi, so in task mode the worker chain could never reach its end.
  - **Execution**: `on_wall` only makes the pure decision (chain + skip rules + combination table), records `agent.wall` and the pending `wall_downgrade` (`cli` / `from` / `from_key` = before, `to_cli` / `to` / `key` = after), with a resumable pause. At an idle boundary (no delivery in flight, `idle` or `walled`) `wall_tick` executes: on a CLI change it first runs `preflight_profile(cli_change_ok=True)` (binary, pi's `--allow-unsandboxed-pi` / pi_home, the combination-table check the actor itself would do); refused → the entry is recorded with `scope: refused`, audit `brake.switch_refused`, and the next entry on the chain is tried (`agent.wall` is updated to the new target; `brake.json` keeps the first decision); all refused → stop as before (`brake.switch_exhausted`, the slot stays walled and the fleet paused). A passing entry starts the next generation with `wall_rotate`: the launch overrides `worker_cli` / `supervisor_cli` + that CLI's model key (worker: `brake.MODEL_KEY`; supervisor: `brake.SUPERVISOR_MODEL_KEY`, claude → `supervisor_model`, pi → `pi_model`); a worker switched to pi keeps the run's default thinking, a supervisor switched to pi takes the run's supervisor pi thinking, else `discovery.supervisor_pi_thinking`; handoff skeleton (the same `skeleton` as a handoff_ratio rotation), screen saved, tab in place, old tab killed, v2 `confirm_retired`. After the new generation is READY only the wall's own pause is lifted (compared against `fx:brake.paused_by`; an F hold / operator pause / exceeded budget all stay paused; the audit name is still `brake.downgraded`). If another card swap (batch B night / live profile) has already changed the slot (comparing the **pre-switch cli + model**) → voided (`brake.downgrade_superseded`). A slot that hit a wall at startup with the chain exhausted restarts its next generation with the original launch after the operator resumes (same for the supervisor slot).
  - **Supervisor**: the main fleet's supervisor slot also rotates down the supervisor chain (Astra → Fable → Opus → Sol → DeepSeek Flash), and the new generation's CLI may differ from the old one: tab `supervisor-1.<generation>`, not entered into the worker routing name table. The delivery channel follows the slot's current-generation config (`runtime_cfg` / `slot_cfg`: Stop hook mailbox for a Claude supervisor, plugin + operator_input for a pi supervisor); the reply-channel wording of already queued messages is written at enqueue time for the old CLI (pi: `Use fleet_decision …`; official CLIs: `… JSON from AGENTS.md`), and at rotation `RuntimeV2.reword_pending` rewrites it for the new CLI inside the transaction (audit `queued.reworded`; the closing sentence of service worker requests, `fleet_result` ↔ "exactly one JSON object", likewise), and `Fleet.request_message` now words by the slot's current-generation CLI. Sub-fleet supervisors (adopt / parent_config) and the advisor (§27) are outside this item: they still only stop.
  - **Not done**: switching back to the head of the chain automatically after the quota resets (not decided); parsing reset times from wall wording.
  - **Remaining gaps**: the watchdog's dialog table is chosen by the run-level CLI (`watchdog.Watchdog.cli`) and official-CLI card rendering chooses slots by the run-level CLI (`RuntimeV2.render_cards`); this has been so since night card swaps, so a slot that changed CLI is still treated with the run's default CLI in these two places; mixing Claude TUI slots + Pi bridge slots in one run and in-place rotation between a Claude supervisor ↔ pi supervisor have had no real-cmux acceptance (unit tests: `tests/test_wall_chain.py`).
- A v1 task's `pause` now also stops delivery (it used to apply only to service / v2).

### E before F: the watchdog's wall gate (`Fleet.wall_gate`)

- Interface: `Fleet.wall_gate(slot, screen, source='watch') -> record | None`. The watchdog (F, §24, `watchdog.Watchdog.check`) calls it after every screen capture, before the dialog allowlist / unknown-dialog hold / stalled ladder / frozen. It looks only at the last `brake.GATE_TAIL`=25 lines of the screen (`brake.screen_wall`).
- Returns a record: the wall has tripped the brake (pause, `agent.wall`, audit `brake.tripped`, `brake.json` source=watch, optional notification; under v2 inside one transaction). On this tick the watchdog **presses no key, does not hold, does not kill or rotate** that slot: a wall menu (e.g. `❯ 1. Yes, switch…`) is never answered as a dialog. Calling again when the same agent's wall has already braked only returns the original record and does not trip twice. Returns None: no wall; the watchdog follows its own table.
- The hold exit (F's `turn_ended` → `release_held`) only puts the slot back to idle and **does not lift the pause**; F's hold rewrites the pause reason, but the brake record is kept separately in `fx:brake`, and `resume` rechecks the budget card every time and refuses when over the line. Exception (`RuntimeV2.handoff_pause_arm` / `handoff_pause_lift`): a pause caused by the slot's own handoff delivery being held (handoff timeout etc.) registers an ownership marker `agent.handoff_pause` at hold time (conditions: the fleet was not paused before the hold (the same handoff held twice in one tick, by expire and then `finish_rotation`, counts once), and the old pane is not a tab behind tmux); `RuntimeV2.tick` rechecks at the end of every tick, and only when all hold does the daemon resume automatically: the pause has not been rewritten by another reason (`fx:service.at` unchanged; every pause rewrites it; only at counts, not reason), the task is still running, the old generation is confirmed closed (kill + `confirm_retired`: handoff delivery cancelled + `stop_confirmed`, `launch_ready=1`), the new generation is READY (idle, `runtime_ready=1`, its own new lease), the brake has not tripped (`fx:brake.tripped` / `paused_by` empty) and the budget is not exceeded. Lifting is audited as `execution.auto_resumed`, and the slot and the supervisor each get a notice card; if only the brake is in the way → audit `execution.auto_resume_refused`, and it waits for the operator's resume as before. Lifting writes no lease / message: uncertain deliveries of other slots stay held. Slots behind tmux, pauses already set by another reason at hold time, and pauses with several stacked reasons stay as before.


## 24. Five-state watchdog (transport v2, batch F; renumbered from §20 to §24 on the integration branch)

v2 transport only; v1 `check_stalls` / `on_stall` are unchanged. Code: `fleetmux/watchdog.py`, called by `RuntimeV2.tick` outside the Redis intents (screen capture / key presses / kill / new generation are all side effects); settlement (hold / enqueue / publishing a result) still goes through `transaction`. Every non-idle slot is judged once per `watch_interval` (default 5 s); a state change writes the audit `agent.state` (from / to / evidence).

| State | Criterion | Action |
|---|---|---|
| `idle` | No in-flight delivery and not starting | Nothing; never judged dead however long it lasts (E1) |
| `working` | In flight, and **any** of the three signals in the table below moved within `stall_window` (default 300 s) (one moving resets all window baselines), and the transcript / rollout moved within `stall_timeout` (default 1200 s) | None |
| `prompting` | The last `tail_lines` lines of the screen match the dialog-table allowlist (also checked while starting; the trust dialog comes before READY) | Save the screen to `<run>/watchdog/`; **a text match is not an authorization**: also verify ① the pane belongs to this fleet's registry and is not adopted by a sub-fleet, ② the entry's `verify` rule proves the concrete action / path in the dialog lies within the run's existing authorization; only when both pass send the configured keys (audit `watch.prompt_answered` with `authorized`), otherwise treat it as an unknown dialog: save the screen + pause dispatch + mark the slot `prompt_wait` (lease untouched; once a human answers, that turn settles normally), audit `watch.prompt_unverified`. If delete / move words appear at the bottom of the screen (§24 delete classification), no key is pressed even when the allowlist authorizes it. The same dialog on the same delivery still present after `max_hits` (3) answers → treat as `stalled` |
| `prompting` (unknown) | Matches `unknown_markers` but is not on the allowlist | Save the screen + pause dispatch + mark the slot `prompt_wait` (not a hold); press no key at all |
| `stalled` | In flight, and ① **all** three signals still for a full `stall_window` (a slot whose Claude Stop for this delivery was deferred for background tasks and whose transcript has not moved since does not take ①; see "Stop deferred" below); or ② transcript / rollout still for a full `stall_timeout` (hard cap: catches a screen that keeps spinning while the stream behind it has died) | Escape, at most `stall_escapes` (3) times, spread evenly over `stall_grace` (300 s); no hold. If the **transcript** moves meanwhile → `working` (the screen does not count: Escape itself changes the screen, and counting it would mean the ladder never reaches frozen) |
| `frozen` | stalled for a full `stall_grace`; or the screen digest unchanged after 3 Escapes; or `ps` sees the tty process in state `U` (Linux `D`) twice in a row | Save screen → skeleton handoff note → new generation (launch-held) `tab_into` in place → `kill_pane` (only surfaces in this fleet's registry; kills their tty process tree) → `confirm_retired` (delivery cancelled, `stop_confirmed`) → open the launch gate → worker: send the supervisor a `FROZEN` report, the original instruction is **not rerun**; supervisor: the original message is requeued with a note; service: publish the request as `stalled`. **Since §43 an official Claude worker first resumes its own session** (`claude --resume`, same order, same tab) and gets that delivery again; only a resumed generation that freezes again takes the fresh-generation path here. Run paused / adopted by a sub-fleet / pane not in this fleet's registry → hold only, no kill |

- **The three stall signals (`feat/stall-5min`)**: stall detection changed from "transcript only, 30 minutes" to "three signals, 5 minutes + a 20-minute transcript hard cap". A 10-minute pytest / `sleep 420` leaves the transcript still; lowering only the threshold to 300 would Esc-interrupt a running tool, so all three must be still.

  | Signal | How it is read | What counts as alive |
  |---|---|---|
  | Session record | `Fleet.last_activity`: mtime of the Claude main transcript + sub-agent transcripts, the Codex rollout, the pi session file, and the delivery time `busy_since` | Newer than the window baseline |
  | Screen | First 16 hex chars of the sha256 of each tick's `capture_output` (a separate field from the frozen criterion's `esc_digest`) | Differs from the window baseline; an empty capture = unreadable |
  | Process-tree CPU | One `ps -axo pid=,ppid=,tty=,time=`: cumulative CPU seconds of the processes on the pane's tty (tmux panes use `#{pane_tty}`) **plus all their descendants by ppid**. Tool subprocesses of Claude Code / Codex start their own session (tty `??`) and are invisible to `ps -t`, hence the ppid walk | Sum of positive per-tick increments within the window > `stall_cpu_pct` (default 5) % × `stall_window` (default 15 s); a tick where the total drops because a child exited counts 0; `ps` failure / no process on the tty = unreadable |

  Baselines live in slot fields `quiet_since / quiet_act / quiet_digest / quiet_cpu / quiet_gain` (same style as `esc_digest / esc_at`); `classify` stays a pure function with new inputs `quiet` (seconds all three signals have been still together) and `stall_window`. **An unreadable signal cannot prove stillness**: the window restarts at that tick and only the hard cap applies (the fake mgr has no CPU probe, so existing tests are unaffected). Baselines reset on slot idle / settlement / same-generation rebind / frozen rotation. The new signals only govern "entering stalled"; leaving stalled still depends on the transcript alone. The `frozen` criterion "`ps` sees U/D twice in a row" is unchanged and still looks only at the tty's direct processes.
  - **Stop deferred = waiting for its own background tasks, not stillness (branch fix/watchdog-background-wait)**: In an incident a run froze three times (g1 / g2 / g3), each exactly `stall_window` after `turn.deferred`: the Claude turn had ended and the input box sat empty waiting for its own `run_in_background` / `nohup` pytest; Escape on an empty input box does not change the screen, so after 3 tries frozen was certain, and killing the session also killed the pytest in its tree. Process-tree CPU did not save it (a `nohup` process orphaned to pid 1 cannot be found by ppid; a sleep- or I/O-heavy test does not accumulate enough over 10 minutes). Fix: when the hook `fleet.claude_stop` sees `background_tasks`, besides the audit `turn.deferred` (now with `types`) it also sends a `turn_deferred` event (`agent / generation / run_id / thread / tasks[{id,type,status,description}]`); the daemon (`RuntimeV2.handle`), after checking same run / same generation / same session, writes the slot fields `bg_wait=<current delivery>`, `bg_wait_at`, `bg_wait_act=<last_activity at that moment>`, `bg_tasks` only when the lease is `received`; otherwise it only audits (held and idle slots are not written). Watchdog `check`: `bg_wait == current scope` and `last_activity <= bg_wait_act + BG_WAIT_GRACE` (30 s: Claude Code writes the hook result into the transcript about 1 s after the Stop hook returns, and that does not count as waking) → this tick skips `quiet_for`, `quiet = 0`, `quiet_since` reset; if the condition fails (another delivery / the transcript moved after the grace = the background task finished and woke the turn) → clear `bg_wait` and restart the window from this tick. **Unchanged**: the `stall_timeout` hard cap (`age` still counts: a hung background task still goes stalled → Escape → frozen), `ps` U/D, dialogs, the wall gate, `classify` itself; a stall in the middle of a turn has no Stop and gets no marker. `bg_wait` is cleared wherever `quiet_since` is: slot idle / settlement, hold-exit release, same-generation rebind, frozen rotation. This corrects a misjudgment, so there is no switch (`--stall-window 0` still turns the whole window rule off). Tests `tests/test_turn_deferred.py`, `tests/test_watchdog_stall_window.py` (five deferred cases).
  - **The CPU threshold is not 0 (difference from a literal "CPU does not grow")**: measured locally, an idle Claude TUI (text pasted, no Enter) still creeps at 0.6%-1.5% of one core (an isolated session gained 0.88 s in 150 s; a Claude session loaded with MCP servers in a user terminal gained 0.9-1.35 s in 90 s), Codex idles at 0.16%, pi at 0; with a literal "no growth" Claude could never be judged stalled. 5% is a 3x margin over idle creep; a child doing real work (pytest near 100%) passes it easily; Claude / pi drawing their own spinners use about 1.4%-4.5%, which is covered by the screen signal, not CPU.
  - **Screen assumptions for the three CLIs (measured in an isolated local tmux, one capture every 5 s)**: Claude Code 2.1.280 running a 100 s python sleep in a foreground Bash tool → on-screen tool timer `(1m 12s)` + spinner timer, 12/12 ticks changed; Codex 0.162.1 running `sleep 240` → `Working (3m 43s • esc to interrupt)`, 29 of 30 ticks changed; pi 1.2.0 (`Loader` spinner at 80 ms per frame + bash line `Elapsed 1m 28s`) 12/12 changed. All three with "pasted, no Enter" idle: 0 of 30 changed. So long tool calls are not misjudged; a paste without Enter is judged stalled after about 5 minutes. An API retry countdown changes the screen → not stalled (that is the startup READY deadline path, untouched here).
  - **The advisor slot (§27) gets no exemption and no separate threshold**: the resident advisor is always official Claude; during long thinking its screen keeps changing with a seconds counter, so the window never judges it stalled; a consultation with all three signals still for 300 s has already exceeded `ADVICE_WAIT` (180 s), so its answer is dropped as late anyway (`advice.late`); conversely, exempting it would mean that when the advisor truly hangs, every dispatch first waits 180 s and then goes out with defaults, until the hard cap. A one-shot `claude -p` advisor is not a pane and is not the watchdog's business.
  - **Impact**: the `stall_timeout` default 1800 → 1200 lives in `fleet.DEFAULTS`, shared by v1 / v2, so v1 `check_stalls` also goes from 30 to 20 minutes (the one exception to "v1 unchanged" in this section); `stall_window` / `stall_cpu_pct` apply only to the v2 watchdog. The v2 wall gate's `stalled_screens` still captures by `stall_timeout` (the watchdog `check` passes the wall gate first itself, so a window-judged stalled slot that hits a wall also gets no key press).

- **Frozen rotation order and failure**: start the new generation first, then kill the old one (same order as `respawn` / handoff, which keeps the layout). Until the old generation is confirmed retired the new one cannot get work: it is `starting` (`pump` only delivers to `idle`), the old lease still holds the delivery (`offer` needs an idle lease, `register_slot` reports `busy`), and with `launch_ready=0` its READY is refused. If any step fails (new generation does not start / cannot enter the original tab / old generation cannot be killed or survives the kill) → kill the new generation first, restore the slot fields to the old generation, hold (delivery stays uncertain, not cancelled, not rerun), audit `watch.rotate_failed`; only if the new generation also cannot be killed can two sessions coexist: slot held + launch gate closed + task blocked, and the report names both panes for a human.
- Dialog table `fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml#watchdog_prompts` (replaceable with `--prompt-table`), hot reloaded by mtime; a bad edit is audited as `watch.table_rejected` and the old table stays. Every entry must have a `verify`: `own_cwd` (all paths in the dialog are this slot's directory, trusted at spawn), `paths_in_run` (all paths are in this slot's directory or `<run>/workspace`, i.e. already authorized by `--add-dir`); a path that cannot be read → no key. Long paths wrapped by the TUI are joined back as "consecutive single-token lines". Default table: `read_outside` (Enter), `trust_folder` (recorded from Claude 2.1.280: the default selection is "No, exit", keys `Down, Enter`, and the screen must show "❯ No, exit"), `trust_folder_legacy` (Codex / older Claude, Enter, wording not recorded). **Deletion always goes to the user for confirmation**: the `dangerous rm` dialog is not on the allowlist; it is listed in `unknown_markers` (save screen + hold the slot + no key); the old `rm_in_cwd` check is deleted; the fleet installs no PermissionRequest hook that auto-answers delete confirmations.
- **A dialog waiting for a human is not a hold (defect F, option B)**: `RuntimeV2.prompt_wait` only writes the slot fields `prompt_wait=<scope>` (the in-flight delivery id; for a starting slot `startup-g<generation>`) / `prompt_wait_at` / `prompt_route=user`, audits `execution.prompt_wait`, posts one notice card each to the slot and the supervisor, and pauses dispatch; **lease, message and slot state are untouched**. For a marked slot the watchdog: marker ≠ current scope → clear (`watch.prompt_cleared reason=scope_changed`); transcript / rollout has not moved → skip this tick (no capture, no stalled / frozen ladder, zero key presses, even if the dialog scrolls off the bottom); transcript moved (the human answered, the worker continues) → clear the marker, back to `working`, judge normally; slot idle / after READY → clear (`inactive`); slot goes held / dead etc. → clear (`hands_off`). `resume` does not clear the marker: if the human resumes without answering the dialog → the slot keeps waiting and the fleet is not paused again; if the human answers without resuming → that turn settles normally, the report is recorded, and dispatch waits for resume. A starting slot carrying this generation's `startup-g` marker is not judged dead by `expire()` on `startup_deadline` (waiting for a human is not failing to start); READY registers as usual.
- **Delete classification (defect F′ 8.1)**: `watchdog.dialog_class` does a case-insensitive, word-boundary match on the bottom `tail_lines` lines against the built-in word list `DELETE_MARKERS` (rm / rmdir / unlink / del / delete / remove / rimraf / shred / trash / purge / wipe / erase / truncate / drop table|database|schema / git clean / git reset --hard / git push --force|-f / git branch -D / git checkout -- / git restore / dd if= / > /dev/null / **mv / move / rename** (moving and renaming count as deleting) / 删除 / 清空 / 移除 / 清理 / 移动 / 改名 / 重命名 (Chinese markers the code matches: delete / empty / remove / clean up / move / rename / rename), English words including inflected forms) or `delete_markers:` in `fleet.yaml#watchdog_prompts` (append-only; built-in words cannot be removed; a bad hot-reloaded table keeps the old one) → `delete`; an unreadable screen → no classification (goes to the user). Err on the strict side: misclassifying as delete only costs the user one extra question. The delete class always goes to the user; neither the program (including the allowlist) nor the supervisor presses keys. **With `security.yaml` (P8i / P8k, D8 clarification)**: Claude's PreToolUse gate lets **this one tool action** through only on **a valid single-use ticket from the user personally** (`fx:guard:ticket:<action_hash>`, written by `RuntimeV2.user_dialog_answer` after two-step confirmation); this is not the program or the supervisor answering a delete dialog for the user, and the delete dialog itself still goes to the user per this item. Without a policy the hook's existing baseline (`test_no_permission_hook_answers_delete_dialogs` and the like) is unchanged.
- **Non-delete dialogs go to the supervisor (defect F′ 8.3; rule: apart from deleting files, anything a worker needs a human for is handled by the supervisor)**: an allowlisted dialog that cannot be verified / an unknown dialog is classified `other`, and when task mode is running, not paused, the slot belongs to this fleet and is not adopted, the slot is not the supervisor, and the supervisor is not held / dead / starting / handoff / clearing / walled etc. and is not itself waiting on a dialog → `RuntimeV2.prompt_escalate`: mark the slot `prompt_route=supervisor` (likewise no slot hold, no pause), `fx:escalations['dialog:<slot>:g<generation>'] = {kind: dialog, generation, session_id, scope, delivery_id, prompt, screen, digest(first 16 hex chars of the sha256 of the screen bottom), keys_approve, keys_reject: [Escape], message_id, delivered_at}`, queue a `[dialog]` for the supervisor (raw screen bottom + how to answer), audit `watch.prompt_escalated`. `keys_approve`: for an allowlist entry, the table's keys; for an unknown dialog `Enter` only when the cursor visible at the screen bottom is on Yes (`❯ 1. Yes` / `❯ Yes`), otherwise no keys (a supervisor approve also goes to the user). The supervisor answers `approve | reject` in `escalations` of its decision JSON (`revise` is a format error); `escalation_apply` only marks the record `answered` and **presses no key**. On the next tick the watchdog recaptures the screen and presses only if generation / session / scope / pane ownership / screen-bottom digest / same dialog / delete re-classification **all** hold (approve presses `keys_approve`, reject presses Escape, audit `watch.prompt_answered authorized=supervisor:<decision>`), then clears the marker and deletes the record; dialog gone → `dialog.stale(dialog_gone)`, no key; a different dialog → `dialog_changed`, no key, reclassify and register again; identity changed → `dialog.stale(identity)`; reclassified as delete → `dialog.reclassified_delete`, goes to the user. If a human answers in the pane while waiting (transcript moves) → delete the record and judge normally. **Fallback to the user** (= the delete path: `prompt_wait`, pause, notice): supervisor unavailable (at registration or while waiting), record gone (e.g. a new task cleared `fx:escalations`), no answer within `dialog_escalation_timeout` of the supervisor receiving `[dialog]` (default 600 seconds, config key, `validate_config` requires ≥ 60), the supervisor replies RUNNING without answering (the first bounce-and-correct does not count as a strike, the second goes to the user), the supervisor itself hits a dialog; audit `dialog.to_human(reason=…)`. service mode also goes to the supervisor (see the item below). Rejecting a trust dialog presses Escape, which makes that Claude session exit (then liveness → hold); the supervisor prompt says so.
- **A worker reporting Blocked goes to the supervisor (defect F′ 8.4)**: the `Blocked:` section of a task-mode worker report (`blocked` in the v2 settlement JSON; `none / - / 无 / n/a` do not count; `无` = "none", matched by the code) → `fx:escalations['blocked:<slot>'] = {kind: blocked, slot, item(contract owner / running row / latest dispatch id), reason, message_id}`, and the supervisor's `[worker report]` gets a `[blocked]` how-to-answer appended. The supervisor answers `approve | revise(note required) | reject` in `escalations` → the worker is queued `[Supervisor ruling] [<item>] <decision>: <note>` (for a contract task it is recorded under that row and the row returns to running; the next report settles it as usual), audit `blocked.ruled`; dispatching to that slot in the same reply = answered (answer and dispatch together → the dispatch wins, no separate ruling is sent, audit `escalate.answered_by_dispatch`; this used to be a format error, and in an incident a rotated supervisor did it twice, plus a correction queued at the tail that was stale by delivery, and the three strikes blocked the run); the supervisor's correction message goes to the **head** of its pending queue (changed the same day, like worker corrections); a RUNNING reply to that report that neither answers nor dispatches → the first bounce-and-correct does not count as a strike, later ones do. A supervisor replying `BLOCKED` still `finish('blocked')` (the human escape hatch stays open); the prompt says only delete / move matters or things truly only the user can decide warrant BLOCKED. The fleet is not paused. **A ruling is bound to the generation that asked (found on a real machine, branch fix/subfleet-grants)**: the record also stores the asking `generation` / `label`; if at ruling time the slot has rotated (or the label changed) → audit `blocked.stale`, not delivered, the same check as service mode's "request is no longer dispatched" (`Fleet.ruling_stale`, one stale path shared by both modes). Cause: in an incident a ruling answering worker-1's generation 0 was queued only after it was adopted by a sub-fleet, and after reclaim it went to generation 2 (worker-1.2); the new session had no context, reported Blocked, and cost an extra round of "COMPLETED refused … unsettled deliveries". Old records without `generation` are not checked. When a split is already approved, a ruling for the same task is handled per §44 "approval reserves the slot".
- Self-check: `capture_fail_alarm` (3) consecutive capture failures write one `watch.capture_fail` (error; reported once per run of consecutive failures).
- **Hold exit**: when a slot is held (uncertain / expired), **any turn end of the same session and same generation** (Claude Stop via `tui_turn`; since defect E the turn no longer has to be started by this delivery: Claude session turns are serial, so no turn is running at Stop; a Stop with unfinished background tasks never enters `tui_turn` anyway, it only audits `turn.deferred` and sends the `turn_deferred` event, and on a held slot the daemon only audits and does not release) makes the hook send `turn_ended`; **Codex** has no official withdrawal and another turn's end cannot prove the queued one will not run, so the round in the rollout carrying that delivery's token must have reached `task_complete` (`codex_queue.token_turn`); not started / not finished → `turn.ignored` as before; after checking identity the daemon does `resolve_uncertain(cancelled, execution_stopped=True)`, the slot returns to idle, the result is **not applied**, the pause state is untouched (audit `hold.released`), and a Codex slot then does `codex_queue.clear` (otherwise the next delivery is refused with "still unsettled"). Forged events from an old generation / another session / a slot that is not held are always refused. **In task mode the exit informs the supervisor**: when a worker slot is released, the last text of that turn (tail ≤ 4000 chars) is queued to the supervisor as `[<slot> report]\nHELD-RELEASED: the program held this slot (<uncertain_reason>) and did not apply the result of its turn; …` (written like the `API ERROR` report for exhausted API errors below; sent only while the task is running), audit `hold.released` with `notified`; service mode does not send it (the request already has a public result, and `result.late` keeps the raw text). Settlement semantics do not change, so there is no switch; it guarantees the hold exit always gives the supervisor a turn, so a run cannot sit with every agent idle and a dispatch unsettled.
- **An API error holds only that slot (StopFailure; decision: hold only the slot that failed)**: only for official Claude workers on the task-mode hook channel (not the supervisor or advisor; service mode see "service mode: API error / model refusal" below; §28 clear-after-every-result unchanged). Cause: in an incident an attach-1 turn of a fleetmux run ended with "Login expired · Please run /login" (`authentication_failed`); the old design only audited StopFailure + wrote `turnend`, the in-flight delivery stayed `received`, the daemon kept renewing the lease, and nobody settled; then closing the laptop lid let every Claude lease expire, the "lease expiry" item below held every busy slot and paused the whole fleet, the pi supervisor's link-loss restart was blocked by the pause (§35 "no restart while paused"), and a turn ended by StopFailure never gets another Stop, so the hold exit never came either; the whole fleet stopped for hours. Now:
  - **StopFailure = turn ended = execution stopped**: the hook (`fleet.claude_stop_failure`) still audits + `turnend`, and in task mode also sends a `stop_failure` event (without waiting for the daemon); **only the delivery handed over by this session's hook** (`hook_delivered`: the bound message / delivery / session match the lease) carries identity; a delivery still in the mailbox and not yet handed over belongs to a later turn (the failed one was some other turn a human typed), so it is only audited and is still handed over by the fallback hook. The daemon (`RuntimeV2.api_error_retry`) checks same run / same generation / same session, lease `received` (or already held as `uncertain / expired` by the lease-expiry item below; in the incident the hold came first, then the StopFailure), PEL still owned by that consumer (`stop_gate`, as in §28), message is a worker / continuation instruction; then it commits `failed` (result `{stop_failure: raw text}`, **not applied, not a report**).
  - **Redeliver the same message to the same slot**: copy the original message the way `wall_settle` does (same request_id / kind / deadline / mid-turn rotation fields, with a `STOP_FAILURE_RESUME` paragraph prepended: "retried after an API error: check the directory first, do not redo"), placed at the **head** of the slot's queue; counting reuses §17's `replay` and `max_attempts`: this is attempt number `replay` (default 1), redelivered only if `replay < max_attempts`, and the new message gets `replay+1`. **No separate retry queue.**
  - **Only that slot is held during backoff**: slot `state=held` (equivalent to `hold(pause=False)`: no dispatch, watchdog hands-off, still counted in `max_busy`), writes `stopfail_at / stopfail_g / stopfail_retry_at`; backoff seconds `runtime_v2.STOP_FAILURE_BACKOFF = (60, 120, 300, 600)`, the k-th by `replay` (past the end, the last one). **The fleet is not paused and the supervisor is not held**; the slot and the supervisor each get a notice card `<槽> API 错误（原文前 200 字），N 秒后自动重试第 k 次` ("<slot> API error (first 200 chars of the raw text), automatic retry number k in N seconds"; a UI card that starts no supervisor turn), audit `execution.held` (`paused: false`) + `stop_failure.retry`.
  - **Automatic recovery**: the daemon's own tick (`stopfail_tick` in `expire`) returns the slot to idle when the time comes, and `pump` redelivers the head message to the same slot; no external timer (§1). If before then a Stop hook of **the same session and same generation** is waiting (`armed` key: a human logged in again, typed something, and the turn ended normally) → release early (`stop_failure.released reason=a normal turn ended`; to guard against a stale `armed`, it is ignored for `ARMED_TTL` 15 seconds after the StopFailure). The redelivery reaches Claude through the hook mailbox: if a Stop hook is waiting it is handed over directly; otherwise by the fallback hook. **The StopFailure group gets its own asyncRewake `claude-rewake`, the same as the Stop group's** (task mode, non-advisor), because a turn woken by the fallback hook that ends in StopFailure again has neither a Stop hook nor the old fallback hook; the new fallback increments the `rewake` counter and the old one yields automatically.
  - **Attempts exhausted**: still only that slot is held (`held`, no more dispatch, no timed release), **no pause, task not judged blocked**; the supervisor is queued `[<slot> report] API ERROR: …` (raw text, attempt count, and "the instruction was not rerun, it may be half done, the slot stays held until that session ends a turn normally; reassign it or reply BLOCKED"), audit `stop_failure.exhausted`; a managed run (`lifecycle_close.managed`) also sends tg / parent via `lifecycle_close.notify` (`slot_held`, a subprocess, sent in the tick outside the transaction). After that, a normal turn end of that session → released to idle through `armed` as above (no redelivery).
  - **By default only one retry**: `max_attempts` defaults to 2 (the run in that incident also had 2), so by default there is one redelivery after 60 seconds and a second failure exhausts it; for more (120 / 300 / 600 seconds) start the run with a larger `--max-attempts` (it governs both §17 resume replay and this item). Reason: a StopFailure can come mid-turn (in the incident attach-1 had already run tools for a minute), so a redelivery replays half-done work and must share the resume-replay cap.
- **service mode: an API error / model refusal holds only that slot**: Cause: in an incident one review request on the exit slot of a service sub-fleet was refused by the model, and Claude ended the turn with StopFailure (`invalid_request`); the old design only audited in service mode, the in-flight delivery stayed `received`, the daemon kept renewing the lease, 2 minutes later the request timed out → `hold` + the whole fleet paused (`执行状态未确认，已暂停派工`, "execution state unconfirmed, dispatch paused"), nobody resumed it, and all 426 requests in the following week failed. Now:
  - **Entry same as task mode**: in service mode the hook `fleet.claude_stop_failure` also sends the `stop_failure` event (likewise only for the delivery handed over by this session's hook), and reads the `stop_reason` of the last main-chain assistant message from the tail of the session record; if it is `refusal` it adds `refusal: true` (`fleet.claude_refused`). The daemon `api_error_retry` checks the same way, commits `failed` (not applied), then hands over to `RuntimeV2.service_stop_failure`.
  - **The request fails at once; the fleet does not redeliver**: publish `failed` (`error` = `API error: …` or `model refusal: …`); whether to retry is up to the requester (e.g. the requester's bridge program's own retry rules), consistent with §7 "service requests are not rerun" and opencode's "a refusal is not redelivered and the model is not switched".
  - **Only that slot is held, released after backoff**: `state=held`, backoff seconds as in `STOP_FAILURE_BACKOFF`, the k-th by this slot's consecutive failure count `stopfail_streak`; when the time comes the same `stopfail_tick` returns it to idle (or a normal turn end of the same session releases it early). The fleet is not paused and the supervisor is not held; the slot and the supervisor each get a notice card (including `连续第 k 次，上限 N`, "consecutive failure k, cap N"), audit `execution.held` (`paused: false`).
  - **A refusal gets a new session**: on `refusal` the slot is also marked with §37's `fresh_pending=1` and `stopfail_fresh=1`, and after release it goes through `fresh_rotation` (save the screen, generation +1, no handoff note): with requests queued in the mailbox the swap happens in `deliver` (the §37 path); with an empty mailbox `idle_rotate` swaps in place when it sees `stopfail_fresh`, instead of clearing it as "dispatch cancelled" like an ordinary `fresh_pending`. The refusing session takes no more work (in the incident the same session took the next batch the next night and was refused again). `fresh_rotation` clears both markers whether the swap succeeds or starting the new session fails (on failure, as in §37: the old session stays). An ordinary API error keeps the session, and the next message is handed over by the StopFailure group's asyncRewake (service mode now has it too).
  - **Consecutive cap**: `stopfail_streak` accumulates across generations and resets to zero when any request completes normally (`ok`); reaching `service_stopfail_max` (key in the run's config.json, default `runtime_v2.SERVICE_STOPFAIL_MAX = 3`; no start flag yet) → the slot stays held with no timed release and no pause, audit `stop_failure.exhausted`, a supervisor card + tg / parent notice for a managed run (`stopfail_notices`; a sub-fleet with `parent_config` does not count as managed and only gets the card; alerting relies on the requester's own consecutive-failure alert, e.g. a review bridge); after that only a normal turn end of that session releases it.
  - **Unchanged**: a timeout without StopFailure (unknown whether execution stopped) still holds (since §43 only that slot, no fleet pause); the supervisor, the advisor and §28 clear-after-every-result slots do not take this path. Verified by `tests/test_service_stop_failure.py` (8 cases).
- **Claude / Codex worker lease expiry holds only that slot (same incident; tui slots now try reclaim first and fall to this item only when reclaim fails, see "lease reclaim" below)**: the daemon renews official CLI slot leases itself (`renew_tui`), so "lease expired while the dispatch deadline has not passed" only shows the daemon did not run for a while (laptop lid closed, daemon stuck), not a worker error, just like a bridge slot whose lease was not renewed in §35 (`link_lost`, `pause=False`). A task-mode worker slot (not the supervisor or advisor; service mode unchanged) then gets `hold(pause=False)` (no public_status passed; the slot and the supervisor still get notice cards, otherwise nobody would see a hold that does not pause): the delivery is marked `uncertain` and the slot gets no more work; **duplicate-execution protection still holds** (no redelivery, the slot takes nothing new), other slots and the supervisor carry on; the hold exit is as before (a turn end of the same session → release, result not applied). If the dispatch deadline has also passed → hold + pause as before; the supervisor slot and a failed `renew_tui` renewal still pause; a service request timeout holds only the slot executing it since §43, no pause.
- **Lease reclaim: a lease expiry caused by the daemon's own absence holds no slot (default on)**: the previous item, holding only that slot, still stalls work: in an incident the lid was closed for 17 minutes, on waking integrator-1 was held, 40 minutes later its turn ended with a complete report, the hold exit discarded it as "result not applied", the supervisor knew nothing, and every agent sat idle for 1 hour 40 minutes; in service mode the same scenario also pauses the whole fleet. Now when an official CLI slot (`tui_slot`, including the supervisor; the advisor is excluded, its hold already restarts it in place) has an expired lease while the message's `deadline_at` has not passed, **it is reclaimed first**: `RuntimeV2.reclaim_tui` checks the lease is `received`, consumer = `tui-<slot>-g<current generation>` (the daemon is this slot's only consumer and renewer, nobody else can claim the PEL, so reclaim causes no second execution), and the pane is alive (`get_pane_status`, not-in-registry liveness check), then `MessageBus.reclaim` (`LUA_RECLAIM`: the same identity / consumer / `received` checks as `LUA_RENEW`, except it ignores `lease_until`; if `deadline_at` has passed it returns `deadline` and writes nothing) extends the lease to `min(now + lease, deadline)`, Lua audits `reclaimed`, Python audits `lease.reclaimed` (`gap_s` = seconds the lease had been expired). Reclaim writes Redis directly, not as an intent effect (it corrects the present; replaying intents should not carry it). Three hook-in points: in `expire()` before both hold branches; in `renew_tui`, when the lease has already expired, reclaim first and then renew (`LUA_RENEW` holds by itself on expiry, so it cannot wait for it to return expired); in `settle()` before the expiry check (for a Stop queued in the event stream during sleep, `commit_gate` reads the reclaimed lease at apply time). Reclaim impossible (pane gone, deadline also passed, identity mismatch) → hold per the previous item. Switch: config `lease_reclaim: on|off` (`start --lease-reclaim`; given on `--resume` it changes, omitted it is kept; an old run without the key = on); `off` = the previous item's behavior. **Watchdog counterpart**: `RuntimeV2.tick` records `last_tick` (wall clock); a gap between two ticks ≥ `max(30 s, stall_window / 2)` (lease duration when `stall_window` is 0) is a `daemon.gap` (audited with `gap_s` and slot names): every in-flight slot gets `quiet_since=0`, `act_floor=now` (a slot waiting on a dialog gets no floor: it would read "activity after the dialog" as the human having answered), and `Fleet.last_activity` uses `act_floor` as a lower bound, so the first tick after waking does not count the slept time as worker stillness (otherwise `quiet` / `age` would both include the whole sleep and go stalled at once, or even hit the hard cap); after that a transcript still for a full `stall_window` goes stalled as usual. Verified by `tests/test_lease_reclaim.py`.
- **service mode (decision: option B, service works the same)**: in service mode `fx:escalations` holds only the two record kinds `dialog:` / `blocked:`; `parse_service_decision` accepts `escalations[]` (only those two key kinds, same shape as task mode), `on_service_supervisor` checks / applies them; the first non-answer is bounced without counting toward `bad_replies`, after that as in task mode (dialog goes to the user, blocked counts strikes). Non-delete dialogs: same as task mode (same verification, timeout `dialog_escalation_timeout`, fallback to the user); the `[dialog]` message is bound to the request id the worker is working on, kind `followup` (delivered even if the request ends midway). **blocked**: a service worker has no Blocked section; by convention its answer is exactly `{"blocked": "<reason + proposal>"}` (only when the request's own reply keys do not include `blocked`; `none` etc. do not count) → that delivery settles but **no result is published**, the request stays `dispatched`, `blocked:<slot>` is recorded (with the request), and the supervisor is queued `[blocked] … (request <id>)`; after the supervisor rules, the same request is sent to that worker again (`request_message` + `[Supervisor ruling]`), and the worker's next answer is published as usual. In service mode dispatching another request does not count as answering; if the request reaches a terminal state first (timeout etc.) → the record is void (audit `blocked.moot`) and a late ruling is only logged. A pi worker's `fleet_result` lets `{"blocked"}` past the local required-key check. Delete-class dialogs still go only to the user, zero key presses.
- **Known gaps (defect E / F / F′ batch)**: if after the supervisor answers "approve" the TUI redraws before the next tick and the screen-bottom digest changes, it is treated as "a different dialog" and the supervisor is asked again; the `[dialog]` timer only starts when the supervisor receives the message, so while the supervisor stays busy the worker is bounded only by the lease `deadline_at`; the hold exit for pi slots is still not covered (plan ⑦-4; meanwhile, while the supervisor slot is held the same run refuses new tasks and suggests starting a new run, which is only a stopgap; the exit itself is still to do); old instructions piled up in the FIFO while held / waiting on a dialog are delivered in order after release (plan ⑦-3). API error redelivery and lease expiry: ~~for a lease-expiry hold, a turn that finishes normally after waking still only takes the hold exit, the result is not applied, and the supervisor gets no message~~ (fixed: lease reclaim + the hold exit informs the supervisor, see above); when the supervisor slot itself is held and then released, the supervisor gets no separate notice (its decision was not applied; there is only the UI card); support for the StopFailure group's asyncRewake is only inferred from how the hook executor in the Claude Code 2.1.280 binary is written (asyncRewake goes through the generic command-hook branch) and has not been verified by triggering StopFailure on a real machine; if unsupported, the redelivery lands in the mailbox with nobody to hand it over, falling back to the old watchdog stall path (stalled → frozen rotation, no rerun). Stop deferred: two weaknesses of the process-tree CPU probe are not fixed: a `nohup … &` process orphaned to pid 1 cannot be found by ppid, and taking positive per-tick increments of the total swallows other processes' increments in the tick a child exits; Claude slots now rely on the deterministic `turn_deferred` instead; pi / Codex slots have no Stop payload and are not covered; for a worker pane adopted as a sub-fleet supervisor, the hook returns before `delegated_route`, the event goes to the parent fleet (the slot is `delegated`, ignored by the watchdog), and the sub-fleet's watchdog never sees the marker; when a session is killed, `nohup` orphans keep running in the worktree, as is.
- **Tab gone (liveness check; decided after an incident; `RuntimeV2.tab_missing`)**: every 30 seconds, when a slot's tab is found missing → **it counts as "stopped" only when tmux proves the session has ended** (`cmux_manager.tmux_session_alive`: server answers and the session is absent / `no server running` / socket missing → False; a non-tmux tab or tmux unreadable → None, no evidence). ① A stopped worker (READY, not paused, not in handoff / clearing): follow §17 `start --resume`'s per-slot handling of dead windows: `clear_slot` releases lease and queue → `respawn` the next generation in place → task uses `requeue_lost`, service uses `replay_request` to replay the lost dispatch / request (counts `replay`; over `max_attempts` a task is judged blocked and service sends timeout; `replay: false` does not replay); **a Claude slot first resumes its own session** (the new generation's argv carries `claude --resume <original session>`; the transcript must exist); if the resumed generation times out without READY, or is lost again after resuming → a fresh generation + skeleton handoff note (if the working directory has a PROGRESS.md it goes into the next step); a fresh generation that never reached READY is only held when lost (prevents a restart loop); while paused it is only held and restarted at the next check after resume. ② Session still there (only the client tab closed) → `reattach_client` reopens a client tab alongside, inheriting the registry entry, no hold. ③ No evidence → hold only that slot (`hold(pause=False)`, cards still sent), **no fleet pause**; the supervisor (never in tmux, no evidence) is still held + paused. ④ A supervisor_slot that is delegating (§29) is never held (a hold would break the hook rerouting): stopped → `subfleet.delegated_lost` takes the existing reclaim (named `stop_child`, §19 records failed) → collect starts the next generation with a skeleton handoff note → autostart / engine brings it up again; requests the sub-fleet had not answered at that moment are written into the handoff note per §29 and redelivered by the caller (a sub-daemon that itself dies still goes through revive + `--resume`, replaying per §17 in the child's Redis); session still there → reattach the parent tab and sync `pane` in `subfleet.json`; a §19 placeholder tab gone → audit only. A supervisor adopted by a sub-fleet that stopped → hold only, no pause, wait for the parent fleet to reclaim it; session still there → reattach the view tab.
- **The wall gate comes before the watchdog (wired at integration)**: after capturing, `Watchdog.check` first calls E's `Fleet.wall_gate` (§23 "E before F"); a wall hit → in this tick that slot gets no key press, no hold, no kill, no rotation. Neither F's nor E's branch wired this line; the integration branch integration/batch-20261013 added it (test `test_wall_on_screen_trips_the_brake_and_the_watchdog_presses_nothing`).
- Parameters: `--stall-timeout` (existing; default 1800 → 1200, aligned with the sector-rotation driver; under v2 it is the transcript-only hard cap), `--stall-window` (default 300; 0 = window rule off, only the hard cap), `--stall-cpu-pct` (default 5), `--stall-grace`, `--stall-escapes`, `--prompt-table`; invalid config (negative / non-numeric) is refused by `validate_config`. A named sub-fleet (§29) entry `timeouts: {stall, stall_window, request}` can set `stall_window` (the entry's value; if absent the built-in default 300, not the parent's); `stall_cpu_pct` is not a time limit, is not in `timeouts`, and sub-fleets follow the parent config. `walled` (wall wording) is not part of this item; it belongs to the brake (E).
- **Dialogs are judged by structure, not only by text**:
  - **Cause**: in an incident run (release 4cd1156, Claude Code 2.1.316), approve-1 wrote "Q1: delete dialogs such as dangerous rm have no registered "approve" key…" in its final report. The watchdog judged it an unknown dialog and marked `prompt_wait`, and the whole fleet paused dispatch for about 20 minutes. The saved screen had no dialog: Claude was "running Stop hooks…", and the screen bottom was the normal input box `❯` and the `bypass permissions on` status line.
  - **Root cause**: `match_prompt` joins the bottom `tail_lines` (25) lines into one lowercase text and does only substring matching against allowlist `match` / `any` and `unknown_markers`, without telling a dialog apart from text the agent itself wrote on the screen (report body, tool output, dialog text quoted in test fixtures). The allowlist has the same problem: an acceptance42 pi screen showed, in tool output, another machine's Claude trust dialog and matched `trust_folder`; the `own_cwd` check failed only because pi's status line `9.4%/400k` was read as the path `/400k`.
  - **New criterion** (`watchdog.input_box`, only for claude and pi slots; the CLI comes from `fleet.agent_cli`): when a Claude Code or pi dialog appears, the CLI's own input box is replaced. In pi's source `interactive-mode.js`, `showTopExtensionDialog` first does `editorContainer.clear()` and then places the dialog; none of the 14 saved real Claude dialog screens or the 2.1.316 trust-dialog recording has an input box. So while the screen bottom is still an input box, there is no dialog, and neither the allowlist nor the unknown markers count. A screen is recognized as an input box only when all of the following hold; if any one fails, text matching applies as before:
    - The last two horizontal rules are ≤ 12 lines apart; a rule may carry a label (e.g. `── f91472563-approve-1 ─`, `── ⠋ Working ──`);
    - Non-empty lines below the lower rule ≤ 8 (status lines);
    - From the upper rule to the end of the screen there are no dialog parts: numbered cursor `❯/›/>/→ 1.`, pi cursor `→ `, `Esc to cancel`, `Enter to confirm`, `esc cancel`, `↑↓`. A pi dialog is itself between two DynamicBorders, and this condition rules it out;
    - For Claude, the first line inside the box starts with `❯` (in 2.1.316, `❯` plus a no-break space).
  - **Unchanged**: codex, opencode, dsh and calls that pass no CLI still use text matching. `dialog_class`, `DELETE_MARKERS` and `delete_markers` still apply to screens already judged to be dialogs. The paths after a screen is judged a dialog (verification, to the supervisor, to the user) are not changed by a single line.
  - **Samples**: all 22 local saved screens, plus 1 recorded 2.1.316 trust dialog and 2 synthetic ones, are in `tests/fixtures/watchdog_screens/`, file names ending in `real` / `false`; test `tests/test_watchdog_screens.py`.
    - 14 real dialogs: 3 Dangerous rm from claude-hook (delete), 11 LSP plugin recommendation from fleetmux (other).
    - 8 false positives: 1 channels approve-1 (Claude); 2 acceptance42, 5 layout-finish (pi).
    - 2 synthetic: a pi confirm dialog (synthesized from the structure of `extension-selector.js`, not recorded); the body of this false-positive screen plus a real rm dialog.
    - After the fix: real dialogs are still recognized with unchanged classes, and none of the false positives is judged a dialog any more.
  - **Known gaps**:
    - There is no recording of the 2.1.316 Dangerous rm dialog (the step that triggers rm in an isolated session was not done). This version's criterion is inferred from the older version's saved screens and the 2.1.316 idle screen and trust dialog.
    - There is no recording of a real pi dialog, only synthetic samples.
    - codex, opencode and dsh have no saved screens and still use text matching, so the same kind of false positive can still occur with these three CLIs.
    - If a future CLI version keeps the input box below a dialog, this criterion will let the dialog pass. The err-on-the-strict-side guarantee rests only on the fact that "a dialog replaces the input box", so after a CLI upgrade, capture a dialog screen again and check.

## 25. Night switch: roster profile column + `night on|off` (in development; renumbered from §20 to §25 on the integration branch)

Requirement: when switching to night mode, hot-reload the workers' tasks, unload the current workers, and continue from the checkpoint with the free tier of FlashNext. This section builds on §16 roster hot reload, §17 resume from a checkpoint and §12 profiles; it does not change slot/generation identity, the one-page layout, the handoff flow or CLI boundaries.

- **Roster `profile` column**: `workers: [{name, role?, profile?}]`, where `profile` is a profile card id. At start / hot reload the daemon resolves the card (`profile_launch.resolve_launch`) and calls `runtime_v2.validate_config` on the slot's merged config (the combination-table entry point; reads `fleet.yaml#cli_pairs` once item A took it over); an unknown card or a refused combination → the whole roster is refused (§16 semantics), not one rule relaxed.
- **Changing the model = the slot's next generation**: the slot name stays (renaming in the roster would be refused as "already used in this run", so switching back to day would be impossible). For a slot whose `profile` changed: after the in-flight turn finishes and the slot is idle (no in-flight delivery, idle confirmed), write a machine-skeleton handoff note the same way as a handoff, save the screen, start the next generation with `launch_held` (the new card's CLI/model), `tab_into` in place, close the old tab, v2 `confirm_retired`; the queue is not drained, and delivery resumes as usual once the new generation is READY. While the switch is pending the slot takes no new deliveries. A preflight or window-close failure → block the task per §12, no silent swap.
- **Checkpoint briefing**: once every slot in this switch is READY, the supervisor gets one `[Night switch <task>]` / `[Day switch <task>]`: each slot's new label and profile, outputs verified / missing, completed dispatches (the same `RuntimeV2.progress()` as the §17 briefing), queued instructions left on slots (stating plainly not to dispatch them again), and "dispatch only what is missing". With no running task it is only audited.
- **Trigger**: `start --roster day.yaml --night-roster night.yaml` (slot names in the two rosters must match); `fleetmux night on|off|status --config`: validates both rosters first, then atomically writes `<run>/night.json` (the /sleep trigger file). The daemon reads it every tick to decide which roster to follow. The supervisor is not swapped (a Fable supervisor waits for the Claude supervisor adapter).
- **Brake placeholder**: Redis `fx:night {on, brake_card: night|day, since}` + audit `night.on/off`; deciding the night card cap belongs to the brake item (design v2 2.5).
- **Relation to live profiles**: while a task's `apply_profiles_live` is in effect, roster card swaps are deferred until that task ends (audit `roster.profile_deferred`), so the two sources do not flip back and forth.
- **v2 per-slot CLI**: places in the v2 transport that decide "official CLI window / Pi bridge" now read the slot's current-generation config (`runtime_cfg`); slots without a profile are exactly as before.
- **Changing CLI is allowed only for the night card swap (BC review r1 P1-01)**: `preflight_profile(launch, cli_change_ok=False)` under v2 still refuses "this slot's worker CLI differs from the run's"; only the roster card swap `swap_profile` passes `cli_change_ok=True`, because every one of its slots has already passed the combination table in `night.resolve_launches` (`pair_refusal` + `validate_config`). Other paths such as task-level live profile switches still refuse a CLI change. **Mixing Claude TUI slots and Pi bridge slots in one run has not passed real-cmux acceptance; the §4.13 acceptance must be done before production night use.**
