- **Not accepted**: only fake-cmux / real-Redis regression tests; `codex+pi` is not allowed by today's matrix (waiting for A's combination table), and the full acceptance of a real day Opus → night FlashNext → morning switch back (the design v2 § 4.13 acceptance sentence) has not been done.
- **Unification with §22 rotation thresholds, §12 model cards, §23 brake (decided at integration)**: there is only one "per model" mechanism: the night switch only changes the slot's `profile` (i.e. the `runtime_launch` override) and brings no threshold of its own; before every delivery §22 `handoff_limits` re-resolves the rotation threshold from the slot's **current-generation running model** (roster worker row > roster `handoff:` table > config table > profile card `handoff_ratio` (§12, layer 3b) > built-in / flag), so a slot switched to FlashNext automatically uses the FlashNext card's 0.5 and switching back to Opus automatically restores the 1M window. The `profile` column and §22's `window` / `handoff_ratio` columns and `handoff:` table are validated in **the same roster by the same parser** (`roster.parse_entries` + `roster.handoff`); any invalid one refuses the whole roster; whichever roster the daemon follows (day / night, decided by `night.json`), that roster's `handoff:` table is used. New rows hot-added to the roster: rows with `profile` are counted by `night.resolve_launches` (including `model_usage.check_slots` when C is present), rows without by C's `default_capacity_problem` against the default model; one row is never counted twice. E's per-pool wall downgrade is void for a slot whose card this section has swapped (§23 `brake.downgrade_superseded`); E's night cash cap reads this section's `fx:night.brake_card`.

## 26. Acceptance fixes (P0 / P1 from the real-cmux acceptance of 373d22c; §23 on main, renumbered to §26 on the integration branch)

Acceptance report: an internal plan. This section fixes only the defects the report names; the single daemon, slot/generation identity, one-page layout, task/service protocol and the rest of §16-§22 are unchanged. Principle: things with side effects (writing files across workspaces) are done by trusted Python; models write only in their own directories.

- **P0 sub-fleet delivery path**: the sub-fleet's rules (CONTRACTS.md "path lives in the producer's own directory", the dispatch tail "write only in your own subdirectory") conflicted with "write to the parent path", Claude workers refused to write, and all 4 sub-fleets failed. Now: a parent output `<parent ws>/<slot>/<rest>` is mapped to `<child ws>/worker-1/<rest>` (outputs not under that slot's directory go into `worker-1/` by file name), the mapping is recorded in `deliver` of `subfleet.json`; the child goal writes only the child path and says "nobody writes outside this workspace; after COMPLETED the parent daemon copies"; a contract parent task's goal lists only interface ids + the parent `contracts.json` (to read the shape). `collect` copies when the outcome is `completed`, before any check: `worker-1/<rest>` first, otherwise the same relative path under another sub-fleet worker's directory (only if exactly one), skipping symlinks and empty files; nothing is copied unless completed; a copy error only records `subfleet.deliver_failed`, panes are reclaimed as usual, and "output missing" judges it failed. After copying, the "output missing" check and `escalation_finished`'s literal interface check run as before: **the parent interface's `path` is still the parent path, semantics unchanged**. Paths in the `[sub-fleet interfaces]` notice are now absolute (they used to be relative to the child workspace, which the parent supervisor would read as its own).
- **P1 resume cannot reattach Claude**: a natively installed Claude process is named `claude.exe`. `RESUME_CLIS` narrowed to `('codex', 'claude')`; `has_cli_process` compares the executable name from `ps -o comm=` with `.exe` stripped; `node` counts only when the script path in `ps -o args=` contains the CLI name (npm install); a bare shell or unrelated node process is not a live CLI (resume review P2-N1).
- **P1 READY check**: the startup reply passes when its last non-empty line (with markdown emphasis `*`, `_`, backticks and a trailing `.` stripped) equals `READY`; `NOT READY` does not count. Failing (and not failed / aborted / launch not ready) → first type one correction "reply exactly READY" into the pane, slot still `starting`; only a second wrong reply is blocked under the original rule. Corrections are recorded as `startup_nudged = startup_deadline`, once per READY request (start, adopt, reclaim, rotation); the typing happens after the transaction (like `rotation_pending`).
- **P1 sub-fleet attempt cap**: when the sub-fleet records of one task id (`sub/M0`, `sub/M0-attempt-<n>`, counting failed ones and ones rolled back half-started) reach `--max-attempts` (default 2) → `subfleet.check` refuses (`max-attempts N: sub-fleet M0 already started N time(s)…`), covering escalate acceptance, approve, `spawn_child` and the operator's `subfleet spawn`. In a contract task an escalate refused this way → the task row is `failed` (with the reason) and the supervisor is told it may redispatch differently or finish; the worker is not told "carry on yourself". Ordinary tasks follow the original refusal path (both worker and supervisor get the reason).
- **P1 choice dialog hang**: every Claude pane (workers, and sub-fleet supervisors that were worker panes) starts with `--disallowedTools AskUserQuestion`. Prevention only; detecting "a turn stuck on an interactive dialog" belongs to the watchdog item.
- **P2 `Interfaces:` line**: when the literal interface file check has passed but the report has no valid `Interfaces: IF-x@n provided: ready` line → the task row's reason names the missing line, the worker gets a hint filed under that task, and the supervisor gets a line "worker asked, no need to redispatch"; the worker's next report re-settles the task. No hint when the file itself fails the check (still not ready).

**Verification**: `python3 -m pytest -q tests` passes in full (new `tests/test_acceptance_fixes.py`: contract parent task → escalate → approve → sub-fleet writes only its own directory → parent copies, checks pass, interface ready, M0 done, parent supervisor receives completed (regression of the acceptance P0 path); another child worker directory's output is accepted, `worker-1` takes precedence; nothing copied unless completed; READY last-line check, pass after one correction, blocked on the second; the attempt cap judges failed at escalate, and `check` reports "already running" first while the task is still running; the `Interfaces:` hint then re-settles to done. `test_cmux_control` adds process checks for `claude.exe` / npm node / bare shell / unrelated node; `test_fleet` asserts argv carries `--disallowedTools AskUserQuestion`; the `test_v2_codex_claude` startup case now blocks only after two wrong replies).

**Known gaps (P2)**: after a sub-fleet is reclaimed the old child workspace still keeps a history tab (`close_all` only closes registered surfaces; why history was not closed under real cmux is not established and needs a real-machine repro); the new child workspace stealing focus, the child goal carrying the parent worker's full instruction verbatim (including the escalate instructions), `roster.changed` re-recording handoff after resume, and M1 no longer receiving `[contract] ready` after being judged done on a stub are report P2s left untouched; the delivery mapping always lands in `worker-1/`, so if the child supervisor gives the final draft to another worker and both places write a file of the same name, `worker-1` wins; not re-verified on real cmux / Claude sessions; independent review, merge and deployment not done.

## 27. Advisor: picks model / effort / domain before dispatch, rules on relay or replacement mid-way (both phases merged)

The design follows an internal plan (v0.7; its nine user-boundary items are fixed and its open questions are implemented with the defaults); implementation, reviews (final review PASS) and the merge-seam reviews are recorded there. The single daemon, slot/generation identity, the one-page layout, the task/service protocol, the §12 Phase 5 rotation mechanism and §16–§26 are all unchanged; the advisor is not a second dispatcher.

- **What it is**: a one-off consultation the daemon runs on behalf of the supervisor: `claude -p --model fable --effort high`, a stripped session (`--setting-sources ""`, `--strict-mcp-config`, `--disable-slash-commands`, `autoMemoryEnabled:false`), cwd fixed at `<run>/advisor/`, 90 s timeout with 1 retry. Not resident, takes no slot, not in the layout. **(v2 task mode now uses a resident advisor tab instead, see the "Resident advisor" item below; this one-off call remains only for v1 and sub-fleets; a service main fleet is also resident.)** **It listens only to the supervisor**: input = the task brief + this dispatch instruction + the supervisor's optional `advise_note` + the slot's current state + that CLI's model cards and capability table + the domain list; no worker conversation. The advisor never talks to workers directly.
- **Switch = roster `advisor: on|off`, default off, hot-reloaded** (§16, the run does not restart): the roster is the only entry point - the user editing the file, `fleet advisor on|off`, or the supervisor's decision key `advisor`; `start --advisor` / `start --resume --advisor` are just sugar that writes `advisor: on` into the roster; config.json does not store the switch. With a night roster (§25) the switch is written to both day and night rosters, and `night.check_rosters` requires both to have the same `advisor` (the advisor is per run; the night switch does not toggle it). Kit validation follows the "go-live point": on a `set_advisor(on)` hot reload a broken kit refuses to turn it on and does not write the roster (audit `advisor.rejected`); at `start`, either `--advisor` or a roster already saying `on` is validated before any resource is opened; if the daemon starts with the advisor online and the kit is broken, the whole thing is refused.
- **Always consulted**: while the advisor is online, every dispatch passes through it before delivery (including a contract task held by `depends_on`, which is consulted again when released). Notices / corrections are not consulted. **When it is off nothing changes** (the full existing test suite passing with zero changes is the regression evidence). Turning it off only stops new consultations: generations already running or started from a config sheet are not rolled back; a slot held waiting for its first sheet is released with defaults; sheets still waiting for a ticket and not yet launched are dropped (`advice.wait_dropped`; queued messages are delivered by the runtime the slot is running).
- **Config sheet**: `{model, effort, domains[], reason, capability?}`. `model` is chosen only from the profile cards of that slot's CLI (Claude slot = the four static cards `claude-{fable,opus,sonnet,haiku}` in the `profiles` block, linked to the same-named model cards, counted in the §12 votes); `effort` is written into `runtime_launch.extras`, and the drift check is extended to extras; `domains` ⊆ the kit's full list ∩ the profile card's `kit.domains` whitelist; pi / codex slots must have empty `domains` and effort is ignored; `reason` must cite the capability table `config/fleet.yaml#capability_matrix.yaml` (domain × task type × model, each cell carries evidence; `untested` cells may not be chosen unless everything is `not_enough`).
- **Taking effect**: launch fingerprint = (model, effort). Fingerprint changes → §12 Phase 5 rotation (generation 1.x); with the advisor online no double opt-in is needed, but it **first passes `card_gate`** (host mismatch → `block_profile`; tickets full → the message stays queued, `profile_state=pending`, the sheet is stored in `advice_wait`, the gate is retried every tick and switches once a ticket is free, no repeat consultation). While a codex slot's native sub-agent is unfinished, `switch_profile` only defers and starts no new generation; the sheet likewise stays in `advice_wait` and is not delivered to the old runtime. Only new domains → **hot load, no rotation**: that domain's `RULES.md` is prepended to the delivered instruction, `kit.link_domains` links skills / agents into the worker's `.claude/`, `installed_domains` accumulates; removing domains does nothing. A first-generation Claude slot (held only if the advisor was online when the run started) has `launch_held=True` and waits for its first sheet (no 60 s limit, the tick does not time it out, `others_active` does not count it as busy); on release it writes the launch, installs the pack and assembles CLAUDE.md.
- **One launch record per slot**: `runtime_launch` (model / effort) + `installed_domains`, changed only when a generation actually starts - first-generation release, rotation before dispatch, the new generation of a rescue respawn / reassign; sheets waiting for a ticket or deferred live only in `advice_wait` / `advice_preset`, never staged into the record. Handoffs, non-advisor rotations and the next generation after v2 `--resume` **inherit this record** (model, `--effort`, linked domains and the RULES in CLAUDE.md), regardless of whether the advisor is on right now, with no new consultation; `release_default` and `init_worker` reset the record to the slot default; a daemon started with the advisor off loads the kit on demand for slots inheriting a record (failure logged as `advice.kit_unavailable`). **Decision: the launch record is inherited unconditionally, and only a new advisor sheet changes it**: the layout (`--setting-sources project` / `--strict-mcp-config` / `--effort` / autoMemory off, RULES assembled into CLAUDE.md, `.claude/` linking only the chosen domains) is part of the record too - `installed_domains` of `''` = the slot's default layout (user-level settings / skills / MCP, a full kit copy, CLAUDE.md with only the role description), a JSON list (including `[]`) = the advisor layout; a pending sheet staged in `advice_domains` also counts as the advisor layout. `advised_layout` reads only the record and no longer asks `advisor_online()`; the `advisor` key in each generation snapshot is written or removed according to the layout and never leaks in from the daemon's in-memory switch (previously a default slot with a roster card was assembled into advisor argv while the advisor was online). The advisor switch decides only **a slot's first generation** (`spawn_ready`): online → a v2 Claude slot is held for its first sheet / v1 gets the advisor layout with zero domains; off → defaults. A held first generation writes no record until released (`release_hold` writes the sheet, `release_default` the defaults); on `resume`, a slot still held and never released starts its next generation with defaults and drops `advice_hold`. `advisor on/off` changes no record (`release_default` / `init_worker` act only on first generations not yet started and never clear the record of a running slot). An old run without record fields = defaults (byte-identical to the default record, nothing to backfill). A hot-loaded sheet is also an applied sheet: once a default-layout slot gets domains hot-loaded, its record becomes the advisor layout and the next generation starts from the sheet (project settings only).
- **kit** (`fleetmux/kit.py`, `--worker-kit DIR`, assembled by an external build script): `core/CLAUDE.md` (the project core rules without the domain index section, plus a no-EverMemOS exemption line), `context_domains.yaml`, `domains/<domain>/{RULES.md,skills/,agents/}`, a full `mcp.json` (lazy-loaded, not split by domain; without evermemos / wechat), `MANIFEST`. Validated as a whole at startup; an invalid kit is refused before any resource opens (same as a bad roster value). Before a rotation `.claude/{skills,agents}` is cleared and then relinked; a Claude slot's `CLAUDE.md` = role scope + core + this time's domain RULES; the spawn prompt no longer asks to read AGENTS.md; `claude_argv` adds `--effort --setting-sources project --strict-mcp-config`, and the settings file adds `autoMemoryEnabled:false`. Coexists with the no-typing batch (merged into the live line): every Claude worker generation started by the advisor (first release, rescue respawn, handoff rotation) still carries the hook channel (blocking Stop hook + asyncRewake + StopFailure, settings `env` with `CLAUDE_CODE_STOP_HOOK_BLOCK_CAP=0`) and `DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER=1`, and its directory still gets `REVIEWER.md`; the advisor's own one-off `claude -p` also carries `DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER=1`. `advisor` / `consult` and `withdraw` coexist in the decision JSON. **No memory layer anywhere in the fleet**: workers / advisor neither read nor write memory and are not connected to EverMemOS.
- **Failure handling**: advisor fails to start / times out / returns non-JSON / sheet fails validation / card JSON is broken → after 1 retry, use the last sheet of the same task, otherwise dispatch with the slot's current runtime + `domains: []`, audit `advice.unavailable`, the supervisor gets `[advisor unavailable] 按缺省派出` (advisor unavailable, dispatched with defaults), **never blocking**. Any failed step of a Phase 5 rotation follows the original §12 rules. When the task brief itself turned on Phase 5 live (switching / applied-live), the advisor steps aside. Service mode is not consulted.
- **Audit and display**: `fx:advice:<ticket>` (input summary, output, duration, advisor tokens and `total_cost_usd`, how it took effect: respawn / hotload / none); `advice_ticket / advice_reason / installed_domains / advice_last / advice_wait` in `fx:agent:<slot>`; control line `adv=<model>/<effort>/<domains>`; the supervisor gets `[advisor] <slot> → <model>/<effort>, domains: […]（<reason>；<effect>）`. The pi supervisor's `fleet_decision` schema allows `dispatch[].advise_note` and the §20 `dispatch[].id`.
- **Phase two (implemented, feat/advisor-integ)**: following the judgment section of AGENTS.md (Blocked/Not done due to a tool or capability gap, the same error two rounds in a row, Done but the Evidence fails acceptance and still fails after one correction), the supervisor adds `consult[{worker, task, finding, want}]` to its decision; the advisor with `kind="rescue"` rules `respawn` (same slot worker-N → worker-N.1, via the §4 handoff + a new sheet installed from scratch, `handoff_hint` goes into the next-step of the handoff skeleton, the original instruction is redelivered) / `reassign` (task withdrawn, another idle slot starts from the sheet, the supervisor dispatches again) / `give_up` (BLOCKED with a reason); at most 2 relays in the same slot. Mid-way changes always rotate, never hot load. respawn / reassign also pass `card_gate` before changing the model: host mismatch blocks; respawn with tickets full does nothing, does not count as a relay and tells the supervisor it was not executed; reassign with tickets full or deferred stays in `advice_preset`, and when the supervisor redispatches it goes through the gate per the sheet. With the advisor off, `consult` only gets one `[advisor off]` reply; a consult whose advisor is turned off mid-consultation gets the same reply. The pi supervisor schema allows `consult[]` and `advisor`.
- **Delivery channel (after merging integ/no-typing-batch)**: the advisor opens no delivery path of its own. `[advisor] …` / `[advisor off] …` / `[advisor unavailable] …` for the supervisor are still only appended to the end of the next supervisor message (`ADVICE_NOTES`; a consult-only reply is queued on its own) and travel on the supervisor's channel with that message: Codex supervisor = `codex queue`, whose queued text ends with an opaque delivery token `(fleet delivery <token>)` that binds the turn; pi supervisor (new default) = bridge; Claude supervisor = the original typed path. Content for a Claude worker (dispatch instruction + prepended domain RULES, relay notes, rescue handoff hints) goes into that slot's per-generation Stop-hook mailbox (v1 `fx:hook:<slot>:g<n>:mailbox`, v2 with the run prefix) and is no longer typed into the input box; the mailbox is keyed per generation, and a rotation (Phase 5 / rescue respawn / handoff) bumps the generation first and then retires the old tab, so the old generation's hook sees `alive()` false and exits empty-handed, and a late Stop from the old generation logs `turn.stale` without settling. A slot held for its first sheet has not exec'd its CLI yet, so no hook is waiting; the watchdog treats it as "starting with no delivery" and only watches. Whether the hooks and env carried by `--settings` still take effect under the advisor layout (`--setting-sources project`) is not verified on a real machine (BLOCK_CAP is also backed by the actor process environment). The launch record remains the only source for the next generation: the batch only reads `runtime_launch`, and a hook slot returning from a sub-fleet goes `v2.respawn` → `spawn` → `advised_layout`. §28 clear-after-result and the hook channel are mutually exclusive (enabling §28 requires `--no-claude-hook-delivery`, i.e. typed); a §28 same-generation rebind only changes thread / transcript / ctx and the bus session+fence, never `runtime_launch` / `installed_domains` / the layout; a held first generation has no delivery and never enters clearing.
- **Resident advisor (requirement: show the advisor window explicitly and let it hand off and rotate normally; `feat/advisor-resident`; design increment in an internal plan v0.3, review r1 FAIL → r2 PASS)**: applies to transport v2 + the main fleet (task and service mode; service via `feat/advisor-resident-service`); v1 and sub-fleets (`adopt` / `parent_config`) still use the one-off `claude -p` above. In service mode a consultation happens only when a held first-generation Claude worker's first sheet is released (dispatches and direct external requests are not consulted; supervisor_slot is not held); consultation messages are bound by ticket and are exempt from the "drop once the request is final" rule; §28 applies only to workers, and the advisor tab is not among the workers, so they never meet.
  - **Slot identity**: slot name `advisor`, included in `names` (bus, Stop hook, watchdog and §22 rotation all use it), **not in `workers`**: it cannot be dispatched to (not in the `dispatch_targets` / `NAMES` routing table), does not count toward `max_busy` or `model_usage` (like the supervisor it takes no ticket; the fable card's `max_concurrent: 1` counts workers only), does not get CONTRACTS.md, and is never seen as busy by `others_active`. The roster / `--worker-names` may not use `advisor` as a worker name. Generation labels `advisor` → `advisor-1.1` → `advisor-1.2` (`slot_base`).
  - **Explicit window**: after `start_all` lays out the page it starts the advisor tab and `tab_into` puts it into the supervisor column (after the supervisor tab); cmux metadata `type=advisor`. Rotation / watchdog rotation uses `tab_into(new, old)` to take the same position; on `--resume` the original tab is reused in place if it exists, otherwise it is put back into the supervisor column, and on a full page rebuild it is placed after the layout is done.
  - **Fixed argv** (`advisor.resident_argv`, not through `runtime_launch`, never rewritten by a sheet): the one-off argv minus `-p` / `--output-format` → `--model fable --effort high --setting-sources "" --strict-mcp-config --disable-slash-commands --system-prompt-file <run>/advisor.md --tools Read --add-dir <run>/handoffs`, with `--settings` replaced by that generation's settings file (Stop hook + hook channel asyncRewake / StopFailure + `autoMemoryEnabled:false` + `DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER=1`), plus `--append-system-prompt <DECLARATION>` when the hook channel is on; cwd `<run>/advisor/`. `<run>/advisor.md` = `agents/advisor.md` + `RESIDENT_NOTE` (answer READY on the first turn, every message is one consultation, how to answer a correction message). **Always the official Claude CLI**: `agent_cli` / `tui_slot` / `runtime_cfg` hard-code claude / fable / high by slot name and do not follow the run's `--worker-cli` (codex / pi); the actor does no supervisor × worker CLI pair check for it.
  - **Switch and readiness are separate**: `advisor_enabled()` (= the former `advisor_online()`, kept as an alias) governs first-generation holding, consult acceptance, the off notice and idempotency; `advisor_ready()` = on and the advisor tab idle / busy. `advisor_tick` (daemon tick, outside the transaction): on but no tab → start the first generation; turned off → once the current consultation is done (lease not offered / received) the tab is retired (queued consultations withdrawn) and removed from `names`; turned on again → start the next generation.
  - **Asynchronous consultation (tickets)**: before dispatch (`advise_before_delivery`, first-generation `release_hold`) and for rescue, the call becomes "push onto the advisor's bus queue + hold a ticket": `fx:advice:<ticket>` is the ticket's state (`pending → answered → ok / unavailable`, with `mid / attempt / phase / deadline / request`); the dispatching slot records `advice_pending`, rescue records `fx:advice:rescue_pending` (one at a time; a correction attaches the currently idle slots, so a candidate that turned busy during the answer is never asked about again), the message stays in its original queue, the daemon keeps forwarding, and reads the ticket every tick after that. The time limit is fixed at 180 s (`ADVICE_WAIT`): 180 s for waiting for the advisor's READY / queueing, then another 180 s for answering once the advisor has the message (offered / received); on timeout the still-queued message is withdrawn and defaults apply. Several arriving at once are served serially in arrival order (the advisor does one at a time). The first time a wait starts (advisor not ready, or another consultation ahead) one `[advisor] waiting: …` note is sent, riding on the supervisor's next business message (`fx:advice:notes`, merge seam M1).
  - **Held first generation**: on at run start → held workers are consulted once the advisor is READY; the advisor fails to start (`spawn_failed`) or no usable sheet within 180 s → `release_default` (default layout) and `[advisor unavailable] 按缺省派出` is sent. Advisor turned off mid-way → slots still held get `release_default` at once, in-flight tickets are closed with defaults at once (`advisor_abandon`, queued messages withdrawn).
  - **Settlement branch**: in an advisor turn `tui_candidates` hands over only the raw text; the advisor branch of `settle` calls `advisor_settled` after `release` to store the text in the ticket; no Done / Evidence check, no `report`, no `remember`, no task state change, no brake. On reading the ticket it is validated against that slot's sheet (`validate` / `validate_rescue`; JSON taken from a Markdown code block also works); bad once → queue an `[Advisor correction <ticket>]` for the advisor (with the original input and the error); still bad → this one uses defaults, **not counted as blocked**.
  - **Faults never block**: watchdog declares it dead (the watchdog kills it and starts the next generation in place as usual; failure only holds, never `finish blocked`), tab closed (liveness check holds), usage wall (the turn's text or the screen is a wall: no brake, no fleet pause), startup timeout / no READY after startup (marked dead, not `finish blocked`), lease expired / delivery failed (hold) - `RuntimeV2.hold` only marks `advisor_fault` for the advisor slot: it does not pause the fleet, sends no card to the supervisor, and holds no other slot; the in-flight consultation message becomes uncertain / cancelled, and the slots waiting on it use defaults on the next tick; `advisor_tick` starts the next generation (skeleton handoff note, `source=advisor-restart`; at least 60 s between two restarts, none while the fleet is paused). **Only one path starts the next generation: `advisor_restart`** (fault restart, §22 rotation, watchdog rotation and `--resume` all use it; review finding P0-01): retire the old tab first, then start the new generation and put it back in the supervisor column, so two advisor sessions never coexist; old tab won't close (held) / new generation fails to start (dead + `spawn_failed`) / cannot be placed in the supervisor column (only `advisor.placement_failed` logged) all count only as advisor faults - not raised into the daemon, not blocked, no pause, no other slot held, no dispatch cancelled; queued consultations are withdrawn at once, the slots waiting on them dispatch with defaults on the next tick, and it retries after 60 s. When a task ends, the advisor's in-flight turn is not held; a late answer is dropped (`advice.late`).
  - **Rotation (§22)**: judged before delivery against the fable threshold (1M window, `--handoff-ratio` / roster table), rotating once over the threshold; the advisor only has Read and cannot write a draft, so no handoff turn is sent: `start_rotation` goes straight to `advisor_restart(source=handoff)` with the daemon's skeleton handoff note: recent sheets (kind / slot / output sheet + reason of the last 10 tickets in `fx:advice:recent`), **with no worker conversation at all**. Rotation starts only at an idle boundary, after the in-flight item settles; queued consultations are taken over by the successor.
  - **Test doubles**: tests that inject `advisor_runner` (including v2) still use the one-off synchronous path (no advisor tab), existing assertions unchanged; the resident path is driven by `tests/test_advisor_resident.py` through the real Stop-hook entry point.
  - **Verification**: full suite `pytest -q tests -p no:cacheprovider` with a clean PATH, twice rc=0, 1664 passed 5 skipped each (baseline 1027f4f8 was 1647 passed 5 skipped, +17 in `test_advisor_resident.py`); review pending.
  - **Not verified on a real machine**: `--system-prompt-file` and `--append-system-prompt` taking effect together, the Stop hook carried by `--settings` still firing under `--setting-sources ""`, `--tools Read` plus `--add-dir` reading handoff notes without a permission prompt, where cmux `tab_into` actually lands in the supervisor column, a real Fable answering READY on the first turn and returning sheets per `RESIDENT_NOTE`, a real rotation; how to verify: start a dev fleet once with `advisor: on` and check the `turn_ended` / `settled` events, the `advice.queued → ok` audit and the tab position.
- **Known boundaries (P2 list, tracked for go-live)**: a one-off advisor call (v1 / service / sub-fleet) blocks the daemon synchronously for up to 180 s (the resident advisor is asynchronous and no longer blocks) (the 300 s lease does not expire; check the audit's `elapsed_s` first); after fallback / `--resume` a runtime without effort meeting a medium sheet rotates one extra time (the correct fix = the fallback sheet writes `DEFAULT_EFFORT`, not changing the fingerprint comparison - changing the fingerprint collides with the drift check and turns fail-open into fail-closed; that was reverted once); Phase 5 live and the advisor on the same slot with unequal extras get blocked; `take_pending_advice` matches by substring; `--advisor` without `--worker-kit` gives zero skills without a warning; removing from the roster a held worker that was never dispatched to does not retire it; the four static cards take part in `route_task` (no difference at the planning layer); the night switch / roster hot reload hitting a slot that is held or rotating is not covered; the wait marker in v1 uses the message hash and waiting has no timeout; a rescue respawn hitting a codex native sub-agent is not handled separately; phase-two review findings P2-1…P2-8 (P2-7 fixed in the merge); design items §12-4/7 (agent directory)/8 not verified on a real machine. **Go-live precondition**: a dev fleet really runs "first dispatch held and released → rotation → hot load" once, which also closes the §12 open item "v2 Codex supervisor + Claude worker live hot switch not tested on a real machine".

**Verification**: `python3 -m pytest -q tests` 1295 passed 1 skipped (= 1219 of 659084f4 + 67 from phase two + 9 from the merge seam; includes cherry-pick 1a0579ed: the night card-swap test brings its own claude stub, and the `fleet.yaml#walls` haiku alias follows cli_tier_map's 5.5; skip = the "feature C not merged" placeholder in `test_night_switch`; the pi schema TypeBox contract test actually ran and passed). Clean PATH (`PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/redis/bin:/opt/homebrew/bin:/usr/bin:/bin`) 1292 passed 4 skipped: the 3 extra skips are because there is no node on that PATH (fnm is installed elsewhere), so three pi contract tests skip. Reviews: phase one Fable r1–r3 (r3 PASS), merge seam Fable r1 PASS, phase two Fable r1 PASS; review of this merge seam (feat/advisor-integ → merge/advisor-into-deploy) pending. No separate deployment done. fix/launch-record-inherit (launch record inherited unconditionally, P2-1): 1304 passed 1 skipped (+9 in `test_advisor_inherit.py`), review pending. integ/fleet-20261014 (merging docs/plan-change-via-supervisor + integ/no-typing-batch + cherry-picks cbaaccf5/0c08f7e4 + fixes on this line): `python3 -m pytest -q tests` 1567 passed 2 skipped (skips = `test_codex_via_pi.py:335` "pi+claude/task verified by a real smoke", the `test_night_switch.py:435` "feature C is merged" placeholder; the pi contract tests ran with node and passed); clean PATH 1564 passed 5 skipped (3 extra = no node on that PATH: `test_pi.py:358` / `test_pi_decision_schema.py:15` / `test_pi_v2_contract.py:17`). Review pending.

## 28. Clear the context after every result (clear_after_result)

Decision: a resident Claude Code service worker clears its context with `/clear` after every result, putting token savings first. The single daemon, private Redis, slot/generation, layout, handoff and the task/service request and result protocols are unchanged; this section only adds a session boundary behind an explicit switch and does not change §1–§27.

- **Switch and entry**: `start --clear-after-result`, strictly boolean in config, default false; allowed only with `lifecycle=service`, `mode=service`, official Claude workers and Redis v2 typed delivery. **Mutually exclusive with Claude Stop-hook delivery** (merged in integ/fleet-20261014): the hook channel is on by default, the Stop hook blocks to take the next item, so a worker is never idle between two items and `/clear` can never be pasted (the hook PID never exits → hold after 20 s); therefore with the switch on and `claude_hook_delivery` not false, `start` / `start --resume` / the daemon's config validation refuse outright (`clear_after_result.validate`) and suggest adding `start --no-claude-hook-delivery` (writes config `claude_hook_delivery: false`); `claude_hook.enabled()` also always returns false while the switch is on (backstop). With the switch off, hook-channel behavior is unchanged. StopFailure has a single entry point, `claude-stop-failure`: switch on → this section's `stop_failure` event; switch off → the hook channel's audit `hook.stop_failure` + the `turnend` key (plus the `stop_failure` event of §24 "an API error holds only that slot": task mode redelivers after backoff, service mode fails the request immediately and holds only that slot). `--clear-timeout` / config `clear_timeout` defaults to 20 s and must be a finite positive number. `start --resume` keeps the saved switch and timeout. When off, no new hooks are added and StopFailure events are only audited; the supervisor is never cleared.
- **Hooks**: a worker with the switch on keeps Stop and adds `SessionStart` (matcher `clear`) → `claude-clear` and `StopFailure` → `claude-stop-failure`. The latter two only turn the stdin JSON into a persistent event and do not wait for the daemon. Hook commands start Python with `exec` and report their own PID; the generation is still fixed in the launch argv.
- **Gate after the result**: once a request delivered to a worker publishes a final state (ok, invalid, failed, timeout, etc.), the same intent sets `clearing`, `clear_required=1` and a separate deadline. A queued request that times out before reaching the slot does not clear it. `pump` / `deliver` never deliver to a clearing slot; ordinary schema errors and StopFailure do not rerun the request. A timeout or a failure that cannot prove the turn stopped keeps the current delivery / PEL and must wait for that delivery's own Stop / StopFailure to prove the turn ended; publishing a result does not count as proof of stopping.
- **Safe paste point**: the result transaction only writes state and types nothing. A later daemon tick pastes through the existing `send_prompt_to_idle(pane, '/clear', bracketed=False)` only when there is no current delivery, a valid turn-end PID was received, that PID has exited (only ESRCH counts), and two consecutive looks at least 250 ms apart show Claude's empty input box at the bottom and the idle footer, with no running / cancel / background-task indicator. The screen is only an input-idle gate and is never used to settle or to decide that a turn is complete. `pasted` is persisted before typing; a failure or interruption is never blindly retried; a capture failure, an unrecognized TUI or a still-living PID all wait until the deadline and then hold.
- **Same-generation rebind**: the `cleared` event carries the new session / transcript and the old thread / clear token it read. It checks run, slot, generation, old session and old fence, and requires the current delivery to be empty, the lease idle and the PEL drained. The shared Lua preflight rechecks at the actual apply; bus session + fence +1, agent thread / transcript, ctx reset and the audit are applied in one intent; prepared can be replayed, applied is not redone. Ordinary `register_slot` and `bridge_session` still refuse a session change within the same generation. After the rebind it stays `clearing/confirmed` until the SessionStart hook has also exited and the TUI is idle, then becomes idle; the next delivery binds to the new session.
- **Manual clear and failures**: a manual `/clear` while idle takes the same rebind path; with an in-flight delivery, an old binding mismatch, an undrained PEL or a conflicting same-generation clear notice → the existing hold, no settlement, no guessing of the current session. Clear not confirmed in time (including a missing hook or a failed paste) → hold, keeping clear_required; a held slot with no in-flight delivery can still repair its binding with a valid clear notice, but the existing hold's run-pause semantics remain, and a completed clear does not lift a pause on behalf of the operator or the brake.
- **StopFailure**: only an in-flight service worker delivery of the same run / generation / session can be terminated by it. The delivery / fence / PEL consumer checks remain; it publishes failed with the hook's error text at once and ACKs that delivery, with no retry and no wall redelivery branch; then it goes through the same clear gate. If a public timeout already exists the result is not overwritten; only the execution is wound up and cleared. Old generation, old session, duplicate or no-in-flight failures are only audited.
- **Other lifecycles**: rotation is re-evaluated only at the idle boundary after the clear completes, and start_rotation is refused while clearing; idle reclaim does not reclaim a clearing slot, and service idle-stop also counts clearing as activity. The watchdog is hands-off for clearing and the clear deadline takes over: no Escape, no frozen rotation ladder. The §24 held-slot exit keeps the full identity check and goes to clearing first when it finishes; a held slot whose binding conflict came from a manual clear may not be released by an old callback. An ordinary daemon restart stays held/paused; an explicit `--resume` for a slot whose clear is unfinished, whose rebind is being confirmed, or whose binding conflicts takes the original next-generation respawn, confirming the old pane's retirement and the READY gate, and does not reattach an old pane that may have switched sessions.

**Verification**: 78 new tests (effect plan / input gate / real Redis integration). Real machine (Claude Code + Haiku 5.5, 3 workers, frozen directory): an unbracketed paste of `/clear` executes, SessionStart(source=clear) carries the new session, Stop works as usual after clearing; end to end, 24 real prompts gave 23 ok (1 invalid due to the model's output format, unrelated to clearing), 0 cross-contamination, clear.pasted / slot_rebound / clear.ready 24 each, 8 per worker; one round before the idle-detection fix (the session name was printed on the top border) actually walked "confirmation timeout → hold → fleet paused". Review Opus r1 PASS (reassigned after the Fable quota ran out). Merged into integ/fleet-20261014 (+ hook-channel mutual exclusion): `python3 -m pytest -q tests` 1650 passed 2 skipped; clean PATH 1647 passed 5 skipped (3 extra = the pi contract tests without node); 7 mutual-exclusion + launch-record cases are in `tests/test_clear_after_result.py`. Review pending.

## 29. Named sub-fleets: roster declaration, external start/stop, service sub-fleets (merged, not deployed)

The design follows an internal plan v0.6 (§1–§5; its §3.1 dual-attach display = supervisor_slot runs in the fleet's own tmux, merged via `feat/named-subfleet-tmux`). Numbering: §28 on the live branch was already taken by clear-after-result, so this section became §29. For a fleet whose roster has no `subfleets:` block, nothing in this section applies; the M<n> sub-fleets that §19 starts via escalate are not affected by the roster, but they now also use this section's tmux dual attach (§44 "worker-area mapping").

- **Declaration** (`roster.py`): the roster yaml gains an optional `subfleets:` block, each entry `{name, supervisor_slot, supervisor_role, roster, autostart?=false, mode?=service, claude?, worker_model?, timeouts?{stall, stall_window, request}, max_busy?, drain_s?=600, clear_after_result?, clear_timeout?, claude_hook_delivery?, worker_domains?}` (the three clear keys: bool / positive seconds / bool; `worker_domains`: a list of domain names, may be `[]`; a wrong type rejects the whole roster); the name must not clash with a worker, one worker supervises at most one sub-fleet, the `supervisor_role` / `roster` files must exist, and the child roster itself must validate. One bad entry rejects the whole roster (§16). Only validation for the new block is added; parsing of existing workers / limits / handoff is untouched. Hot reload (`reload_roster` → `named_subfleet.declare`): adding a row declares it (an autostart one is delegated on the next tick); removing a row → a running one goes draining and is reclaimed once nothing is in flight; added back before reclaim → cancelled; an edited entry → takes effect on the next up; the §16 rule "names used in this run reject the whole roster" applies only to workers, not to sub-fleet names. Sub-fleets are not in `dispatch_targets()`.
- **`check()`**: with `named=<entry>` it skips `valid_child_id` / `task_level` (depth) / `max_attempts`, and "already running" is judged by name; model-card concurrency is computed from the entry's `worker_model` (if unset, the fleet's own default); workers≥1, `child_workers_max`, `max_children`, `max_agents_total`, slot idle·READY·nothing in flight, and official Claude only, as before.
- **`child_config`**: a named sub-fleet's `claude` / `worker_model` / `stall_timeout` / `stall_window` / `request_timeout` / `max_busy` / `clear_after_result` / `clear_timeout` / `claude_hook_delivery` come from the entry, and if unset from the fleet's own defaults (`DEFAULTS`: clear off, `clear_timeout` 20 s; `request_timeout` no limit (§43); `claude_hook_delivery` unset = hook delivery on; `claude` unset = the `claude` on PATH), **never from the parent**; `worker_cli=claude`; workers / roles / profiles come from the entry's `roster` (§16 hot reload works inside the sub-fleet as usual); `mode` per entry; a service sub-fleet has `lifecycle=None`, receives no task, has no time limit and writes no `result.json`; session name `fleetmux:<name>`; `supervisor_role` is appended to the child supervisor's AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md on delegation (rule files are backed up first as in §19 and restored on reclaim). The CLI combination table allows adopted claude+claude up to service.
- **External start/stop** (`named_subfleet.py`): the parent fleet (service) receives `{"id","op":"subfleet.up"|"subfleet.down","name","reason"}` and handles it in a separate branch of `Fleet.request` - not through `validate_request`, not into `REQQ`, not subject to `request_timeout`; a resend with the same id is rejected as a duplicate via `fx:subfleet:op:<id>` / the result / a tombstone. The program gate first answers what the state can answer: not declared → `rejected(program)`; external down of an autostart one is refused; up when already up / paused → `ok` with the same address; down when already down → `ok`; up without enough headroom → `rejected(program)`. Everything else asks the parent supervisor: the service decision JSON gains an optional `subfleet: {id, decision: approve|reject, note}` (v2 may only answer the id bound to this item), approve / reject only; the pi supervisor's (the default supervisor) service `fleet_decision` TypeBox allows this optional field too (`id` may be omitted and is bound to this request by the runtime; `decision` required, `note` text), and the Python side's `parse_service_decision` uses the same rules. After approval the daemon tick executes it: up waits for the slot to be idle·READY (over 600 s returns `timeout: slot busy`) → rotation gate → delegation → once the child supervisor is READY returns `ok {state, redis_socket, run_dir}` (over `profile_ready_timeout + 120` s returns `timeout: child not READY`); down first sets the parent hash to `draining` (callers treat it as unavailable), waits until `REQQ` in the child Redis is empty and no slot has anything in flight / queued, or reclaims directly after `drain_s` (audit `subfleet.drain_timeout`, returns `timeout: drain exceeded`), and returns `ok {state: down}` after reclaiming. Receipts are all written to `fx:result:<id>`; result cards gain a `rejected` status. The recovery reconciliation of a parent `start --resume` (service) cancels all supervisor messages not yet delivered, approvals included: after `requeue_requests`, approvals are re-sent for ops still `awaiting` in `fx:subfleet:opq` (audit `subfleet.request_requeued`), and `approved` ones are executed by the tick as usual, so every accepted op reaches a final state (review finding P0-01). A resend with the same id and the same op / name while the op is unfinished → returns `in_progress` (with `op_status`) and starts no new approval; the same id with a different op, or after a result exists → still rejected as a duplicate.
- **Outcome of a service sub-fleet**: an external down / a removed row / a parent stop / a reclaim on parent resume is recorded as `down` (`subfleet.json` state `down`), not failed, and **no "[Sub-fleet … failed]" is sent to the parent supervisor**; a child daemon exiting for no reason (non-autostart) is recorded as failed, also without notification.
- **Address**: the parent Redis hash `fx:subfleet:<name>` = `state` (down / starting / up / paused / draining) / `slot` / `redis_socket` / `run_dir` / `up_at`, etc.; the `status` tree carries `name / mode / autostart`. A sub-fleet pausing itself (`fx:service.paused=1`) → parent hash `state=paused`, back to `up` after resuming.
- **autostart**: entry `autostart: true` → delegated on the tick when the slot is first idle·READY (without the parent supervisor). Child daemon gone → before the tick's reclaim branch, restart Redis on the original socket + `daemon --resume`; the 3rd time within 1 hour → `subfleet.crashloop`, the original reclaim branch, parent hash `crashloop=1`, no more automatic restarts (it can start again after an approved external up, which clears the flag). Parent `--resume`: autostart ones are brought up as in §19; `autostart: false` ones are reclaimed and the parent hash is written `down`.
- **Rotation gate** (N1): before each delegation, check the slot's context usage (§22 threshold, the transcript of the same Claude session); over the threshold → `start_rotation` first, delegate after the new generation is READY; a new session that was never delegated and never dispatched to and is over the threshold only logs `rotate.impossible` and is delegated as usual. While an autostart sub-fleet is up, the parent checks every 30 s when the child supervisor is idle; over the threshold → draining (`subfleet.rotate`) → reclaim → rotation gate → delegate again, so the same over-threshold session is never delegated again as is. (The design said "the child daemon checks every tick"; the implementation sits in the parent daemon: same session, same transcript, same result, one cross-process channel fewer.)
- **Child run directory**: a named sub-fleet reuses `sub/<name>/` on every up, and all files from last time move to `sub/<name>/archive/<date>/<HHMMSS>/`.
- **View hooks** (after merging the A2 dual attach): parent side `subfleet.parent_attach_view(f, slot, sid, task_id, ws_title)` (on delegation: slot runs in the fleet's own tmux → no placeholder, no release, returns the second client's info, `rec['view']=1`; ordinary cmux pane → §19 placeholder + release) and `subfleet.parent_detach_view(f, rec, child_mgr, child_run)` (on collect: view → `detach_child_views` disconnects any client left on the child side as a backstop and renames the parent tab in place; otherwise §19 handback / adopt back to the placeholder); the child side keeps A2's `attach_child_view(f, sid, peer, info)` (`start_adopted`) / `detach_child_view(f, sid)` (`hand_back`). **Enabling key**: `start --roster` / `start --resume --roster` write `roster.load_subfleets()` into `cfg['subfleets']` before the first spawn; when `reload_roster` accepts a new roster it updates the in-memory `self.cfg` too and writes it back to config.json via `persist_roster` (`tmux_slot` reads it on every spawn; `tmux_slot(cfg, slot, info)` now also honours `tmux_view=1` on the slot's agent hash, set at §44 approve, see §44 "worker-area mapping"). A sub-fleet config does not inherit `subfleets`. **Hot-loaded slot that already has an ordinary pane** (chosen minimal behavior): this delegation goes through §19 move-surface and audits `subfleet.view_fallback`; from its next generation the slot lives in the fleet tmux and is dual-attached from then on; no forced rotation for this.
- **View hooks on the fleetmux backend (M4; internal design v0.7 §5.1 / §6.2)**: the whole tree shares the root fleet's single `fleetmux server`, one page per fleet. A parent fleet writes two binding keys into the child config only when it is itself `backend: fleetmux`: `fleetmux_root_session` (the root session, passed down from parent to grandchild) and `fleetmux_page` (the child fleet's `task_id`: `M0`, `M0.1`, or the name of a named one; the retry directory `sub/M0-attempt-2` is still `M0`); the temporary child managers built by `spawn_child` / `rollback` carry them too; `fleet.manager_for` calls `bind_page` when it sees `fleetmux_page`, so the child daemon, actors, hooks, `--resume` and `named_subfleet.revive` all connect to the root server and land on this page. Named sub-fleets keep the tmux dual attach: the parent page's tab stays put, and the child side's `attach_child_view` `view-<session>` lands at the top left of the child page; M<n> sub-fleets now use the same dual attach (§44 "worker-area mapping": the worker position on the parent page is the supervisor's live session, the top left of the child page is a view of the same session); only a slot still in an ordinary pane uses the §19 placeholder + cross-page `adopt` (same PTY, same pid), and on the normal path approve has already rotated the slot into tmux. On reclaim, the child manager's `close_all` only closes this page's panes and marks the page reclaimed, **never** closes the server, and never touches sidebar bash panes (bash is a tab in the root page's worker area, adopted only by the root fleet, see §33.6); `collect` then opens a read-only `history_view <child run>` tab for this page in the parent registry (together with reclaimed grandchild fleets); before a fleet with the same name / number starts again it is cleared first (`unarchive_page`), and a root `--resume` rebuilds the reclaimed pages first and then brings up the live sub-fleets. A child daemon writes none of kv `sidebar` / `stop_request` / `restorable` (only the root daemon pushes the sidebar); a sidebar card's `page` comes from the `task_id` in the child run's `subfleet.json`, only the latest attempt's card of a given `task_id` carries `page`, and the display keys of `subfleet.tree()` are unchanged. The cmux backend is byte-for-byte unchanged; WezTerm still refuses (§33.4).
- **Audit**: `subfleet.declared / request_in / approved / rejected / autostart / autostart_wait / up / paused / draining / rotate / drain_timeout / stopping / down / crashloop / revive_failed`; `subfleet.up` carries the control line `<name>: up via <slot> since HH:MM`.

- **Reclaim = retire the old session and start the parent slot's next generation, with a handoff note (decided as option A of the design; implemented)**: the no-typing batch rules that a Claude worker on the hook channel never types (§19 R1-01), so on reclaim a named sub-fleet no longer "switches the same session back to the worker rules in place"; instead the parent slot goes through a §4 rotation:
  - **The old session writes a handoff note**: `collect` sets the slot back to idle and records `subfleet_return` (sub-fleet name, up / down times, outcome, the request ids the sub-fleet answered and their states, the request ids still unanswered at down - a planned down reads them from the child Redis into `subfleet.json` before sending stop to the sub-fleet; on a crash / parent stop they are read on the spot if the child Redis is still there), then `start_rotation` with the prompt `handoff.SUBFLEET_RETURN_PROMPT` (a same-format variant of `DUMP_PROMPT`: says the sub-fleet is down and the supervisor role is over). Delivery follows the live rotation path into that slot's per-generation hook mailbox, with no typing. A sub-fleet with `adopt.hook` true (new runs): during delegation that session also uses the hook mailbox as the child supervisor, and its Stop hook waiting on the child mailbox follows the routing back to the parent mailbox after reclaim and takes the handoff delivery directly (§19 "hook mailbox redirection"); in an old run (child supervisor typed) no parent-side Stop hook is waiting at reclaim, and the asyncRewake backstop started by its last Stop (`claude_rewake`) **does not exit** while routing points to a typed child supervisor and the slot's own channel is the hook: it waits in place during delegation and, after reclaim, hands the handoff delivery to Claude under the original backstop rules (no hook waiting / settling, past GRACE). In both cases that turn's Stop has been routed back to the parent channel, settles with the §21 binding → `finish_rotation` merges the model's three sections.
  - **Skeleton fallback**: if no one takes the handoff delivery within `RETURN_WAKE_S` after reclaim (180 s = the stopping mark of the last Stop + GRACE margin) (no live rewake) → `subfleet.return_untaken`, rotate directly with the daemon's skeleton handoff note. An untaken handoff delivery never ran, so **no hold, no fleet pause**; one that was taken but exceeds `handoff_timeout` holds per the original §4 rule (a slot in an ordinary cmux pane lifts the pause automatically once the new generation is READY, per the §23 "hold exit" exception; a slot running in the fleet tmux does not, and still waits for the operator to resume). The delegated session is already dead (`collect` cannot get it back) → no waiting: skeleton handoff note `handoffs/<slot>-g<n>-return.md` + the next generation. The skeleton's Done section lists the sub-fleet name, up / down times and the answered requests (latest 30 ids + states), and "Needs a human decision" lists the request ids still unanswered at down; when the model wrote a handoff note, its three sections win per §4.
  - **Next generation's launch prompt**: "You are fleet agent <label>, a worker of the main fleet; the sub-fleet <name> supervised by the previous session is down and that role is over; read the handoff note <path> and AGENTS.md, only read in this turn, reply READY", no longer "This run was resumed after an interruption".
  - **Late Stop**: after the rotation a Stop from the old session is judged stale by generation and does not settle (the live rotation logic). Outcome / receipts / no notification to the parent supervisor are unchanged.
  - **During delegation the same session is still shown in two places** (the parent tab shows the worker, the sub-fleet shows the supervisor, §3.1). Only the typing fallback path (`claude_hook_delivery: false`) still brings the same session back and types a notice that it is a worker again.
- **Clear-after-result (§28) inside a sub-fleet**: entry `clear_after_result: true` makes the sub-fleet's Claude workers `/clear` after every item per §28; the sub-fleet is subject to the same mutual exclusion of §28 and hook delivery: clear on and `claude_hook_delivery` not false → that sub-fleet gets `rejected(program)` **at up** (autostart logs `subfleet.autostart_wait`), with the reason "add claude_hook_delivery: false to its subfleets entry"; it also needs `mode: service` and transport v2. A service named sub-fleet's `lifecycle` is None (its parent manages it), and the §28 validation treats "`named` + `parent_config` + lifecycle None" as a service lifecycle.
- **Baseline domains of a sub-fleet (decision)**: the entry's `worker_domains` is the sub-fleet's own `--worker-domains` baseline (the §27 advisor only adds on top); **if set, it replaces the parent's value** (`[]` = no baseline, e.g. a memory-extraction memx sub-fleet that does not install the futures skills); **if unset, it inherits the parent's value** (unlike keys such as `claude` / `worker_model` that "never come from the parent": the baseline is the domain set the parent fleet gives the whole tree, and inheriting by default keeps sub-fleets like tgog from losing their domains). When non-empty, `check()` validates it before up with the `parse_worker_domains` rules: the parent config must have `--worker-kit` that is a domain kit, and each name must be in its domain list; otherwise → `rejected(program)`.
- **Advisor of a sub-fleet (decision)**: a child roster may have `advisor: on|off`, default off; the parent's switch is never inherited (`child_config` drops `advisor`); the child daemon reads its own roster's switch at startup (§27), and a bad value rejects the whole parent roster. When on, the sub-fleet's Claude workers are held for a sheet per §27; `worker_kit` is passed in with the parent config.
- **Night roster**: `check_rosters` of `start --night-roster` / `night on|off` requires the night roster's `subfleets:` block to match the day roster's (compared as parsed entries, relative paths resolved against each file's own directory); a mismatch rejects the whole roster and lists the mismatching names - named sub-fleets are fixed per run, and the night switch never adds, removes or changes them.

**Verification**: the named part of `tests/test_subfleet.py` (roster validation, design items 1–10, hook-channel reclaim rotation, 3 for the clear keys, 4 for the advisor), `tests/test_subfleet_named_tmux.py` / `tests/test_subfleet_tmux.py` (dual attach), `tests/test_night_switch.py` (night subfleets consistency), `tests/test_pi_decision_schema.py` (pi service schema contract, skipped without node); full-suite results are recorded in an internal plan. **Not done**: a parent workspace tab-title suffix "(also supervisor of sub-fleet <name>)", real-machine cmux / Claude verification, independent review, merge into the live branch, deployment.

## 30. Start a fleet from a worker kit: `start --kit-fleet NAME`

Decision: each fleet's **business** role prompts and roster templates go into the worker kit (assembled by an external build script, §27); a fleet release = fleet code + kit. This section only adds one roster source; §16 roster semantics / hot reload, §17 resume, the §27 advisor switch and §29 named sub-fleets are all unchanged.

- **Protocol prompts stay out of the kit**: `fleetmux/agents/*.md` (supervisor / worker protocol, contracts, peer notices, advisor, etc.) are part of the fleet protocol, stay in the fleet code and ship with it; the kit carries only business roles (the files `role` / `supervisor_role` point to) and rosters.
- **Kit layout**: `<worker_kit>/fleets/<NAME>/roster.yaml` is required; the same directory may hold child rosters (e.g. `memx_roster.yaml`), `roles/*.md`, etc., and paths in the roster are relative to the roster's directory as in §16 / §29. `<NAME>` matches `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$`. The kit's MANIFEST includes these files. `kit.py` neither reads nor validates `fleets/` (extra directories are accepted as usual).
- **Why copy**: kit directories are versioned by hash and read-only; the roster is hot-edited while running (§16 adding / removing workers, `fleet advisor on|off` writing back via `roster.write_advisor`), so what is in the kit is only a template.
- **New start**: `start --kit-fleet NAME` requires `--worker-kit` as well, and the kit must contain `fleets/NAME/roster.yaml`; mutually exclusive with `--roster` / `--workers` / `--worker-names` (same rules as `--roster`); an invalid name, missing kit, missing file or both given → SystemExit before provisioning, stating what is missing. Validation runs the original `--roster` path directly on the roster in the kit (including `--night-roster`, pi models, model-card votes); after the run directory is created the whole `<kit>/fleets/NAME/` tree is copied to `<run>/fleet-config/` (adding owner write permission), the roster is re-parsed from the copy (absolute paths of roles / child rosters point into the copy, so deleting the kit breaks nothing), and from then on it is exactly `--roster <run>/fleet-config/roster.yaml` (subfleets, advisor switch, hot reload).
- **config.json**: `kit_fleet: {"name": NAME, "kit": <absolute kit path>, "roster_md5": <md5 of roster.yaml in the kit>}`, a provenance record only, not read by the daemon; `roster` points to the copy.
- **Resume**: `start --resume` keeps the existing copy in `<run>/fleet-config/` and **does not copy again** (hot edits must not be lost); a missing kit directory does not matter; a `--kit-fleet` given on resume is only used for the flag-exclusion / name checks, and NAME is not compared with the config but ignored.
- **Known boundaries**: a kit upgrade never reaches a running or resumed run (start a new run to get the new version); the only way to tell the copy from the kit is `roster_md5` against the kit version's roster; child rosters / role files are not hashed.

**Verification**: `tests/test_kit_fleet.py` (new start from a read-only kit copies + the copy is writable + config identical to `--roster <copy>`; child-roster relative paths resolve in the copy; missing kit / missing `fleets/NAME` / invalid name / given together with `--roster`, `--workers`, `--worker-names` → refused before provisioning; after editing the copy and deleting the kit, `--resume` does not copy again and keeps the hot edits; `Kit` accepts `fleets/`). **Not done**: one real-machine cmux start, independent review, merge into the live branch, deployment.

## 31. Extra tabs: roster `tabs:` (minimal version)

Decision: the HTTP shell for EverMemOS memory extraction on the deployment host (`evermem_fleet_shell.py`, :8895, forwarding to the memx sub-fleet) used to be run by hand in a separate cmux workspace; now it is declared in the kit roster and opened as a tab after control in the memx sub-fleet's workspace, not hard-coded. Only one top-level roster key is added; without `tabs:` this section has no effect.

- **Declaration** (`roster.py`): a top-level `tabs:` list, each item `{name, command, cwd?, env?}`. `name` matches `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$` and is unique in the roster; `command` is a non-empty string; `cwd` is relative to the roster directory (`~` expanded), defaults to the roster directory, and must be an existing directory; `env` maps variable names → strings (`8895` needs quotes); any other key or any invalid item → the whole roster is rejected (§16 `roster.rejected`). Whichever fleet loads this roster (main fleet or named sub-fleet) gets the tab in its workspace.
- **When it opens** (`tabs.open_all`, the `tabs` part of `Fleet.tick`): each time the fleet's daemon starts (`start`, `start --resume`, a named sub-fleet's `daemon --resume` revived by its parent), it opens once on the tick when the roster is first accepted; after that it is left alone: **if the tab is closed / the program exits while the daemon lives, it is not reopened automatically**, it waits for the next fleet start; hot-editing `tabs:` also takes effect only at the next fleet start.
- **Clear the same program before opening**: first close tabs left in this registry by the previous daemon that cmux still shows; then for each tab find processes whose command line exactly equals it via `ps -axww -o pid=,command=`, SIGTERM all of them, wait up to 5 s, SIGKILL any still alive (wait another 2 s), and open only once all are confirmed gone; `ps` failing or a process that cannot be killed → do not open, log `tab.start_failed`. **Matching rule**: tab argv = `shlex.split(command)`; the process's `ps` command line must equal the argv joined with spaces, the only relaxation being that the first word may also be argv[0] resolved through `which` or its `realpath`, or (macOS framework Python) `Resources/Python.app/Contents/MacOS/Python` under the same `Python.framework/Versions/X.Y` (framework Python re-execs into it and rewrites argv[0] in `ps`); every other word must match exactly. One argument more or less, or a different interpreter, does not match. So `command` must be a program that is exec'd directly (no pipes / `&&`); `env` takes no part in matching.
- **Position and ownership**: `spawn_window` runs `cd <cwd> && exec [env K=V …] <command>` (the program is the process in the surface, with the same command line as the roster), opens it in this fleet's workspace, then `move-surface --after` moves it into the pane holding history / control: after the previous tab, otherwise after control, otherwise after history; `--focus false`, title = `name`. The surface is registered as owned by this fleet (`type: tab`, `tab: <name>`), so lifecycle close / a sub-fleet reclaim's `close_all` closes it too; an explicit `stop` wind-up also closes it via `tabs.stop_all` and counts it toward "all stopped cleanly". Not an agent: not in the worker roster, no deliveries, not in prompts, no quota.
- **Audit**: `tab.cleared` (which pids were killed) / `tab.started` / `tab.start_failed` / `tab.dock_failed` / `tab.stopped`.
- **Security**: tab programs run as the fleet user, and **the roster is the authorization** - whoever can edit the roster (the kit or the run copy) can make the fleet run any command and kill processes with the same command line.

**Verification**: `tests/test_roster_tabs.py` (roster parsing and rejection of bad values; matching rule including a framework-Python stub; real processes: cleared by SIGTERM, one ignoring SIGTERM gets SIGKILL, the same program with different arguments is untouched; opens only once, docked in order after control, old tabs closed first; not opened if clearing fails; stop closes tabs; `dock_tab` / `spawn_window` registration). **Not done**: full suite, real-machine cmux, review, merge into the live branch, deployment.

## 32. service dispatch checks state + rotate as soon as idle

Found on a local all-FlashNext service fleet: the supervisor did not pick idle workers automatically and reviews could not find the right worker; a worker over the rotation threshold never rotated. Three changes; the protocol, identity and rotation flow (§4) are unchanged.

- **Routing messages carry worker state** (`Fleet.worker_board`, after the original `Workers:` line of `route()`, which is kept): one line per dispatchable worker `- <label>: <state>[, N queued][, has request <id>…][, role: <first non-empty line of the role file, ≤120 chars>]`. In-flight requests are found by `status=dispatched` + `worker` in `fx:req:*` (works for v1 / v2). The state is a snapshot at enqueue time; the supervisor prompt adds one sentence: "a worker you dispatched to in an earlier turn that has not reported back counts as busy".
- **service supervisor prompts** (`service_supervisor.md` / `_v2` / `_tui_v2` each get two prohibitions): while a worker is idle, never queue a request behind a busy worker, unless the request explicitly continues that worker's own unfinished request, and say so in `reason`; when a worker with a reviewer role exists, review / acceptance requests may not go to anyone else, and never to the author of the work under review. Roles come from the roster's `role` files (§16); this section adds no new roster field.
- **Rotate as soon as idle** (`Fleet.idle_rotate`, for an idle supervisor / worker with an empty mailbox in `pump()`): usage is read only once per (generation, delivered), and over the threshold → `start_rotation`; the same guards as `deliver()` (profile card switch in progress, a v2 non-TUI slot without `bridge_available`, a slot adopted by a sub-fleet, a Codex native sub-agent running); a freshly started session (delivered=0) is not judged here, and `rotate.impossible` still belongs to delivery time. Subject to `max_busy` and pause (a handoff counts as busy). The advisor slot does not use this (§27 manages it itself).
- **Known boundaries**: mid-turn rotation for long turns is covered by §34 (daemon probe + forced prompt); a slot `held` by a request timeout stays `held` after a late settlement (`settle.late` only audits) and does not rotate on idle; it needs a human / the watchdog - the 85k incident was exactly this case, not covered by this section (since §43 this holds only that slot and does not pause the fleet; a Claude hook slot is released when its turn ends in the same session).

**Verification**: `tests/test_fleet.py` adds rotate-on-idle over the threshold (delivered 1 rotates, 0 does not), usage read only once per (generation, delivered), and routing messages containing state / queue / in-flight requests / role; `test_rotated_worker_gets_next_name_and_old_name_still_routes` moves `handoff_timeout=0` earlier for the new timing (rotation now happens on the idle tick).

## 33. Terminal backends: cmux or WezTerm

Two choices: cmux, and a replacement backend modeled on cmux. Goal: macOS keeps using cmux; Linux (including WSL2 on Windows) runs the same supervisor + workers in WezTerm. Native Windows, a full Rust rewrite and headless Zellij / tmux backends are **out of scope**. This section changes no identity / handoff / layout baseline (§3, §4, §2); cmux backend behavior is unchanged.

### 33.1 Interface (only one place touches the terminal)

The fleet touches the terminal only through the one object built by `fleet.manager_for(cfg)` (formerly `fleet.py:210`). That object's public methods are the backend interface, written in `fleetmux/backends/base.py`:

- `METHODS`: the list of 39 methods (session `available` / `session_exists` / `get_or_create_session` / `claim_run` / `workspace_id` / `select_workspace` / `keep_windows_open` / `owns` / `close_all`; window spawning `spawn_window` / `spawn_interactive_cli`; read/write `send_prompt_to_idle` / `send_key` / `capture_output` / `capture_to`; state `get_pane_status` / `has_cli_process` / `process_states` / `process_cpu`; tab focus `rename` / `focus` / `kill_pane`; layout `arrange` / `dock_tab` / `dock_control` / `place_right_of` / `place_after` / `tab_into` / `tab_panes`; browser cell `browser_goto` / `browser_url` / `reload_browser`; handover `release` / `adopt`; tmux view `tmux_view_info` / `attach_view` / `tmux_session_alive` / `reattach_client` / `detach_view`), with signatures identical to `CmuxSessionManager`.
- `TerminalBackend` (`typing.Protocol`) is the type view of the same list; `Capabilities(browser, nested_tabs)` and the class attributes `supports_browser` / `nested_tabs` are the only capability bits the fleet reads.
- Constructors are uniformly `(session_name, agents_title, control_title, state_dir=None)`; `backends.build(name, session, **extra)` imports the matching module lazily.
- Availability of each backend: `available()` (class/static method; for cmux it is `cmux_available()`), `unavailable_reason(name)` (cmux decided by `cmux_available()`; WezTerm reports which program/version is missing).
- `tests/test_backend_interface.py` compares the signatures and constructor parameters of both implementations method by method and requires `METHODS` to miss nothing and add nothing; **adding a backend = implementing the list + passing this test**; changing the interface = changing both implementations and the fleet together.

### 33.2 The two backends

- **cmux** (`fleetmux/backends/cmux.py`): the original `cmux_manager.py` moved as is (behavior unchanged); `fleetmux/cmux_manager.py` only forwards (`sys.modules[__name__] = backends.cmux`, both module names are the same module object), so existing imports and the tests' monkeypatches of `cmux_manager._run` / `cmux_available` all keep working. `supports_browser = True`, `nested_tabs = True`. macOS-only paths such as `/Applications/cmux.app` appear only in this file.
- **WezTerm** (`fleetmux/backends/wezterm.py`): one private `wezterm-mux-server` per run (short-path unix socket, written in a minimal `wezterm.lua` under the run's `.runtime/wezterm/<token>/`; every CLI call carries `WEZTERM_UNIX_SOCKET` and `--no-auto-start` and **never** connects to the user's own WezTerm); `supports_browser = False`, `nested_tabs = False`. The method mapping and the availability check (`wezterm` and `wezterm-mux-server` on PATH, version ≥ 20240203 from `deps.yaml`) follow an internal design draft.

### 33.3 Selection and inheritance

- `start --backend cmux|wezterm|fleetmux`; when not given (`backends.base.default_backend`):
  - **macOS unchanged**: `sys.platform == 'darwin'` and cmux **installed** (`CMUX_BIN` / the cmux.app path or `cmux` on PATH, `backends.base.cmux_installed`) → cmux; if cmux does not answer (ssh / Terminal.app) it refuses as before with "run this inside a cmux terminal" and **never silently switches to wezterm**. On a Mac without cmux, with the WezTerm backend available → wezterm (tightened by a core review).
  - **Non-macOS (Linux, including WSL2) → fleetmux** (M3 py-switch, merged after the operator passed the "M0–M2 Mac/VM acceptance"). Installing cmux on Linux does not make it a cmux host.
  - An explicit `--backend wezterm` (or `cmux`) still applies; WezTerm can still be chosen by hand on Linux. If the explicitly chosen backend is unavailable on this machine → refuse before starting and say what is missing (`fleet.resolve_backend`, in `start` before provisioning).
- The chosen value is written to `backend` in the run's `config.json`; `--resume` treats an old run without a `backend` key as cmux (`resolve_backend(cfg.get('backend') or 'cmux')`) and does not re-run the default rule; `manager_for` only reads it, and the daemon / actor / `--resume` / sub-fleets (the child config is copied from the parent config) all follow it instead of deciding on their own. **An old config without a `backend` key (a run started before this change) is treated as cmux**, equivalent to the original behavior.
- Automatic selection never pushes a run to WezTerm on macOS: with cmux installed it is always cmux; without cmux and with the WezTerm backend unavailable too (not installed or not merged), the answer still falls back to cmux and reports the original "run this inside a cmux terminal" kind of message.
- `scripts/check_deps.py::pick_backend`: an explicit `--backend` or `FLEETMUX_BACKEND` wins; otherwise on darwin it is cmux if `cmux --version` runs and wezterm if not; on non-macOS it is `DEFAULT_BACKEND`, which is `fleetmux` since M3. The row order of the manifest plays no part.
- `fleet.yaml` gets no block: the backend is a per-run choice, not a table.

### 33.4 Layout degradation (WezTerm)

- cmux layout (§2): supervisor top left, worker tabs on the right, history/control tabs bottom left, relying on "tabs inside a split". WezTerm has no such layer: **one window; tab 1 = supervisor (left) + control (bottom-right split); one tab per worker**; `tab_into` / `dock_*` / `place_*` degrade to "put into the matching tab / no-op returning True" without error; with `supports_browser = False`, `arrange()` lays out the workers, puts control into tab 1 (bottom-right split, implemented by the WezTerm backend itself) and returns `None`.
- Layout and history are the only two places where the fleet reads capability bits (`Fleet.announce_history_page()` / `subfleet.follow_jump()` read `supports_browser`); every other call path uses the original interface, and apart from `available()` / `supports_browser` / `nested_tabs` the cmux backend is line-for-line identical to before the move.
- History page: with `supports_browser = False` no browser cell is created; `Fleet.announce_history_page()` prints the `<run>/history.html` path in control and, when a graphical environment exists, opens it once with the system opener (macOS `open` / Linux `xdg-open`; otherwise it only prints); `refresh_history()` calls `reload_browser` only when a browser cell exists; `subfleet.follow_jump()` returns at once when there is no page (and no longer calls cmux every tick). The cmux branches all keep their original calls.
- **Known limitation: WezTerm does not support sub-fleets** (found in review). A sub-fleet works by handing the parent fleet's live panes to the sub-fleet's registry (`release` / `adopt`, tmux view); WezTerm runs one private mux server per run, so a sub-fleet can neither see nor take the parent fleet's panes. Therefore under `backend: wezterm` every sub-fleet entry refuses with the same reason (`subfleet.backend_problem`): `subfleet.check` (split subtasks / `subfleet spawn` / named sub-fleet up / escalation approval), the child daemon's `start_adopted` (blocked outright), `start` / `--resume` with a roster containing `subfleets:`, and a roster hot reload that introduces `subfleets:` (the whole roster is rejected, the old roster stays). The cmux path is unchanged. To use sub-fleets, use `--backend cmux`; cross-server handover is a separate track. The fleetmux backend no longer refuses since M4 (one server, several pages, see §33.6 "Sub-fleets"), so this sentence now applies only to WezTerm.

### 33.5 Small platform changes

`fleetmux/platform.py::keep_awake_prefix()`: macOS `caffeinate -i`; on Linux `systemd-inhibit --what=idle:sleep --why=fleetmux` when `systemd-inhibit` exists and a trial run with `true` succeeds (5 s timeout); otherwise empty (when started from an ssh session or on a headless server, polkit refuses the inhibitor with "Failed to inhibit: Access denied", and a daemon wrapped in it dies at start). The two original `caffeinate` calls in `fleet.py` and the one in `subfleet.py` now call it. `deps.yaml` gates by backend (`when: cmux` / `when: wezterm`, the latter including `wezterm-mux-server`, minimum 20240203), and `scripts/check_deps.py` picks the backend with the same §33.3 rule and skips items that do not belong to it.
