**Verification**: `tests/test_backend_interface.py` (interface complete), `tests/test_backend_selection.py` (selection / inheritance / degradation), `tests/test_platform.py`, `tests/test_check_deps.py`, WezTerm fake-CLI and real mux server tests (impl-wezterm branch), and the `ubuntu-latest` full job of `.github/workflows/tests.yml`. The real-machine smoke test (`start --backend wezterm` with a pi supervisor + worker) is left to the orchestrating session and is not done inside this fleet.

### 33.6 fleetmux backend (Linux default since M3)

Decision (plan A): write our own fixed-layout terminal in Rust that serves only the fleet: `fleetmux`. The code lives in the repo under `rust/`, a separate cargo workspace, MIT licensed; `cargo deny check licenses` admits only MIT / Apache-2.0 style licenses. Its sole design basis is an internal design document; the wire protocol is `rust/PROTOCOL.md` (protocol version 1). This section records only what the fleet side wires up; it does not change the §2 / §3 / §4 baselines, and cmux and WezTerm backend behavior is unchanged.

- **Selection** (§33.3): since M3 py-switch, fleetmux is the default on non-macOS when `--backend` is not given; macOS is unchanged and needs `start --backend fleetmux`; `--backend wezterm` is still available on Linux. Once chosen it is written to `config.json` per §33.3, and the daemon / actor / `--resume` follow it. (From M0 to M2 it could only be chosen by hand; the default was unchanged.)
- **Structure**: one private `fleetmux server` per run holds every pane's PTY, parser (alacritty_terminal), layout and screen. The fleet sends a JSON-lines protocol (17 commands) over `<state_dir>/fleetmux/<token>/ctl.sock` in the runtime directory; people watch with `fleetmux attach --runtime <dir> [--no-mouse]`. attach is a separate client; several can be open at once, and closing them does not affect the agents.
  - Runtime directory 0700, socket 0600; the peer uid must match.
  - The runtime directory also holds `server.json`, `panes.json`, `layout.json`, `start.lock`, `server.log`. After the server shuts down, `layout.json`, `start.lock` and `server.log` remain (R7).
  - `start.lock` belongs to the starter, i.e. the lock the Python backend takes before starting the server. The server process itself does not block on this lock (fix-m0-2). On the normal path the starter holds the lock and then starts the server, and on this path atomic publication of the socket guarantees a single instance. This **does not mean** every race is gone: concurrent starts without the starter lock while a stale socket exists still carry a recorded TOCTOU residual risk; the hard-link publication path on Linux is also not yet verified.
- **Python backend**: `fleetmux/backends/fleetmux.py` implements all 39 `METHODS`, plus `meta_path` / `_meta` / `_save_meta` / `_tag`. The registry is `<state_dir>/<session>.fleetmux.json`, deliberately named differently from the cmux / WezTerm `<session>.json`.
  - Capability bits: `supports_browser = True`, meaning "has a history cell", not a browser; `nested_tabs = True`.
  - Availability: `$FLEETMUX_BIN` or `fleetmux` on PATH. `fleetmux --version` must print `fleetmux <x.y.z> (protocol 1)` with a version ≥ `programs.fleetmux.min` in `deps.yaml` (`when: fleetmux`); otherwise it refuses before starting.
- **Layout**: three fixed groups. Top left is the supervisor group; bottom left is `history` (`python -m fleetmux.history_view <run>`) and `control`; the right side is the worker group. On rotation the successor is inserted back at the original tab position.
  - Tab style (fix-m1-tabs): each tab is a clickable color block with left/right padding and gaps between tabs; only a click inside a block selects that tab, and clicks on gaps or on the blank end of the tab bar select nothing. Three selection levels: the selected tab in the focused group is the most prominent (cyan, bold), the selected tab in an unfocused group is gray, and unselected tabs are dark gray blocks. Unseen output is marked `•`. Tab colors use only the 16 ANSI colors, so each terminal shows its own palette. The borders of the three cells are always shown, and the focused cell's border is highlighted.
  - Dragging: drag the vertical divider to change the left/right ratio; drag the left column's horizontal divider to change the top/bottom ratio; the horizontal divider has a visible drag handle. Both ratios are clamped to 10%–90% and stored in `layout.json`.
  - Keys (prefix `C-b`), mouse, paging, copy, multiple viewers and the 256-color downgrade: see `PROTOCOL.md` §10–§14.
  - Prefix + `o` opens `history.html` on the machine where attach runs; if it cannot, it only shows the path.
- **`start` wiring**: with the fleetmux backend, `start` prints one line `fleetmux attach --runtime <dir>` in the terminal. `start --attach` execs that command after start completes; with other backends, `--attach` refuses with exit 2.
- **Install**:
  - From source: `cd rust && cargo install --path crates/app` (toolchain per `rust/rust-toolchain.toml`);
  - Prebuilt: `scripts/install_fleetmux.sh` downloads GitHub release `v<min>` and installs nothing if the sha256 does not match. Releases are built by `.github/workflows/release.yml` for four targets; publishing is the orchestrating session's job, and no release was ever published from inside this fleet.
- **OSC 52 in the outer terminal** (measured by the orchestrating session during M1 acceptance): cmux accepts it; WezTerm accepts it on both macOS and Linux; Terminal.app does not; gnome-terminal (VTE) does not; iTerm2 is **not tested**. Terminals that do not accept it can only rely on `pbcopy` / `wl-copy` / `xclip` on the server's machine.
- **Crash recovery order**: after the server dies (e.g. `kill -9`), **first** `fleetmux reap --runtime <dir>`, **then** `fleetmux start --resume`. The first spawn of a new server rewrites `panes.json`, after which reap can no longer find the old process groups. Demos or verification may only be done on a dedicated test run; never kill / reap a server the user is using or another fleet.
- **Sub-fleets (M4)**: no longer refused. The whole tree shares the root fleet's server, one page per fleet (the fixed layout is unchanged, just repeated); `subfleet.backend_problem` refuses only WezTerm (original wording unchanged). A runtime capability gate `subfleet.pages_problem` is added: when the server's `hello` reply lacks `pages: 1` (an M3 server), `check()` (split / `subfleet spawn` / named up / escalation approval) and the child daemon's `start_adopted` refuse and state the reason; when no server is running it does not judge (the version belongs to the root that starts the server). Binding keys, view hooks, the read-only record page and the sidebar card's `page` are in §29 "View hooks on the fleetmux backend". A sub-fleet's manager never starts or stops the server (`end_server` sends nothing); only the root starts it, and the root closes it last. Raising `deps.yaml`'s `fleetmux.min` to the M4 release number is a separate commit (the integrator checks the release number).
- **Sidebar bash shells land in the main fleet's worker area and are managed by the main fleet** (replaces an earlier rule that gave each bash its own page `_bash-<pane id>` outside the registry): any new bash opened by double-clicking in the fleetmux sidebar is mapped into the main fleet's worker area and comes under the main fleet's management. A bash opened by double-clicking blank space / the top-bar `+` / `^b c` is started by the server as a tab at the end of the **root page**'s `workers` group (title still `bash-<n>`; when the current page is a child page it still lands on the root page, switches back to the root page, selects that tab and focuses its group, un-zooming any zoomed pane first); clicking a sidebar bash card likewise switches to the root page and focuses that tab, and clicking a fleet card hands focus back to the supervisor group. The root daemon's per-second tick (`fleetmux_sidebar.tick` → `adopt_shells`) and `arrange` take the root page's `type: shell` panes into the registry (`type: shell`, `label` = title), move any that are not in the workers group into it and delete vanished ones from the registry; when the registered set differs from what this daemon last listed, the history page is rewritten (`history_slots` lists them at the end via `shell_slots()`, slot = pane id; `start_all` writes the history page before `arrange` adopts, so the first tick always rewrites it once); `arrange` orders them after the roster workers. A bash opened while no fleet runs still lands on the root page and is adopted when the next root fleet starts. It is **not a worker**: it takes no slot, receives no dispatch, does not count toward `--max-busy`, is not in the roster, and a sub-fleet's manager never adopts it (`adopt_shells` returns at once when bound to a page). The card's `×` close semantics are unchanged (it asks first when busy). Implementation: Rust `server/view.rs` (`new_bash` / `show_bash` / `fleet_view` / `sync_bash`), `render/draw.rs` (the placeholder screen steps aside when a bash card is selected); Python `backends/fleetmux.py` (`adopt_shells` / `shell_slots` / `arrange` / `_kill_idle_shell`, which deletes the registration on close), `fleetmux_sidebar.py`, `fleet.history_slots`.
- **Sidebar bash pages close with the fleet**: a bash (`type: shell`; a tab in the root page's worker area, registered by the root fleet but not a worker) is closed together with the root fleet (`close_panes` / `close_all`, the same path as sidebar × ↺ and lifecycle close) as long as the shell sits at its prompt (pane record `fg_pgid` = `pgid`), and is deleted from the registry; one with a foreground program running, or on an old server that does not report `fg_pgid`, is left in place together with the server, and the close result line names the pages left; when no page remains, `close_all` stops the server as usual. When the server stays because of a busy bash page, the daemon no longer pushes the sidebar, so the closer itself moves this run's card out of running / paused / stopping (a resumable run stopped at its deadline writes a `closed` card and kv `restorable` as on the sidebar × path, showing `到点停 · 可恢复` (stopped at deadline, resumable) and ↺; others such as `fleet.sh stop` write `stopped` / `已停止` (stopped), with no × and no ↺; sub-fleet cards write `已收回 · 随根关闭` (reclaimed, closed with the root)); otherwise idle reclaim (§13.4) would never let the server go. Reclaiming a sub-fleet never touches bash pages. `pane.kill` (the bash card's ×, the same escalation path as a forced `server.shutdown`) also sends the same signal to the process group of the PTY's foreground job, so a foreground job that ignores SIGHUP is no longer left orphaned; background jobs (`cmd &`), a shell exiting by itself and `fleetmux reap` after a crash are excluded (PROTOCOL.md §4.13).
- **Limitations**:
  - No embedded browser.
- **Verification scope**:
  - In the fleet: `scripts/fleetmux_gate.sh m0` / `m12` both went green (m12 last on the fixed fix-m1-tabs commit `def90a63`, on a Mac). They run all cargo tests plus the relevant pytest subset; after B25 merged, only `m3` is used.
  - External acceptance: the operator ran the M0–M2 real-machine acceptance on a Mac and a Linux VM at the fixed commit `64f7be96` and reported "M0–M2 Mac/VM acceptance passed". The three perf items (server CPU with 8 panes flooding output, idle CPU with no wakeups, memory for 10,000 lines of scrollback) met target on both machines; `htop` end to end and the logind `KillUserProcesses` warning ran green for real on the VM.
  - New tab style of fix-m1-tabs: the operator re-checked 7 items with a real mouse in cmux and WezTerm on the release built from `b1de5637`; all passed.
  - Replay snapshots of real CLI recordings (B24): 9 recordings were redacted under the operator's conditions and imported into `rust/fixtures/recordings/`; the targeted `replay` tests are green.
  - **Not done yet**: the full `m3` gate and the final review.

### 33.7 Sends into the Claude editor must be verified

Requirement: what the fleet types into the Claude Code input box must equal the verified body and must not be concatenated with an old draft; if it cannot be verified, do not press Enter and hold explicitly. This overrides the Claude-typing part of §33's "backend behavior unchanged" and tightens the typed channel of §11 / §28; it does not change the official CLI, hook delivery, Redis, generations, leases or the result acceptance protocol.

- **Incident mechanism**: the three backends' `send_prompt_to_idle` used to only paste, wait a fixed time and press Enter; the return value was a transport ACK, with no check of idle, an empty editor, the body or residue after submission. The pre-key / pre-paste real-machine captures already showed the old startup request still in the input box, with `running Stop hooks`; the registered text was a 373-character old prefix + a 458-character new body = 831 characters. A successful `C-u` only means the key API succeeded; per [Claude's official docs](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/interactive-mode#text-editing) it deletes only to the start of the current line, not the whole buffer. The earlier r6 lacked pre-paste evidence; the new evidence proves residue before sending, but cannot tell whether earlier it came from an unconsumed Enter, an aborted submission or a restored rejected prompt. `promptKept:false` or after-the-fact screens must not be taken as causal proof for this step.
- **Shared implementation**: `claude_input.py` is called by the cmux / wezterm / fleetmux `send_prompt_to_idle` and recognizes Claude by the `cli_type=claude` registered at spawn (including adopt and raw `/clear`); non-Claude CLIs keep the original behavior. Sends to the same registry and the same pane are serialized by a file lock, so senders across managers / processes cannot interleave either. Ordinary Claude bodies are forced through bracketed paste; only the original `/clear` stays raw. The first startup prompt still goes through CLI argv; the Claude worker's Stop mailbox, the Codex queue and the Pi bridge are unchanged.
- **Order**: full bottom input box + recognized idle state → if non-empty, a single `Ctrl+C` → read an empty box twice in a row → paste only (no Enter) → wait for rendering, verify the body twice in a row (see below) → one Enter → verify the draft is gone. The whole-prompt clearing semantics of `Ctrl+C` come from [the official General controls](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/interactive-mode#general-controls); it is not sent while a run / Stop hook / unknown screen is present, nor on an empty box, to avoid interrupting a turn or exiting on a second Ctrl+C. The screen is not used to decide turn end or settlement.
- **Failure**: if any step (clear, paste, screen read, body comparison, Enter, draft gone) is not confirmed, it returns False and `last_error` names the stage with `Claude input held:`; paste/Enter are not retried. With a draft present and idle still confirmable, it tries one whole-buffer clear and verifies it; when busy or unreadable it does not press keys blindly and reports `editor cleanup unconfirmed, inspect pane`. `RuntimeV2.type_into` passes the reason to the original hold (lease uncertain, pause); a failed startup nudge send also holds at once instead of ignoring the bool; `/clear` likewise keeps the detailed reason. An operator driver that calls a backend directly must check False/last_error and must not add an Enter; backends themselves do not connect to Redis. A request registered after being intercepted by a hook may still be running; a False return does not mean it was not accepted.
- **Verification scope (corrected per P1-01 to P1-04)**: replaces the earlier rule "multi-line, wrapped and collapsed pastes are always held". Before pasting, the full input box must still be confirmed idle and empty; after pasting, the whole box text and the intended body are compared with all whitespace removed from both, or, when the box holds only one `[Pasted text #N +k lines]` placeholder, this paste counts as verified. The pasted body itself is not rewritten. Each footer line is split on runs of at least two spaces, only the left segment is matched, and the `· ← N agent(s)` / `· ← for agents` suffixes are accepted; when the body starts with `/`, command-menu lines and indented continuation lines may stand in for the footer. Trailing whitespace, control/zero-width characters and unknown screens are still rejected. Terminal text cannot prove that logical line breaks and whitespace match exactly; the lock only constrains fleet senders, and races with manual keystrokes / third parties between the last screen read and Enter still need real-machine verification; the terminal interface has no atomic compare-and-submit.
- **Verification boundary**: new fake-editor tests went red then green (including residue concatenation in all three real backend methods, false success from key ACKs, restore after submission, error cleanup, concurrency, busy and invisible-screen rejection), and a test-private Redis verifies hold/uncertain/audit, no retry and the startup nudge. No real fleet was started/stopped, and no real Claude TUI acceptance, independent review, merge, push or deployment was done. Still to test on the real TUI: multi-line clearing with Ctrl+C on the actual version, input box/render timing, editor changes after hook interception, repeated manual input registering the body verbatim, submitting long/multi-line input and collapsed pastes, footers with status/agent suffixes, slash-menu recognition.

## 34. Mid-turn rotation (daemon probe + forced auto-handoff prompt)

Requirement: every mode must be able to auto handoff; it is basic functionality. Keep it simple: the daemon runs a probe, controls every session window, and forces each worker (Claude Code, Codex, pi and the like) to run the auto-handoff prompt. This overturns the first-line principle of `pi_extension_v2.ts`, "agent_settled only reports; it never requests continuation", and the end of §32, "Known boundary: still no rotation in the middle of a long turn" — both assumed rotation can only happen between turns; from this section on, the daemon also forces rotation while a turn is running.

- **Probe**: a separate thread inside the daemon; loop interval = max(1 s, time taken to check all busy slots this round), single-threaded and sequential, never overlapping or piling up. It looks only at busy slots (busy, lease received, the in-flight message not itself a handoff delivery); idle slots still belong to §32 `idle_rotate`. Each round it first `stat`s the slot's current-generation session file (mtime, size) and skips it if unchanged; if changed it reads only the tail (≤64KB), never the whole file.
- **Usage measure**: pi slots use pi's own algorithm — `calculateContextTokens` plus trailing messages not yet counted by a model call, estimated as `ceil(chars/4)` (UTF-16 length); the probe copies this measure rather than inventing its own. After a compaction and before the next model response, usage is unknown (not 0; the last value read is kept). Claude slots use the existing `claude_transcript_usage`, Codex slots the existing `handoff.read_usage`.
- **Mid-turn line**: `midturn_ratio` defaults to 0.8. When usage ≥ 0.8 × window and this generation has not been nudged yet → the daemon bypasses the delivery gate, pastes a fixed one-line prompt into the slot's window and presses Enter; the prompt carries an `fx-<16hex>` id (the `midturn_id`), once per generation.
- **Turn settlement**: when that turn ends with the message carrying the mid-turn mark and no valid formal report → record `handoff_split`: not complete, not BLOCKED, no attempt counted; the continuation message goes to the head of the queue (FIFO head), rotation follows the original §4 flow (the model's draft if the model edited it, otherwise the skeleton), and the successor generation continues the same dispatch.
- **Fallback**: 300 s after the prompt with the turn not ended → send it once more; another 300 s still not ended → hand over to the existing `Watchdog.rotate`, which kills the window, does a machine-skeleton handoff + starts a new generation in place + continues.
- **Limit**: one dispatch is split at most 3 times (`midturn_splits` is counted on the message); past that it is not split again but turned BLOCKED with `请拆小` (please split it smaller) for the supervisor / service to handle.
- **Clock**: a service request's timeout clock pauses during mid-turn rotation; when the split chain ends (settled, turned BLOCKED, canceled) the paused duration is added back on resume; the task's overall `hours` budget is unaffected, and the hard budget does not pause (§17 / §23).
- **Three bypasses**: the mechanism must get past three gates that would otherwise block mid-turn input — the pi extension's `input` hook lets mid-turn prompts with the `fx-` prefix through (it would otherwise swallow them or turn them into operator_input); the Claude supervisor's `UserPromptSubmit` hook lets registered mid-turn prompts through; Codex `codex_queue.bound` is relaxed to "a second user message equal to the registered mid-turn prompt still counts as this generation".

**Known boundary**: the probe can see tool results that are appended but not yet sent to the model (the pi measure estimates them as ceil(chars/4)), but the CLI checks for self-compaction within milliseconds of appending a tool result, faster than the 1-second poll — a single large batch of tool results can push usage from below the mid-turn line straight past the CLI's self-compaction point in one step, before the probe can send its prompt. With a 200k window (pi DeepSeek) the 0.8 mid-turn line leaves only 23,616 tokens before the self-compaction point, so finishing the handoff before compaction is not guaranteed; this risk was accepted and 0.8 stays (whether to lower it separately for windows of 200k and below is left as a separate decision). pi's WorkBuddy DeepSeek now declares a 400k window; measured through the gateway it reaches 1M, with a single 612,564-token request passing; OpenCode free DeepSeek measured 185k passing and 260k rejected, a real limit of about 200k (32k of it reserved for output), so the 168k (= 200k − 32k) declared by the pi extension is correct and unchanged.

**Verification**: the design draft `docs/midturn-handoff-design.md` (r3) §7 lists the full unit-test and end-to-end smoke plan (new files `tests/test_midturn_v2.py` + `scripts/smoke_midturn.sh`); not yet implemented, run, reviewed, merged into the live branch or deployed.

## 35. Restart the session when the link breaks

Requirement: "communication lost, execution state unconfirmed; waiting for reconciliation" is a disconnection; on detecting a broken link the fleet should restart that session according to the actual situation.

- **First tell whether it is a broken link**: bridge faults are of two kinds. One only restates a slot hold the daemon has already made (`daemon holds execution` / `settlement unconfirmed/held` / `recovered PEL may have executed` / a manual-input collision / opencode hold, `runtime_v2.BRIDGE_HELD_REASONS`); the pane shows `已被 fleet 扣下：<daemon 写在消息上的原因>` (held by the fleet: <reason the daemon wrote on the message>) and the continuation limit shows `已达续做上限，等监工拆小` (continuation limit reached, waiting for the supervisor to split it); when the daemon gets such a fault and the slot is already held, it only audits, without holding the slot a second time or pausing the whole fleet. Everything else is a broken link (the bridge's own Redis error, child process exit, the lease cannot be renewed); the pane shows `通信中断；fleet 将自动重启本会话` (communication lost; the fleet will restart this session automatically). In an earlier incident, two `通信中断` (communication lost) messages in a Feishu fleet were in fact a slot hold caused by a reviewer's bad report BLOCKing the whole task (gap G1), not a broken link.
- **How the daemon detects a broken link**: ① busy slot: the lease `lease_until` has passed while the dispatch `deadline_at` has not (the bridge stopped renewing) → broken link; both passed is the original timeout. ② idle slot: the bridge's lease-renewal thread writes a heartbeat key `<prefix>:hb:<slot>` each round (value = generation, TTL 3 periods); a heartbeat of this generation that appeared and then vanished → broken link. A bridge that never wrote a heartbeat (old code) is not judged. ③ a link-type fault reported by the bridge itself. All three go to `link_lost`: hold only this slot (`pause=False`) and mark `link_lost=1`. This applies only to bridge slots (pi / opencode); Claude / Codex leases are renewed by the daemon and are excluded; the advisor slot is still restarted in place per §27.
- **Restart**: each watchdog round first handles slots with `link_lost=1`, reusing the frozen-rotation `Watchdog.rotate`: save the screen, skeleton handoff note + terminal snapshot, start the next generation, kill the old session, `confirm_retired` cancels the original dispatch. The original dispatch is **not rerun automatically** (v2 principle unchanged): in task mode the supervisor receives "FROZEN … bridge link lost, canceled and not rerun; check the workspace before dispatching again", in service mode the request is recorded as stalled; a dispatch carrying the mid-turn rotation mark continues per §34. No restart while paused; it happens after resume.
- **After the machine slept** (laptop lid closed): the bridges were suspended with the daemon and their heartbeat keys expired during the gap, which is not a lost link. When a tick finds the previous one more than two bridge renew periods ago (period = `min(10, lease_ms/3000)` s), `RuntimeV2.daemon_gap` gives the bridges three periods (`hb_grace_until`) to beat again before `heartbeat_check` judges ②; a bridge still silent after that is a lost link as before.
- **A startup that fails on the network is retried, not blocked**: a startup event with `failed` true, not aborted, not a wall, whose error is a transport error (`runtime_v2.startup_offline`: `fetch failed`, `ECONNRESET`, `ENOTFOUND`, `EAI_AGAIN`, `socket hang up` …) leaves the slot `dead` without pausing the run and schedules its next generation (`Fleet.schedule_startup_retry`, backoff 30 / 60 / 120 / 300 s, the last repeating). `Fleet.startup_retry_tick` starts it through `wall_rotate` when due, the run is not paused and the network answers (`Fleet.network_up`: a TCP connect to any of `NETWORK_PROBES`); while it does not, the retry is pushed back 30 s with no attempt spent and no new generation, so the short dark wakes of a closed laptop, during which the daemon still ticks, use up nothing. READY resets the count. After 24 failures with the network up the old stop applies (`blocked`, service run paused). Any other startup failure blocks as before. Incident: a laptop woke briefly with Wi-Fi down; the daemon read the expired heartbeat as a lost link and restarted the pi supervisor, whose startup said `fetch failed`; the run paused and a channel message hours after the lid was opened got `supervisor is not ready (state dead)`.
- **No restart while offline**: `Watchdog.link_restart` asks `Fleet.network_up` first (an offline answer is reused for 10 s). While the network does not answer, the slot stays held with `link_offline=1` (one audit `link.restart_waits_network`), no restart is counted and no generation is started; the restart runs on the first round the network answers. Together with the startup retry above, every new generation the daemon starts after a wake waits for the network.
- **Limit**: at most 3 restarts of one slot within 30 minutes (`watchdog.LINK_MAX_RESTARTS / LINK_WINDOW_S`); a further break holds the slot and pauses dispatch, reporting "check Redis / the bridge before resuming"; a failed restart is marked `link_lost=failed` and is not retried every round.

## 36. The daemon and the supervisor watch each other

Requirement: when the daemon exits and the supervisor finds it cannot talk to the daemon, the supervisor brings the daemon back up; conversely, when the supervisor is stuck, the daemon brings the supervisor back. The two heartbeat each other; whoever finds the other gone checks the logs and how long it was stuck, and resumes from the logs. When the supervisor sees no heartbeat for 2 minutes (10 would also do), it runs a command from its loop, and that command is written in AGENTS.md. Origin: in an incident, a fleetmux run's daemon process vanished together with its control tab while Redis and every agent pane stayed alive; nobody noticed for about 6 minutes, a task event went unhandled, and a manual `start --resume` recovered it. Why the daemon exited was never found (the control tab was closed and no stdout was left). Per §1, the whole mechanism lives inside the fleet: no crontab / launchd, no extra resident process.

- **The supervisor watches the daemon (pi supervisor)**: the fleet's own pi extension (`pi_extension_v2.ts`, already installed in the supervisor session) starts a timer after READY and sends `daemon_beat` through its own bridge every `check_interval_s`. The bridge (`agent_bridge.daemon_beat`) reads Redis and `<run>/daemon.lock` directly, not through the daemon, so it still answers when the daemon is dead and the bridge has already faulted for lack of settlement. Fresh heartbeat → do nothing (no turn, no tokens spent); heartbeat older than `stale_after_s` → only then probe daemon.lock (probing takes the lock for an instant, and a daemon that is starting could fail to start if it hits it, so the probe runs only on a suspected outage), and use `runtime_v2.daemon_outage` to take facts from the logs: last heartbeat, the daemon's last audit entry (within 2 s after the heartbeat, skipping `renewed / received / slot_* / turn.* / hook.*`, which bridges / hooks keep writing), the number of events in the stream that the daemon's consumer group has not fetched / acked, held / expired slots. No daemon holding the lock, run not finished (no result.json; in task mode the task still running), session idle, not in a dispatch turn, and `realert_after_s` passed since the last alert → the extension starts **one** supervisor turn with `sendMessage(customType fleet-daemon-watchdog, triggerTurn)` whose body is one line of `[Daemon watchdog] …` facts. This turn is a manual turn (not a dispatch; it cannot deliver a fleet result). Daemon alive but heartbeat stopped (hung) → no turn, nothing killed. The decision is the pure function `daemonWatchdog`, with the contract test `tests/test_pi_daemon_watch.mjs`.
- **The restart command is in AGENTS.md**: the v2 main fleet's supervisor AGENTS.md ends with a generated "Daemon down" section (`fleet.daemon_down_section`) that holds this run's verbatim, paste-ready command: `env FLEETMUX_HOME=… FLEETMUX_STATE=… FLEETMUX_CONFIG=… CMUX_BIN=… <python> <fleet.py> start --allow-development-v2 --resume <run>` (the environment comes from the locations pinned in config.json; `private_config: none` is not written), plus `status` and `logs … | tail -n 30` with the same prefix. Steps: check the logs and `daemon.heartbeat_age_s` → stop if the heartbeat has recovered → first write one line in PROGRESS.md (the resumed daemon will rotate the current supervisor to the next generation within seconds) → run the restart command once (with a live daemon it only prints nothing to resume) → check the heartbeat again. Forbidden: running it a second time for the same outage, starting the daemon any other way, stopping / killing any process, tab or Redis; if the command fails (e.g. sandbox refusal), do not retry, but hand the command and the error to a human in the reply. This section is the only exception to "do not call the fleet CLI".
- **Briefing after resume**: `resume_run` computes the same `daemon_outage` before clearing slots: the `daemon.resumed` audit carries `down_s / last_event / pending_events / held`, and the task-mode `[Resumed task …]` briefing gains a line "Daemon outage (from the logs): last heartbeat …, down N s …; M events were waiting and are processed now; held/expired deliveries found: …". A manual `start --resume` gets the same line.
- **The daemon watches the supervisor**: ① stuck mid-turn (in flight) → §24 watchdog frozen → `rotate` (existing; applies to the supervisor slot too). ② the pi supervisor process is gone → the bridge heartbeat vanishes → §35 `link_lost` → `link_restart` → `rotate` (existing). ③ **New**: after a Claude / Codex supervisor CLI exits, only a shell is left in its tab (`auto_close=False`), with neither a heartbeat nor work in flight → the 30-second liveness check calls `has_cli_process` on the supervisor; two consecutive misses of the CLI process (`cli_missing` counter, reset when seen) → `link_lost('supervisor', 'supervisor CLI exited …')` → the same restart path and limit as ② (3 per 30 minutes). Only an idle / busy supervisor that is READY and not adopted is judged; an idle supervisor whose process is alive is never restarted for "no activity" (only an in-flight turn is judged stuck). ④ Every supervisor rotation (frozen and broken link, not §34 mid-turn rotation) takes a log summary in `rotate` (`Watchdog.revive_summary`): last activity time and seconds stuck, the in-flight message and whether it was applied, the daemon's last 3 committed supervisor decisions; the summary is appended to the end of the new generation's first message (the takeover prompt) and audited as `supervisor.revived` (`stuck_since / stuck_s / in_flight / applied / decisions`), recorded alongside `watch.frozen_restarted`. If the supervisor tab is gone entirely, it is still §24 hold + pause (no evidence of a stop; original design unchanged).
- **Configuration**: `fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml#daemon_watch`: `check_interval_s` (default 120), `stale_after_s` (120), `realert_after_s` (600), in seconds and > 0; a private fleet.yaml can override the whole block. The daemon reads it once when it starts a supervisor generation and passes it to the extension via the environment variable `FLEETMUX_DAEMON_WATCH` (only to the main fleet's pi supervisor; a sub-fleet's daemon is resumed by its parent fleet, §29, so it is not given).
- **Known gaps**: Claude / Codex supervisor sessions have no periodic mechanism to hook into (hooks such as `claude-rewake` are for workers only), so they do not notice a daemon outage by themselves and follow that AGENTS.md section only when a human brings it up; the Codex supervisor runs in a workspace-write sandbox and cannot write the run directory / fleet home, so the restart command will most likely be refused and can only be handed to a human. When Redis is down too, the bridge cannot read the heartbeat and the pi supervisor does not start a turn either. A hung daemon (holding the lock, no heartbeat) can only be handled manually.

## 37. New work in a new session: `dispatch[].fresh`

Problem: when a new task is added, the supervisor does not use a new worker but stuffs the task into an old worker whose context is entirely unrelated, wasting tokens; it should either dispatch to a new worker or close out the old worker that has finished its work. Measured: workers took unrelated packages while carrying 400k–550k tokens of context. This section adds only one dispatch switch and two hints; handoff (§4), generation labels (§3) and the roster (§16) are unchanged.

- **Switch**: a dispatch item may carry `"fresh": true` (default false, booleans only). Task / service decisions, pi's fleet_decision tool (the `dispatch` object in `pi_extension_v2.ts`) and the JSON of Claude / Codex supervisors all use the same key; `parse_decision` / `parse_service_decision` reject non-booleans (the v2 validator calls both of them too).
- **How it rotates**: when the decision lands, only `fresh_pending=1` is set on the slot, and the instruction is queued as usual. `deliver()` sees this mark at the original rotation decision point (after reading usage, before comparing the ratio) and goes to `Fleet.fresh_rotation`: the same order as `finish_rotation` (save the old screen → generation +1, label `ctl-1` → `ctl-1.1`, the old name still routes → start the new generation in place (v2 launch-held first) → take over the old tab position → kill the old session → v2 `confirm_retired` → open the gate), but with **no handoff turn, no handoff note and no relay text in the first message** (it is only told to read AGENTS.md and reply READY). The working directory / worktree, role file and AGENTS.md are untouched; the history page records a generation as usual (`source: fresh`, no handoff note). After READY, the next tick delivers the queued instruction to the new generation. Claude / Codex / pi all take this generic path. Audit `dispatch.fresh`: slot, old→new generation, the dropped context tokens (`dropped_tokens` / `window`), the old transcript.
- **Guards**: target worker not idle (busy / handoff / held / clearing / delegated / starting with work), queued items, an in-flight v2 delivery, `clear_required`, adopted by a sub-fleet, a pending profile swap → **the whole decision is rejected**, and the reason goes through the supervisor's correction channel (like other dispatch rejections, it counts toward the three bad replies). A slot with `delivered=0` (a new session) or with §28 clear-after-each-result is not rotated but delivered to directly (audit `dispatch.fresh_skipped`). Pause and `max_busy` wait per the original rules, and if `deliver()` is not called there is no rotation; while a Codex native sub-agent is running it is deferred.
- **Worker status board**: each line of §32's `worker_board` gains `context used N% (k tokens)` at the end (only for slots that have done work, reading `usage()` live) and `last task: <first line of the last delivery, ≤80 chars>` (v1 `last_msg`; v2 takes the message body of the current `msg_id`; handoff deliveries do not count). service keeps it in the routing message as before; task mode now appends it to each worker report (`Worker status now (state, queue, context, last task)`). No data, no field.
- **Gentle hint, no automatic rotation**: when a dispatch lacks fresh, the target slot has done work and its usage exceeds `fresh_hint_ratio × window` → deliver as usual, and append one line to the end of the supervisor's next message: `[Hint] ctl-1 was at 55% context when it got this dispatch; if the work is unrelated to its last task, add "fresh": true next time.` (stored in `fx:fresh:hints` and cleared after `Fleet.queue` appends it, like advisor notes).
- **Supervisor prompts**: the five supervisor prompts gain a "Fresh session or reuse" subsection after "Adding and retiring workers" (continuing / fixing / retesting its own work → reuse; another package / file / topic → `"fresh": true`; more independent ready work than idle workers → add workers; no work left in a direction → retire), and the reply format gains a `dispatch[].fresh` line; `contracts_supervisor.md` gains a prohibition. The advisor only configures models and does not choose who gets the work; it is unchanged.
- **Configuration**: `fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml#fresh_dispatch.fresh_hint_ratio`, default 0.3, a number in (0, 1]; a private fleet.yaml can override the whole block (`fleet_config.fresh_hint_ratio()`, read live on every dispatch). A bad value does not reject the dispatch; it only records `dispatch.hint_failed` and gives no hint.
- **New session fails to start**: spawn failure (or the v2 new session cannot take over the old tab) → delete the half-started new session, restore the slot's Redis state wholesale to the old generation, and the instruction **stays in the queue for the old session** (not lost, not returned); the supervisor's next message carries a line `[Fresh failed] ctl-1: a clean session did not start (…); your instruction went to the existing session ctl-1.`, audit `dispatch.fresh_failed`. A failure after the old session was already killed (the old session could not be closed) → same as an ordinary rotation: `finish('blocked', … two sessions hold slot …)`.

**Known gaps**: each fresh costs one more CLI cold start (about 10–20 s to READY), during which the slot is `starting` and does not count toward `max_busy` (same as after an ordinary rotation). M items of a contracts task held back by `depends_on` are released later by the daemon without fresh. Under v2, fresh rotation and Claude hook delivery behave as in an ordinary rotation: the old generation's Stop hook dies with the old session, and the new generation uses the hook key of its own generation. A rejection counts as one bad reply; a supervisor that sends fresh to a busy worker three times in a row gets the task judged BLOCKED like any other bad replies (service pauses).

**Verification**: `tests/test_fresh_dispatch.py` (23 items: idle worker rotated cleanly then delivered / Claude worker / busy and queued rejected / new session not rotated / spawn failure falls back to the old session and says so / one-line hint over the ratio / no hint under it / fleet.yaml threshold change and bad value / task and service status boards / service rejection / validator rejects non-booleans / five prompts / v2 branch opens the gate and restores on tab-takeover failure), and the TypeBox contract in `tests/test_pi_decision_schema.py` (task and service both accept `fresh` and reject non-booleans). Not smoke-tested on real cmux / real Redis; not reviewed, merged or deployed.

## 38. Test log of the delivery gate

The dev_task `done_when` gate (`lifecycle_deliver.deliver`) used to put only the **last 1500 characters** of the `test_cmd` output into `rec['tests']['tail']`; the full output lived only in daemon memory and was lost as soon as the gate ended, and timeouts and start failures did not even get those 1500 characters. So in an incident a `tests failed (exit 101)` could not be investigated (the output of the earlier `cargo test` stages was all lost). This section only adds log evidence; verdicts and order are unchanged.

- **Path**: every `test_cmd` run (success, failure, timeout, start failure) writes `<run>/gate/<task_id>-tests.log`; when `deliver()`'s `run` is empty it falls back to the workspace's **parent directory** (`<workspace>.parent/gate/…`, never inside the workspace). An empty `task_id` gives `-tests.log`; the file name is fixed, without a timestamp, and a repeated gate for the same task overwrites it.
- **Header**: the first lines of the log are fixed: `cmd:` (argv), `cwd:`, `started:` (local ISO-8601) and an outcome line — `exit: <code>` / `timeout: <seconds>` / `start failed: <exception>`; then the full `stdout + stderr`.
- **Cap**: when the content exceeds 20 MiB, the **first 1 MiB + last 19 MiB** are kept with a marker line containing `truncated` in between (nothing is dropped silently); capture still uses the original `runner` / `capture_output` semantics; Popen is not changed for this.
- **Timeout**: the partial `stdout` / `stderr` of `TimeoutExpired` are still written to the log (they can be `bytes` even under `text=True`; both are decoded), and `rec['tests']['returncode']` is `None`.
- **Record**: `rec['tests']` is still `{cmd, returncode, tail, log}`; `tail` keeps its original meaning of the **last 1500 characters**, and `log` is an absolute path (`''` if writing failed); the record / `result.json` / Redis `gate:<id>` never hold the full text.
- **problem**: failure is `tests failed (exit N); log: <absolute path>`, timeout is `tests timed out after N s; log: <absolute path>`, start failure is `tests could not start: <exception>; log: <absolute path>`; a failed log write neither raises nor changes the verdict, it only notes `test log not written: …` in problem (on a successful gate too, with `ok` still true). `rec['tests']['log']` is always an absolute path.
- **Audit**: the `tests` of `lifecycle.gate` carries `returncode` / `log` / `tail`, where the audit's `tail` is the **last 20 lines** of those 1500 characters (the 1500 characters in the record are unchanged).
- **Unchanged**: the in-gate order `review → fast_forward_check → tests → merged → enqueued`, fast-forward via CAS, enqueue dedupe, and writing `gate:<id>` only once all stay as before.

## 39. The history page colors follow the terminal background

Origin: a light system appearance + cmux `appearanceMode = dark` gave a dark terminal next to a white history page. The daemon runs in the control tab (a foreground process whose stdin is that pane's tty), so the daemon asks the terminal once, rather than each slot's actor.

- **One query**: at the very start of `Fleet.run()`, `Fleet.capture_terminal_theme()` → `terminal_theme.capture(run_dir)`: in raw mode it writes OSC 11 / OSC 10 queries to `/dev/tty` with a bounded `select` (0.3 s in total), accepts ST or BEL and 8/16-bit components, and afterwards does `TCIOFLUSH` and restores termios with `TCSANOW`.
- **File**: on a reply it writes `<run>/terminal_theme.json` (`{"bg","fg","source":"osc","at"}`, atomic `os.replace`) and audits `theme.captured`; no reply / not a tty / timeout / any error only records `theme.unavailable`, writes no file and never blocks or raises. An old file is kept (it is most likely still right).
- **history**: `history.write_page()` reads that file (strictly `^#[0-9a-f]{6}$`; anything else counts as absent); if present, it injects `<style id="terminal-theme">` after the original `<style>` (same `:root`, overriding `@media (prefers-color-scheme)` by order, including `color-scheme`), picks the dark / light base set by bg luminance and mixes `--panel/--line/--muted` from the terminal bg/fg; without it the page is byte-for-byte as before.
- **Limitation**: if the terminal theme is switched while the fleet runs, the page follows only at the next daemon start (`stop` → `start --resume`, which respawns the control window and queries again); backends that do not answer OSC (such as sub-fleet slots inside tmux) fall back to the current colors automatically. Visual checks on a real machine belong to the delivery gate; this branch has only unit tests.

### 39.1 Lessons from real machines (measured on cmux 0.65.0 + fleetmux b151d9db)

- **Root cause of the white background**: the page honors only `prefers-color-scheme`, which follows the macOS system appearance; cmux's built-in browser with `browserThemeMode = system` renders pages by the system appearance, regardless of cmux's own `appearanceMode = dark`. So "light system + dark cmux" always gives a white background. Do not fix it through cmux browser settings or the system appearance; ask the terminal itself.
- **cmux answers the queries**: it answers OSC 10 / 11 / 12 / 4, in the format `ESC ]11;rgb:1e1e/1e1e/1e1e ESC \` (ST-terminated, 16 bits per component). With an empty ghostty config and `cmux themes` showing `inherit`, this is cmux's built-in default palette: bg `#1e1e1e`, fg `#ffffff`, cursor `#98989d`, ANSI 0–15 = `#1a1a1a #cc372e #26a439 #cdac08 #0869cb #9647bf #479ec2 #98989d #464646 #ff453a #32d74b #ffd60a #0a84ff #bf5af2 #76d6ff #ffffff`.
- **Whoever answers the query is the one followed**: the OSC answer comes from the terminal layer closest to the process. On the cmux backend that is cmux (`#1e1e1e`); on the fleetmux backend it is the fleetmux server's own parser layer (default `#000000` / `#d8d8d8`, `DEFAULT_FG/BG` in `rust/crates/term/src/alacritty.rs`), not the outer cmux. So inside fleetmux the history page follows the fleetmux cell colors; to make it look like cmux, change fleetmux's own palette (branch `feat/fleetmux-cmux-theme`), not this code.
- **How to probe**: the query must run in a real terminal panel (Claude Code / child processes have no tty and cannot ask). Method: `cmux new-workspace --focus false --command "bash -c 'python3 probe.py; sleep 2'"`; the probe writes the query in raw mode, reads with a bounded `select` and writes the result to a file, then `cmux close-workspace --workspace <id> --force` closes it.
- **Do not let tests hang**: the first pty test read the fake terminal without a timeout, and `pytest tests/test_terminal_theme.py` hung for 20 minutes until the watchdog aborted it. Rule: every pty / tty read goes through `select` with a timeout, a single test file finishes within 10 seconds, and one that does not return within 60 seconds is a hang bug to fix first.
- **Acceptance results**: under cmux it got `#1e1e1e` / `#ffffff`, and the history cell matches the terminal cells; under fleetmux gui it got `#000000` / `#d8d8d8`, and the embedded history page is black like the terminal cells. Both are captured only once at daemon start; a theme change mid-run needs a restart to follow.

## 40. Extra writable directories for workers: roster `writable:`

- Origin: the worker role prompt hard-coded "Do not write outside your own directory". Acceptance workers need to write receipts into the acceptance directory and run reproductions on the test VM, and development workers need to write the delivery repo; a strict model (Astra) took this as a developer-level prohibition that neither the role file nor the supervisor's user message could lift, and reported blocked.
- Roster rows gain an optional `writable: [<path>, ...]` (a list of non-empty strings; `host:path` means writing that path on that machine over ssh). It applies only to that worker and appends, after the prohibition line in AGENTS.md, "Exception granted by the roster: you may also write X, Y; everything else outside your directory is still forbidden". Without this key the prompt is unchanged word for word.
- Roster hot reload works as before: a `writable` change takes effect in the slot's next generation (the same path as a role change). Deletions still go to the user under the deletion rules; `writable` does not grant deletion.

## 41. dsh (DeepSeek Harness) workers: dsh-TUI + in-process bridge plugin (v1)

Decisions (from an internal plan): ① the pane runs the third-party dsh-TUI and the fleet loads a bridge plugin into the same process, the same shape as pi, which is not the headless CLI banned by §11 (this replaces v0's "resident ACP session + fleet print-style pane front end"; `dsh_pane.py` is deleted, so there are not two paths); ② dsh workers exist for evaluation against pi workers and are not in the default pairs; ③ the default model is WorkBuddy DeepSeek. The single daemon, private Redis, slot/generation labels, one-page layout, task/service protocol and handoff flow are all unchanged.

- **Shape**: the actor execs `node dsh --profile dsh-tui --patch <model patch> --patch <fleet patch>` (`dsh_adapter.argv`). **argv holds only launcher arguments**: at the first argument the launcher does not recognize (such as `--backend`) it hands that argument and everything after it to the app, and the app's positional argument is a startup prompt — measured: `--backend dsh --patch …` left both patches unloaded and turned the path into a prompt; placed after the patches it added an extra "dsh" prompt turn. So the backend is pinned by `backend: dsh` in the dsh-tui row of the patch (row config beats a remembered /kernel choice), not on argv. The startup prompt goes through the environment variable `FLEETMUX_DSH_STARTUP` (like opencode) and is injected by the plugin with its own message id.
- **Shared profile, per-pane HOME**: dsh-tui is installed once, in a shared DSH_HOME (`start --dsh-home`, default `~/.fleetmux/dsh-home`, recorded as config `dsh_home`); `start` prechecks dsh == 0.2.1-rc.5, node ≥ 22.19 and `@deepseek-harness-tui/dsh-tui` == 0.18.0 in that DSH_HOME's `profiles/dsh-tui`, and refuses otherwise, giving the install command (`DSH_HOME=<dir> dsh plugin --profile dsh-tui add @deepseek-harness-tui/dsh-tui@0.18.0`, about 2.5 minutes); it never installs per run. Each generation's pane HOME = `<run>/dsh-tui-home/<slot>/g<N>`: pre-written `.dsh-tui/onboarding.json {"completed":true,"version":1}` and `.dsh-tui/home.json {"seen":true}`, env `DSH_TUI_NO_LAUNCHPAD=1`, straight into the conversation; from the real HOME only `~/.gitconfig` and `~/.npmrc` are symlinked (only if present; `~/.ssh` deliberately excluded). dsh-TUI's `~/.dsh-tui` state therefore lives entirely in the pane HOME and never touches the user's. The fleet patch moves `session-persistence-jsonl.root`, `storage-json.root` and `dsh-tui-storage-json.root` to `<pane HOME>/dsh/` (measured to work: session logs and storage land in the run, and the shared DSH_HOME no longer grows new sessions; DSH rewrites the empty skeleton `cordis.yml` in the profile at every start and leaves `cordis.patch.yml` alone). Other env: `DSH_HOME` (shared), `DSH_AGENTS_HOME` (an empty directory per generation), `DSH_TELEMETRY_MODE=DISABLED`, `DSH_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1`, `DSH_PERMISSION_MODE=workspace-write`. dsh-tui's npm new-version check at startup only shows a notice, which is accepted (a fake `NPM_CONFIG_REGISTRY` would also break the worker's npm, so it is not used).
- **Single source of models = pi's `models.json`**: `dsh_adapter.model_patch` takes the `dsh_model` entry (with `fleet.yaml#pi_context` applied) and generates the `llm-pi-ai` route (baseURL / api / this one model), `agent-default-model`, and the dsh-tui row `{provider, model, backend: dsh, upstreamRetry: false}` (the patch replaces the config row wholesale, so it writes the full provider + model pin; a half pin is ignored; the TUI is not allowed to seed a retry policy into the route). models.json is read before HOME is switched. The key reaches the dsh-TUI process only through the actor environment (`apiKeyEnv: FLEETMUX_DSH_API_KEY`), never in the patch, config.json, the spawn command line or the argv record, and the plugin strips it when starting the bridge child process. **The default model** is the explicit config `fleet.yaml#dsh.default_model` (`workbuddy-ds/workbuddy-ai/deepseek-v4.1-flash-sg`, through the local gateway): without `--dsh-model`, `start` takes it, prints one line to stderr and records it as `dsh_model` in the run config; `--dsh-model flashnext/…` still works. The `start` precheck also checks that the provider endpoint `GET <baseUrl>/models` lists this model id (with the key as bearer), and refuses if it is not listed or there is no answer. Model card `workbuddy-ds-dsh` (context 400000, `max_concurrent: 2`) sits beside `flashnext-dsh`.
- **Bridge plugin `fleetmux/dsh_bridge.mjs`** (a cordis plugin row; `createRequire(ctx.baseUrl)` gets `defineTool` from `@deepseek-ai/dsh-tools`; a semantic port of `pi_extension_v2.ts`): starts `agent_bridge.py` over a pipe, with the same frames as pi / opencode; binds the root agent of `ctx.agents.roots()` / `agent/created` (the agent id is the DSH session id, which is the bridge's `thread`); writes its own transcript `<run>/dsh-sessions/<slot>/g<N>/<session>.jsonl` (one line per session event, for `event()` validation and the watchdog's mtime signal; DSH's own zstd session log is not used as a signal); logs to `<pane HOME>/dsh_bridge.log`, never to stdout (the terminal belongs to the TUI).
  - One turn: `agent.followup(frozen user message, id generated by the plugin)`; `agent/inbox/claimed {message.id, turn}` matches the id, and `turn/end` of `session/event` (`reason.kind` ∈ completed / aborted / error / blocked / max-tokens) settles it: completed → `settled`; aborted → `aborted`; others → first report `candidate {tool: model, error}` (the wall check reads it), then `failed`. Text = the text segments of this turn's `assistant/message`, joined with a newline per segment (measured: the TUI's "⏵ title" narration and the answer are two segments, and joining them directly glues READY onto the narration so it fails the READY check). Usage = `ctx.get('tokenMeter').measure(session).totalTokens` (synchronous); the window is models.json's `contextWindow`, reported with every frame as `ctx_used / ctx_window`. Model lock: in a fleet turn, if the provider/model of `assistant/message.source` is not `dsh_model` → that turn fails.
  - Report: the `fleet_result` tool (`defineTool`, parameter `{result: {type: object, required: true, additionalProperties: true}}` — object parameters must state additionalProperties) accepts candidates only in the turn of this delivery and not for handoff deliveries; task mode checks locally that done / evidence are arrays of non-empty strings and not_done / blocked are strings, service checks the request's required / allowed keys; on accept it calls `exec.concludeTurn()`. v0's dsh-only branch "final text → candidate" is removed; the role file uses the bridge's `_v2` (fleet_result) version, with the same correction text as pi / opencode.
  - Manual turns (as in pi / opencode): a turn that claimed another id is a manual turn (`availability idle:false`, back to idle at the end, no settlement); a delivery arriving during a manual turn gets `reject_delivery`; a turn that claimed both our id and another id → `hold` (the bridge accepts `hold` for dsh too; `dsh hold` counts among the faults that only restate a slot hold, not a broken link); a mix-in during the startup turn fails the startup. After the daemon judges a startup answer not READY, the next turn with another id (the correction the daemon types into the pane) is treated as the new startup turn.
  - Session switch: a new root agent appearing after binding (`/new`, session restore) — per pi's rule "a bound session is never replaced without retirement": no rebinding; the plugin reports an error and stops the bridge, and the daemon, seeing the heartbeat / link lost (§35), starts the next generation in the same slot.
  - Permissions: an `approval/request` waterfall (`{prepend: true}`, ahead of the TUI's own responders; request = `{agent, toolName, callId, reason, displayReason, signal}`; the command / path are taken from the arguments of the preceding tool-call with the same callId, and `command / file_path / path / pattern / url` go into `resources`) → an opencode-shaped `permission` frame with the same deletion classification (`permissionClass` in `opencode_plugin/lib.js`; the bridge's second check can only be stricter). It also calls `next()` so that the TUI's own dialog asks the person at the pane. Deletion / move classes wait only for that person (a supervisor `permission_reply` is refused with `permission_refused held_for_user`); for other classes, whichever arrives first wins, the person or the supervisor ruling (`permission_reply` once → allowed-once, reject → rejected). No self-approval on timeout while waiting; the outcome is reported as `permission_resolved` (`decided_by` fleet / tui_user / none). The daemon side (`prompt_channel=dsh`, cards, the watchdog pressing no keys) follows v0.
- **Registration** (unchanged from v0): `worker_cli: dsh`, worker only, transport v2 only; `is_bridge` includes dsh; default `max_busy` 2; one `pi+dsh` task row in the combination table (status not changed in v1, pending a decision); `transport_inventory` recognizes dsh processes by `--patch <run>/dsh-tui-home/…`.
- **Not done**: a dsh supervisor; mid-turn rotation (the mid-turn prompt the daemon types into the pane is a manual input in dsh-TUI and would count as a mix-in; when the probe sees dsh it only records a warn + card, and rotation happens only at turn boundaries); resuming a session (rotation = new session + handoff note); joining the default pairs; `deps.yaml` gets no dsh entry (the version gate is in the `start` precheck).
- **Verification (not merged, not deployed)**: `tests/test_dsh_worker.py` (two patches / no key / pane HOME and symlinks / env / argv / version and dsh-tui gates / endpoint gate / default model / model card / actor / fleet_result role / bridge pass-through and hold), `tests/test_dsh_bridge_plugin.py` (a JS fake cordis ctx + fake bridge drive the real plugin: startup and delivery id matching, fleet_result, the four turn outcomes, model lock, handoff turn, manual turn and delivery rejection, mix-in hold, deletion-class permissions handed to the person / others ruled by the supervisor, a new root agent retires the bridge, startup correction turn), `tests/test_dsh_daemon.py` (private Redis). Real smoke tests are recorded in the internal plan.
- **Known risks**: dsh is a release candidate, and the dsh-tui 0.18.0 plugin surface (`agent/inbox/claimed`, the `approval/request` waterfall, `tokenMeter`) was all found by measurement; before upgrading, rerun the internal plan's probes on a development machine, then change `dsh_adapter.VERSION / TUI_VERSION`; if the supervisor rules first on a non-deletion permission, the TUI's own dialog does not close by itself (it only honors the request's abort signal) and stays in the pane until someone presses a key — which then no longer takes effect; `workspace-write` only governs files and does not block network access; `rm -rf` inside the worker directory runs directly under `workspace-write` with no permission request (weaker than opencode); the preset brings its own instruction loader, and v0's line pinning the project root to the worker directory has no effect in dsh-tui (dsh-tui disables the host row), so a worker directory inside a git repo reads the repo root's AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md; a sub-agent's permission request whose tool-call arguments cannot be found is treated as a deletion class and handed to the person; it competes with pi workers for the same endpoint.

## 42. Development-flow role pack devflow: tests first, interfaces frozen first

Requirement: "write the tests first and the interfaces and constraints follow; make this development flow a standard at the supervisor". This section adds an **optional role pack**. It does not change the base `supervisor.md` / `worker.md`, the single daemon, the task/service protocol or the slot/generation identity; a run without `--roles` behaves as before.

- Location: `fleetmux/agents/devflow/` (`supervisor.md` is appended to the supervisor via `--roles <that directory>`; `worker.md` is appended to each development worker via the `role:` column of `--roster`; `roster.example.yaml` is an example roster).
- Flow: round 0 dispatches only the `contract` worker, which delivers the skeleton (real signatures, bodies raise NotImplementedError) + the key table (exactly one writer per key/field) + the contract tests and one end-to-end test without test doubles + the open conventions (for structural ones it picks a conservative answer and writes it into the contract) + `phase0_check.txt`; only after the supervisor greps `IMPORT_OR_SKIP: 0` and `ROWS_WITHOUT_WRITER: 0` and the gate passes are the implementations dispatched in parallel. An implementer fills only the bodies of its own functions; it does not change signatures, the key table or the contract tests and never invents an interface. A gap is written as `接口缺口` (interface gap), `contract` bumps the version and the work is dispatched again. One file goes to one worker per round. The REPORT carries a `CONTRACT_TESTS:` line, and skipped counts as failed. Before completion, all candidates are stacked and the end-to-end test is run once.
- Why: when an external project ran M1/M2/M3 with several workers in parallel, it got fields that "only said who reads them, nobody wrote them", two pieces taking opposite answers to the same convention, tests silently skipped when a dependency was missing, and workers each assuming an interface and redoing work repeatedly.
- Boundary: the gate greps marker lines, which proves "the marker was written", not that the counts are right. Contract tests catch names and shapes, not semantics such as timing or units; semantics are unified up front in `decisions.md`. No acceptance on a real fleet run yet.

## 43. No global request deadline; an expiry holds only its slot; a frozen session resumes first

Requirement: a supervisor check that is not finished but healthy should just keep waiting; remove the 900 s timeout that froze the whole fleet; resuming the old session is acceptable.

Cause: the `request_timeout` default of 900 s came from ff53283 with no recorded reason. Health already has its own owners: the three watchdog signals (§24 transcript / screen / process-tree CPU), the bridge heartbeat and link loss (§35), StopFailure (§24), and the tab / process liveness checks. A wall-clock request deadline is a duplicate health check; it only makes sense when the caller itself needs a fresh answer (for example one review caller passes `timeout_s: 120` per request, evermem passes `timeout_s: 900` per request). And on expiry `expire` called the generic `hold()`, whose default is `pause=True` (a side effect since 8c9f58d): the whole service fleet stopped dispatching, queued requests timed out one after another, the supervisor got no messages, nothing resumed automatically, and only a manual `resume` / `start --resume` helped (in one incident a service sub-fleet stood still for a week this way). This section changes three things on top of the §5 request protocol, §7 failure semantics, §17 replay and §24 watchdog; the single daemon, the task / service protocol, the result states and the slot / generation identity are unchanged.

- **No global deadline**: `DEFAULTS['request_timeout'] = None`, and `start --request-timeout` sets nothing by default. A request without `timeout_s` never expires by wall clock. An explicit `--request-timeout N` and the named sub-fleet entry `timeouts.request` (§29) are still the run's default deadline; a request's own `timeout_s` (10-86400) is unchanged. Every reader goes through `fleet.request_limit(spec, cfg)` (None = no deadline): v2 `expire`, v1 `expire_requests`, `midturn_order`; a sub-fleet entry without `timeouts.request` takes `DEFAULTS`, i.e. None.
  - **Request messages carry no bus deadline**: the `deadline_at` of a service request's messages (the one dispatched to a worker and the one handed to the supervisor for routing) is always `runtime_v2.NO_DEADLINE_MS` (2100-01-01 in milliseconds, 13 digits, still an integer under Redis Lua `%.14g`). The request's own deadline is enforced only by `expire()` scanning `fx:reqq`. Before, message deadline = request deadline, so at expiry three places could hold the slot and pause the fleet before `expire` did: the tick's "lease/delivery deadline" branch, the late check in `settle`, and lease renewal in `renew_tui`; now they fire only when the lease itself expires (original semantics unchanged). Messages still queued for a request that is already terminal are dropped by the "already terminal" check in `deliver()` (existing), no longer by a deadline at offer time. The control details show this constant as the `deadline` of such messages.
  - **While paused**: a request with a deadline keeps counting and goes `timeout` on expiry; one without waits in the queue for the resume. From now on §7 "requests keep queueing and still time out" and the README's "the caller never waits forever" hold only for requests with a deadline; a caller that needs an upper bound passes its own `timeout_s`.
- **Expiry = a stale answer, not a failure; it never pauses the fleet**:
  - Only the worker slot that is executing it is held: `hold(pause=False)` (the same call as the §24 lease expiry and §35 link loss), `timeout` is published as before, and everything else dispatches normally; the supervisor keeps routing new requests to idle workers. A request whose `to` names that slot waits in its mailbox until the slot is released (or expires on its own deadline). A held slot still counts toward `max_busy` (as with StopFailure).
  - The supervisor is never held for this: a request that expires while the supervisor is routing it → only `timeout` is published; the supervisor's turn ends and commits normally, and its dispatch for that request is skipped because the request is no longer in routing (`dispatch.unknown_request`, existing).
  - A late answer: `fx:result:<id>` is still the first result; its state does not change and the late answer is not used. The answer text goes into the audit `result.late` (`request_id`, slot, `label`, `detail.public_status` and `detail.reply`: a JSON object if it parses as one, otherwise `{raw: last 4000 chars}`). Official Claude hook slot: the turn of the same session ends after the hold → `turn_ended` carries the turn text → `release_held` puts the slot back to idle (§24 hold exit, existing) and records `result.late`. pi / bridge slot: a late `settled` still only records `settle.late` plus `result.late`; **the slot has no hold exit** (§24 known gap ⑦-4). Now only that one slot is lost instead of the whole fleet stopping; getting it back needs a manual `start --resume`. The v1 `result.late` log carries `raw`.
  - Only the request-timeout pause source changes. Supervisor BLOCKED, brake / usage wall, a dialog waiting for a human, handoff timeout, the link-loss restart limit, `applying` intent recovery, and a failed lease renewal of an official-CLI slot in service mode still pause as before.
- **A frozen session resumes first (official Claude worker)**: §24 frozen rotation, after its existing guards (only hold while the run is paused, only hold for a sub-fleet adoption, only hold when not in the registry, do not kill when a dialog waits for a human, the advisor takes its own branch, the §34 mid-turn rotation path), launches the next generation of a worker slot with `claude --resume <thread>` when three things hold: the transcript file still exists, this generation is not itself a resumed one (`resume_generation != generation`), and the in-flight item can be redelivered (task: the running task's dispatch / continuation, `replay < max_attempts`; service: a request dispatched to this slot, not `replay: false`, `replay < max_attempts`). The decision and the startup prompt are shared with the §24 "tab gone" case (`RuntimeV2.resumable` / `resume_prompt` / `redeliverable`; `next_generation` now uses the first two as well).
  - **Order** follows frozen rotation: the new generation starts launch-held, `tab_into` puts it in the old tab's position → kill the old process tree and confirm the pane is gone → `confirm_retired` → redeliver → open the startup gate. Until the startup gate opens the actor only waits and does not exec `claude`, so `claude --resume` always starts after the old process tree is confirmed gone and two processes never write the same session; the position stays in the original tab (unlike the "tab gone" case, which can only go to the end of the worker row).
  - **No skeleton handoff note** (the session carries its own context); the screen and terminal snapshots are still saved, and the history page records a generation with `source: frozen-resume`. The startup prompt is the same as for "tab gone": the session was interrupted (it froze: …), the daemon has resumed it, after READY the unfinished work is sent again, check what is already on disk before continuing.
  - **Redelivery** is the same as for "tab gone": task `requeue_lost`, service `replay_request` (`replay` +1; service is routed again, a request with `to` goes back to this slot). No FROZEN report to the supervisor and no `stalled` published. The audit `watch.frozen_restarted` gains `resumed=<thread>` (empty for a brand-new generation).
  - **Falling back to today's behavior**: a resumed generation that freezes again → a brand-new generation + skeleton handoff note, and the original instruction is not rerun (task: a FROZEN report to the supervisor; service: `stalled` published); a missing transcript, exhausted attempts or `replay: false` → the same path as today. A resumed generation that is not READY within `startup_deadline` → the same `resume_fallback` as "tab gone": a brand-new generation + skeleton handoff note (`-lost.md`); **the item already redelivered has not run in the resumed generation, so the brand-new generation runs it once**, without adding to the count.
  - A failed rotation (the new generation cannot start / cannot get into the old tab / the old session cannot be killed) → the existing `abort_rotation` (restore the old generation, hold); the resume marker is restored with it, and nothing is redelivered.
  - Unchanged: the supervisor slot, Codex / pi / opencode / dsh slots and the advisor slot still use today's frozen rotation (possible follow-up: session resume for Codex / pi).

**Verification**: `tests/test_request_no_deadline.py` (8 items: no default / explicit default / `request_limit`; a request without a deadline does not expire in a month, the message deadline is a constant and stays an integer through a real Redis round trip; expiry holds only that slot, still no pause after renewal and tick, other requests are routed and answered normally; an expiry during routing does not hold the supervisor, and the late routing decision is skipped; a late answer goes only to `result.late`; v1 without a deadline does not expire), `tests/test_frozen_resume.py` (9 items: a frozen Claude worker resumes in its original tab, the startup gate is closed while the old one is killed, argv carries `--resume`, the instruction is redelivered with `replay` 2, no FROZEN report; no transcript / exhausted attempts / freezing again after a resume take today's path; a resumed generation that is not READY falls back to a brand-new generation and the item runs only once; when the old session cannot be killed everything is restored and no resume marker is left; the supervisor is unchanged; a replayed service request is answered, and `replay: false` still goes `stalled`). Old tests changed with the constraints: the three frozen cases in `test_watchdog.py` now assert the resume; in `test_midturn_v2.py` the request message deadline is now the constant; in `test_service_stop_failure.py` "a timeout without StopFailure still pauses" now holds only that slot; tests relying on the 900 default now set a deadline explicitly. No smoke test on real cmux / real Claude; not reviewed, not deployed.

**Known gaps**:
- The config.json of a live run stores the `request_timeout: 900` written by the old `start`, and `start --resume` keeps it; dropping that default needs a fresh `start` (or editing that run's config). No pause on timeout and the frozen resume take effect immediately for a resumed run; messages queued before the resume still carry the old deadline.
- The hold exit of pi / bridge slots is still missing (⑦-4), see above.
- The `claude --resume` resume covers Claude workers only.
- The users (docs/contracts.md §4) all pass `timeout_s` explicitly and are not affected by the default change; the note "default 900 s, expiry pauses the whole fleet" in the user docs must be updated together with the deployment.

## 44. Long task split: probe nudge → worker submits a split plan → supervisor approves with models → after rotation it supervises a sub-fleet

Requirement: when a task has run for more than 40 minutes and is not just a plain download or computation but a workflow that keeps moving on, the current worker should be considered for becoming the supervisor of a sub-fleet that splits the task. The worker proposes how to split it to the supervisor, and the supervisor assigns models again and has the worker hand off; the 20-minute and 40-minute thresholds must be configurable in the yaml. All parts already exist: the §34 probe, §20 escalate / approve, §19 adoption, the §4 rotation handoff note; the single daemon, slot/generation identity and task/service protocol are unchanged.

- **Exception to §27**: on this path the supervisor picks the models itself. An approve may carry `supervisor_profile` (the card the worker's next generation uses) and `worker_profiles` (one card per child worker); with the advisor on it may suggest, the supervisor decides. Elsewhere "the supervisor does not pick models" still holds; `supervisor.md` / `supervisor_v2.md` state this as an exception, the original rule is not deleted.
- **Nudge (probe extension)**: only task-mode workers are watched. After `check_minutes` of continuous busy time (counted from this delivery's `busy_since`), the main loop adds `split_since` to the target of the §34 probe thread. The probe reads the tail of this generation's session file (≤1MB, read only when due, separate from the 64KB usage read; read even if the file has not changed) and counts the tool calls of the last `window_minutes`: calls ≥ `min_calls` and distinct files / commands ≥ `min_distinct` counts as "flowing"; otherwise it counts as "one thing that is slow by itself" and is looked at again after `check_minutes`. Every look writes `split_last` (e.g. `calls=37 distinct=12 files=9 in 10m: flowing; nudged`) and the audit `split.checked`. A generation that is in the middle of a §34 mid-turn rotation is not looked at.
- **Only nudge what can be split**: after "flowing" it first passes `subfleet.check(ready=False)` (task-number ownership, depth, sub-fleet count, whole-tree headcount, model quota, slot type); if that fails there is no nudge and the reason goes into `split_last` (`not splittable: …`). In practice this means only official Claude workers of a v2 task fleet are nudged: pi / opencode / dsh panes cannot be handed over (§19), and v1 does not support adoption. At most one nudge per slot per task (task number), and none while the same task already has a pending request.
- **How it arrives**: a one-line prompt `[FLEET LONG-RUNNING fx-<16hex>] …` (task number, minutes run, call count; it asks the worker to finish the current step, start no new one, and send a report in the AGENTS.md format: Done = what is done, Not done = what is left, an escalate JSON at the end, each piece with a `kind` describing the nature of the work; it says explicitly that this is not a context handoff and no handoff note is written). It is registered only in the agent hash (`split_id / split_prompt / split_g / split_message / split_send_pending`), **never as a message**: the clock is not paused, nothing is retyped after 300 seconds, no pane is killed, and the turn settles normally (it is not `handoff_split`). Claude takes the same PostToolUse hook as §34 (`claude_posttool` takes it when there is no pending §34 prompt; taking it once clears it), and it is handed only to the delivery it was registered for (`msg_id == split_message`), so one not taken by the end of the turn never leaks into the next delivery. At the same time the supervisor gets `[long-running] worker-1 已跑 43 分钟，最近 10 分钟 37 次工具调用、改了 9 个文件` (worker-1 has run 43 minutes; in the last 10 minutes 37 tool calls, 9 files changed) plus a line saying to wait for its [escalate] and that the approve may pick models. It uses its own prefix instead of reusing `[FLEET MID-TURN HANDOFF`: the two ask opposite things of the worker (one forbids a report, the other requires one), and a shared prefix would confuse the model. Cost: the pi extension's input gate and Codex `codex_queue.bound` do not recognize this prefix; today they never get a nudge anyway (previous item), and once pi / Codex panes can be handed over both places must let it through. The task number in the nudge is the `[M<n>]` of the first line of the delivery that is running (only when that is missing, the number of the slot's last dispatch), so a dispatch queued behind it is never taken as the task to split.
- **What each CLI provides**: Claude transcript: `tool_use` of main-thread `assistant` lines (skipping `isSidechain` / `agentId`); distinct = `file_path` / `path` / `command` / `pattern` / `url`; file changes = Edit / Write / MultiEdit / NotebookEdit. Codex rollout: `function_call` / `custom_tool_call` / `local_shell_call` of `response_item`; distinct = the argument text (in newer versions a script text, deduplicated only by raw text, coarse); file changes count only apply_patch's `*** Update/Add/Delete File:`. pi session: assistant `toolCall` of `message` lines; distinct = `path` / `command`; file changes = edit / write. All three cut the window by the line's ISO `timestamp`. opencode / dsh have no session file the probe can read → the result is "unknown", recorded as `split.unknown`, **no nudge**.
