Calls older than the tail are not counted (this can only undercount, and undercounting means no nudge).
- **Approve with models**: an approve item in `escalations[]` may add `supervisor_profile` (a card id) and `worker_profiles` (a list of card ids whose length is the number of child workers; if `workers` is also given they must be equal). Shape errors (not a card id, not a list, wrong count, models on a non-approve, models on a dialog / blocked answer) take the original correction path and count as consecutive rejections. A content failure takes the same path as a limit failure: no consecutive-rejection count, the request stays pending, and the supervisor gets `[escalate] approve of M0 refused by the program: <reason>`. `supervisor_profile`: the card must exist, must be a claude card (only official Claude panes can be adopted), and must pass `night.resolve_launches` (CLI pairing and the v2 config check), `card_gate` (host + this fleet's quota) and `preflight_profile` (CLI binary; v2 does not allow changing the CLI). `worker_profiles`: inside `subfleet.check(profiles=…)` they pass `resolve_launches(..., workers)` from the sub-fleet's point of view (the adopted Claude supervisor + these cards), i.e. card exists, pairing, host, quota (`check_slots`); checked once at approve and once when the sub-fleet really starts. Cards come from the fleet config's card library (the same source as roster profiles). The `[escalate]` notice lists the available cards at the end.
- **Rotate, then adopt (order)**: an approve with `supervisor_profile` does not write the start-sub-fleet request directly (`spawn_child` needs the worker idle + READY + nothing in flight, which holds neither before nor after rotation). The request is recorded as `rotating` (the sub-fleet start parameters, the new launch and the current generation are stored in the request record), and the slot gets `split_restore` (the original `runtime_launch`; not recorded when `restore_model` is off). Everything after that happens in `escalation_tick` (daemon tick, outside the v2 transaction): ① slot idle + READY + nothing in flight → write `split_launch` and `start_rotation` (the handoff note prompt is `handoff.SPLIT_PROMPT`: the new session will supervise sub-fleet M0; write what is done, the approved split plan and what is left for each piece); ② the handoff turn settles as in §4, and `finish_rotation` writes `split_launch` into `runtime_launch` before starting the next generation (the same place as the advisor rescue's `rescue_launch`); the new generation starts on the card the supervisor picked, reads the handoff note and returns READY; ③ the generation has changed and the new generation is idle + READY → write the start-sub-fleet request (with `worker_profiles`), the request becomes `approved`, and from there it is the original §20 flow (`process_requests` → `spawn_child` adopts the new pane → the child daemon's first tick starts the child workers on their cards). An approve without `supervisor_profile`: if the slot is already in the fleet's own tmux → adopted in place as before, no rotation; if it is still in a normal pane → it is also recorded as `rotating` (`launch` empty, no `split_restore`) and takes the three steps above as a same-model rotation (no `split_launch`, the handoff note is still `SPLIT_PROMPT`); the new generation starts in tmux and is then adopted (see the worker-area mapping item below). Both may carry `worker_profiles`.
- **Approval reserves the slot (found on a real machine)**: in a real-machine run, a single decision approved the split of M0 and also answered `blocked:worker-1` with reject "skip that line and continue". `blocked_ruling` immediately queued "[Supervisor ruling] [M0] reject … Carry on accordingly" to worker-1, while `split_rotate` waits for the slot to be idle before rotating and the approve itself sends nothing to the worker; worker-1 read "[M0] reject" as the split being rejected, finished the remaining 23 modules itself, and the sub-fleet had nothing to do. Root cause: an approved split did not reserve the slot; the worker did not know, and other deliveries of the same task to the same slot went out as usual. Now: ① within one decision the split answers run first (`on_supervisor` sorts stably by kind), so the order inside the decision does not matter; ② while the request is `approved` / `rotating` / `delegated`, a `[blocked]` ruling for the same slot and same task (the `item` of the `blocked` record) is not sent: it records `blocked.superseded` and appends one line to the end of the supervisor's next message saying it was superseded by the approved split and needs no action (`fx:split:notes`, appended the same way as the §37 notes, without starting a turn of its own); ③ if the worker is mid-turn at approve time (Claude, `state=busy` with a delivery in flight), a one-line program prompt `midturn.SPLIT_APPROVED_PROMPT` is registered on the same PostToolUse channel as the §44 nudge (`Fleet.split_hold`, the same `split_*` fields, handed over once): finish the current step, start no new one, end this turn with a report, no split plan, no handoff note; an idle worker gets nothing, its next delivery is the rotation handoff or the adoption; ④ while the request is `approved` / `rotating`, any dispatch to that slot is rejected as a whole with a correction (`dispatch: the approved split of M0 hands worker-1 over to sub-fleet M0 …`, counted as a consecutive rejection, like "approve and dispatch in the same reply"). Known gaps: during `delegated` the program does not block dispatches; they queue as before and go to the new generation after the collect (the same shape as defect B; the prompts and correction texts say not to dispatch until `[Sub-fleet M0 completed]`, the program only blocks approved / rotating); deliveries that were already queued for the slot before the approve (e.g. an earlier contract notice) are not withdrawn and go first, as in the §20 gap; the `item` of a plain task's `blocked` record is the **last** dispatch id for that slot, so a Blocked reported while M0 runs and M3 is queued is recorded as M3, and splitting M0 does not supersede that ruling.
- **The board marks a slot reserved by a split**: before, `worker_board` printed only the slot's `state`, so a split slot in `approved` / `rotating` showed as `idle` in the supervisor's routing prompt (service) and in the `Worker status now` at the end of each worker report (task); the supervisor dispatched to it and was rejected by `escalation_decision`, counted as a consecutive rejection. Now `worker_board` reads `fx:escalations` once and appends `, split M0 approved: it is becoming sub-fleet M0's supervisor, do not route to it until [Sub-fleet M0 completed]` to that slot's line (the same for `rotating`); nothing is added for `delegated`, since the slot's `state` is already `delegated`.
- **Worker-area mapping**: decision: a worker session of the main fleet is mapped to the supervisor of its sub-fleet (as the cmux version was designed); when a fleetmux task runs past 40 minutes and is split into a sub-fleet automatically, the main fleet's worker must map to the sub-fleet's supervisor, and that mapping was missing. Before, `subfleet.tmux_slot` only recognized the `supervisor_slot` of the roster's `subfleets:`, so a split slot was always false and `spawn_child` took the §19 placeholder + cross-page adopt, leaving only a text placeholder on the parent page (`subfleet.json` `view: 0`). Now: an approve (with or without models; the backend has `tmux_view_info`, i.e. cmux / fleetmux; WezTerm is already refused in `subfleet.check`) sets `tmux_view=1` on the slot's agent hash; `tmux_slot(cfg, slot, info)` is true when it sees the marker (except the `supervisor` slot; split slots inside a sub-fleet count too, because the marker belongs to the slot, not to the roster; rule: the supervisor of any sub-fleet is mapped at its parent worker's position, and the same for a grandchild fleet, i.e. the supervisor of M0.<n> is mapped at a worker position of the M0 page). `Fleet.spawn` passes this slot's hash to `tmux_spawn_args`, so the next generation started by a rotation (`finish_rotation`), `RuntimeV2.respawn` or `--resume` starts in the fleet's own tmux (`<run>/tmux.sock`, session `<slot>-g<generation>`). `spawn_child` → `parent_attach_view` → `parent_view` detects that the new pane is a tmux session → no placeholder, no release, `subfleet.json` `view: 1`; the child daemon's first tick runs `attach_child_view` to open a second client (fleetmux: `view-<session>` at the top left of the child page). Hook mailbox redirection (§19, `adopt.hook`) still routes by the parent slot's `delegated` + generation, regardless of whether the pane moved. Collect: the child side detaches its client and the parent tab turns back into a worker in place; the marker is not cleared and the slot stays in tmux (on the hook channel the next generation started at collect is also in tmux, so a second delegation needs no rotation). A revise / reject after the approve does not clear the marker either; the next generation still goes into tmux, which is harmless.
- **Window size with two clients (found on a real machine)**: the parent page tab and the child page view are two clients of the same tmux session with different sizes (on the real machine parent 52x25, child 41 rows); tmux has only one window size, and `window-size latest` (`cmux.py` `TMUX_CONF`, already set, also the tmux default) follows "the client with the most recent input / size change". Switching pages or tabs produces neither, so the visible client keeps the size of the invisible one and the extra rows/columns are filled with `·`; when the child page view has just attached (the attach itself counts as activity) the parent page immediately looks like this too. Measured on real tmux 3.6a: a same-size SIGWINCH or `switch-client -c <tty>` makes that client the latest, `refresh-client` does not. Fix: ① fleetmux server (`view::nudge_shown_tmux`, at the end of `apply_pty_sizes`): after every layout pass, for panes that have just come into view (the selected tab of each group on the current page, or the zoomed one) and, after a size change, for all panes in view, if the foreground program is `tmux`, send one SIGWINCH to its foreground process group; never to any other program. ② the same server's tmux watch (`boot::tmux_watch` runs `view::tmux_watch_tick` every 100 ms): when the foreground program of a pane on any page becomes `tmux` (a client attached, often later than the layout pass) or a tmux pane's PTY size changed (e.g. the child daemon's `arrange` moves the view into the supervisor group, the kernel sends it SIGWINCH and it takes the window), it sends one more SIGWINCH to the tmux clients in view 300 ms later, giving that client's own resize time to reach tmux first. Who owns the size is decided only by the server, which knows which page is visible. ③ cmux backend `attach_view`: when the new client is not in `current-workspace`, it waits for it to appear and then runs `switch-client` on the existing client (`cmux.tmux_give_back`). The fleetmux backend used to give the size back this way too (1b104f7); real-machine feedback was that the dots were still there right after start and went away after one page switch: after the give-back, `arrange` moved the view to another group and resized it, and the view took the window again; this was removed and ② took over. Test: `tests/test_fleetmux_pages.py::test_the_visible_tmux_client_sizes_the_window_after_attach_and_page_switch[|M0]` (real fleetmux + real tmux, in the order of `start_adopted`: child page worker → view attached before the first worker → `arrange`; the parent page / child page is the current page at attach time, the visible client has no fill without a page switch, then the pages are switched back and forth four times; asserts `window_width == client_width`, `window_height == client_height - 1`; needs `FLEETMUX_GATE=m4`). Boundary: cmux has no hook for workspace switching, so after switching over a key (or a click) in that tab is needed before the size changes; not tested on real cmux; wezterm does not use two clients.
- **`allow_profile_live_switch`**: this path does not go through it. It governs the §12 task-level "Phase 5 live switch" of `profile_assignments` (`switch_profile` / `prepare_profile`); this path, like the §25 roster card change and the §27 rescue respawn, is a rotation the daemon schedules itself at an idle boundary (handoff note + new generation), and before changing the card it still checks the card library, pairing, host, quota and the CLI preflight. So it works with the default off and does not need it enabled.
- **Child worker cards**: `child_config` explicitly writes `worker_profiles = {worker-1: card, …}` for a sub-fleet started by escalate (`{}` when none is given). Before, the child config inherited the parent's `worker_profiles` unchanged; with the same slot names `worker-N`, the parent's own per-slot profiles leaked into the sub-fleet. `start_adopted` adds `apply_roster_launch` after `init_worker` (as `start_all` does), so a child worker's first generation starts on its card; §29 named sub-fleets therefore also start their first generation on the roster card instead of starting on the default and changing the card after READY.
- **Restore the original model on collect**: if the slot has `split_restore` at `collect` → it becomes `split_launch` and the slot's next generation starts with it (`finish_rotation` or `RuntimeV2.respawn`, effective in both places like `rescue_launch`). On the Claude hook channel (default) the next generation starts at collect → restored right away; on a same-session collect over the typing channel (§19) the session keeps the split's model and is restored only at its next rotation. `restore_model: false` → no `split_restore`, the new model stays. If starting the sub-fleet after the rotation is refused and the request reopens: a second approve (even with a different `supervisor_profile`) keeps the original model recorded first; if the supervisor changes it to revise / reject → `split_restore` becomes `split_launch` and the slot's next generation is restored. The restore does not pass the quota gate again (see boundaries).
- **Automatic numbering**: a dispatch item of a plain task without `id` → the program fills in the next free `M<n>` (inside a sub-fleet `<this fleet's number>.<n>`, e.g. `M0.0`), writes it into `fx:items`, and the dispatch's first line carries `[M<n>]`, so any dispatch can be split; one with `id` is handled as before (format check, same semantics). Contract tasks already require a number, unchanged. The old §20 rejection "a dispatch must carry an id" therefore hardly appears in plain tasks any more.
- **Configuration (roster yaml, hot reload)**: a top-level `split:` and each worker entry's own `split:`, keys `check_minutes` (a number ≥0, 0 = no nudge for that worker), `window_minutes` (a number >0), `min_calls`, `min_distinct` (integers ≥1), `restore_model` (boolean); resolved per key: worker entry → top-level table → built-in (40 / 10 / 15 / 2 / true). Hot reloaded with the §16 roster and effective at the next tick; `roster.changed` carries the old and new `split` values; any invalid value rejects the whole file as in §16 and keeps the old values. A run without a roster file uses the built-in values. `restore_model` is fixed by its value at approve time; editing the roster later does not flip an in-flight split.
- **Display**: `split_check_minutes / split_window_minutes / split_min_calls / split_min_distinct / split_restore_model / split_source` of `fx:agent:<slot>` (e.g. `check_minutes: worker; window_minutes: roster; …`, like `handoff_source`) and `split_last`; `status` has an extra `split` block per slot. Audit / log: `split.checked`, `split.nudged`, `split.unknown`, `escalate.rotate`, `escalate.rotated`.

**Known boundaries**: it only nudges and never cuts off; a worker that does not comply runs until it ends by itself, and no further nudge is sent. "Flowing" is judged only from the tool-call stream, without an LLM judgment; the misjudgment rate has not been measured. The "distinct" count of Codex's newer `custom_tool_call` deduplicates by raw script text, which is coarse. Restoring the original model does not pass `card_gate`; if other slots fill the original model's quota in the meantime, the restore may go over quota (it does not block and is not audited separately). On a same-session collect over the typing channel, if a §25 roster card change happens before the next generation, the remaining `split_launch` overrides the roster card once. If the task ends in the middle of a split (`rotating` not finished), `split_restore` is not cleared and the slot keeps the split's model until changed by hand. If a contract notice is queued to this worker between approve and the start of the rotation, it is delivered first (same as the known §20 gap), and the rotation waits for it to finish. Until pi / Codex panes can be adopted they get no nudge (see "How it arrives"). Worker-area mapping: a slot in a normal pane gets one extra same-model handoff turn after an approve without models (in exchange for a live session on the parent page); a marked slot is treated as a tmux slot from then on and inherits the existing §29 differences (after a handoff hold pause, the new generation's READY does not lift the pause automatically; it waits for the operator to resume); re-attaching the view tab on a sub-fleet `--resume` is covered only by the named sub-fleet tests, M<n> is not tested separately. Prompt sync: `handoff.SPLIT_PROMPT` says "on the model the supervisor picked, or the same model when none was picked", and `supervisor.md` / `supervisor_v2.md` and the `[escalate]` notice state that any approve may rotate first (with no models, the same model, so the new session starts in the fleet's tmux and a live session stays at the parent worker position). The whole chain on real cmux / Claude sessions (nudge → split plan → approve with models → new model READY → sub-fleet → collect and restore) has not been tested live (a live test with a 2-minute threshold is still to do).

**Verification**: new `tests/test_long_split.py` (36 cases: the nudge picks the task by the `[M<n>]` of the running delivery, not the last queued one; after a split reopens, a second approve keeps the original model and a reject restores it at the next generation; the `split` block of status; roster `split:` resolution order and whole-file rejection of 8 kinds of bad values, hot reload + status fields + bad values keep the old ones + `check_minutes: 0`; in-window call / distinct / changed-file counts for Claude / Codex / pi sessions, unknown for opencode / dsh and a missing file; the probe reports activity when due even if the file did not change; real private Redis v2: a flowing Claude worker is nudged only once, PostToolUse hands it over only once, no message is written and the clock is not stopped, the supervisor gets `[long-running]`, the worker sends its escalate normally; a registered nudge is not handed to another delivery; a slow one is not nudged and is looked at again after 40 minutes; no nudge when not due, with `check_minutes: 0`, or when `subfleet.check` refuses; approve shape errors count as consecutive rejections; unknown card / pi card as supervisor / fable quota full / child worker card over quota / unknown child worker card → a refusal that is not counted and stays pending; a valid approve records `rotating`; automatic numbering; two real v2 fleets: approve with models → the worker writes a handoff note → new generation claude-opus READY → the sub-fleet adopts the new pane, child workers start on claude-haiku / claude-sonnet → completion and collect → the slot's generation 2 is back on the original launch, with `allow_profile_live_switch` off; an approve without models adopts in place and the parent's per-slot profiles do not leak into the sub-fleet); in `test_escalate.py` "plain tasks must carry an id" changed to automatic numbering, `test_pi_decision_schema.py` / `test_pi_decision_schema_v2.mjs` add the two optional keys of the pi supervisor schema (TypeBox run in node); the dispatch texts of five existing tests gained `[M<n>]` from automatic numbering. Independent review, merge, deployment and real-machine tests are not done. Worker-area mapping (feat/worker-area-split): the e2e of `tests/test_long_split.py` now runs in a fake world with the two-client primitives (`ttree`): after an approve with models the slot has `tmux_view`, the rotation starts in `worker-1-g1`, `view: 1` with no placeholder, the child-side view gets the role change, during delegation `delegated_route` points to the child supervisor, after the collect the next generation `worker-1-g2` is still in tmux and the hook goes back to the parent slot; without models in a normal pane → same-model rotation into tmux, then adoption (`runtime_launch` unchanged, no `split_restore`); without models and already in tmux → adopted in place without rotation; `tests/test_subfleet_tmux.py` covers the marker version of `tmux_slot` / `tmux_spawn_args` and `spawn`; `tests/test_subfleet_fleetmux.py::test_task_subfleet_m0_on_fleetmux_delegates_and_returns` changed to the new semantics (fake + real fleetmux binary, real tmux: rotation into tmux → a live tab with an unchanged pid on the parent page + a view at the top left of the child page → after the collect generation 2 is still in tmux and still on the parent page).

**The supervisor opens a sub-fleet directly**: decision: the supervisor can sort work into tiers: tier one is dispatching a sub-fleet, tier two is dispatching a worker, tier three is answering directly itself. Tier three, "answer yourself", has already landed with feat/supervisor-self-answer (the service decision `answer`); tier two is the existing `dispatch`; this item adds tier one. Switch `supervisor_split` (`start --supervisor-split`, config boolean, default off; when off the item does not appear in the supervisor template, and a decision carrying `split` is invalid as a whole, counted as a consecutive rejection with an explanation that the switch is off). When on, a dispatch item of a service decision may carry `split: {reason, proposal: [{instruction, output, kind?}], workers?, note?, supervisor_profile?, worker_profiles?}`: the program records the request as dispatched to the `to` worker but does not deliver it, runs P7's `service_split_intake` (number M<n>, rewrite the piece ids, §44 limit checks) and **sends no** `[escalate]` question (`message_id` empty, `escalations_missed` does not chase it), then immediately approves it with `escalation_apply` using the supervisor's parameters; from there it is exactly the same path as a split requested by a worker (rotation / adoption / `delegated` answer / `<id>.done`). If the program refuses (limits, `split_judge: off`) → it takes P7's refusal path, hands the request to that worker as a plain dispatch to answer itself and tells the supervisor, which amounts to an automatic downgrade to tier two. When to use which tier is not in the code: it is written in that fleet's supervisor role file (`supervisor.md` in the `--roles` directory, section 4) and tuned by the user through reviews. Audit `split.by_supervisor`. Verification: `tests/test_supervisor_split.py` (7 cases: shape check, invalid when the switch is off, the template shows it only when on, config check, approval sends no [escalate], a limit refusal downgrades to a plain dispatch, real v2 parent and child fleets start a sub-fleet and answer `delegated`).

## 46. Permission boundary of a sub-fleet supervisor: sandbox escapes and the like are never self-approved; they go to the root supervisor

Requirement: use the agent file to limit the permissions of a sub-fleet's supervisor; any kind of sandbox-escape behavior must be reported to the root supervisor. Cause (an incident run): the supervisor of sub-fleet M0 (the adopted worker-1, Opus) told its two Haiku child workers they had an "authorized exception" and could work in the parent worker's directory `worker-1/toy`, and they did; the program only blocked **artifacts** outside the workspace (§23 "artifact path must stay inside the workspace"), while the workspace boundary for the work itself was only written in the prompts. (§45 is taken by the unmerged `feat/fresh-always-impl-1`, so this section uses 46.)

- **Role file of a sub-fleet supervisor**: for a supervisor whose config has `parent_config` (§19 escalate, §29 named, and contract-task sub-fleets all count), `role_scope` appends a "## Sub-fleet boundaries" section to the end of AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md (`fleet.SUBFLEET_BOUNDS`): it and its workers work only inside this sub-fleet's workspace (plus the roots that the roster's `writable:` gives to a specific worker); it **may not grant any exception**, nor tell a worker that there is one. Examples (not exhaustive): writing the directories of the parent fleet / another fleet / another worker, another repository, home configuration, credentials and keys, turning off the sandbox / hooks / permission checks, network access or installs beyond the task, killing processes it did not start, git push / force, deleting or moving outside the workspace, anything else that escapes the sandbox. When it meets one (the work needs it, a worker did / asked for / reported it): it does not grant it and does not do it, that piece is put on hold (other pieces continue), it reports it up with `report_up` in its next decision and waits for the root supervisor's `[top ruling]`; a `[blocked]` a worker raised for this is answered `reject` together with a `report_up`. The top-level supervisor gets no such section and never sees the `report_up` key.
- **Workers of a sub-fleet**: in the same place (`role_scope`, with `parent_config`), one sentence is added after the "do not write outside your own directory" rule (`fleet.SUBFLEET_WORKER_RULE`): an "exception" from the supervisor to work outside the workspace is not valid; only the grants listed above in this file count (the roster's, and those the program issues from the root supervisor's ruling, see "Grants issued from a ruling" below); refuse everything else, do not do it, and explain it under Blocked in the report.
- **Task directory (found on a real machine)**: an M<n> sub-fleet started by a §44 split / §20 escalate continues work in the parent worker's own directory (in a real-machine run the instruction said "In worker-1/toy/ …", and the Haiku child worker cited AGENTS.md and refused to work in the parent's `workspace/worker-1/toy`; it still refused after the root supervisor approved the report_up, because the worker rule only recognized roster grants). Now `child_config` records `task_dir` = the parent slot's working directory (`fleet.agent_cwd(parent, slot)`) for a non-named sub-fleet, and it counts as part of this sub-fleet's workspace: the first item of the child supervisor's "Sub-fleet boundaries" states that it may work and dispatch work there, that this is not crossing the boundary and needs no report (`fleet.SUBFLEET_TASK_DIR_BOUND`; "the parent fleet's directories" in the examples becomes "the parent fleet's other directories"); a child worker gets one more item after "do not write outside your own directory" (`fleet.SUBFLEET_TASK_DIR_RULE`, appended in the same place as the roster's `writable:`), and the §46 sentence "only roster grants count" becomes "only the grants above count"; the sub-task goal (`subfleet.goal_text`) states the task directory's absolute path, and the old "nobody may write outside this sub-fleet's workspace" becomes "…the workspace and the task directory"; the Claude launch arguments of a child worker gain `--add-dir <task_dir>`. The other §46 prohibitions (the parent fleet's other directories, other fleets / workers, credentials, push, etc.) are unchanged; the §23 artifact copy is unchanged: an artifact written directly at the parent path also counts (the copy is skipped when there is no copy on the child side, and the parent's check sees the file as `returned`; tested). §29 named sub-fleets have no task directory. Boundary: this is still the prompt layer, the program does not block writes (same as the known boundary of this section); Codex child workers are not in the v2 matrix (§19), and no writable root was added to the Codex sandbox.
- **Reporting up (`report_up`)**: a child supervisor's decision (both the task and the service JSON accept it) may carry `"report_up": [{"what": "...", "why": "...", "who": "worker-N"}]` (`who` optional); only with RUNNING; a RUNNING carrying it may dispatch nothing (like `escalations`). A top-level fleet supervisor carrying it → correction "report_up is only for a sub-fleet supervisor", counted as a consecutive rejection. Each item writes one notice to `<parent run>/subfleet-notices/`, reusing the §20 interface-notice channel (intermediate levels pass it up unchanged with `os.replace`, without reading it):
  `{"kind": "report", "id": "report:M0:<6hex>", "origin_run": "<child run>", "task": "M0", "supervisor": "worker-1.1", "what": "...", "why": "...", "who": "worker-2"}`
  `supervisor` comes from the child config's `adopt.label` (the parent slot label `child_config` records when it starts the sub-fleet, i.e. the generation that really supervises). The sub-fleet records `fx:reports_up` (pending key → what); while anything is pending `others_active` is true, so a later RUNNING of the supervisor without dispatches is valid (the ruling's arrival gives it a turn by itself).
- **Top level**: `relay_notices` recognizes `kind == "report"` before the interface branch: it queues `[sub-fleet report] sub-fleet M0 (worker-1.1): <what> — <why> (worker: worker-2)` + how to answer + how to judge (approve only what the user's task explicitly allows; delete / move, push, credentials and keys, and anything only the user can decide are always reject; if the task cannot do without it, BLOCKED to the user) for the supervisor, and records `report:M0:<id>` in `fx:escalations` (`kind: report`, `message_id` = this message). The root supervisor answers in `escalations` with `{"task": "report:M0:<id>", "decision": "approve" | "reject", "note": "..."}`; `revise` is not accepted (correction). The `escalations` of service mode also accept the `report:` prefix. **No answer**: the same as `[blocked]` (`escalations_missed`): the first time it is sent back with "answer report:… first" without counting, the next miss counts as a consecutive rejection; **never auto-approved**. A late answer (the record is gone) is only logged, not an error.
- **Ruling sent down**: `report_ruling` writes `{"kind": "report", "ref": "report:M0:<id>", "decision": "approve", "note": "...", "what": "..."}` into the `subfleet-vetoes/` of the **original sub-fleet** (the same directory as the §20 vetoes, straight to the origin, not through intermediate levels); the sub-fleet's `apply_vetoes` recognizes `kind == "report"` before the veto branch, clears that item from `fx:reports_up`, and queues `[top ruling] report:M0:<id> approve: <note>` + (what was reported) + "approve: do only as much as the note says and dispatch to tell the workers concerned; reject: do not do it, drop that piece or use another approach inside the workspace" for the supervisor.
- **Grants issued from a ruling (found on a real machine)**: in a real-machine run the child supervisor used `report_up` to ask for appending 40 lines to a shared `RELEASES.md`, the root supervisor approved, the `[top ruling]` reached the child supervisor, which followed the prompt "dispatch to tell the workers concerned", and the Haiku worker refused twice under the §46 rule (an exception from the supervisor is not valid, only roster grants count); `RELEASES.md` stayed empty. Root cause: the root supervisor's approval had no program channel; it was only text relayed by the child supervisor, and the worker was right not to trust it. Now an approve goes through the existing grant mechanism and becomes a grant issued by the program, instead of yet another text exception:
  - **Reports carry paths**: a `report_up` item may carry `"paths": ["/abs/path", ...]` (non-empty, absolute, normalized; the `writable:` model has no access kinds, it just means "writable"); with `paths`, `who` is required and must be a worker of this sub-fleet (checked for both the task and the service JSON). The notice, the child-side `fx:reports_up` (now `{what, who, paths}`) and the open top-level `report:` record all carry it; the `[sub-fleet report]` message lists the paths and explains that an approve issues grants for them and may narrow them.
  - **Narrow only**: the root supervisor's approve may carry `"paths": [...]` (a new optional key of `escalations[]`, approve only, added to the pi supervisor schema too); each must equal or lie inside a reported path, otherwise the whole reply is corrected and counted as a consecutive rejection (`paths may only narrow what the sub-fleet reported`); answering with paths when the report had none also counts as widening. The ruling file of an approve with paths gains `who` / `paths` (without them, all reported paths). The child side's `report_ruled` checks once more: it grants only inside the reported paths and only to the reported `who` (any other `who` in the ruling file is ignored).
  - **Issuing**: `Fleet.grant` records `{report, paths}` in the `grants` field of that worker's `fx:agent:<slot>` (visible as `slots.<w>.agent.grants` in `status`; log / audit `grant.issued`, visible in history). `role_scope` lists "Grant issued by the program from the top fleet supervisor's ruling report:… : you may also write inside `…`" (`program_grants_notice`) after the "do not write outside your own directory" rule and before the roster grants, so every later generation of that worker carries it; for the generation that is running, `set_grants` rewrites `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` in its directory in place: it replaces only the program grant lines after the "own directory" line and leaves the surrounding kit / domain-rule layout untouched (it does not reassemble the file, to avoid diverging from the several spawn layouts).
  - **How the running worker is told**: no separate delivery is queued (in task mode every delivery requires a report, which would waste a turn); the program records one line `[Fleet grant report:…] … re-read that file …` on the slot (`grant_note`), and the program puts it at the very front of the next dispatch the child supervisor sends it (before `advisory`; in service mode after the `[Request <id>]` header), only once. The trust anchor is the file: the worker rule says only grants in the file count; this line only points the way, and text the supervisor writes itself is still not valid (a supervisor's dispatch always starts with `[Supervisor instruction …]`, so it cannot forge the start of a delivery). The `[top ruling]` the child supervisor receives states that the program has issued a grant to worker-N and that it should dispatch that piece directly.
  - **Scope**: only for this sub-fleet run and only for that worker; when the sub-fleet ends (`Fleet.finish`, with `parent_config`: completion, BLOCKED, failure and stop/cancel all pass through it), `clear_grants` clears the field, restores the files, drops any pointer line not yet delivered, and records `grant.cleared`.
  - **Per CLI**: a Claude worker runs with `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, and only the role text restrains it, so rewriting the files + the pointer line take effect at once (it rereads CLAUDE.md as instructed; a Claude Code session does not reload CLAUDE.md mid-session by itself, untested whether it would); the roster's `writable:` was never mapped to Claude `--add-dir`, and grants do not add it either. Codex worker: the text arrives as well, but the sandbox's `writable_roots` exist only in the launch arguments and the roster's `writable:` is not mapped to workers either, so Codex cannot write there (Codex child workers are not in the v2 matrix anyway, §19). pi worker: no sandbox (`UNSANDBOXED_SCOPE`), same as Claude. opencode: the role is in AGENTS.md of the instance directory, which an in-place rewrite does not find, so it takes effect only at the next generation.
  - **An approve without paths** (needs that are not write paths, such as network access or installs): still only a text ruling, the program issues nothing; the `[top ruling]` says explicitly that the program issued no grant and the worker still does not accept your exceptions outside the workspace. No capability system is built for such needs.
- **The root supervisor's prompt**: the command table of `supervisor.md` / `supervisor_v2.md` gains a `[sub-fleet report]` line after the `[blocked]` line: answer in the escalations shape above; approve only what the user's task explicitly allows and write in the note how far the approval goes; delete / move, push, credentials and keys, and anything only the user can decide → reject, and if the task cannot do without it, BLOCKED to the user; no answer is sent back once and then counted as a consecutive rejection, never auto-approved. The three service-mode supervisor templates did not get this line (the `[sub-fleet report]` message itself carries how to answer and judge).
- **Fixed along the way**: `subfleet.collect`'s `[Sub-fleet M0 completed] worker-1.2 ran sub-fleet M0` used the label of the generation restarted after the collect; now the label is recorded on entering `collect` (the slot does not rotate while delegated), so the notice names the generation that really supervised (`worker-1.1 ran …`), while the second half "… is a worker again (new generation …)" still uses the current label.

**Known boundaries**: this is the prompt layer + a reporting channel; **the program does not block writes outside the workspace**: if a child supervisor grants anyway, or simply does not report, the program cannot tell (artifact paths are still blocked by §23); a hard workspace boundary needs each CLI's sandbox / writable roots, separate work. When the top-level fleet is not running the notice is dropped (same as §20 interface notices), and that piece of the sub-fleet waits until its own deadline. If the sub-fleet ends (deadline, stop, failure) before the ruling arrives, the top-level `report:` stays open: the root supervisor is still sent back / counted until it answers, and the answer is written to a `subfleet-vetoes/` that no daemon reads any more (a direct sub-fleet could clear it at `collect`, but the top level never sees a grandchild's collect, so this was not done). While waiting for a ruling a sub-fleet is not judged stalled: `check_stalls` only looks at busy / starting slots, `lifecycle_close` does not manage a fleet with `parent_config`, and only the task's own `hours` deadline remains. The service-mode top level is verified only at the parse layer (`parse_service_decision` accepts `report:`), with no end-to-end test; the Pi supervisor's `fleet_decision` schema has no `report_up` (a pi pane cannot be a sub-fleet supervisor, §19; TypeBox objects do not forbid extra keys), but `escalations[]` gained `paths` (pi can be a top-level supervisor). Grants issued from a ruling: still the prompt layer, the program does not block writes, it only gives "approved" a program origin; the Codex sandbox writable roots and a running opencode generation do not follow (see "Per CLI" above); remote paths of the form `host:path` are not accepted (local absolute paths only); if after issuing the child supervisor never dispatches to that worker again, the pointer line is never delivered (the files are already updated). Not tested on real cmux / Claude sessions.

**Verification**: new `tests/test_subfleet_boundaries.py` (14 cases: a v1 contract fleet first as child, then as top, `report_up` → notice shape → `[sub-fleet report]` + an open escalation → revise refused, approve → ruling file → `[top ruling]` and `fx:reports_up` cleared; reject sent down; a top-level supervisor carrying `report_up` counts as a consecutive rejection; 4 kinds of bad `report_up` shapes refused in task / service, non-RUNNING refused; service mode can answer `report:`; intermediate levels pass it up unchanged; real private Redis v2: no answer is sent back first without counting, a second miss counts, the record stays open; child supervisor / child worker role texts have it, the top level does not; the line in both supervisor templates); `tests/test_subfleet.py` adoption tests add: the child config's `adopt.label`, the boundary rules in the adopted pane's CLAUDE.md and the child workers' AGENTS.md, and the completion notice naming the generation that supervised (on the hook channel the slot rotates after the collect, and the new generation used to be named). Only the related tests were run, not the full suite; independent review, merge, deployment and real-machine tests are not done.

**Verification (fix/subfleet-grants)**: `tests/test_subfleet_boundaries.py` adds 15 cases: a report with paths → the top-level message lists the paths → widening is corrected → a narrowing approve → the ruling file carries `who` / `paths` → the child-side `grants` field, `grant.issued`, the running AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md rewritten in place (kit frame untouched), the next generation's `role_scope` carries it too, other workers do not → the next dispatch starts with `[Fleet grant …]` and only once → cleared after `finish` and the files restored; an approve without paths issues nothing; the child-side fallback does not widen and ignores another `who`; worker rules; 5 kinds of bad `report_up.paths` (task / service), 2 kinds of bad `escalations[].paths`, paths in an answer when the report had none. `tests/test_long_split.py` adds 4 cases (both in-decision orders seen on the real machine, a busy worker goes through PostToolUse, dispatch correction during the handover), `tests/test_blocked_ruling.py` adds 1 case (a ruling after rotation is `blocked.stale`), and the two pi schema tests add `paths`. Only the related test files were run, not the full suite; review, merge, deployment and real-machine tests are not done.

## 47. A bridge that gets no settlement receipt is not a failure: late receipts from a busy daemon still settle

Reported problem: typing into a pi supervisor pane while its turn is running gets the supervisor held, and the whole fleet stops dispatching.

**Premise corrected**: in the field (a channels development fleet run, release 4cd1156, a pi 1.5.0 Astra supervisor + 11 Claude workers), **nobody typed into the supervisor pane** at 05:02–05:03. The orchestrating session ran `cmux send` to the supervisor surface only twice (04:08:55, 05:00:29), and the audit shows only two `operator_input` for the supervisor (04:08:59, 05:00:31); both times the supervisor was idle, and both were queued, delivered and committed normally per §5. "Typing mid-turn" had nothing to do with this hold.

**The real mechanism**:
- pi session g0: the supervisor finished its `fleet_decision` call at 05:02:41.7 (candidate recorded) → turn ends → the extension's `agent_settled` sends `settled` → the bridge's `event(..., wait=True)` waits for the daemon's receipt, `wait_reply` default 30 seconds.
- At that time the daemon took only **one** event per main-loop round (`RuntimeV2.step` `count=1`) and then ran the whole `tick()` (the watchdog reading the screen slot by slot, etc.). At 05:01:59 the supervisor dispatched to 11 workers at once, busy slots went from 1 to 11, and a daemon round grew from about 1 second to 18–31 seconds (in the audit the Claude workers' `renewed` entries appear in batches at 05:02:20 / 02:51 / 03:15 / 03:33 / 03:51). The supervisor turn's `started` / `candidate` / `settled` queued one after another, and the daemon reached them only at 05:02:51 / 03:14 / 03:33 (lag 31 / 33 / 52 seconds; the previous 33 delivery candidates all lagged 0–3 seconds).
- At 30 seconds the bridge got `pending`, which the old code always treated as "settlement unconfirmed": `fault('settlement unconfirmed/held; no execution replay')` → `mark_uncertain` (the `uncertain` at 05:03:11.9) → when the daemon processed `settled` at 05:03:33 the message was already uncertain → `settle.late` (the supervisor's valid decision was dropped as a late answer) → at 05:03:51 it processed `bridge_fault`: the slot was not yet held → `hold()` pauses by default → `execution.held` + `service.paused`, the whole fleet stopped dispatching, and the pi slot has no hold exit (§24 ⑦-4), so only `start --resume` helped.
- Trigger: the daemon processes a pi / dsh slot's `settled` more than 30 seconds after it was sent. This happens when there are many busy slots (the watchdog reading screens slot by slot stretches each round to 20–30 seconds) and the slot's `started` / `candidate` from the end of the turn are still queued ahead of it. The 04:08 case went fine because only 0–1 slots were busy and the receipt arrived within 1 second, not because of typing or not. This is a third kind of fault that §35 does not distinguish: not a link loss, not a relay of a hold by the daemon, but the bridge itself turning "the receipt has not arrived yet" into a hold.

**What changed** (only the bridge and two plugins; the daemon is untouched):
- `agent_bridge.py`: a `settled` receipt of `pending` (no reply within the wait limit) → no fault, no `mark_uncertain`; it records this call's correlation id as `settling` and keeps the in-flight delivery and the lease. Each round of `renew_loop` first checks the receipt stream of that correlation id: when it arrives it is handled by the original rules (`committed` → send `completed`; `held` / `rejected` → the original fault); likewise, when the message becomes committed it sends `completed` as before, and uncertain / expired fault as before. The `settled` event itself is durable in the event stream (`pending` only comes after a successful XADD), so the daemon will process it sooner or later; while waiting the slot takes no new delivery (`idle_event` is not opened). If the daemon really stopped → the restarted daemon processes this event and then settles, which loses one decision fewer than the old immediate hold. The wait has no limit, consistent with §43 "health has other owners": link loss is covered by the heartbeat / lease (§35), a stalled daemon by §36.
- `pi_extension_v2.ts`: when the `settled` receipt is `pending`, or the extension itself gets no receipt within 35 seconds (the bridge processes commands serially, so this happens when an earlier `operator_input` is still waiting for the daemon, which is exactly "typing into the supervisor while the daemon is busy"), it is likewise treated as late and the status bar shows `已结算，等 fleet 确认` (settled, waiting for fleet confirmation); when the late `completed` frame arrives it reports idle once more (before, idle was reported only once after the `settled` receipt, when the delivery was still in flight, so it could not be reported and the slot would never get its next delivery); `completed` no longer resets an ongoing `manual` phase to idle.
- `dsh_bridge.mjs`: likewise reports idle once more on the `completed` frame. The opencode plugin already reported idle on `completed`, unchanged.
- Manual input mid-turn (not reproduced on a real machine: it was not the cause in the field): by the original §5 semantics, text in the pi supervisor's input box (pi 1.5.0 also goes through the `input` hook during a turn, `streamingBehavior: "steer"`) is turned into an `operator_input` and queued; the current turn is unaffected and it is delivered as a regular delivery after the turn settles. The existing behavior already does this; only a test proving it was added. "Queue" was chosen over "reject": §5 says text a human types in the terminal must not silently disappear, and in task mode with a task running, extra instructions go through the original queue, so queueing is the existing design.

**Verification**:
- New `tests/test_bridge_late_settle.py` (real private Redis + real AgentBridge + real daemon step/pump/tick; the test can stall the daemon to act as a busy daemon): ① a late receipt causes no fault, the message stays received, no `uncertain`; after the daemon is released: committed, the bridge sends `completed`, the slot is not held, the fleet is not paused, no `settle.late`, the supervisor's decision is dispatched to worker-1 and answered, and the supervisor can take its next delivery (old code red: `sup.faulted`); ② a late `held` receipt still holds the slot (old code red); ③ a mid-turn `operator_input` → accepted, the current delivery committed, the typed text then delivered as the next delivery (a confirmation test, green on the old code too).
- `tests/test_pi_extension_v2.mjs` (through `test_pi_v2_contract.py`, the fake bridge gains `settle_pending`): on `pending` no idle is reported and no link-interrupted status is shown; after the late `completed` idle is reported once and the next delivery is taken (old extension red: never gets the state / idle).
- Same mjs: after `settled` is sent the fake bridge does not reply and the extension times out at 35 seconds (cut to 30 ms in the test) → treated as late, idle reported after `completed` (old extension red: the handler throws an unhandled rejection).
- `tests/test_dsh_bridge_plugin.py::test_a_late_commit_reports_idle_again` (the fake bridge returns `pending`; old plugin red).
- Smoke test on real pi 1.5.0 (`scripts/smoke_pair.sh pi claude task`, isolated `FLEETMUX_HOME` / private Redis, an Astra supervisor + 2 official Claude workers): `kill -STOP` on the daemon when the supervisor got its first delivery, and after the supervisor wrote its candidate at 05:28:57 another 40 seconds before `-CONT`. At 05:29:30 the supervisor's status bar showed `已结算，等 fleet 确认`; the daemon woke and processed `settled` at 05:29:39 (about 42 seconds after it was sent) → committed, with no `uncertain` / `settle.late` / `bridge_fault` / `execution.held` / `service.paused` at any point; 8 seconds later the supervisor took its next delivery, the task was COMPLETED, and all four checks PASS.

**Known gaps**:
- Daemon throughput is unchanged: each round still takes only one event, and the watchdog's slot-by-slot screen reads are in the main loop, so with many busy slots event latency is still tens of seconds (settlements and the `指令已排队` (instruction queued) receipt of `operator_input` both arrive late; past 30 seconds `operator_input` still shows `输入未确认` (input unconfirmed), but the event is queued and will be processed). This is the root of the latency; separate work.
- A late receipt is picked up by `renew_loop`, at most one lease-renewal period later (`min(10 s, lease/3)`). The receipt stream the daemon writes (`<prefix>:reply:<correlation id>`) has no expiry, so a late `rejected` is never missed.
- On the dsh plugin's normal path the `completed` frame and the `settled` receipt each report idle once (the duplicate is harmless).
- §24 ⑦-4 (pi / bridge slots have no hold exit) is unchanged; this section only stops the wrongful hold.

## 48. Never type into a Claude input box; outside messages go through `operator`; a vanished confirmation dialog resumes automatically; a usage warning is not a wall

Decision: in no scenario does the fleet type into a Claude input box. Background, from an incident in the channels dev fleet: the orchestrating session could only talk to the supervisor by typing into its pane with `cmux send`; at one point reviewer-1's Claude "Dangerous rm" confirmation dialog paused the whole fleet; about 2 minutes later Claude Code 2.1.316 rejected the command itself, the dialog disappeared and the slot's `prompt_wait` was cleared, yet the fleet stayed paused: 8 messages for the supervisor were queued and never delivered, for 15 minutes, until a manual `resume`. This section does not change the §1 division of labor, the single daemon, the private Redis or the task/service protocol; it does three things.

### 48.1 `operator`: outside messages to the supervisor bypass the input box

- `fleetmux operator --config <run>/config.json "<text>"` (no text or `-` reads stdin; `--wait` defaults to 30 seconds). The CLI reads the current `generation` / `thread` of `fx:agent:supervisor` and posts an `operator_input` event on the fleet's private Redis bus (the same event and the same `wait_reply` as the pi extension and the Claude `UserPromptSubmit` hook `claude_prompt`; the extra `source: cli` only goes into the audit), then prints the daemon's reply JSON: `accepted` (service carries `id`, task carries `task_id`) exits 0; `rejected` carries `reason` and exits 2; no reply in time is `pending` (with a correlation id; the event is already in the stream and the daemon will process it sooner or later, do not resend) and exits 3. Accepted text reaches the supervisor as a formal delivery per §5: pi through the bridge, a Claude supervisor through the Stop hook mailbox.
- The definite outcome per supervisor (the `operator_input` branch of `Fleet.handle`; the former single message "supervisor is not ready or this input belongs to an old session" is split into separate reasons, and the CLI makes the same decision locally first, `operator_channel_problem`): pi -> accepted; Claude (a directly started v2 supervisor with `claude_supervisor_hook_delivery`, or an adopted supervisor with `adopt.hook: true`) -> accepted, delivered through the hook mailbox; Codex -> rejected: "a Codex supervisor has no operator queue: `codex queue` only queues the daemon's own deliveries; type in the Codex supervisor pane yourself, or submit with `request` / `task -`" - this follows the existing design: no new channel is invented, and `fx:advice:notes` is not borrowed (that is only a note appended to the end of the next supervisor message, not a delivery); a Claude supervisor without a hook mailbox (old run) -> rejected with an explanation; wrong generation / session -> "belongs to an old supervisor session"; supervisor not ready -> reports its current state; v1 run -> rejected.

### 48.2 When the confirmation dialog is gone, a pause caused only by it lifts automatically

- This reuses the §4 handoff-timeout approach "whoever paused it lifts it" (`handoff_pause_arm` / `handoff_resume_tick`): `RuntimeV2.prompt_wait` (dialog handed to the user: pause dispatch) reads `fx:service` once before `pause()`; after the pause, `prompt_pause_arm` records `prompt_pause = {scope, at, generation, label}` on the slot. It records only when the fleet was not paused before (or the earlier pause was this very slot's own dialog, same `at`); otherwise nothing is recorded - someone else's pause is not its to lift.
- At the end of every tick (`handoff_resume_tick`), `prompt_pause_lift` runs for each marked slot: if `fx:service` is still paused and `at` has not changed (any `pause()` rewrites `at`: BLOCKED, a slot hold, the link-loss cap, a manual pause, another slot's dialog, a daemon restart recovery), the run still exists, the generation is unchanged, this slot's `prompt_wait` is cleared (the dialog was answered, rejected by the CLI itself, or the turn moved on - the watchdog clears it on transcript activity / end of delivery / scope change), no other slot is waiting for the user to answer a dialog, and the brake has not tripped / the budget is not exceeded -> `resume()`, audit `execution.auto_resumed` (`detail.source = prompt_wait`), and the slot and the supervisor each get a card `确认框已不在…已自动解除` (the confirmation dialog is gone ... lifted automatically). Blocked by the brake / budget -> `execution.auto_resume_refused`, left to a human. `at` changed or the run ended -> the mark is dropped and the pause stays. Dialog still there -> the mark stays until the next tick.
- The slot need not be idle: after the CLI rejects the command the turn is still running and the lease is still received; what resumes is dispatch to other slots. A dialog ruled on by the supervisor (`prompt_escalate`, route=supervisor) never pauses in the first place, so it is never marked. Delete-type dialogs remain "user only": the program never presses a delete dialog for the user; this only handles the pause after the dialog is already gone. The shared tail `pause_owner_resume` (brake check -> resume -> audit -> card) is factored out; the handoff-timeout path now calls it too, with unchanged behavior.

### 48.4 A usage warning is not a wall (added the same day)

Observed: in the channels dev fleet the whole fleet was braked to a stop twice within 40 minutes, audit `brake.tripped` "model_pool wall … no lower tier on this", while the screen showed Claude Code 2.1.316's reminder line "You've used 78% of your weekly limit · resets 3am (America/Toronto) · /usage-credits to keep using…" (the same at 84%). Root cause: the `model_pool` entry of the wall table had a bare `/usage-credits`, which also appears in the reminder line, so it was taken as a pool wall; Opus has no lower tier -> the brake paused.

- The wall table gains `notices` (`fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml#walls`): `You've used <N>% of your` and `/usage-credits to keep using` (the second half when a narrow pane wraps the reminder onto two lines). `brake.classify_wall` first drops lines that match `notices`, then checks account level -> pool level in order; so a reminder line is neither an account wall nor a pool wall.
- The bare `/usage-credits` in `model_pool` is replaced by the sentence of the wall itself, `Run /usage-credits to continue`; the texts for a real exhausted limit are unchanged: account level "You've hit / reached your weekly / session / usage … limit", "… limit reached", "Claude usage limit reached"; pool level "You've reached your Fable limit", "Set up usage credits".
- Tests: `tests/test_brake.py::test_percent_usage_reminder_is_not_a_wall` (the original 78% / 84% texts, with the `⎿` prefix, wrapped, `screen_wall`; red on the old code: classified as model_pool) and `test_spent_pool_and_account_limits_still_trip_next_to_a_usage_reminder` (a reminder line followed by a real wall still classifies as model_pool / account).
- Known gap: a private fleet.yaml (`FLEETMUX_CONFIG`) that overrides the whole `walls` block must add `notices` itself and drop the bare `/usage-credits`; the wall regex in OpenClaw's `multi_agent_daemon.py` (whose comment says to change it in step) lives in another repo and was not touched on this branch.

### 48.3 Inventory: every path that could type into a Claude pane

There is only one typing primitive at the bottom, `send_prompt_to_idle` (three backends; a Claude pane goes to `claude_input.send`). Handling is in three layers: ① configuration layer - a configuration that would need typing is refused outright at `start` / `start --resume` / daemon / actor / roster load (`runtime_v2.typed_claude_problem`, the reason quotes the rule); ② call sites - the Claude branch never calls the typing primitive, it holds the slot / marks it blocked and writes the reason; ③ last line - `claude_input.send` refuses unconditionally (no paste, no keys), so no backend can type into a pane registered as Claude.

| Path (file:line before the change) | When it fires | Before | Handling |
|---|---|---|---|
| `runtime_v2.type_into` 2572 | v2 delivery to a Claude slot without a hook mailbox (worker `claude_hook_delivery: false` / §28; the Claude supervisor of an old run; an adopted supervisor without `adopt.hook`) | typed | refused at the configuration layer; at the call site Claude is not typed into, the slot is held with the reason |
| `RuntimeV2.step` startup nudge 688 | a non-hook Claude slot not READY after start | typed | same as above (slot held, "startup nudge not sent") |
| `RuntimeV2.clear_tick` 1620 (§28 `/clear`) | `clear_after_result` after every result | typed `/clear` | `--clear-after-result` refused at start (a hook has no way to trigger `/clear`; for a clean context use §37 `"fresh": true`); the call site no longer types, it holds the slot |
| `Fleet.send` 6222 (v1) | a v1 run sends a pointer to a non-hook Claude | typed | refused at the configuration layer (v1 Claude supervisor, `claude_hook_delivery: false`); the call site refuses to send and records `send.refused` |
| `subfleet.adopt` 1010 | a sub-fleet adopts a Claude pane as supervisor with `adopt.hook` false (parent run older than `adopted_supervisor_hook_delivery`) | typed the role change | the parent fleet's `subfleet.check` refuses to open that sub-fleet; the child config validation refuses too; the call site marks blocked without typing |
| `subfleet.collect` 1281 | sub-fleet reclaimed, role-change notice to a non-hook Claude worker | typed | Claude always takes the hook branch (the next generation starts with CLI arguments, the original §19 R1-01 approach) |
| `Fleet.midturn_type` 4967 (§34) | mid-turn rotation prompt | Claude already used the PostToolUse hook (`claude_posttool`), no typing | unchanged (the bracketed argument for Claude is removed; Claude never gets here) |
| start / rotation / handoff / fresh first prompt | every Claude session start | CLI argument (`prompt_file` -> `"$(<file)"`) | unchanged |
| READY correction, three-strike correction, handoff delivery, sub-fleet end notice, advisor note, peer notice | while running | all via Redis delivery -> hook mailbox (same as `type_into`) | unchanged; the non-hook case is the same as the first row |
| operator messages | someone outside wants to talk to the supervisor | only `cmux send` typing into the pane | new `operator` subcommand (48.1) |
| `--no-claude-hook-delivery` / `--clear-after-result`, roster `subfleets[].claude_hook_delivery: false` / `clear_after_result: true` | user configuration | switches for the typing channel | flags kept but refused at start with the reason; the whole roster is rejected |
| `lifecycle_close` | closing a workspace | closes panes only, sends no text | unchanged |
| `backends/cmux.py:_type_command` | when cmux opens a pane it pastes the start command into the **login shell** | a shell, not a Claude input box | out of scope, kept |
| Control keys: watchdog `KEYS` (Enter / Escape / arrow keys / `y` `n` `1`-`3`, pressed only when a confirmation dialog is recognized), the various `Escape` turn aborts (`fleet.py` 4469 / 6843, `runtime_v2` 2232 / 2890, `subfleet` 1267, watchdog 737) | answering a dialog / aborting | key presses, not typing text into the input box | left as is, as required |
| Test tools `scripts/smoke_claude_input.sh`, `scripts/smoke_pair.sh` check 4 | smoke / acceptance fixtures simulating a human typing | via `send_prompt_to_idle` | now refused on a Claude pane (broken; to be made manual or switched to testing `operator`); not a fleet runtime path |

pi / Codex / opencode / dsh panes are outside this section (Codex uses `codex queue`; pi / opencode / dsh use the bridge).

**Verification**: new `tests/test_no_claude_typing.py` (real private Redis + real daemon step/pump/tick + fake panes; red: 35 failures on main, all green on this branch) - ① operator: a pi supervisor accepts and queues with zero pane traffic; a Claude hook supervisor accepts and delivers through the Stop hook mailbox with zero typing; a Codex supervisor is refused both locally and by the daemon with a reason; an old generation gets its own reason; the CLI exits 2 on rejection and exits 0 on accepted stdin input. ② dialog gone: sole cause -> automatic resume, one audit and one card each, other slots dispatched as usual; afterwards a manual pause / another slot's hold / another slot's dialog / a daemon recovery -> no resume, mark dropped; already paused before -> no mark; brake tripped -> `auto_resume_refused`; another slot still waiting for the user to answer a dialog -> waits until that one is gone too; dialogs ruled by the supervisor are not marked. ③ no typing: all seven typing configurations are refused by `validate_config` (the reason cites §48), the five hook-channel ones pass; the two retired `start` flags are refused before provisioning; the two roster keys reject the whole roster; `claude_input.send` neither pastes nor presses keys; `type_into` / startup nudge / `clear_tick` / v1 `Fleet.send` hold the slot or refuse on a Claude slot without a hook, `mgr.sent` stays empty; all three backends refuse a Claude pane (`test_claude_editor_send.py::test_claude_pane_is_never_typed_into`); an old parent run may not hand a Claude pane to a sub-fleet (`test_subfleet.py::test_adopted_supervisor_handover_without_a_hook_channel_is_refused`).
Old tests changed as a consequence: cases that only verify the typing channel itself are marked `RETIRED` (skipped, the reason points to this section) - `test_claude_editor_send.py` (87 paired-backend paste transactions), `test_claude_editor_runtime.py` / `test_claude_editor_review_repro.py` (old typed runs), `test_clear_after_result*.py` (§28 behavior), `test_claude_supervisor_hook_edges.py` (old v2 run kept typed), 11 cases and 1 parameter for typed adoption / typed reclaim in `test_subfleet.py`, 3 in `test_subfleet_named_tmux.py` and 2 in `test_subfleet_tmux.py` (all use typed same-session reclaim); `test_acceptance_fixes.py` / `test_escalate.py` / `test_long_split.py` / `test_subfleet_named_tmux.py::adopt_view_child` (borrowed by `test_liveness_selfheal.py`) now receive role changes through the real hook; the `tree` / `svc` fixtures of `test_subfleet.py` now pin `adopted_supervisor_hook_delivery` (a new start pins it anyway), and `adopt_child` waits for the role change through the real hook, so most sub-fleet cases now run on the hook channel.

**Known gaps**:
- Two slots raise dialogs one after the other: the second `pause()` rewrites `at`, the first mark becomes void, and the second is not marked because the fleet was already paused; even after both dialogs are gone a manual `resume` is needed. This is the literal, conservative reading of "if there is another pause source, stay paused".
- Upgrading old runs: Claude supervisor runs started before this version (no `claude_supervisor_hook_delivery`), v1 Claude supervisor runs, runs with `--no-claude-hook-delivery` / `--clear-after-result`, and parent runs older than `adopted_supervisor_hook_delivery` that adopt a Claude pane - on this code `start --resume` and daemon start both refuse with an explanation; start a new run.
- §28 clear-after-every-result is disabled: no hook can trigger `/clear`. To save tokens use §37 `"fresh": true` instead (the cost is one cold start and generation +1 per item); "automatically fresh after every result" would be separate work (mark the slot `fresh_pending` when the result is published). The §28 text and code are kept as history and are unreachable through configuration.
- A Codex supervisor still has no programmatic operator entry (only typing in its own pane, or `request` / `task -`).
- The smoke / acceptance scripts in the table above now fail where they simulate a human typing into a Claude pane; not changed.
- Some of the retired typed sub-fleet cases test mechanisms unrelated to the channel (autostart crash loop, roster hot-reload removal, drain timeout, parent resume reclaiming an external sub-fleet, same-session reclaim, tmux two-workspace display and crash reclaim); they all used typed reclaim before. On the hook channel reclaim means "retire the session, start the next generation", so they need to be ported separately.
- This section had no isolated smoke test on the real Claude / pi / Codex TUIs; the results for the three supervisor kinds come from integration tests with real Redis + real daemon + real hook entry points.

## 49. Blocked manual input goes back into the input box; pressing Enter again does not register it twice

- **Incident**: in the channels dev fleet the orchestrating session pasted a change-of-plan instruction into the Opus supervisor's box; the `UserPromptSubmit` hook (`claude_prompt`) registered it as `operator_input` per §5 and blocked it; a few minutes later it was registered twice more, seconds apart, so the supervisor received the same instruction three times, while the original text stayed in the box the whole time.
- **Mechanism (measured on the real TUI, Claude Code 2.1.316)**: the line for which the hook returns `decision: block` is put back into the input box as is; pressing Enter again fires the hook again. The fleet used to register it as a new instruction each time.
- **Approach**: after `claude_prompt` registers successfully (`accepted`, or the event is in the stream but the reply has not arrived), it writes the sha256 of the line with leading/trailing whitespace stripped, the generation, the session and the registration id into `<prefix>:hook:<slot>:operator_last`. Submitting the same line again in the same generation and session -> not registered, audit `hook.prompt_resubmit`, reply `这段文字已登记过（<id>），没有再排队` + `按 Ctrl+C 清掉即可` (already registered (ID), not queued again; the box holds the restored text, clear it with Ctrl+C; to really send it again, change it first). Only the "previous one" is compared: if other text was submitted in between (the box content changed), typing the same sentence again registers as usual. A rejected (`rejected`) line is not recorded; resending after the state is fixed is handled as usual.
- **Scope**: only the manual input entry of a Claude supervisor; outside messages bypass the input box through §48 `operator`. The pi supervisor's input box is outside this section.
- **Test**: `tests/test_claude_supervisor_hook.py::test_restored_operator_line_resubmitted_is_not_queued_twice` (real private Redis + real daemon; red on the old code: the second Enter queued again).

## 50. One command name: `fleetmux`

This section is the design record of the change.

- **One entry point**: the only command is `fleetmux`. The Rust binary itself handles `--version`, `--help` / `-h`, `server`, `attach`, `reap`, `web`, `gui --runtime D`; every other subcommand (everything in `fleet.py` `cli()`: start, task, request, status, stop, resume, pause, results, logs, operator, channel, subfleet, advisor, night, agents, plan, peer, chrome, gui without `--runtime` ...) is `exec`ed as is by `rust/crates/app/src/forward.rs` to the Python command line of the same release: `<release>/.venv/bin/python <release>/fleetmux/fleet.py <args>`. exec replaces the process, so argv, stdin / stdout / stderr and the exit code are Python's own. Rust does not know the fleet subcommand table; a new Python subcommand needs no Rust change.
- **Order for finding Python** (first match wins): ① `$FLEETMUX_PYTHON_CLI` (an executable, run as is, e.g. `<release>/.venv/bin/fleetmux-py`; set but not executable is an error, no further search) -> ② the release containing this binary (two levels above `<release>/bin/fleetmux`, tried once via the invoked path and once via the real path after resolving links) -> ③ `~/fleetmux-current`. A directory counts if it has an executable `.venv/bin/python` and `fleetmux/fleet.py`. None found: stderr lists the places checked and how to set `FLEETMUX_PYTHON_CLI` or install a release with `install_release.sh --activate`, exit code 127; a failing exec itself exits 126.
- **`gui` does not loop**: Rust only checks whether the word `--runtime` is among the arguments: if so it opens the window itself, otherwise it forwards to Python. Python's `gui` (scans resumable runs, writes grey cards) finally only execs `fleetmux gui --runtime <this HOME's runtime>`, a form that Rust handles itself and never forwards again (pinned by `tests/test_fleetmux_command.py`, `crates/app/tests/forward_cli.rs`). `fleetmux gui --devtools` without runtime -> forwarded to Python, argparse reports an unknown argument, exit 2.
- **Other behavior unchanged**: the `fleetmux --version` output format is unchanged (`deps.yaml` / `check_deps.py` / `backends/fleetmux.py` rely on it); no arguments still prints usage on stderr and exits 2; `--help` prints two lines to stdout and exits 0, the second line says to use `fleetmux <subcommand> --help` for fleet subcommands. No new crate was added; `cargo deny check licenses` is as before.
- **The Python CLI's own entry point is `fleetmux-py`**: `pyproject.toml` installs the console script `fleetmux-py` (`fleetmux.fleet:main`) into `<release>/.venv/bin/`, for launch scripts and `FLEETMUX_PYTHON_CLI`; `python -m fleetmux.fleet` works as well. It is not called `fleetmux`: that name belongs to the Rust binary in `<release>/bin/`, and whichever came first on `PATH` would win. An earlier version of this section kept the pre-fleetmux command, environment variables and release paths as permanent aliases; that was reversed: the aliases are gone, and only `fleetmux home migrate` (§50.1) still knows the old names.
- **Environment variable names**: every variable is `FLEETMUX_*`, read by that name only (four were renamed rather than prefixed: `FLEETMUX_BIN`, `FLEETMUX_WEB_BIN`, `FLEETMUX_REAL_BIN`, `FLEETMUX_THEME`). Programs that do not import the package read the same names: `scripts/install_release.sh` (`_PYTHON`, `_RELEASES`, `_TOOL_SHA`), `scripts/check_deps.py` (`_BACKEND`), the Rust `fleetmux` (`FLEETMUX_WEB_BIN`), `launchers/*.command` and `extensions/*.ts` (`_PI_DIR`, `_LEGACY_LAUNCHER`).
  - Do not confuse: `FLEETMUX_CONFIG` is the path of the private fleet.yaml; `FLEETMUX_CONFIG_DIR` (default `~/.fleetmux/config`, see §50.1; channel credentials / security.yaml) is something else.
- **Release directory**: the root of `install_release.sh` = `$FLEETMUX_RELEASES`, else `~/fleetmux-releases`; `--activate` points `~/fleetmux-current` at the new release. Nothing else next to `HOME` is created or moved.
- **Session names**: the main fleet's registry / session is `fleetmux`, a sub-fleet's `fleetmux:<task id>` (registry files `<state>/fleetmux*.json`, runtime dirs `<state>/fleetmux/<token>`, `<state>/wezterm/<token>`); an unmanaged Redis socket of an older config is `/tmp/fleetmux-<token>.sock`. Plugin ids: dsh `fleetmux-bridge`, opencode `fleetmux.bridge` / `fleetmux.bridge.tui` (package `fleetmux-opencode-bridge`).
- **Not changed**: the Redis keys `fx:*`, the `<run>/config.json` field names (including `cmux_state_dir` / `cmux_bin`, which name the cmux terminal backend), the kit and roster formats (external contracts).
- **Tests**: `tests/test_fleetmux_command.py` (readers in a new process getting the `FLEETMUX_*` values, `usage: fleetmux`, Python gui only execs the form with `--runtime`, install_release root and current link - temporary HOME); `tests/test_install_release.py` (the start command names `<release>/.venv/bin/fleetmux-py`); `rust/crates/app/tests/forward_cli.rs` (argv / stdin / stdout / stderr / exit code passed through unchanged when an unknown subcommand is forwarded, `gui` dispatch with / without runtime, search order, 127 and the hint when nothing is found, a wrong `FLEETMUX_PYTHON_CLI` does not fall back, `--version` format, `--help`, no arguments exits 2).

### 50.1 One data directory: `~/.fleetmux/`

Each user's fleetmux data lives under one root, `~/.fleetmux/`:

| Content | Default | Environment variable (wins) |
|---|---|---|
| fleet state root: `runs/`, `latest.json`, `codex-home/`, private `fleet.yaml`, `quota.json`, `discovered.yaml`, `dsh-home/` | `~/.fleetmux/` | `FLEETMUX_HOME` |
| window registry, fleetmux runtime | `~/.fleetmux/state/` | `FLEETMUX_STATE` |
| channel accounts, allowlists, leases, `secrets.env`, `security.yaml`, cursor mirrors | `~/.fleetmux/config/` | `FLEETMUX_CONFIG_DIR` |
| launcher private `KEY=value` | `~/.fleetmux/launchers.env` | `FLEETMUX_LAUNCHER_ENV` |
| session progress board | `~/.fleetmux/board/` | `FLEETMUX_BOARD_DIR` |
| a second fleet's home / state | `~/.fleetmux/fleets/<name>/`, `~/.fleetmux/fleets/<name>/state/` | `FLEETMUX_HOME` / `FLEETMUX_STATE` |

- **Resolution (`fleetmux/paths.py`; every reader calls only this)**: the environment variable when set, else the default above. The private `fleet.yaml`, `quota.json` and the shared `dsh-home` follow the default root, **not** `FLEETMUX_HOME` (a per-fleet home under `~/.fleetmux/fleets/` reads the same private config).
- **Start-up guard**: the human-typed commands check once for data or settings under the names used before fleetmux (`fleetmux/home_migrate.py` `check`): old data whose new place is not in use yet, a `FLEETMUX_HOME` / `FLEETMUX_STATE` path under an old name, an old environment variable without its `FLEETMUX_*` value. `start` / `resume` / `setup` / `gui` stop with one line saying what to do (exit 2); `status` / `stop` print the line and go on. A fleet started by an older release is stopped with that release's own CLI before migrating (its main run records no session name, so this version would look at another registry). Internal entry points (hooks, actors, the daemon) never check, so an agent in a pane never sees the line and never starts a migration itself. Old data is never read in place.
- **Migration only: `fleetmux home migrate [--dry-run]`** (a Python subcommand; the Rust `fleetmux` forwards it as is, §50; `fleetmux/home_migrate.py` is the one module that knows the old names). It moves `~/.cmux-fleet` -> `~/.fleetmux` (child by child, since `~/.fleetmux` exists already for `board/`), `~/.local/state/cmux-fleet` -> `~/.fleetmux/state`, `~/.config/fleetmux/launchers.env` -> `~/.fleetmux/launchers.env`, `~/.config/fleetmux` -> `~/.fleetmux/config`, every per-fleet home `~/.cmux-fleet-<name>` (only one holding fleet data) -> `~/.fleetmux/fleets/<name>` and `~/.local/state/cmux-fleet-<name>` -> `~/.fleetmux/fleets/<name>/state` (only together with its home, or when that home is already migrated, and never when the old home has a `state/` of its own). Each move is a `rename` on one file system and leaves a symlink at the old path; across file systems, when both sides hold content, or when an old home child is named like another item (`board` / `state` / `config` / `launchers.env` / `fleets`) the item is skipped and reported, never merged, exit 1; data is never deleted (only an emptied old directory is `rmdir`ed before its symlink). After the moves, under `~/.fleetmux` and every `~/.fleetmux/fleets/<name>`: registry files and runtime dirs named after the old session (`cmux-fleet*`) get the `fleetmux` name, and every run record (`latest.json`, `runs/*/config.json`, sub-fleet `sub/*/config.json`) is rewritten: moved paths to their new place (by path prefix, so `run`, `fleet_home`, `cmux_state_dir`, `private_config`, `roster`, role files ... all follow), `/tmp/cmux-fleet-<x>.sock` to `/tmp/fleetmux-<x>.sock`, session names `cmux-fleet` / `cmux-fleet:<id>` (also inside a workspace id such as `fleetmux:cmux-fleet`) to `fleetmux` / `fleetmux:<id>`, `CMUX_FLEET_*` environment names to `FLEETMUX_*`. Run records then no longer depend on the symlinks; other files in a run (roster, prompts, a home's `start.sh`) may still name old paths, which the symlinks keep valid. Old releases (`~/cmux-fleet-releases`) are not moved: a record may still pin one as its `python`. Any fleet process on this machine (the fleet CLI of this or an older release, `agent_bridge.py`, `fleetmux server|gui|attach|web`, anything holding a path inside a directory about to move, such as a fleet's `tmux -S` socket) or a run whose daemon holds its lock -> refuse, list them, exit 2 (`--dry-run` only reports). Repeated runs -> `nothing to do`, exit 0.
- **Launchers**: `launchers/start-dsh.command` reads `~/.fleetmux/launchers.env` (or `$FLEETMUX_LAUNCHER_ENV`); a file still at the pre-fleetmux place stops it with one line pointing at `fleetmux home migrate`. `guard` counts `~/.fleetmux/launchers.env` as a config file.
