- **Tests**: `tests/test_paths_unify.py` (temporary HOME: environment variables win, defaults under the root, private files do not follow `FLEETMUX_HOME`, inventory of the root and `fleets/*`, launcher file); `tests/test_home_migrate.py` (the guard's lines and exit codes, dry-run, moves + symlinks, per-fleet homes, run-record rewrite with the symlinks removed afterwards, records under an already-new home, refused while a fleet runs, skips, idempotent, process recognition, the CLI in a new process).

### 50.2 Package and directory names

- **Python package**: `fleetmux/` (`python -m fleetmux.fleet`, `fleetmux/fleet.py`); console script `fleetmux-py` (§50).
- **Rust workspace**: `rust/`; the binaries are `fleetmux` and `fleetmux-web`.
- **Environment variables**: see the "Environment variable names" item of §50.

## 51. Named sub-fleet workers can run pi; a new session after every result: `worker_cli` / `pi_model` / `fresh_after_result`

Background: three sub-fleets under an ops service fleet needed their workers switched to pi + DeepSeek (WorkBuddy, through a local gateway), but `subfleet.child_worker_cfg` hard-coded `worker_cli = 'claude'` (§19); these three sub-fleets used to get a clean context from the §28 `/clear` after every item, which §48 has retired.

- **Roster keys** (`subfleets[]` entries, `roster.subfleet_cli_errors`): `worker_cli` accepts only `claude` (default) / `pi` - opencode / dsh startup needs the dedicated `start` checks (`opencode_start_problem` / `dsh_adapter.start_problem`), which sub-fleet startup does not run, so they are not allowed yet. `worker_cli: pi` requires `pi_model` (the `provider/model` of pi models.json) and may carry `pi_thinking` (same values as on a roster row); `pi_model` / `pi_thinking` without pi rejects the whole roster. Rows of a pi sub-fleet's child roster may not set `profile` (a card would switch that slot to another CLI, and mixed CLIs are refused under v2 anyway; all workers run the entry's `pi_model`).
- **Child config**: `child_worker_cfg` takes the entry's `worker_cli` (unset = claude, never inherited from the parent fleet); `pi_model` / `pi_thinking` / `fresh_after_result` work like the other entry keys (the entry's value, else the built-in default, never inherited from the parent); the parent fleet's `pi_models` / `pi_thinkings` / `pi_home` are never carried into the child config. The supervisor is still the adopted Claude pane of the parent fleet; the pair claude x pi service is already in the cli_pairs table.
- **pi home**: `spawn_child` builds an isolated pi home `sub/<name>/pi-home` for a pi sub-fleet (`pi_adapter.prepare_home`, source = the parent config's `pi_agent_dir`, the same as `start` does for a pi run; a named sub-fleet archives the previous round's directory every time it starts, so it never collides with an existing one).
- **Start gate** (`subfleet.check` -> `named_pi_problem`): the parent config's `pi` binary can be found and the parent fleet runs with `--allow-unsandboxed-pi` (pi has no sandbox, the same requirement as `start`); otherwise the program refuses `up` with a reason.
- **`fresh_after_result: true`**: when `RuntimeV2.publish` publishes a result that really was delivered to this slot (the same test as the original §28), it marks the slot `fresh_pending=1` + `result_fresh=1`; once the slot is idle and settled on the bus (`bridge_available=1`), `Fleet.idle_rotate` immediately runs the existing §37 `fresh_rotation` (next generation in the same slot, no handoff note), so by the time the next request arrives the new session is usually READY; if the request comes before the rotation, the fresh branch of `deliver()` rotates. `result_fresh` distinguishes "rotate after a result" from "a fresh dispatch was withdrawn": for the latter idle_rotate still clears the mark (the first version only set `fresh_pending`; the idle pump cleared it as a withdrawn dispatch and a worker answered its second item in the old session - covered by the added `test_a_result_fresh_mark_rotates_the_idle_worker_instead_of_being_dropped`). Works for any CLI; no typing. Chosen over a dispatch-level `"fresh": true` because the callers involved (a memory shell and a scanner) dispatch directly with `to`; the supervisor makes no dispatch decision, so nobody could set `fresh`.
- **Cost / gaps**: one extra CLI cold start + one READY turn after every result (about 10-20 seconds for pi), during which the slot is `starting` (a request arriving right after waits for it); every rotation saves an old screen snapshot and writes a history line (the `handoffs/` of a high-frequency sub-fleet grows). A pi pane returns results through the `fleet_result` tool; if the business instructions say "do not use any tools", the role file must state that `fleet_result` is the exception.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_subfleet_pi_workers.py` (roster keys accepted / rejected, no profile on pi rows, the child config inherits neither the parent's CLI nor its pi tables, after `up` the child config is pi + its own pi home and passes `validate_config`, refused by the program when the parent lacks `--allow-unsandboxed-pi`, `fresh_pending` after a published result with `fresh_after_result` on / off); 12 red on main, all green on this branch.

## 52. First-run discovery: local agent CLIs / login state / models; the default pair follows the user's ranking

Requirement: a fleetmux configuration wizard - the first time fleetmux opens it finds all local models by default, with the same strategy as Paseo and T3 Code; the models suited to supervise are astra, fable, opus, sol; all other models suit workers, in priority order opus5.5 > sol > deepseek > GLM > kimi > qwen3.8.

- **Same approach as T3 Code / Paseo**: only CLIs that are installed locally, recent enough and logged in count; fleetmux never installs or logs in for the user, and says plainly what is missing and how to fix it. `fleetmux setup` probes once and rewrites the cache; `fleetmux start` / `fleetmux gui` probe once automatically when there is no cache and print the result table in the current terminal. `fleetmux gui --runtime D` opens the window directly from Rust without Python and does not trigger it (§50).
- **Revised ranking (replaces the ranking above)**: for the supervisor the priority before a wall is Astra, fable, opus, sol, deepseek flash; for workers it is opus, sol, sonnet, terra, deepseek flash. It lives in `fleet.yaml#discovery`: `supervisor: [astra, fable, opus, sol, deepseek flash]`, `worker: [opus, sol, sonnet, terra, deepseek flash]`; unlisted models still rank last. A space inside a pattern = the parts appear in order, not necessarily adjacent (`deepseek flash` matches `deepseek-v4.1-flash-sg`, not `deepseek-v4-pro`). These two chains are also the order followed after a wall; the execution is in §23 "on a wall, move down a model chain" (same branch, `feat/wall-fallback-chain`).
- **Default worker thinking level is high**: `fleet.yaml#discovery.worker_thinking: high`. Without `--pi-thinking` the pi thinking level of the whole run takes it (previously off); a Claude worker not taken over by the advisor always gets `--effort <it>` (a slot the advisor takes over still uses the advisor's level, and the advisor's default level `advisor.DEFAULT_EFFORT` also changed from medium to high); a worker that switches to pi after a wall takes it as its thinking level (it travels with the wall chain into Redis `fx:wall:chains.worker_thinking`). An explicitly given level always wins.
- **Probing** (`fleetmux/discover.py`): CLIs are looked up on the login shell's PATH (a GUI launch lacks PATH, and cmux pane shim paths expire with the session), recording the realpath; minimum versions come from `deps.yaml`; login state: claude `auth status`, codex `login status`, pi / opencode count as logged in when their model list is non-empty; models: claude takes the aliases of model cards with cli=claude, pi `--list-models`, opencode `models`, ollama `list` (failure = installed but not running); each provider in pi `models.json` that has a `baseUrl` gets one `GET <baseUrl>/models` (no key, no proxy, 401 still counts as online), and models on an offline gateway are not candidates. Each probe has a 10-second timeout; probes run concurrently.
- **Ranking and default**: `fleet.yaml#discovery` (a block of the same name in the private file overrides it whole). Model ids are lowercased and split into words on `- / _ .` for contiguous matching; within one pattern newer versions come first, then `cli_order`; `only_via` enforces §6 (Claude models only through the official claude, gpt only through pi). Models that match only a supervisor pattern and no worker pattern (astra, fable) are supervisor-only; all others are workers, unlisted ones last. The default pair = the first pair, in order, of supervisor candidates x worker candidates whose `mode` in `cli_pairs` is verified; higher-ranked ones dropped because of the combination table are written into the result table as `passed over`.
- **Cache**: `<fleet home>/discovered.yaml` (0600, overridable with `FLEETMUX_DISCOVERED`); the private `fleet.yaml` is not written. `start` uses its default pair only when neither `--supervisor-cli` nor `--worker-cli` is given, and fills in only the model arguments not given (`--supervisor-model` / `--worker-model` now parse with default None and are filled in start: command line > cache default > `DEFAULTS`); without a cache or with `FLEETMUX_DISCOVERY=off` the behavior is as before. A failed probe only warns and never blocks start.
- **Privacy**: the login probe keeps only `logged_in` / `auth_method` (claude also returns the email and org, which are discarded); `models.json` is read only for provider names, `baseUrl` and model ids, `apiKey` is never read out or written to disk, and `auth.json` is not read; the cache and the output contain no gateway addresses. When the private `fleet.yaml` is readable by other users the result table suggests `chmod 600`; it does not change it for the user.
- **Not in this cut**: refusing an unusable `--pi-model` against the cache before starting (a stale cache would refuse wrongly; it would need a fresh probe); the result table in a fleetmux pane; the advisor's automatic model choice still only considers models with a model card. The default pair is checked as verified only for `discovery.mode` (task): when starting with `--mode service`, if the service row of the chosen pair is not verified, the combination table refuses it as usual (today pi+claude is verified in both modes). `discover.py` reads no repository file at import time (installed as site-packages without `scripts/` / `deps.yaml` it still starts, it just skips the minimum-version check); getting PATH from the login shell takes up to 10 seconds and is the main source of a slower first start.
## 53. Channels: WeChat / Feishu / Telegram / email / app push (this section is the P0 interface freeze)

Goal: give fleetmux inbound interfaces for WeChat, Feishu, Telegram, email and an app, so that fleetmux replaces every function of the earlier OpenClaw assistant gateway; WeChat must never go through a decryption route; deletes can be approved from a phone; the app is Paseo for now. The interface, item by item, is in `docs/contracts.md` §8; this section only states the design constraints. The single daemon, the private Redis, the slot / generation labels, the task / service protocols, the set of result states and the meaning of every existing Redis key are unchanged; **a fleet without `channels:` behaves exactly as before**.

- **In one sentence**: a channel = a second keyboard for the supervisor + a second exit for fleet events. All inbound traffic reuses §5's `operator_input` (the same path as typing in the supervisor pane: service creates a `ui-<uuid>` request, a running task queues `[Operator instruction]`, no task goes through `typed_task_allowed`; the refusal texts already exist); no new dispatch path is created; outside changes to the plan are told only to the supervisor, never given straight to a worker. Outbound watches existing state (`fx:result:*`, `fx:task`, `fx:escalations`, end of lifecycle) and pushes it out.
- **Where it attaches**: the resident service fleet on this machine (main fleet, transport v2). Task fleets and sub-fleets do not attach; a roster with `channels:` makes `start` refuse on those runs. One channel account serves only this machine's fleet; there is no cross-machine or cross-fleet routing (phase one only talks to the resident service fleet on this machine).
- **One actor child process per fleet, not a thread inside the daemon (approved by the supervisor, ruling C13)**: a crash or hang in a network library (lark-oapi and the like) must not take the daemon down, so the channels run in one child process of the daemon (`fleetmux channel actor <config.json>`). Inside it every roster entry (`type:account`) has its own inbound thread and its own outbound account queue (`fx:channel:outq:<type>:<account>`, each with its own rate limit / retry / dead letters); **a problem with one account (needs a new login, lease held by another run, adapter crash) stops only that account: the actor does not exit, other accounts are unaffected, the fleet is not paused and no slot is held**; an account with bad credentials retries when its account file changes or every 10 minutes, one whose lease is taken retries every 5 minutes. lark-oapi 1.5.3 allows only one WS per process, so a roster has at most one Feishu entry (added to `parse_channels` in P0b). The daemon starts and supervises this one process the way §1 has "the daemon manages agents": after an exit it restarts with a backoff starting at 30 seconds, doubling, capped at 5 minutes, and reset after 10 stable minutes (all configurable defaults, run config `channel_supervise`); persistent failure only raises an alert; a run without `channels:` starts no actor process; nothing is hung on launchd / cron. The daemon process imports no adapter. Feishu without lark-oapi only makes Feishu inbound `missing_dependency`; sending works as usual. Proxies are bypassed only per client scope; the process environment is never changed. When the parent daemon is gone, the actor exits by itself within 5 seconds and releases its leases.
- **Purely deterministic mapping, no LLM guessing intent**: plain text = operator input; `/status` is read-only; `/approve` `/reject` only answer the delete pending items pushed by item 5.2; any other `/word` gets `不认识的命令` (unknown command) and does not enter `operator_input` (as §5 "slash commands are not enqueued again"). Every refusal text goes back to the chat; silent swallowing is forbidden; senders outside the allow-list are only audited (`channel.drop_policy`), with no reply. A native Codex supervisor has no `operator_input` entry (§5 input boundary), so the reply is `这个 fleet 的监工不收中途输入` (this fleet's supervisor takes no mid-turn input). Images / files are downloaded to `<run>/inbox/files/`, their paths are appended to the body, and the message goes on as text; anything too large or unrecognized is refused explicitly (§5 "never drop an image silently").
- **origin (the settled form of design-draft item 11.4; rulings C7 / C8)**: the conversation that started a request is stored in a separate key `fx:channel:origin:<request id>`; **it is not put into `fx:req:<id>`**: `publish` deletes `fx:req:<id>` when the request is settled and the public result does not carry it, so outbound would not find it; a separate key also leaves `publish` and resume replay untouched. **The actor writes this key after the fleet answers `accepted`, with `EX` 7 days; `Fleet.handle` does not write it** (`Fleet.handle` is not changed for channels). There is a small window between the actor writing origin and the worker answering, so when the outbound watcher sees a result without origin it checks again next round while the result is under 60 seconds old, and only after 60 seconds treats it as an external request (marked pushed, not sent). A service request's result is pushed back to the originating conversation; when big work is moved to a task sub-fleet, the sub-fleet's follow-up result `<id>.done` (ok / failed) returns to the conversation of `<id>` by `envelope.origin_id('<id>.done') == <id>`. Requests without origin (command line, other programs) are not pushed. Splitting **reuses §44's existing mechanism** (the escalate answer carries `proposal` / `workers`, program-assigned `M<n>` numbers, approve may carry models, adoption after rotation); P7 only adds the service side: the original request is settled as `ok` + `delegated` in the `escalation_tick` beat that reads spawn `accepted`, followed by `<id>.done`, with the sub-fleet at `lifecycle=None` and the time limit `split_budget_h`. When a split settles, its failure / replay semantics and the sub-fleet lifecycle are in contracts §8. **Resume pins a split (branch fix/split-resume-pin)**: `start --resume` used to ignore the record of an `approved` / `rotating` split; `replay_request` reset the request to `routing / worker=''`, the ownership check in `process_requests` then always refused the spawn and the record fell back to `escalated`, so the approval was voided; for `rotating`, a restart before `split_rotate` wrote `split_launch` made `respawn` rotate and the second branch write the spawn directly, losing the model the supervisor chose. Now `resume_run` calls `Fleet.split_pin_on_resume` before `respawn`: if the request still exists (`fx:req:<id>` not settled), the slot still exists and the request is not owned by someone else, it is pinned back to `dispatched / worker=<slot>` (restoring the ownership from before the restart, not counted as a `replay`, audited as `split.pinned`; `requeue_requests` skips it, audited as `request.pinned_to_split`), and the spawn then proceeds normally (an existing spawn request file runs next beat; `rotating` goes through rotation); for `rotating` without `rotating_at` yet, `split_launch` is written first and `respawn` starts the new generation with the card the supervisor chose (when the slot is resumed with an unchanged generation, the first branch of `split_rotate` rotates as usual). Only a split that cannot be pinned (request already settled / slot not in the roster / request owned by another slot) is voided: `split_drop` + one line to the supervisor, `[escalate] M<n> approval lapsed on resume: <reason>`. An approval is a decision the supervisor has persisted (possibly with a chosen model); resume already honors an `accepted` spawn (S11), and it honors `approved` / `rotating` the same way; voiding is only the fallback. Known boundary: §17 `restart_lost` (one slot's session lost, not `--resume`) also replays that slot's requests through `replay_request`, and is not wired to pinning in this change.
- **Where proactive pushes go**: following OpenAlice `connector-registry.ts` "most recent interaction": task final states, confirmation dialogs, end of run and daemon down go to the channel / conversation of the allow-listed user's most recent private chat (`fx:channel:last`, mirrored to the local `state/last.json`); if the user has never written → the first channel in the roster whose account file sets `PUSH_TO` (`PUSH_TO` must be the allow-listed user's own one-to-one conversation; it cannot make the bot open a conversation, and a Telegram user must `/start` first); if neither exists → audit `channel.no_target`. Each channel pushes only the events listed under its roster `push:`.
- **Identity and security**: the allow-list accepts only the user (Telegram user id, Feishu open_id, WeChat `ilink_user_id`, email sender address **and** both DKIM and SPF passing and aligned with the From domain in the topmost `Authentication-Results` of the trusted receiving server; AR is only a mail header, not independent authentication, and holds only if the receiving service strips forged AR headers of the same name; to be verified on a real machine, no anti-spoofing claim is made), and only private chats. Every TLS connection verifies the certificate and the host name. The policy is enforced in the adapter base class (allow-list, private chat, authenticated, `message_id` idempotent for 1 hour), and every adapter runs the same parameterized contract tests. Credentials and the allow-list live only in the local `~/.fleetmux/config/` (§50.1; old default `~/.config/fleetmux/` until migrated; changeable with `FLEETMUX_CONFIG_DIR`), never in the repo, a kit, the run directory or any directory a worker can read; errors, audits and logs never carry credential values. Inbound text gets no extra privilege: what it can do = what typing in the supervisor pane can do.
- **One consumer per credential**: a machine-level lease `<config dir>/leases/<type>-<account>.lock` (flock + holder run id). If it cannot be taken → that channel does not open, audit `channel.lease_busy`, shown in control, never silent (Telegram allows one poller per token; Feishu delivers an app's events to one connection only). The lease covers only receiving; sending takes no lease, so the supervisor can still `channel send` while the daemon is down.
- **Deletes can be approved on a phone (design draft §5.2)**: only delete-type terminal confirmation dialogs (§24 delete classification; whether opencode / dsh delete permission requests are covered is left open), with a pending record registered only in runs that configure `channels:` (`fx:channel:pending:<p-id>`, `p-` + 8 hex digits, P6b's `channels/approve.py`; not in `fx:escalations`); the card is pushed only to the allow-listed user's private chat; two-step confirmation: step one `/approve <p-id>` makes `approve.handle_command` reply with a confirmation card carrying a one-time nonce (16 hex digits); step two `/approve <p-id> <nonce>` or `/reject <p-id>` goes as a `dialog_user_answer` event to `RuntimeV2.user_dialog_answer` for checking (the nonce appears only in the card sent to the user's private chat; Redis stores only its hash; it never enters audits / supervisor messages / exec); the confirm step lasts 120 seconds and the record 1800 seconds (configurable positive defaults in run config.json, approved by the supervisor; checks and behavior test-first in v2); expired means no key press; before pressing, the watchdog re-captures the screen on its next beat and runs the full `answer_dialog` check (generation, session, scope, pane ownership, screen-tail digest, same dialog, delete classification); if anything changed it does not press and replies "changed, please look again"; **approval presses only a consent key explicitly registered in the prompt table; inferring Enter from `YES_CURSOR` is forbidden; with no registered key no approval is offered** (where it is registered: the `watchdog_prompts.user_approve` block of an explicit `--prompt-table` file, empty by default, without widening what `config/fleet.yaml` and the private fleet.yaml may override; an entry's `match` may be the cursor text written by the operator, `keys` takes only keys the entry registers explicitly; extending `parse_table` / `TableCache` belongs to P6c. By default nothing is registered, so by default a phone can only reject; with a registration a positive approval works as usual and the seven review checks are unchanged); reject presses Escape; **pressing a key does not resume dispatch automatically** (the `prompt_wait` pause stays; the receipt points to the existing resume entry; the earlier `YES_CURSOR` fallback and the `channel_user_resume` auto-resume are void, the reviewed `6b879bd` is authoritative, and no automatic resume is allowed); audits `dialog.user_answered` / `dialog.user_refused`. **This item changes how §24's "delete-type dialogs always go to the user; neither the program nor the supervisor presses a key" is carried out, not the principle**: the authority for the key press comes from the user's own two-step confirmation in a private chat; the program only presses on the user's behalf. Supervisor decision JSON and the fleet CLI have no branch leading to this path (meaning delete dialogs `kind=dialog`; P8l's local `channel approve` only accepts `kind=action` + `tier=high` + `confirm.high == desk`, see contracts §8 "high-tier out-of-band methods"); a `dialog_user_answer` event without the correct nonce, sender and conversation is never accepted, pinned by tests; but the separate key and the nonce are **not** a security boundary against processes of the same OS user (see this section's known gaps). Non-delete dialogs are still ruled by the supervisor (§24); the channel only informs and does not take over the ruling.
- **Lifecycle notices**: when the roster has `channels:`, the `tg` path of `lifecycle_close` goes through the channels instead (the new config wins); without it `FLEETMUX_TG_CMD` works as before. Daemon down cannot be announced by the daemon itself: §36's "Daemon down" section of the supervisor AGENTS.md gains a step `channel send --event daemon_down`.
- **Direct to a worker (branch feat/service-tiers-direct)**: requirement: let a Slack caller talk straight to a worker without relaying through the supervisor every time. The lower layer has long had "a service request with `to` is dispatched directly" (memory extraction has always used it); only the channel entry was missing. Now: ① a message that opens by naming `@dev-1` / `dev-1:` → the program sends it as a service request with `to`; the supervisor is not involved; ② when the worker's result is pushed back, the thread under that message is recorded as belonging to that worker, and a reply in the thread goes straight back to it (same slot, continuing the session); ③ a new message that names no one goes to the supervisor as before. All parsing is deterministic; no model guesses. Every pushed result ends with a one-line hint on how to reach the worker directly. On Slack every reply goes into the thread under the user's message, and results are also `reply_broadcast` to the conversation (text posted only in the thread is visible on a phone only after opening it). Interface: contracts §8 "direct to a worker".
- **app**: Paseo for now (zero development: supervisor-pane input inside Paseo already goes through `operator_input`); pushes use `type: exec` (the outbound item JSON is handed to a configured external command, with no business address built in; the command is the `COMMAND=` line of the local account file, split into argv by `shlex` rules and run with `shell=False`, no shell). The delete-approval `channels/approve.py` belongs to P6b. A future self-built fleet app plugs in as a new adapter on the same envelope / mapping / outbound, with no separate dispatch path.
- **Slack (the 6th channel)**: a Slack app exists, and Socket Mode receiving of direct messages, replies in the same conversation, proactive pushes via `conversations.open` and button callbacks were tested on a real machine. The interface is the same as the other channels (contracts §8 "Slack specifics"): account file `SLACK_BOT_TOKEN` / `SLACK_APP_TOKEN` / `SLACK_ALLOW_USERS` (empty = reject all); the optional dependency `websocket-client==1.8.0` is only for fleets that configure slack, and when missing it only stops this one channel; **every send uses `unfurl_links=false`, `unfurl_media=false`**; Slack tokens (`xoxb-` / `xoxp-` / `xapp-`) are masked whole by `redact`. The P9a contract is committed separately after the second security freeze batch; the P9b / P9c implementation is done by slack-1.
- **WeChat**: only Tencent's official ClawBot (iLink protocol, rewritten with the Python standard library from the source of the MIT plugin 2.4.10; protocol details in contracts §8 "WeChat specifics", none of it verified on a real machine; the QR-scan `redirect_host` only changes the polling address, the business address comes from `confirmed.baseurl`; `bot_agent` honestly says fleetmux; the batch cursor is committed only after the batch is settled, not commit-first-then-process as the plugin does); local decryption, reading the WeChat database and pulling keys from memory are **forbidden**. QR login is the WeChat module-level `LOGIN` (per the 2.4.10 source, the POST that fetches the QR code carries no `base_info`; business POSTs do); the batch cursor is stored as `get_updates_buf` in this account's store; session expiry `-14` → health `needs_login`. Phase one is plain text only: `PENDING = ()`, inbound media is explicitly refused (`微信第一期只收文字`, WeChat phase one accepts text only); CDN media needs the protocol's AES and comes later. Provenance (P8): this phase does not carry `ref_msg`, does not query a quote cache and does not download quoted media, and P8b gives WeChat no always-empty provenance function; fixed media labels are the closed-constant text exception (no file names); top-level TEXT / VOICE is treated as direct input but its real author is not guaranteed; iLink cannot detect forwarding, which is listed as a capability limit, and no claim is made that source detection is complete on every platform (contracts §8 "WeChat specifics"). Three protocol questions (can a non-OpenClaw client send and receive, can a push go out without `context_token`, how many ClawBots can one WeChat account bind) need a real QR-scan test by the user and are outside the development run.
- **Copied, with the business removed**: the send/receive parts are copied from an earlier private notification bus (`channels/base.py`, `throttle.py`, queue retry / dead letters, `daemon.py` backoff, `dispatch.py` idempotency) and its notifier (plain urllib sending, 4000-character segments), with everything business-specific removed: group IDs, open_ids, account templates, trading cards, the engine banner, STTE, `oc-relay-feishu`. One contract test scans the `fleetmux/channels/` source to keep business leftovers out.
- **P0b patch (before A2)**: health states are unified as `ok` (normal) / `needs_login` (credentials rejected, or account file missing / broken; the error kind is still recorded as `auth` / `bad_config`; only this account stops) / `retrying` (transient failure); the actor reads only the `health()` state and never parses error text. Every error string and audit field goes through `channels.redact` (account values, `Authorization` / `Bearer`, `bot<token>` in URLs, values whose names contain token / secret / password). A roster has at most one Feishu entry (lark-oapi: one WS per process). Button commands longer than 64 bytes are not truncated: the Telegram adapter itself degrades the whole card into typed commands, and the shared `card_problem` no longer rejects long commands (M4b). New audits `channel.cursor_reset` / `channel.lease_reclaimed` / `dialog.user_resumed`. The send metadata `Channel.fleet_name` / `send_ref` (`channels.fleet_label(cfg)`) are only instance attributes; constructor and send signatures do not change. The actor hash gains `sending / sending_at / drain / drained`; the heartbeat runs on a separate thread every 10 seconds; the parent check looks only at `getppid`.
- **Security pack P8 (P8a frozen-part patch)**: every security mechanism is a code gate hung on the local `security.yaml` (not in the roster); a fleet without security.yaml behaves exactly as before (the only approved exception: when a cleaned email body has several lines and the first line is `/…`, it is delivered to the supervisor as `email_body` data and no longer treated as a command; only a single-line `/command` is a command, contracts §8 "email specifics" D-1). P8a changes only the frozen parts: the envelope gains `provenance` (`text` holds only what the user typed by hand), commands gain `/stop` and a third `/approve` argument, push events gain `security`, audits gain 17 `security.*` names, receipts gain 8, pending records gain `kind / tier / action_hash / action_text / request_id`, plus the new file `channels/security.py` (`parse_security` / `DEFAULTS` / `POLICY_KEYS` / `policy`). The other mechanisms (spotlighting, a tool-less reader, the `claude-pretool` pre-gate and `fx:guard:ticket`, `/stop` hard stop, the `chain-` audit chain, out-of-band confirmation desk / second_channel / totp, sandbox) are implemented by P8b–P8o per contracts §8 "security pack". With security on, PreToolUse authorizes the action only by a valid single-use ticket from the user; this is not the program / supervisor answering a delete dialog for the user (§24 delete classification, D8); the hook baseline without a policy is unchanged. Seams added by P8a: `handle_inbound` takes optional `policy=None` / `stop=None` (return protocol unchanged), `actor.channel_audit` is the single channel audit entry (writing this run's existing v2 audit stream), `base.Channel.link_preview = None`, 17 audit names (including `security.tool_call`, audited but never blocked, guaranteed only on P8j's pi bridge path; other CLIs by capability, with no coverage claim); `/stop`'s `kill` ends the run, which must be started again, and the stop marker is `<run>/state/stop.flag`. **Security freeze batch 1 (contracts §8)**: `audit_log.uappend` (default on; on macOS it tries to set `UF_APPEND` on the chain file; this guards against mistakes on a tamper-evident chain, it is not tamper-proof and the owner can revoke it; run `chflags -R nouappend` before deleting a run directory); `sandbox.codex_hooks` (default off; Codex is not treated as having a pre-gate until verified on a real machine); `security_source` is pinned when enabled; `fleetmux channel audit --verify`; the canary is planted at `start` and refilled at `start --resume` (a missing / broken / moved file refuses the resume); without a policy the `legacy_view` view is delivered (option M); `stage=allowed` only proves the hook let it through; the ticket key suffix is the local `action_hash`, or `route_hash` when routed; the epoch lives only in `fx:service.stop_epoch`, and any malformed shape fails closed. **Security freeze batch 2**: the stop protocol (contracts §8 "hard stop `/stop`" Z1–Z10, #17); the confirmation schema (C1–C7, O1, O7); sub-fleet root confirmation and the ancestor chain (`guard.verify_ancestry` is the only check; a sub-fleet without taint counts as clean only when the strict chain check passes and no ancestor carries taint; dropping or altering taint is refused, malformed taint becomes `['unknown']`; X1). Known gaps of the §53 hard stop `/stop` (core-3 Z11, [M14] approved): ① each stop request presses Escape at most once per slot; the stop request is persistent but a press on every slot is not guaranteed, and `sent` only means the backend accepted the key; ② Redis Lua does not roll back; the publish protocol converges by validating before writing + writing the dedup record last + replaying with the same id, so the epoch may be bumped once too often; ③ `pause` / `interrupt` act only on this fleet; sub-fleets are handled only in `kill`'s explicit stop tail through the existing `subfleet.stop_children`, and receipts and docs never claim the whole tree stopped; ④ `Fleet.resume()` does not clear the epoch; every confirmation from before the stop is void after a resume; ⑤ pressing Escape on a `starting` slot may make the CLI in its start-up dialog exit; ⑥ in the `kill` beat the audit mirror still runs once; audits after the explicit stop tail are no longer mirrored (P8g's convention); ⑦ a fleet without `security.yaml`: `/stop` replies `unknown_command`, with no `fx:channel:stop*` key, no `stop_epoch` field and no marker file.
- **Packages and freeze**: after P0 (this section, contracts §8, `channels/__init__.py` / `base.py` / `envelope.py`, `tests/test_channels_contract.py`) is frozen, these are implemented in parallel: P1 actor / lease / throttled queues / CLI / daemon supervision / roster validation / `FLEETMUX_TG_CMD` compatibility, P2 Telegram, P3 Feishu, P4 WeChat, P5 email, P6 delete approval + exec + daemon-down notices, P7 big work moved to a task sub-fleet; changing the interface must be reported to the supervisor, the commit's first line starts with `contract:`, and affected packages are retested. In the contract tests the rows of packages not yet landed are red (`red:P2-telegram` …, `test_red_p1_*` …) and turn green as each package lands; at the checkpoint after everything is merged the full suite must be all green.

**Verification (P0)**: `python3 -m pytest -q tests/test_channels_contract.py -k "not red"` all green (the frozen parts themselves + an in-process fake adapter running the whole adapter contract); `-k red` is the red skeleton of each package and is expected to fail. No real network, no real credentials, no listeners opened.

**Known gaps (P0 stage)**: residual risk of approving deletes from a phone: the supervisor, workers and daemon run as the same OS user, Claude slots are not sandboxed, and all of them can connect to the same private Redis socket, read and write the run directory and read process memory; such a process that really sets out to forge an answered pending record, or to read the nonce sent to the chat, cannot be stopped. The separate key, the one-time nonce and keeping the nonce out of prompts and audits guard against mis-taps, crossed items, replays and accidental borrowing; they make no claim against a deliberate forgery by a program with the same privileges; anything stronger needs a different OS user or a signed approval service, outside this phase. Inbound confirmation: the platform cursor advances only after the fleet answers (accepted, or deterministically refused; seen is only the actor's dedup claim and does not mean reliable receipt, see contracts §8 "Inbound acknowledgement, idempotency and replay boundary"); there is no inbox stream, and transient failures never advance the cursor, have no cap and never go to dead letters, so an entry that keeps failing blocks the messages behind it until the fault clears (cap / dead letters / who decides = still open); messages older than the platform's retention (Telegram 24 hours) while the fleet is stopped cannot be recovered. Cursors (Telegram offset, WeChat `get_updates_buf`, IMAP `UIDVALIDITY` + UID) are stored only in the run's private Redis; a brand-new run, a first binding or a `UIDVALIDITY` reset has no usable cursor; how to persist across runs is still open. For email, the first cursor-less start and a `UIDVALIDITY` reset are settled (P5b): the cursor is set to the current highest UID, **no backlog is fetched and nothing is replayed**, and `channel.cursor_reset` is audited (`reason / old / new / skipped`), so the skip is not silent; mail that arrives during downtime under the same `UIDVALIDITY` is still fetched from the cursor. For the other channels, behavior without a cursor must likewise be visible in audits and `channel status`. Catch-up after an IMAP disconnect and whether Feishu redelivers events missed during a disconnect are to be tested on a real machine. Real-link acceptance (a real bot, a real Feishu app, a ClawBot QR scan, a Gmail app password) is outside the development run and is done by the orchestrating session after delivery.

**Known limits and real-machine checks (stage B, from the package reports, docs checkpoint M131)**: ① **Slack acks before forwarding**: a Socket Mode frame is acked before the actor hands it to the fleet; if the actor process dies in between, that frame is lost (Slack does not redeliver acked frames); this loss window is accepted in this phase, and at-least-once is not claimed. ② **Feishu's default transport is not covered by tests**: since M89 the default is `feishu.py`'s own `http_transport` (with response headers), while the tests only use an injected fake transport; real-machine acceptance must really send a file or image once and subscribe to `card.action.trigger`. ③ **Email IDLE not checked against a real server**: the real behavior of Python 3.14 `imaplib` IDLE against Gmail is untested and belongs to the real-machine receive acceptance after delivery; the AR authentication premise is likewise to be verified on a real machine. ④ **Out-of-memory (OOM) behavior of the hard-stop publish is not guaranteed** (contracts §8 Z5, M98): not measured with a small `maxmemory`; never claim "refused before the script starts, zero writes". ⑤ **WeChat backoff / email pause make `poll()` return `[]` at once**: spinning is prevented by the actor's `inbound_loop` (`poll(20)` per round, a healthy round sleeps a full 1 second, failures back off 30→300 seconds, M79); WeChat's 3600-second no-API pause after `-14` lives only in the adapter instance's memory, so a separately created `send_direct` instance still calls the API and gets `-14` again. ⑥ **Feishu quoted originals and merged forwards are not fetched**: only one `file_refused` is returned (`飞书引用的原文未取，需要的话请直接粘贴` (the quoted Feishu original was not fetched; paste it directly if needed) / merged forward); no text is faked. ⑦ **`prompt_wait` overwrites `fx:service.reason`**: an earlier operator pause stays paused, but its reason text is replaced by the delete dialog's reason (existing behavior, unchanged in this phase). ⑧ **Throttling**: receipts (`reply`) and `user_only` items are never held, but they count toward the storm window, so a run of receipts can push ordinary items in the same conversation into storm merging; notify_bus's `is_too_old`, synchronous circuit breaker, metrics and business gates were not copied. ⑨ **Lease files are never deleted**: a normal release clears the holder, so `channel status` does not show the previous holder; only a crashed / killed holder leaves a record, for reclaiming (`channel.lease_reclaimed`). ⑩ **Default outbound push**: when the actor's `Channel` has no `ch.push`, `DEFAULT_PUSH` applies (escalation off), not all events (M119 C2). ⑪ **A Feishu merged forward gets two refusals** (recorded, not fixed, M144): P3b already sends a generic refusal for `merge_forward` (`飞书 merge_forward 消息暂不支持`, Feishu merge_forward messages are not supported yet), and P8b's `feishu_unfetched` adds `(合并转发 <message_id>)` (merged forward), so the user gets two `file_refused`. ⑫ **The actor does not retry when the `/stop` marker cannot be written** (not done, M144): when writing the marker fails, the user only gets a receipt ending in `请在电脑处理` (please handle it on the computer); the actor's inbound loop keeps no request in memory and does not retry each round. ⑬ **The parent fleet is stopped after a split spawn is accepted but before it is marked `delegated`** (recorded, not fixed, supervisor M160): the spawn answer is already `accepted`, but the escalations block of the same beat has not yet moved the record from `approved` to `delegated`; if the parent fleet is then stopped **without `--resume`**, `split_settle_accepted` does not run and `split_finished` only accepts `delegated`, so no `<request>.done` follow-up is sent; a resume with `--resume` goes through S11 and does not have this problem. Future fix: let `split_finished` also accept records that are "`approved` + answer `accepted`".
